\section{Order and simplicity in source 02}\label{sse:pf:sec:order}

\begin{remark}[Orientation points of source 02]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:orientation}
Three points of source 02's taxonomy are not spelled out in the base.
(a) \emph{Pointwise fixation} of a named subcollection such as $\R$, $\On$
or $\Oz$ is a different condition from mapping that collection into
itself; the two are listed as separate rows of its table, and
\cref{sse:pf:thm:oz} shows how far apart they are for $\Oz$.
(b) The omega-map is not the exponential: $\Omega(1)=\omega$ while
$\exp(1)=e$, and one may not replace $\omega^x$ by
$\exp(x\log\omega)$ for arbitrary surreal $x$.
(c) The prefix maps are not arithmetic translations: for $p=+$,
\[
 P_+(0)=1,\qquad P_+(1)=2,\qquad P_+(-1)=\tfrac12,
\]
so $P_+$ is not additive and does not fix $0$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Foundational points \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:foundations}
Source 02's foundations section agrees with \cref{sse:sec:foundations}
and adds two observations. First, \emph{bounded does not mean small}: a
bounded interval of $\No$ contains a proper class of elements, since order
bounds do not bound birthdays; the bounded subfield of
\cref{sse:pf:thm:bounded} makes this visible even for subfields. Second,
no set-sized linear order without endpoints can fill every cut formed by
its own subsets: taking all its elements as the left side already
contradicts the proposed filling property. A universe-indexed
construction must therefore distinguish its small cut-index types from
the larger type containing all its elements.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Order homogeneity under global choice \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:orderhomog}
Under global choice, a set-sized partial order isomorphism between
subclasses of $\No$ extends to an order automorphism of $\No$. Choose a
set-like global enumeration of the sign sequences. At each ordinal stage
the partial map has set-sized domain and range. The order requirements
for the next element form a set cut; its interval of solutions is a proper
class, so the cut can be filled while avoiding the set already used. Then
perform the inverse step. The union of the set stages is a class
bijection. This is an extension statement for \emph{set-sized} partial
maps, not for arbitrary proper-class partial maps, and it avoids any appeal
to a set-sized saturated model of the wrong cardinality.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Sign blocks of ordinal length \src{02}]\label[proposition]{sse:pf:prop:block}
Let $\delta>0$ be a set ordinal, and let $D_\delta(s)$ replace every sign
of $s$ by a block of $\delta$ copies of that sign. Then $D_\delta$ preserves
and reflects both $<$ and $\pref$, fixes the empty sequence, and
\[
 \bday(D_\delta(s))=\delta\ordmulpf\bday(s).
\]
For $\delta>1$ it is proper. It need not preserve addition.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The concatenation of $\bday(s)$ blocks of length $\delta$ has length
$\delta\ordmulpf\bday(s)$. A first sign disagreement of two inputs remains a
first disagreement of the corresponding blocks, with the same comparison.
If one input terminates first, its image terminates at the start of the
corresponding block. These observations prove preservation and reflection
of both orders. When $\delta>1$, every one-sign sequence is omitted.
Finally $D_2(1)=2$, whereas $D_2(1/2)$ has signs $++--$ and value $5/4$,
not $1$.
\end{proof}
The order of the ordinal product matters: it is $\delta\ordmulpf\bday(s)$,
not $\bday(s)\ordmulpf\delta$; neither is multiplication in the surreal
field.

\begin{wnote}
This extends \cref{sse:prop:repeat} from integers $m\ge2$ to ordinals
$\delta\ge2$, and the base's further conclusions extend with it. The proof
of \cref{sse:prop:repeat} applies verbatim: an image point $z=D_\delta(x)$
is isolated by $(z\concat(-),z\concat(+))$, because any image point
extending $z$ begins its continuation with $\delta\ge2$ copies of one sign;
$D_\delta$ is cofinal and coinitial since $D_\delta(\alpha)$ is the ordinal
$\delta\ordmulpf\alpha\ge\alpha$; hence its image is discrete and it is
nowhere continuous. For $\delta\ge\omega$ the map moves even small
numbers far: $D_\omega(1)=\omega$ and $D_\omega(-1)=-\omega$.
\end{wnote}

\begin{proposition}[Fixing any prescribed set \src{02}]\label[proposition]{sse:pf:prop:localized}
For every set $A\subseteq\No$ there is a nonidentity proper embedding of
$(\No,<,\pref)$ fixing $A$ pointwise. It can also be chosen to fix every
ordinal pointwise.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Choose an ordinal $\alpha$ at least as large as every birthday in $A$,
and a sign sequence $p$ of length $\alpha\ordaddpf1$ containing a minus sign.
Inside the cone $P_p[\No]$ put
\[
 F(p\concat s)=p\concat D_2(s),
\]
and outside it put $F(x)=x$. \Cref{sse:prop:prefix,sse:pf:prop:block}
handle pairs inside the cone. Convexity of the cone handles numerical
comparisons across its boundary. A prefix of a member of the cone either
extends $p$ or is a prefix of $p$; this proves preservation and reflection
of simplicity across the boundary. The tails consisting of one sign are
omitted, so $F$ is proper, and $F(p\concat(+))=p\concat(++)$ shows it is
not the identity. No element of $A$ extends $p$, by length, and no
all-plus sequence extends a sequence containing a minus sign.
\end{proof}

\begin{wnote}
Applying \cref{sse:pf:prop:localized} to the set $A\cup\R$ (every real has
birthday at most $\omega$) gives a proper nonidentity
$(\No,<,\pref)$-embedding fixing $A\cup\R\cup\On$ pointwise: the tree-level
counterpart of the field-level \cref{sse:thm:stabilizer}. This is the home
of that statement in the report; source 07's \code{ord:localized} points
here. It does not contradict \cref{sse:prop:treebirthday}: an embedding
need not exhaust the predecessors of its image points.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[Tree-swap automorphisms \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:swap}
If numerical order is forgotten, choose a sign $\epsilon(s)\in\{-1,1\}$
for every node $s$ of the sign tree and put
\[
 \Swpf_\epsilon(x)(\beta)=\epsilon(x\restriction\beta)\,x(\beta)
 \qquad(\beta<\bday(x)).
\]
The inverse is recovered recursively from the previously recovered input
prefix. $\Swpf_\epsilon$ preserves birthdays and the prefix relation, but
generally not numerical order; the constant choice $\epsilon\equiv-1$ is
global sign reversal $x\mapsto-x$. A prefix-tree automorphism is thus a
third notion, distinct from an order automorphism and from an automorphism
of the doubly ordered structure (the base's prose in \cref{sse:sec:tree}).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[What canonical-cut preservation is not \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:cutcaveat}
Merely sending separators to separators is automatic for order embeddings
and is weaker than the hypothesis of \cref{sse:prop:cutrigid}. Likewise,
replacing the pointwise option image $f[L]$ by a set-theoretic image object
$j(L)$ changes the assertion; \cref{sse:pf:rem:cutfailure} gives a precise
example in which the two differ exactly at the critical point.
\end{remark}

\section{Hahn lifts and explicit field embeddings in source 02}\label{sse:pf:sec:hahn}

Source 02's additive and double lifts are the base's $A_f$ and
$J_f=H_{A_f}$ (\cref{sse:thm:addlift,sse:thm:doublelift}); the following
items go beyond the base.

\begin{proposition}[Multiplicativity criterion for the first lift \src{02}]\label[proposition]{sse:pf:thm:lift}
For an order embedding $f:\No\to\No$, the additive lift $A_f$ of
\eqref{sse:eq:addlift} is a unital field embedding if and only if $f$ is
additive.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
If $f$ is additive, then $f(0)=0$ and $A_f=H_f$ is a field embedding by
\cref{sse:thm:fieldlift}. Conversely, if $A_f$ is multiplicative, apply it
to two monomials:
\[
 \omega^{f(a+b)}=A_f(\omega^{a+b})=A_f(\omega^a)A_f(\omega^b)
 =\omega^{f(a)+f(b)}.
\]
Uniqueness of the monomial exponent gives $f(a+b)=f(a)+f(b)$.
\end{proof}
The first lift need not fix the real constants as elements: it sends $r$
to $r\omega^{f(0)}$. This is consistent with $\R$-linearity, which is
scalar linearity rather than preservation of $1$; the second lift repairs
it automatically.

\begin{remark}[Ranges of the lifts \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:range}
The image formula \eqref{sse:eq:addimage} gives more than truncation
closure: a Hahn-summable family of elements of $\im A_f$ has its sum in
$\im A_f$, by pulling the family back along $A_f$ (summability is
reflected). The same holds for $J_f$. None of this gives numerical
convexity or simplicity-initiality; \cref{sse:prop:convexfield,sse:thm:initial}
preclude both for a proper self-copy. Combining \eqref{sse:eq:addfixed}
with \eqref{sse:eq:fieldfixed}, the fixed field of a double lift has the
explicit two-level form
\begin{equation}\label{sse:pf:eq:twolevel}
 \Fix(J_f)=\NF{\Fix(A_f)}=\NF{\NF{\Fix(f)}},
\end{equation}
where the inner $\NF{\Fix(f)}$ is an actual additive subgroup of $\No$
used as the exponent group of the outer Hahn field.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The compression double lift}
Let $c(x)=x/(1+|x|)$ be the interval compression of
\cref{sse:prop:compression}, and write $A_c$ for its additive lift and
$J_c=H_{A_c}$ for its double lift.

\begin{theorem}[A bounded strong field copy fixing $\R$ and $\omega$ \src{02}]\label{sse:pf:thm:bounded}
$J_c$ is a proper, strongly $\R$-linear field self-embedding of $\No$ with
\begin{align*}
 J_c(r)&=r\quad(r\in\R), & J_c(\omega)&=\omega,\\
 J_c(\omega^\omega)&=\omega^{\omega^{1/2}}, & \Fix(J_c)&=\NF{\R}.
\end{align*}
Its image is bounded; source 02 records the deliberately loose bound
\begin{equation}\label{sse:pf:eq:loosebound}
 |J_c(x)|<\omega^{\omega^2+1}\qquad(x\in\No).
\end{equation}
The image is a real-closed proper-class subfield, closed under Hahn
summation and truncation, but neither initial nor convex.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\Cref{sse:thm:doublelift} gives a strongly $\R$-linear field embedding,
proper because $c$ is not onto. The first lift satisfies
$A_c(1)=\omega^{c(0)}=1$ and $A_c(\omega)=\omega^{c(1)}=\omega^{1/2}$, which
gives the displayed values. Every exponent of a nonzero $A_c(x)$ lies in
$(-1,1)$, so $|A_c(x)|<\omega^2$; the leading exponent of a nonzero
$J_c(y)$ has the form $A_c(a)$, which is below $\omega^2$, and
\eqref{sse:pf:eq:loosebound} follows. The only solution of $c(a)=a$ is
$a=0$, since for $a\ne0$ cancellation would give $1+|a|=1$; so
\eqref{sse:eq:addfixed} gives $\Fix(A_c)=\NF{\{0\}}=\R$, and
\eqref{sse:eq:fieldfixed} gives $\Fix(J_c)=\NF{\R}$. The image is isomorphic
to the real-closed field $\No$; it is a proper class because an inverse map
on a set-sized range would make the domain a set. Summation and truncation
closure are \cref{sse:pf:rem:range}; initiality or convexity would force
surjectivity by \cref{sse:thm:initial,sse:prop:convexfield}.
\end{proof}

\begin{wnote}
The bound sharpens: every exponent of a nonzero $A_c(x)$ is below $1$,
so already $|A_c(x)|<\omega$, every exponent of $J_c(y)$ is below
$\omega$, and $|J_c(y)|<\omega^\omega$ for all $y$. This is the bound of
source 04 (\cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-hahn}). Bounded field copies as such
are the base's \cref{sse:thm:boundedcopy} (fixing a prescribed set as
well) and already the collection's \lbl{opa:as:thm:parameters}, built there
from the same compression on an upper tail; what $J_c$ adds is the exact
fixed field $\NF{\R}$, compared with the base's maps
$J_{f^+_\gamma}$, whose fixed field is $\NF{\NF{\No_{\le\gamma}}}$.
By \cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy} (and \cref{sse:cor:strongstabilizer}) $J_c$
is nowhere continuous: it preserves every Hahn-summable family yet has a
uniformly discrete image, while intrinsically its image is a densely
ordered real-closed field.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[A set-sized fixed field of a class map \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:size}
Every reverse-well-ordered subset of $\R$ is countable: in its decreasing
enumeration, assign a distinct rational between consecutive members.
Hence $\NF{\R}=\Fix(J_c)$ is a \emph{set} of cardinality $2^{\aleph_0}$,
although it is the fixed field of a proper-class map. The countability
concerns real \emph{exponents}; general surreal supports need not be
countable.
\end{remark}

\begin{example}[An explicit ordinal-fixing map \src{02}]\label[example]{sse:pf:thm:ordinals}
Put $f_{\mathrm{sh}}(x)=x-1$ for $x<0$ and $f_{\mathrm{sh}}(x)=x$ for
$x\ge0$. Both pieces are increasing and the negative piece takes values
below $-1$, so $f_{\mathrm{sh}}$ is a cofinal order embedding omitting
$[-1,0)$. By \cref{sse:thm:doublelift}, $J_{f_{\mathrm{sh}}}$ is a proper
cofinal strongly $\R$-linear field embedding; it fixes every ordinal by the
argument of \cref{sse:thm:explicit} (Cantor normal forms have ordinal
exponents, fixed by $f_{\mathrm{sh}}$ and then by $A_{f_{\mathrm{sh}}}$), and
\begin{equation}\label{sse:pf:eq:shiftvalue}
 J_{f_{\mathrm{sh}}}\bigl(\omega^{\omega^{-1}}\bigr)=\omega^{\omega^{-2}}.
\end{equation}
This is the case $A=\varnothing$ of \cref{sse:thm:stabilizer} with a
different exponent map from the base's $f_-$ of \eqref{sse:eq:fminus}.
The construction is unconditional: no measurable-cardinal hypothesis lies
behind an ordinal-fixing field embedding (contrast
\cref{sse:pf:thm:large}). Replacing the negative branch by $2x$ gives the
base's automorphism $g$ (paragraph after \eqref{sse:eq:Jfixed}), a
nonidentity field \emph{automorphism} fixing every ordinal; naming the
ordinal subclass therefore does not encode the ambient set theory in the
ordered-field language.
\end{example}

\begin{remark}[Fixed fields of exponent dilations \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:dilations}
For a positive surreal $q$, the dilation
$S_q(\sum r_a\omega^a)=\sum r_a\omega^{qa}$ of \cref{sse:prop:zeroderivative}
is a strongly $\R$-linear field automorphism with inverse $S_{1/q}$. For
$q\ne1$ the exponent map $a\mapsto qa$ fixes only $0$, so
\eqref{sse:eq:fieldfixed} gives $\Fix(S_q)=\R$ exactly. For example
$S_2(\omega)=\omega^2$. These are field symmetries that are neither
exponential (\cref{sse:pf:prop:d2}) nor differential
(\cref{sse:pf:rem:d2diff}).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[On the weighted classification \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:weighted}
Source 02 states \cref{sse:thm:weighted} with ``onto exactly when $\tau$ is
onto'', which is the image formula there. It stresses that the
classification concerns a carefully specified subclass: it does not assert
that every field embedding preserves the chosen monomial section, fixes the
real coefficient section, or preserves infinite normal-form sums, and an
automorphism decomposition such as that of \cite{KKS} cannot simply be read
as an embedding decomposition, since factors requiring an inverse may cease
to exist. The maps $H_{\tau,\chi}$ preserve the relation ``is an initial
normal-form truncation of'' and transported cutoffs as in
\eqref{sse:eq:truncation}; preserving the \emph{same} numerical cutoff is a
different, much stronger requirement, and prefix truncation of signs is a
third notion.
\end{remark}

\section{Named constants, omnific integers, and limits in source 02}\label{sse:pf:sec:constants}

\begin{remark}[What a field embedding fixes automatically \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:automatic}
A unital field embedding $\sigma:\No\to\No$ fixes $\Q$, preserves and
reflects positivity (positive elements of a real-closed field are nonzero
squares), hence preserves order, and fixes every real algebraic number: the
ordered real roots of a rational polynomial are individually characterized
by their position in that finite ordered set (equivalently, they lie in the
real-closed fixed field of \cref{sse:prop:fixedgeneral}). It need not fix a
real transcendental $r$: rational comparisons show only that
$\sigma(r)-r$ is infinitesimal, which the residue map of
\cref{sse:prop:valueaction} cannot see. If $\sigma$ maps $\R$ into $\R$,
it fixes $\R$ pointwise, because an increasing field embedding of $\R$ is
determined by rational cuts. ``Fixes the residue field'' and ``fixes the
coefficient section'' are different statements.

Source 02 illustrates the motion of reals by homogeneity: for a real
transcendental $r$ and a positive infinitesimal $\varepsilon$, the
assignment $r\mapsto r+\varepsilon$ is an ordered isomorphism
$\Q(r)\to\Q(r+\varepsilon)$, and ordered-field homogeneity of $\No$ (a
set-sized back-and-forth through real closures, using choice) extends it
to a field automorphism. That homogeneity theorem is printed by source 07
as \cref{sse:an:fnd:thm:homogeneity} and is the collection's
\lbl{saut:thm:orderedhomogeneity}; the base's Taylor automorphism
\eqref{sse:eq:taylorshift} gives an explicit formula for an automorphism
moving a real by an infinitesimal.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Pointwise omnific rigidity \src{02}; after \lbl{odg:thm:fractions}]\label{sse:pf:thm:oz}
One has $\Fracpf(\Oz)=\No$. Consequently a field self-embedding fixing every
omnific integer pointwise is the identity.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For nonzero $x$ choose a positive surreal $\beta$ so large that $a+\beta>0$
for every $a\in\supp(x)$; this is possible because the support is a set:
choose $\beta$ above $0$ and above the set of negatives of its members.
Then $\omega^\beta$ and $x\omega^\beta$ are omnific integers, the latter
with zero constant coefficient, by \eqref{sse:eq:oz}. Hence
$x=(x\omega^\beta)/\omega^\beta$ is a quotient of omnific integers, and a
field embedding fixing numerator and denominator fixes $x$.
\end{proof}

\begin{wnote}
$\Fracpf(\Oz)=\No$ is \lbl{odg:thm:fractions} of
\fn{docs/surreal/omnific-diophantine-geometry} (``global monomial
clearing'', proved there for every set $S$ with one common monomial), with
the Lean declarations \code{surreal\_eq\_omnific\_fraction} and
\code{omnificSurrealIsFractionRing} in
\fn{Surreal/Foundations/OmnificSupportBounds.lean} (lines 114 and 133 at
HEAD);
source 02 does not cite it. This theorem is the report's home for the
statement; sources 05 and 06 point here. It contrasts with
\cref{sse:prop:oz}: preserving $\Oz$ as a predicate is compatible with every
proper map of \cref{sse:sec:stabilizers}, including the bounded
$J_c$, whereas fixing $\Oz$ pointwise forces the identity.
\end{wnote}

Within the strong category a weaker hypothesis already suffices: a
strongly $\R$-linear embedding fixing every monomial fixes every normal form
and is the identity. The word ``strong'' is what licenses the passage
through an arbitrary set-sized normal-form sum.

\begin{remark}[Three kinds of limit \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:limits}
By \cref{sse:prop:setdiscrete}, $1/n$ does not converge to $0$ in the full
surreal order topology: the neighborhood of radius $1/\omega$ contains no
term. This does not make $\No$ discrete; it says that no \emph{set} of
other points approaches a point at all surreal scales. Class-indexed
approximation is a separate formal notion, not a standard set-indexed net.
Three kinds of ``limit'' must therefore be kept apart: unions of increasing
prefix chains (as in the closure theory of \cite{BVH} and
\cref{sse:thm:closedtree}), Hahn sums of support-controlled families, and
numerical order-topological limits. The pair $J_c$ (bounded, summation
preserving, nowhere continuous) and $J_{f_{\mathrm{sh}}}$ (cofinal, hence
continuous by \cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy}) shows that properness is not the
obstruction to continuity; failure of cofinality is.
\end{remark}

\section{Exponential, omega-map and differential constraints in source 02}\label{sse:pf:sec:exp}

Source 02's displacement lemma, kernel lemma, exponential--valuation
rigidity, omega-map rigidity and cofinal two-map comparison are the base's
\cref{sse:lem:amplify}, \eqref{sse:eq:expkernel},
\cref{sse:thm:exprigidity,sse:thm:omegarigidity,sse:thm:expunique} with the
same proofs; source 02 attributes the displacement argument to the
repository's exponential-rigidity report \cite{RepoExp} and states that it
``is not presented as a new result of this article''. It stresses that the
bound $B$ in \cref{sse:lem:amplify} is a field element, which is decisive in
$\No$ because all finite surreals are bounded by the single surreal
$\omega$. The following items are additional.

\begin{remark}[Nontrivial exponential automorphisms exist \src{02}; imported homogeneity]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:expauto}
\Cref{sse:thm:exprigidity} does not say that every exponential automorphism
is trivial. In the o-minimal exponential structure, $\omega$ and
$2\omega$ have the same type over $\R$: every definable one-variable set
over $\R$ is eventually constant at positive infinity, so both realize the
same cut over the definably closed set $\R$. The elementary map between
their definable closures over $\R$ extends to an automorphism of
$(\No,\exp)$ by the homogeneity of the surreal exponential field for
set-sized elementary data, which source 02 imports from the literature
\cite{KKS} and does not prove. By \cref{sse:thm:exprigidity}, such an
automorphism necessarily moves some exact natural values. The argument uses
elementary exponential data, not an arbitrary partial field map.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[A value action that does not lift exponentially \src{02}; after EAR \lbl{thm:dilation}]\label[proposition]{sse:pf:prop:d2}
The field automorphism $S_2$ of \cref{sse:pf:rem:dilations} does not commute
with $\exp$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
$S_2$ induces multiplication by $2$ on the value group. If it commuted with
$\exp$, then with $\nu=\val\circ\exp$,
\[
 \nu(S_2x)=\val(S_2(\exp x))=2\nu(x)=\nu(2x),
\]
so $S_2x-2x$ would be finite for every $x$ by \eqref{sse:eq:expkernel}. At
$x=\omega$ this difference is $\omega^2-2\omega$, which is infinite.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
This is the case $r=2$ of \lbl{thm:dilation} of
\fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity}, which shows that no
rational dilation $r\ne1$ of the value group has \emph{any} exponential
lift; source 02 cites that report but not this theorem. It is printed here
once for the report (home 02, as the earliest Part containing it); source
05 proves the obstruction for every positive rational dilation $q\ne1$.
\end{wnote}
Even a cofinal, onto, strongly linear field symmetry can therefore fail the
exponential condition: an arbitrary action on the value group is not an
exponential lifting theorem.

\begin{remark}[Dilations are not differential \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:d2diff}
By \cref{sse:prop:differential}, a field embedding commuting with the
Berarducci--Mantova derivation $\partial$ (normalized by $\partial\omega=1$)
fixes $\R$ and satisfies $\sigma(\omega)=\omega+r$ with $r\in\R$. In
particular $S_2$ is not differential, since
$\partial(S_2\omega)=\partial(\omega^2)=2\omega\ne1$. These are necessary
conditions, not a classification; the derivation and its normalization
must be named precisely, since changing either changes the centralizer.
The additivity of the translation parameter $r$ is source 06's
\cref{sse:sr:exp:translationparameter}.
\end{remark}

\section{Elementarity and definability in source 02}\label{sse:pf:sec:model}

\begin{proposition}[Elementarity in three reducts \src{02}]\label[proposition]{sse:pf:prop:reducts}
Read formula by formula (\cref{sse:sec:foundations}):
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item a numerical order embedding of $\No$ is elementary in the language of
dense linear orders;
\item an ordered additive embedding of $\No$ is elementary in the language
of divisible ordered abelian groups;
\item a field embedding of $\No$ is elementary in the language of ordered
real-closed fields.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Each theory has quantifier elimination in its language, and an embedding
preserves and reflects atomic formulas. For (ii), $(\No,+,<)$ is a
divisible ordered abelian group and an ordered additive embedding preserves
and reflects the atomic order and linear relations.
\end{proof}
Items (i) and (iii) are \cref{sse:prop:elementary}; (ii) is the additional
clause. \wtag\ In particular every additive lift $A_f$ of
\cref{sse:thm:addlift}, which is an ordered additive embedding whether or
not $f$ is additive, is elementary for divisible ordered abelian groups.
The bounded $J_c$ is an elementary ordered-field self-embedding;
there is no contradiction, because the external bound in
\eqref{sse:pf:eq:loosebound} is not in the image subfield and a first-order
formula with parameters from the image cannot name it. For the exponential
language one needs in addition model completeness of the real exponential
field and the fact that $(\No,\exp)$ satisfies its theory
\cite{Wilkie,vdDE}; an exponential field embedding is then elementary in
that language, but a pure field embedding need not preserve $\exp$
(\cref{sse:pf:prop:d2}).

\begin{remark}[Adding simplicity changes the formulas \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:prefixlanguage}
An embedding of $(\No,<,\pref)$ need not be elementary. The root is
definable as the unique prefix of every element, and $P_p$ moves it for
nonempty $p$. Even a root-fixing embedding need not be elementary: $D_2$
maps the immediate prefix-successor $+$ of the root to $++$, which is no
longer an immediate prefix-successor, and the existence of an intermediate
prefix is first-order in this language. (This is the base's paragraph after
\cref{sse:prop:elementary}, with the witness made explicit.) One must also
distinguish preservation of a birthday \emph{function into an ordinal
sort} from an inequality between externally computed birthdays: an
elementary embedding of a multisorted structure may move the ordinal sort,
so it need not preserve birthdays as fixed external ordinals.
\end{remark}

Source 02 proves the definability rigidity of \cref{sse:thm:definable}
(stated there for field self-embeddings, equivalently field endomorphisms)
by o-minimal calculus instead of by the fixed field.

\begin{proof}[Second route to \cref{sse:thm:definable} \src{02}]
Let $h$ be a definable field endomorphism of the real-closed field $K$
with o-minimal expansion. A definable one-variable function is continuously
differentiable on some nonempty interval, after a finite cell decomposition
\cite{pf-Tame}; so $h$ is differentiable at some point $a$. Additivity gives
\[
 \frac{h(a+t)-h(a)}{t}=\frac{h(t)}{t},
\]
so $h$ is differentiable at $0$, and by the same identity at every point,
with one constant derivative $d$. The definable mean value theorem and
$h(0)=0$ give $h(x)=dx$ for every $x$. Since $h(1)=1$, $d=1$.
\end{proof}
For $\No$ the argument is read in the formula-by-formula o-minimal
structure, or in set-sized elementary exponential fragments; parameters do
not evade the conclusion. The map $c$ is semialgebraic, but $J_c$ invokes
the globally specified Conway normal-form operation, which is not definable
in the pure ordered field or its o-minimal exponential expansion.
``Definable in the ambient set theory'' differs from ``definable in the
surreal field structure'', and a class relation with an elementary-embedding
property differs again from a set-theoretic elementary embedding of $V$.

\section{Birthday fragments and universe changes in source 02}\label{sse:pf:sec:fragments}

\begin{proposition}[Cutoff cardinality and sign maps at cutoffs \src{02}]\label[proposition]{sse:pf:prop:cutoffsize}
Let $\kappa$ be an infinite cardinal and $\No_{<\kappa}=\{x:\bday(x)<\kappa\}$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $\No_{<\kappa}$ is a set of cardinality
\begin{equation}\label{sse:pf:eq:cutoffsize}
 |\No_{<\kappa}|=2^{<\kappa}:=\sup_{\lambda<\kappa}2^{|\lambda|}.
\end{equation}
\item For $p\in\No_{<\kappa}$ and $0<\delta<\kappa$, the prefix map $P_p$ of
\cref{sse:prop:prefix} and the block map $D_\delta$ of
\cref{sse:pf:prop:block} send $\No_{<\kappa}$ into itself.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(i) There are $2^{|\alpha|}$ sign sequences of length $\alpha$; summing
over $\alpha<\kappa$ gives \eqref{sse:pf:eq:cutoffsize}. (ii) The birthday
formulas $\bday(p)\ordaddpf\bday(s)$ and $\delta\ordmulpf\bday(s)$ stay below
the infinite initial ordinal $\kappa$, by the cardinality bounds for ordinal
sums and products of two ordinals below $\kappa$; commutativity of ordinal
arithmetic is not used.
\end{proof}
The birthday cutoff $\kappa$ and the carrier cardinality $2^{<\kappa}$
should never be silently identified.

\begin{remark}[Which cutoffs are fields: the claim of source 02]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:epsilon}
Source 02 states: ``For arbitrary ordinal cutoffs, closure under field
operations is not automatic. The precise standard theorem is that
$\No_{<\lambda}$ is a subfield exactly at epsilon-number cutoffs
$\omega^\lambda=\lambda$; these fields are real closed and admit the
inherited exponential structure'', citing van den Dries and Ehrlich together
with their erratum \cite{vdDE}. It adds that every uncountable cardinal is
such a cutoff. For an uncountable \emph{regular} $\kappa$ it records the
description \eqref{sse:eq:kappahahn} of the base; at singular cutoffs,
bounding the support cardinality is not enough, since the birthdays of the
exponents can be cofinal in the cutoff, and the exact bounded-exponent
formulation must be used instead.
\end{remark}
\begin{wnote}The claim was checked when this report was written. The
``if'' direction is van den Dries--Ehrlich with its erratum \cite{vdDE};
the collection states it in \fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/birthday-cutoffs-and-hereditary-sets}
and, as the corrected characterization, in the subsection
\lbl{found:sub:cutoffs} of \fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/foundations}.
The converse has a short self-contained proof. Suppose $\No_{<\lambda}$ is a
subfield. Since $\No_{<1}=\{0\}$ and $\No_{<2}=\{-1,0,1\}$ are not fields,
$\lambda>2$.
\emph{(i) $\lambda=\omega^{\omega^\gamma}$ for some $\gamma$.} An ordinal has
itself as birthday, and the surreal product of two ordinals is their
Hessenberg natural product, which is an ordinal at least as large as their
ordinal product. So $\alpha,\beta<\lambda$ implies $\alpha\beta<\lambda$, and the
ordinals above $2$ closed under ordinal multiplication are exactly the
$\omega^{\omega^\gamma}$.
\emph{(ii) $\lambda$ is an epsilon number.} If $\gamma=0$ then $\lambda=\omega$
and $\No_{<\omega}$ is the ring of dyadic rationals, which omits $1/3$.
Let $\gamma\ge1$ and suppose $\lambda$ is not an epsilon number; then
$\omega^\gamma<\lambda$ (if $\omega^\gamma=\lambda=\omega^{\omega^\gamma}$ then $\omega^\lambda=\lambda$),
and $\gamma$ is not an epsilon number either (otherwise
$\lambda=\omega^\gamma=\gamma$ would be one), so $1+\gamma<\omega^\gamma$. Gonshor's
sign expansion of $\omega^y$ \cite[Chapter~5]{Gonshor} replaces each minus
sign of $y$ preceded by no plus sign with $\omega$ minus signs, after a
leading plus. For $y=-\omega^\gamma$ this gives
$\omega^{-\omega^\gamma}$ the signs $(+)$ followed by $\omega\cdot\omega^\gamma$
minus signs, so its birthday is $\omega^{1+\gamma}<\omega^{\omega^\gamma}=\lambda$
(consistently, $\omega^{-1}$ has birthday $\omega$ and $\omega^{-2}$ has
birthday $\omega\cdot2$). Its inverse is the ordinal $\omega^{\omega^\gamma}=\lambda$,
of birthday $\lambda$, so $\No_{<\lambda}$ is not closed under inverses, a
contradiction. Hence $\No_{<\lambda}$ is a subfield exactly when $\lambda$ is an
epsilon number. This argument is the writer's, unrefereed, and rests on the
cited sign-expansion formula. The clause on the inherited exponential structure is the source's, citing the same paper, and was not checked here.\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[Internal sums, universes and transitive models \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:universes}
The first and double lifts work internally on $\No_{<\kappa}$ for
uncountable regular $\kappa$ provided their exponent maps take that field
into itself and only the families admitted by \eqref{sse:eq:kappahahn} are
summed (\cref{sse:prop:cutofftransfer}); an external family of size
$\kappa$ must not be passed through an internal ``$<\kappa$-strong'' map as
though no size bound existed. In a proof assistant, $\No_u$ built from the
ordinals small in a universe $u$ lives in a higher universe; a map
$\No_u\to\No_u$ is an ordinary function at that level, cut and sum index
types remain $u$-small, and moving to $\No_v$ for $v>u$ is a change of
universe, not an additional birthday inside $\No_u$. For transitive models
$M\subseteq N$, a sign sequence coded in $M$ belongs to both, but $N$ may
have additional sequences of old lengths; algebraic absoluteness
(\cref{sse:pf:lem:absolute}) does not imply that the collection of all
sequences is unchanged, nor that saturation persists in the outer model, so
the self-embeddings internal to $M$ and those available in $N$ can be
different collections. Set-sized fragments have honest endomorphism sets
and automorphism groups with ordinary satisfaction and cardinal counting;
the price is that fragment-level constructions are not automatically
compatible, and a coherent system must satisfy $f_\lambda(x)=f_\mu(x)$ on
overlaps (with output bounds) before a class union is taken.
\end{remark}

\section{Embeddings induced by large cardinals (conditional)}\label{sse:pf:sec:large}

\paragraph{Hypothesis.} Throughout this section assume an \emph{amenable
elementary embedding} $j:V\to M$, where $M$ is a transitive inner model
containing all ordinals and $j$ has critical point $\kappa$. A
normal-measure ultrapower provides this setting under the corresponding
measurable-cardinal assumption; amenability ensures that restrictions of
$j$ to sets and the set-indexed image constructions below are legitimate in
the chosen class framework. For a surreal sign sequence $s$ put
\begin{equation}\label{sse:pf:eq:Jj}
 \mathcal J_j(s)=j(s)\in\No^M\subseteq\No^V.
\end{equation}
This is an embedding induced from a universe embedding, not a claim that
$\mathcal J_j$ is elementary for membership on $V$. The collection's
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/large-cardinal-embeddings-and-normal-forms}
develops this map, its strong companion and their normal-form defects in
full \cite{pf-RepoLC}; source 02 read only that report's README and isolates
the short arguments needed for its taxonomy.

\begin{lemma}[Absoluteness of old cuts and arithmetic \src{02}; compare \lbl{lce:lem:absolute}]\label[lemma]{sse:pf:lem:absolute}
Let $M\subseteq V$ be a transitive inner model with all ordinals. Numerical
order and prefix order on $\No^M$ are restrictions of those on $\No^V$.
For sets $L,R\in M$ of old surreals with $L<R$, the simplest separator
computed in $M$ is the simplest separator in $V$. Consequently the field
operations and the omega-map on old inputs are absolute.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
First disagreement and the prefix condition are absolute. Let $z\in M$ be
$M$'s simplest separator of $L,R$. Every proper prefix of $z$ is in $M$,
being a restriction of the set function $z$ to an ordinal of $M$. If an
ambient separator $y$ failed to extend $z$, then either $y$ would be a
proper prefix of $z$, or the first disagreement would exhibit a proper
prefix of $z$ numerically between $y$ and $z$. Either way a proper prefix
of $z$ lies in the convex class of separators; it belongs to $M$,
contradicting minimality of $z$ there. Hence $z$ is a prefix of every
ambient separator. The genetic definitions of addition and multiplication
use set-sized cuts of expressions in simpler inputs; well-founded induction
on those inputs, followed by the cut assertion, proves absoluteness of the
field operations, and the recursive cut definition of $\omega^x$ gives the
omega-map in the same way. No claim about new sequences of old length is
needed.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
\lbl{lce:lem:absolute} is the same statement with canonical normal forms
and strongly summable $M$-families added; it assumes the same reals as
well, which source 02's cut-by-cut argument on old inputs does not need.
Source 02's lemma is not a conditional statement: it uses no large
cardinal.
\end{wnote}

\begin{theorem}[The induced surreal embedding \src{02}; conditional on an amenable elementary $j:V\to M$ with critical point $\kappa$; after \lbl{lce:prop:J}]\label{sse:pf:thm:large}
Under the hypothesis of this section, $\mathcal J_j$ is a proper cofinal
self-embedding of $\No$ preserving the ordered field operations, the prefix
relation and the omega-map. It satisfies
\[
 \bday(\mathcal J_j(s))=j(\bday(s)),\qquad
 \mathcal J_j(\alpha)=j(\alpha)\quad(\alpha\in\On),
\]
and fixes every sign sequence of birthday less than $\kappa$. In particular
it is not the identity and fixes $\R$ pointwise.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Elementarity transfers the defining sign-sequence relations and arithmetic
identities into $M$, and \cref{sse:pf:lem:absolute} identifies these
operations with the ambient ones. The domain of $j(s)$ is $j(\bday(s))$,
which is the birthday identity. The all-plus sequence of an ordinal goes to
the all-plus sequence of the image ordinal. If $\bday(s)<\kappa$, that
domain and every sign are fixed, so $j(s)=s$; since every finite ordinal
and $\omega$ are fixed by $j$, $\kappa>\omega$, which bounds the birthdays
of all reals.

No ordinal maps to $\kappa$: those below $\kappa$ are fixed and the others
map to ordinals at least $j(\kappa)>\kappa$. If a nonordinal sequence
mapped to the all-plus sequence $\kappa$, elementarity of the all-plus
property would make the input an ordinal. Thus $\kappa$ is omitted and the
map is proper. Finally $j(\alpha)\ge\alpha$ for ordinals $\alpha$, so the
image ordinals are cofinal in $\No$.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
This re-proves, under the same hypothesis, \lbl{lce:prop:J} of
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/large-cardinal-embeddings-and-normal-forms}
(ordered-field embedding fixing $\R$, $J(\omega^a)=\omega^{J(a)}$,
$\bday(Jx)=j(\bday x)$, prefix preservation and reflection, elementarity in
the ordered-field language); properness is \lbl{lce:cs:prop:proper} there.
Source 02 adds nothing to those statements except the explicit omitted
point $\kappa$ and the fixation of all sign sequences of birthday below
$\kappa$. It is the report's home for $\mathcal J_j$; source 06's
measurable first-failure statement (its \code{found:prop:firstfailure})
concerns the same map.
\end{wnote}
$\mathcal J_j$ is a conditional nontrivial \emph{field-and-simplicity}
embedding, which none of the unconditional block maps of
\cref{sse:pf:sec:order} supplies. Being cofinal, it is continuous
(\cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy}). It is elementary in the ordered-field
reduct, but source 02 does not infer that the inclusion
$\No^M\subseteq\No^V$ is elementary in expansions by birthdays, prefixes or
set coding, and extending the conclusion to other recursively defined
analytic operators requires their own absoluteness verification.

\begin{remark}[The first pointwise cut failure \src{02}; conditional on $j$ as above]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:cutfailure}
As a surreal ordinal, $\kappa=\{\alpha:\alpha<\kappa\mid\varnothing\}$, and
this is its canonical cut. Every option is fixed by $\mathcal J_j$, but
$\mathcal J_j(\kappa)=j(\kappa)>\kappa$; so $\mathcal J_j$ does not preserve
this cut pointwise. What elementarity preserves is the transformed
\emph{set} $j(\kappa)$ of options, not the pointwise image
$j[\kappa]=\kappa$. On the other hand, if $L$ and $R$ each have cardinality
less than $\kappa$, then $j(L)=j[L]$ and $j(R)=j[R]$ (enumerate them by
ordinals below the critical point), and elementarity with
\cref{sse:pf:lem:absolute} gives
\[
 \mathcal J_j(\{L\mid R\})=\{\mathcal J_j[L]\mid\mathcal J_j[R]\}.
\]
So $\mathcal J_j$ preserves every cut with both sides of size $<\kappa$ and
fails at a canonical cut of size $\kappa$: the least size of a pointwise
cut failure is exactly $\kappa$. This is consistent with
\cref{sse:prop:cutrigid}, whose hypothesis concerns \emph{all} set-sized
(indeed all canonical) cuts.
\end{remark}
\begin{wnote}
The collection has no statement about pointwise cut preservation of
$\mathcal J_j$; this remark is new to it. It is the cut-theoretic analogue
of the strong-summation threshold \lbl{lce:cor:firstfailure}.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[A transparent summation defect \src{02}; conditional on $j$ as above]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:summation}
Consider the legitimate normal form
\begin{equation}\label{sse:pf:eq:hkappa}
 x=\sum_{\alpha<\kappa}\omega^{-\alpha},
\end{equation}
whose support is decreasing of reverse order type $\kappa$. Each term is
fixed by $\mathcal J_j$, but applying $j$ to the whole indexed normal-form
object and using absoluteness gives
\begin{equation}\label{sse:pf:eq:summationdefect}
 \mathcal J_j(x)=\sum_{\beta<j(\kappa)}\omega^{-\beta}
 \ne\sum_{\alpha<\kappa}\mathcal J_j(\omega^{-\alpha})=x,
\end{equation}
the extra tail having leading term $\omega^{-\kappa}$. Thus $\mathcal J_j$
is not strongly additive for all set-indexed Hahn sums, although it is an
ordered-field and prefix embedding. For a summable family indexed by a set
of cardinality less than $\kappa$, an enumeration below $\kappa$ makes the
transformed index set exactly the pointwise image, and $\mathcal J_j$
commutes with the sum. The failure is an exact index-size obstruction: no
test of addition, multiplication, or even of every countable family can
detect \eqref{sse:pf:eq:summationdefect} when $\kappa$ is measurable.
\end{remark}
\begin{wnote}
\eqref{sse:pf:eq:summationdefect} is \lbl{lce:thm:witness}
($J(h_\kappa)=h_{j(\kappa)}$) and the witness of \lbl{lce:cor:firstfailure}
(the least size of a family on which $J$ fails strong summation is exactly
$\kappa$), an instance of the exact defect formula \lbl{lce:thm:defect}.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[Why Kunen's theorem is not being evaded \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:kunen}
Kunen-type inconsistency statements concern elementary embeddings
$j:V\to V$ in a suitable class theory with choice and enough replacement
\cite{pf-HKP}. Here the target is $M$, and the inclusion $M\subseteq V$ is
not assumed elementary for membership, so the composite is not a forbidden
elementary self-embedding of the universe. Conversely, a field
self-embedding such as $J_c$ does not secretly supply a $j:V\to V$: its
language lacks membership, and even with a simplicity relation added one
would have to prove an interpretation of the relevant set theory, its
preservation by the map, and the class axioms before any set-theoretic
inconsistency theorem applies.
\end{remark}

\begin{wnote}Added 4 October 2026, batch~89. The research-report collection's
\fn{SetTheory/Cardinals/docs/reports/ordinals-and-order-types/naming-elementary-embeddings}
(batch-89 manuscript~05; AI-assisted, unrefereed, not formalized) cites this
report as a control example. In its urelement kernel models every injection of
the atoms lifts to an amenable elementary self-embedding of the set-theoretic
universe that fixes every pure set, so moves no ordinal
(\lbl{nee:thm:lifts}), and naming such lifts can destroy Replacement in the
expanded language (\lbl{nee:thm:profile}). Those embeddings act on a universe
with atoms, not on the surreal field, and give no embedding of the pure-set
presentations of $\No$; nothing here changes.
\end{wnote}

\section{Lexicographic coding and iteration in source 02}\label{sse:pf:sec:connections}

\begin{proposition}[The word action \src{02}]\label[proposition]{sse:pf:prop:words}
Let $f$ be an order embedding of $\No$, $\alpha$ a set ordinal, and
$s:\alpha\to\No$ a word. Put $f_*s=f\circ s$. For words of the same length,
$f_*$ preserves and reflects the lexicographic order; it also preserves
injectivity, and so acts on injective enumerations of set-sized subsets.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The first differing coordinate of two words of the same length is unchanged
by $f_*$, and its comparison is preserved and reflected by $f$. Injectivity
of $f$ preserves injectivity of words.
\end{proof}
For variable-length words a termination convention must be chosen and
preserved, as in the sign-sequence order itself (lexicographic with a
central termination symbol). Pointwise transport is generally not a
self-map of enumerations of the entire carrier: a proper embedding omits
values, and no bijection $\alpha\to\No$ exists for a set ordinal $\alpha$.
If a well-order is represented as a relation rather than an enumeration,
transport means pushing the relation forward to the image. Source 02
offers this as a bridge to the repository's work on lexicographic
well-order codes; these coding categories must be kept separate, for the
same surjectivity and size reasons as surreal self-embeddings.

\begin{remark}[Iteration without an inverse \src{02}]\label[remark]{sse:pf:rem:iterates}
The composition law of \cref{sse:thm:doublelift} gives $J_f^n=J_{f^n}$ for
$n\in\N$. For the compression,
\[
 c^n(x)=\frac{x}{1+n|x|},
\]
by induction on $n$ for $x\ge0$ (indeed
$c(x/(1+(n-1)x))=x/(1+nx)$) and oddness. This is a controlled family of
increasingly compressed order maps and field embeddings $J_{c^n}=J_c^n$; it
asserts no convergence as $n\to\infty$ in the full order topology, which
\cref{sse:prop:setdiscrete} rules out for ordinary sequences. For
prefix-chain-preserving maps a different iteration is available, taking
unions at set limit stages when the iterates form an increasing prefix
chain (\cref{sse:thm:closedtree} and \cite{BVH}); a proper-class union need
not be a surreal, and this iteration is not interchangeable with Hahn sums
of iterates or with numerical convergence. Compare the base's iterates
$f_-^n(a)=a/(1-na)$ in \cref{sse:sec:fixed}.
\end{remark}

\section{The formalization plan of source 02}\label{sse:pf:sec:lean}

Source 02 grounds its plan in the same universe-indexed
\code{SignSequence} carrier displayed in \cref{sse:sec:foundations}
\cite{RepoSigns}; that representation directly supports the prefix and
block constructions. It read in full the simplicity module
\cite{pf-RepoSimplicity}, which supplies
\code{existsUnique\_prefix\_of\_ordConnected},
\code{existsUnique\_minimum\_birthday\_of\_ordConnected} and
\code{existsUnique\_simplest\_separator}. The last has an explicit
separator-existence premise: its uniqueness conclusion must not be cited as
an existence theorem for all cuts, and small-cut existence is a separate
construction in the library. The header of
\code{Surreal/Algebra/ExponentialProfile.lean} lists generic ordered-field
results, including displacement amplification and profile rigidity, and
names the actual surreal exponential instantiation as pending
\cite{RepoProfile}; source 02 does not promote that inventory to a new
kernel-verification claim.

\paragraph{Interfaces that prevent overstatement.} Start from an order
embedding and add separate predicates or structures rather than one opaque
``surreal embedding'' class; a single record forcing surjectivity would
lose most examples, and a bare field-homomorphism proof would lose the
distinctions needed for cuts, sums and analytic operators.

\begin{longtable}{P{48mm}P{100mm}}
\toprule
Interface & Essential field or obligation \\
\midrule
\endhead
\code{PrefixEmbedding} & $x\pref y\leftrightarrow f(x)\pref f(y)$. \\
\code{BirthdayNonIncreasing} & $\bday(f(x))\le\bday(x)$ for every input. \\
\code{SmallCutMorphism} & a quantified small index universe and equality of the two simplest-cut operations. \\
\code{StrongMap} & summability of the image family and equality of sums; index size stated in the type. \\
\code{MonomialMap} & an additive ordered exponent embedding and a positive real character, with a reconstruction theorem. \\
\code{ExactValuePreserving} & equality of natural values, not merely equivalence of comparison relations. \\
\code{ExpCommuting} & $f(\exp x)=\exp(fx)$ for the actual named exponential. \\
\code{DerivationCommuting} & equality for the specified derivation and normalization. \\
\code{InitialRange} & downward prefix closure of the image, separate from prefix preservation of the map. \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\paragraph{A dependency-ordered sequence.} (1) Pure sign combinatorics:
ordinal concatenation, block replacement, first disagreement and prefix
reflection; \cref{sse:thm:birthday} then needs only canonical options,
their birthday bounds and the simplest-separator theorem, and
\cref{sse:thm:initial} one additional small-preimage argument, whose
smallness should be proved rather than assumed in a universe-indexed
implementation. (2) Set-sized Hahn series over an abstract ordered exponent
type: transport, support equality, summability reflection, convolution
compatibility, composition and the fixed-support lemma, and only then
transport through the library's identification with actual surreal normal
forms. (3) The topology criterion, the displacement lemma and the
equal-action comparison in a generic ordered-field module; the concrete
exponential theorem as an instance whose obligations visibly include the
actual natural valuation, total exponential, monotonicity, exponential law
and the finite-kernel statement. (4) A large-cardinal module separating
the ambient set-model embedding, absoluteness, transport of set codes and
comparison with pointwise images, with two names such as
\code{imageSet} and \code{pointwiseImage} rather than one overloaded
notation, so that the critical-point discrepancy of
\cref{sse:pf:rem:cutfailure} is not discovered only after an invalid
rewrite.

\paragraph{Verification levels.} Source 02 distinguishes four
deliverables: a written proof; a Lean statement with unproved assumptions;
a kernel-checked generic theorem; and a kernel-checked instantiation for
the actual constructed surreal operations, the last two to be reported with
an axiom audit and exact dependency versions. It records that the
repository README at its snapshot reports Lean/mathlib \code{4.32.0} and an
axiom audit allowing \code{propext}, \code{Quot.sound} and
\code{Classical.choice} \cite{RepoMain}, without having run that build.

\section{Research questions of source 02}\label{sse:pf:sec:questions}

Source 02 formulates its questions so that a positive construction, a
negative theorem, or a relative-consistency result would each be progress.
They are not all claimed to be new open problems; the two published
questions it cites are pinned to KKS version 3.

\begin{question}[Beyond the strong single-term class \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:beyond}
Which field self-embeddings admit a factorization into a
coefficient-section change, a strong monomial transport, and an internal
leading-term-preserving map? Which parts of an automorphism decomposition
survive without inverses?
\end{question}
A first target is a necessary and sufficient condition for the image to
admit a compatible chosen real section and monomial section;
\cref{sse:thm:weighted} solves reconstruction only once these strong
hypotheses are available, and the non-surjective case must not be obtained
by replacing ``automorphism'' by ``embedding'' in a group factorization.
Compare the base question ``Beyond monomial images'' in
\cref{sse:sec:agenda}.

\begin{question}[Fixed fields of double lifts \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:fixedfields}
Which additive subgroups and real-closed subfields occur as $\Fix(A_f)$ and
$\Fix(J_f)$? What restrictions come from being the fixed-point class of a
numerical order embedding?
\end{question}
\Cref{sse:pf:eq:twolevel} reduces the problem to fixed sets one layer down.
$\Fix(J_c)=\NF{\R}$ and $\Fix(S_q)=\R$ show that set-sized and very small
fixed fields arise; a next step is to prescribe $\Fix(f)$ while controlling
cofinality and the omitted intervals. Compare the base question
``Prescribe the fixed field''.

\begin{question}[Cofinal versus bounded images \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:cofinalbounded}
Can image subfields of self-embeddings be classified by their cuts in
$\No$, intrinsic saturation, and ambient topological separation? Which
bounded real-closed subclasses are isomorphic to all of $\No$?
\end{question}
The continuity criterion (\cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy}) applies once an
embedding exists; it does not characterize which subfields are images. The
bounded double lift is one example, not a classification.

\begin{question}[Ordinal-fixing symmetries \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:ordinalfixing}
Describe the strong field embeddings fixing $\On$ pointwise, and
distinguish those that preserve the omega-map from those that merely fix
ordinal values.
\end{question}
\Cref{sse:pf:thm:ordinals} shows a large gap between these conditions. A
promising invariant is the action of the first lift on nonordinal
exponents occurring inside the exponent of a monomial. Compare the base
question ``$\omega$-map compatibility'' and \eqref{sse:eq:omegafunctional}.

\begin{question}[Field and prefix simultaneously \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:fieldprefix}
Determine the existence and consistency strength of nonidentity proper
embeddings of $(\No,+,\cdot,<,\pref)$ under additional natural requirements,
such as definability in set theory, fixation of all ordinals, or strong
summability.
\end{question}
The conditional $\mathcal J_j$ of \cref{sse:pf:thm:large} gives existence
without global strong summability; the block maps give unconditional
existence without arithmetic. A satisfactory theorem should identify which
extra condition closes the gap, rather than infer a Kunen contradiction
from notation. Compare the base question ``Field and simplicity
together''.

\begin{question}[Critical points recoverable from surreal structure \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:criticalpoints}
For an embedding preserving field operations and prefixes, can the least
failure of pointwise cut preservation recover a critical point or a
measure, under suitable definability and coding hypotheses?
\end{question}
For induced embeddings the failure is explicit at $\kappa$
(\cref{sse:pf:rem:cutfailure}). A converse requires an interpretation
theorem and an actual construction of a measure; a birthday discontinuity
by itself is not that construction.
\begin{wnote}
For the induced maps themselves the collection already recovers the
measure, though from a summation defect rather than a cut failure:
\lbl{lce:thm:measure} reads the normal measure derived from $j$ off one
defect coefficient of $J(h_A)$, $A\subseteq\kappa$, and
\lbl{lce:thm:measurablecriterion} characterizes measurability of $\kappa$ by
a functional on these masks without assuming $j$. The question for
abstract field-and-prefix embeddings and for cut failures remains open.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Summability thresholds \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:summability}
Under which hypotheses on an additive or field embedding does preservation
of all sums below a cardinal force preservation of all set-sized sums? When
can the first failure have a prescribed cardinality?
\end{question}
\eqref{sse:pf:eq:summationdefect} is a measurable-cardinal conditional
obstruction to an unrestricted upgrade. The repository has substantially
broader claims about these thresholds \cite{pf-RepoLC}; source 02 asks for
independent review and formalization of those claims before they are used
as a classification theorem.
\begin{wnote}
For induced maps the threshold is exactly $\kappa$
(\lbl{lce:cor:firstfailure}).
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Noncofinal exponential self-copies \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:noncofinalexp}
Does $(\No,\exp)$ admit a noncofinal field self-embedding commuting with
$\exp$ in the specified GBC framework? What additional support or
definability assumptions forbid it?
\end{question}
The bounded Hahn example $J_c$ does not answer this: it is not exponential.
Any positive example would be discontinuous in the full order topology and
would move exact natural values by \cref{sse:thm:exprigidity}. Compare the
base question ``Proper exponential copies''.
\begin{wnote}
Source 04 answers the question positively under GBC with elementary
transfinite recursion and a class satisfaction relation, which go beyond
source 02's GBC framework: \cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary} gives
bounded proper elementary self-embeddings of the exponential field. In bare
GBC it remains open here.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Faithfulness without a cofinal reference \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:faithfulness}
Can two distinct exponential field embeddings induce the same natural
value-group embedding when neither image is cofinal?
\end{question}
\Cref{sse:thm:expunique} settles the case with a cofinal reference map; the
proof shows why a bounded reference image cannot amplify a fixed
difference. Removing the hypothesis needs a new argument or a
counterexample. Compare the base question ``Remove or justify the
cofinality hypothesis''.

\begin{question}[Omega-map automorphisms \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:omegaauto}
Classify the nontrivial automorphisms commuting with the omega-map, and
determine whether an order isomorphism of the generalized epsilon numbers
extends to such an automorphism.
\end{question}
These are the directions of Questions~5.6 and~5.7 of \cite{KKS} (v3). The
conditional $\mathcal J_j$ is proper and does not settle the automorphism
question; \cref{sse:thm:omegarigidity} treats only the exact-value kernel.
Compare the base question ``$\omega$-map compatibility''.

\begin{question}[Differential--exponential centralizers \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:diffexp}
For the actual Berarducci--Mantova derivation, which field embeddings
commute with both $\partial$ and $\exp$? Which real translations of
$\omega$ extend to such maps, and with what summability properties?
\end{question}
The necessary condition $\sigma\omega=\omega+r$ (\cref{sse:prop:differential})
is not a sufficiency theorem and selects no unique extension. Compare the
base question ``Differential centralizers'' and source 06's
\cref{sse:sr:exp:translationparameter}.

\begin{question}[Minimal expressive expansions \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:expansions}
Which natural expansions of the ordered field make a nontrivial Hahn lift
internally definable? Which of them remain tame enough for useful
quantifier elimination or decidability results?
\end{question}
\Cref{sse:thm:definable} rules out o-minimal hosts. Naming support
extraction, coefficient functions, a monomial section or a discrete integer
predicate changes the problem substantially; the interpretation strength
should be measured, not guessed.

\begin{question}[Compatibility at singular cutoffs \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:singular}
Classify coherent self-embedding systems on epsilon-number birthday
fragments, especially at singular limits, and determine which give class
embeddings after union.
\end{question}
The support-cardinality condition alone fails at singular cutoffs; a
criterion must control the birthdays of transported exponents and the
agreement of the stage maps on overlaps. Compare the base question
``Universe and cutoff absoluteness'' and the club of closure ordinals in
\cref{sse:sec:formal}.

\begin{question}[A verified embedding library \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:library}
Can the transport theorem, double lift, exact fixed-support theorem,
continuity criterion, and concrete exponential rigidity instance be added
to ProveIt with minimal extra assumptions and an explicit axiom audit?
\end{question}
These form a dependency-ordered project: generic Hahn algebra,
sign-sequence rigidity, generic topology, then the actual surreal
instantiation. The first milestone should be a kernel-checked statement
distinguishing strong summation from continuity via one explicit proper
double lift. Compare the base question ``Near-term formalization''.

\begin{question}[Class principles and parameterized homogeneity \src{02}]\label[question]{sse:pf:q:classprinciples}
What is the weakest class theory needed for each global extension theorem
used in surreal embedding arguments? Can the results be recast as coherent
set-level schemes with explicit choice and recursion requirements?
\end{question}
This separates termwise constructions using only set histories from
back-and-forth procedures that choose arbitrary classes at stages, and would
make comparisons between GBC, stronger class recursion and
universe-polymorphic type theory precise. Compare the base question
``Minimal foundational strength''.

\section{Source audit and regression checks of source 02}\label{sse:pf:sec:audit}

\subsection{Repository boundary}
Source 02 pinned all repository references to snapshot
\code{af04de3fa423f8f3253ee28db9afeb7fc6171604} and read, through connected
GitHub tools: the surreal project \fn{README.md} (partly truncated; no
claim to have audited every entry or the formalization ledger);
\fn{Surreal/Foundations/SignSequence.lean} lines 1--110;
\fn{Surreal/Foundations/SignSequenceSimplicity.lean} in full;
\fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity/README.md} (theorem
inventory and status; partly truncated, not an audit of the whole
manuscript); \fn{Surreal/Algebra/ExponentialProfile.lean} lines 1--160
(not kernel-checked); and lines 1--170 of the README of
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/large-cardinal-embeddings-and-normal-forms}
(the 109-page article itself was not read, so its equalizer, independence
and measurable-spectrum assertions were not imported). Its repository
claims were checked when this report was written:

\begin{longtable}{P{80mm}P{68mm}}
\toprule
Claim of source 02 & Verdict \\
\midrule
\endhead
\code{ExponentialProfile.lean} leaves the actual surreal instantiation pending & true (module header) \\
\code{existsUnique\_simplest\_separator} has an existence premise; cut filling is separate & true (\fn{SignSequenceSimplicity.lean}:72; existence in \fn{SignSequenceCutOperation.lean}, \code{exists\_separator\_of\_small\_sets}) \\
\fn{existsUnique_prefix_of_ordConnected} and \fn{existsUnique_minimum_birthday_of_ordConnected} exist & true (lines 41 and 52, at the pin and at HEAD) \\
the sign carrier is universe indexed; bounded fragments small, carrier not small & true (\fn{SignSequence.lean}) \\
README reports Lean/mathlib \code{4.32.0} and the three-axiom audit & true (README lines 117 and 128 at the pin) \\
the exponential-rigidity report resolves KKS Question~5.4 (v3) and its generic formalizations do not yet instantiate the surreal exponential & true (EAR \lbl{thm:no}, \lbl{cor:question54}; header of the Lean module) \\
the large-cardinal report develops $J$ and its normal-form defects & true (\lbl{lce:prop:J}, \lbl{lce:thm:defect}) \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
No claim of source 02 says the collection lacks something it has; its
omissions are the uncited re-proofs listed in the note of
\cref{sse:pf:sec:glance}.

\subsection{External literature boundary}
Source 02 checked the normal-form and cutoff results against van den
Dries--Ehrlich, in particular its set/class conventions, its
Proposition~4.7 and its exponential-field discussion, and inspected the
published erratum, which corrects a support-order bound while retaining the
main results \cite{vdDE}. Bagayoko--van der Hoeven (read as
arXiv:2305.02001v1) was used for the setting of surreal substructures,
concatenation and prefix-chain closure, not as a claim that the field lifts
preserve simplicity \cite{BVH}. Kaplan--Krapp--Serra was read as
arXiv:2509.22374v3 (submitted 23 April 2026; PDF dated 27 April 2026);
printed page~10 was checked for Propositions~5.1, 5.2, 5.5 and
Questions~5.4, 5.6, and the source does not depend on any proposed
infinite-support example of a nonstrong automorphism from that paper
\cite{KKS}. Berarducci--Mantova was checked for the derivation's constant
field, exponential compatibility and $\partial\omega=1$ \cite{BM}. The Kunen
discussion follows Hamkins--Kirmayer--Perlmutter \cite{pf-HKP}. Model
completeness \cite{Wilkie} and o-minimal calculus \cite{pf-Tame} are
imported, not reproved.

\subsection{Finite regression checks}\label{sse:pf:subsec:checks}
The delivered script ships as \fn{code/02-paradox-free-validate.py}
(Python 3.10+, standard library only, exact rational arithmetic, random seed
20261003), with its recorded output
\fn{data/02-paradox-free-validation_results.json}: status PASS, 198{,}220
assertions; a fresh rerun when this report was written reproduced the
output byte for byte up to line endings. Its 29 groups test, on all sign
words of length at most 6, first-disagreement comparison against dyadic
values, and order and prefix preservation by $P_+$, $P_{+-}$, $D_2$, $D_3$
and the localized map of \cref{sse:pf:prop:localized} (with $p=+-+$),
including its fixation of short words and of all-plus words; on a rational
grid, the range, inverse, unique fixed point, order and iterates
$c^n(x)=x/(1+n|x|)$ of the compression; on random finite supports,
additivity, composition and the fixed-support criterion of the compression
lift, multiplicativity of the lift by $a\mapsto 2a$, and the counterexample
showing that the compression lift is not multiplicative; on Laurent
polynomials under the substitution $t\mapsto2t$, the twisted displacement
identity \eqref{sse:eq:twisted}, a counterexample to its untwisted form,
and explicit two-probe amplification witnesses; and the explicit values
$D_2(1/2)=5/4$ and $P_+(-1)=1/2$. The script writes
\fn{validation_results.json} beside itself (under its original name), so
it should be rerun on a copy. The shipped
\fn{code/02-paradox-free-build.sh} also compiles the source's own
\fn{surreal_self_embeddings.tex}, which is not shipped; its data record
\fn{data/02-paradox-free-build_validation.json} carries three SHA-256 values
of the delivered files (verified correct at intake) and is a provenance
record, not a manifest.

A successful run checks finite algebra and the implemented formulas only.
It cannot prove reverse-well-order preservation at all ordinal lengths,
test a proper-class image, verify a measurable-cardinal hypothesis, or
establish global strong summability from finite samples; the written
proofs carry those conclusions.

\section{Status and non-claims of source 02}\label{sse:pf:sec:status}

Source 02 states, in its article, README and \fn{02-paradox-free-PROOF_STATUS.md}:
\begin{itemize}
\item ``Proved here'' means that a mathematical proof is supplied; it does
not mean historically new, independently refereed, or Lean-checked. No
priority claim is made for the deductions assembled; the new combinations
and examples have not received independent specialist review.
\item The large-cardinal construction is conditional on its explicitly
stated embedding hypothesis (an amenable elementary $j:V\to M$); it does not
assert an elementary membership embedding $V\to V$, and a field-embedding
conclusion is kept distinct from an elementary-embedding conclusion in an
expanded surreal language.
\item The exponential--valuation argument is attributed to the repository's
exponential-rigidity research and ``is not presented as a new result''. The
repository's generic algebraic formalizations do not, by themselves,
provide a machine-checked instance for the actual surreal exponential; that
bridge remains a formalization task.
\item The taxonomy is an interpretation framework, not a classification of
every expansion of the surreal field; \cref{sse:thm:weighted} classifies
only a specified subclass.
\item The research questions are not all claimed to be new open problems.
\item The finite checks are regression tests, not a formal verification of
the transfinite theorems; the double-lift theorem and its infinite-support
consequences rest on the written proofs.
\item No new Lean file is included and no Lean or Mathlib build was
executed; module headers are reported as source inventory, not re-verified
kernel results. The entire repository was not audited.
\item The broader threshold claims of the large-cardinal report need
independent review before use as a classification theorem.
\item Standard structural results on the surreal field, its exponential,
its derivation, model completeness and o-minimal calculus are imported from
the literature, as is the homogeneity of the exponential field used in
\cref{sse:pf:rem:expauto}.
\item Delivery: the PDF compiled without unresolved references or LaTeX
warnings and all 36 pages were visually reviewed (provenance only; the PDF
is not shipped).
\end{itemize}
% ================= source 04
% ===================================================================
% Part III: source 04 (Surreal_Self_Embeddings_Atlas), sub-prefix sse:at:
% ===================================================================
\part{Source 04: A Structural and Foundational Atlas}\label{sse:part:at}

\section{Source 04 at a glance}\label{sse:at:sec:glance}

Source 04 is \emph{Self-Embeddings of the Surreal Numbers: A structural and
foundational atlas}, subtitled ``Order, simplicity, Hahn series,
exponentiation, model theory, and universe-sensitive formalization'', dated
3 October 2026 and headed ``Research monograph''. Its title page reads
``Research article prepared with ChatGPT'' (also its PDF author field); it
is the only one of the six sources that names the tool used. It pins
\code{VladimirReshetnikov/ProveIt} at commit
\code{a006a77a0a8bacdffe089b70d3b963b17228a089} \cite{at-Repo}. The delivered
PDF has 35 pages; the source has 54 numbered environments (14 of them
research questions), two appendices (a proof-obligation ledger and a
compact audit of common invalid arguments), a README, a source audit, and
a finite regression script. Its foundational setting is GBC (G\"odel--Bernays
class theory with global choice) for class maps, a ZFC definable-class
reading for the explicit support substitutions, and \emph{GBC plus
elementary transfinite recursion (ETR), with a fixed class satisfaction
relation}, for its model-theoretic section only
(\cref{sse:at:sec:model}); it treats regular-cardinal cutoffs and
universe-polymorphic types separately.

Its distinctive contributions, all printed in this Part, are: the explicit
bounded double lift $J_c$ with bound $\omega^\omega$
(\cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-hahn}); a proper cofinal embedding with fixed field
exactly $\R$ (\cref{sse:at:ex:fgap}); the exact iterated-image formula
$K_\infty(J_f)=\NF{\NF{\bigcap_nf^n(\No)}}$ (\cref{sse:at:thm:coreformula});
bounded-image and proper cofinal \emph{elementary} self-embeddings of every
o-minimal class expansion of the surreal field, including the surreal
exponential field, under GBC+ETR
(\cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary,sse:at:thm:cofinal-elementary}); the
abstract exponential value-displacement and cofinal pair-rigidity theorems
(\cref{sse:at:thm:exp-displacement,sse:at:thm:pair-rigidity}); the exact
criterion for a monomial lift $H_g$ to commute with $\exp$
(\cref{sse:at:prop:expcriterion}); the failure of nontrivial rational exponent
dilations to be differential (\cref{sse:at:prop:ratdil}); the converse
classification of conjugation-compatible surcomplex extensions
(\cref{sse:at:prop:complex}); and a five-stage Lean integration plan
(\cref{sse:at:sec:lean}). Its conclusion section is expository: no single
rigidity answer exists; support images control explicit field embeddings,
cofinality controls ambient continuity, set-cut saturation controls
elementary extensions, and the twisted Leibniz identity controls
exponential growth rigidity.
