\section{Foundational setting and small examples of source 04}\label{sse:at:sec:setting}

Source 04 reads concrete constructions in ZFC through defining formulas
with set parameters, states assertions about arbitrary class maps in GBC,
and uses GBC+ETR only in \cref{sse:at:sec:model}; it calls that foundation
``a sufficient foundation, not a minimality claim'' and refers to the
relation between ETR and class truth as a genuine foundational issue
\cite{at-GitmanHamkins,at-ClassForcing}. Its name for the set-cut
interpolation property of $\No$ is used below:
\begin{equation}\label{sse:at:eq:eta}
 L,R\text{ sets},\quad L<R\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\exists x\ (L<x<R)
 \qquad(\text{the }\eta_{\On}\text{ interpolation property}).
\end{equation}
It distinguishes three notions that the base treats more briefly
(\cref{sse:sec:modeltheory}): an \emph{external} set-theoretically definable
map may mention birthdays, normal forms and a global well-order; an
\emph{internally definable} map in $(\No,+,\cdot,<,\exp)$ is given by one
first-order formula with finitely many parameters; a \emph{class map} in GBC
need not be definable without class parameters. ``Elementary'' always refers
to a specified language, and a field self-embedding is not an elementary
embedding of $(V,\in)$ (as in base \cref{sse:sec:foundations}).

\begin{remark}[Small examples \src{04}]\label[remark]{sse:at:rem:examples}
(i) For $p=+$ the prefix map has $P_+(0)=1$, $P_+(1)=2$, $P_+(-1)=\tfrac12$;
it does not preserve $0$, so there is no conflict with field rigidity. Its
image of the empty ambient cut is $1$, whereas the empty cut is $0$ in
$\No$: a prefix map preserves the \emph{relative} simplest-between operation
of its image, not the ambient one (compare the base remark on relative cuts
after \cref{sse:prop:cutrigid}).
(ii) The block map $D_\delta$ (each sign repeated $\delta$ times, $\delta$ a
nonzero ordinal; home 02, \cref{sse:pf:prop:block}) fixes $0$ and commutes
with negation; $D_2(1)=2$, so it is not unital. Prefix and block maps refute
the assertion that every order-and-simplicity self-embedding is the identity.
(iii) The first lift $A_f$ satisfies $A_f(r)=r\,\omega^{f(0)}$ for $r\in\R$, so
it fixes $\R$ pointwise exactly when $f(0)=0$. For the compression $c$ the
first lift $A_c$ fixes $1$ but is not multiplicative:
$A_c(\omega^2)=\omega^{c(2)}=\omega^{2/3}\ne\omega=A_c(\omega)^2$, because
$c(2)=2/3\ne1=2c(1)$. A single exponent relocation by an order map is not yet
a field embedding; the second lift is the crucial step.
\end{remark}

\section{Hahn lifts: a bounded copy, fixed fields, and cores}\label{sse:at:sec:hahn}

\subsection{An explicit bounded field copy}
The following is the same map as source 02's bounded embedding
(\cref{sse:pf:thm:bounded}), with an exact image formula and a smaller
explicit bound; base \cref{sse:thm:boundedcopy} with $A=\varnothing$ gives a
different map with the bound $\omega^{\omega^{\gamma+1}}$, $\gamma>0$.

\begin{theorem}[A bounded-image field self-embedding \src{04}; after \lbl{opa:as:thm:parameters}]
\label{sse:at:thm:bounded-hahn}
Let $c(x)=x/(1+|x|)$ and $J_c=H_{A_c}$. Then $J_c$ is a proper strongly
$\R$-linear ordered-field self-embedding with
\begin{equation}\label{sse:at:eq:boundJ}
 \im(J_c)=\NF{\Gamma_c},\qquad
 \Gamma_c=\NF{(-1,1)},\qquad
 |J_c(x)|<\omega^\omega\quad(x\in\No).
\end{equation}
It fixes every real and fixes $\omega$, but
$J_c(\omega^\omega)=\omega^{\omega^{1/2}}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The image description is \eqref{sse:eq:addimage} followed by
\eqref{sse:eq:fieldimage}. If $0\ne\gamma\in\Gamma_c$ has leading exponent
$a<1$, then $|\gamma|<\omega$: the ratio $\omega/\omega^a=\omega^{1-a}$
dominates every positive real coefficient. Thus every element of $\Gamma_c$
lies between $-\omega$ and $\omega$. If $0\ne y\in\NF{\Gamma_c}$ has leading
exponent $\gamma<\omega$, the same comparison gives $|y|<\omega^\omega$. The
image is therefore proper. Since $c(0)=0$, $A_c(1)=1$ and
$J_c(\omega)=\omega$. On the other hand $A_c(\omega)=\omega^{c(1)}=\omega^{1/2}$,
which gives the displayed moved value.
\end{proof}

The image is a real-closed field, being isomorphic to $\No$, yet it is bounded
in the ambient field. Applying the bound to $1/y$ shows that every nonzero
image element satisfies $|y|>\omega^{-\omega}$: the image has a uniform
ambient gap around $0$, despite being densely ordered internally. (The
repository's parameter theorem \lbl{opa:as:thm:parameters} has the same kind
of two-sided bound $\Omega^{-1}<|y|<\Omega$ for its discrete copy.) Since the
only fixed point of $c$ is $0$, \eqref{sse:eq:fieldfixed} gives
$\Fix(J_c)=\NF{\R}$ (source 04's Example~5.3; printed by source 02): the
real-exponent Hahn subfield, not merely $\R$; $\omega^r$ is fixed for every
real $r$, although $\omega^\omega$ is not. By \cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy},
$J_c$ is nowhere continuous, while it preserves all strong sums.

\begin{wnote}
The bound $\omega^\omega$ is smaller than source 02's bound for the same map
and than the base's bound for its (different) bounded map; it is not claimed
to be optimal. For instance $\omega^{\omega-1}$ also bounds the image, since
$\omega^a<\omega-1$ for every exponent $a<1$.
\end{wnote}

\subsection{A proper cofinal embedding with fixed field exactly the reals}
\begin{example}[A proper embedding with fixed field exactly $\R$ \src{04}]\label[example]{sse:at:ex:fgap}
Let
\[
 f_{\mathrm{gap}}(x)=
 \begin{cases}x+1,&x<0,\\x+2,&x\ge0.\end{cases}
\]
This is an increasing injection onto $(-\infty,1)\cup[2,\infty)$ with no fixed
points. Hence $J_{f_{\mathrm{gap}}}$ is proper (\cref{sse:thm:doublelift}) and,
by \eqref{sse:eq:addfixed} and \eqref{sse:eq:fieldfixed},
\[
 \Fix(J_{f_{\mathrm{gap}}})=\NF{\NF{\varnothing}}=\NF{\{0\}}=\R.
\]
Its image is cofinal: the inner exponent group contains monomials
$\omega^{f_{\mathrm{gap}}(a)}$ with arbitrarily large exponents, and the outer
field then contains arbitrarily large monomials. By
\cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy} this is a proper \emph{continuous} field
embedding whose fixed field is precisely the designated real field, in
contrast to the larger fixed fields $\NF{\R}$ of $J_c$ and $\NF{B}$ of
$J_-$ (\eqref{sse:eq:Jfixed}).
\end{example}

\subsection{The iterated-image core of a double lift}
Base \cref{sse:prop:core} is stated in source 04 for any injective class
self-map $j:X\to X$, with the same proof: $j$ restricted to
$K_\infty(j)=\bigcap_{n\in\N}j^n(X)$ is a bijection, and this is the largest
subclass on which $j$ restricts to a bijection. The quantifier over $n$ is
set-sized, so no collection of class images is formed.

\begin{theorem}[Iterated-image formula \src{04}]\label{sse:at:thm:coreformula}
For every increasing injection $f:\No\to\No$,
\begin{equation}\label{sse:at:eq:coreformula}
 K_\infty(J_f)=
 \NF{\NF{\textstyle\bigcap_{n\in\N} f^n(\No)}}.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The composition law gives $J_f^n=J_{f^n}$ (\cref{sse:thm:doublelift}). For
any set-indexed family of classes $(A_i)$, the support definition gives
$\bigcap_i\NF{A_i}=\NF{\bigcap_iA_i}$. Apply this twice to the image formula
$\im J_{f^n}=\NF{\NF{f^n(\No)}}$.
\end{proof}
This contains base \eqref{sse:eq:hcore} (one level) and \eqref{sse:eq:Jcore}
(the case $f=f_-$) as instances. For the compression, direct algebra gives
\[
 c^n(x)=\frac{x}{1+n|x|},\qquad c^n(\No)=(-1/n,1/n)\quad(n\ge1).
\]
With $\mathfrak m$ the additive group of all infinitesimals (including $0$),
\begin{equation}\label{sse:at:eq:corec}
 K_\infty(J_c)=\NF{\NF{\mathfrak m}}.
\end{equation}
This is strictly larger than $\Fix(J_c)=\NF{\R}$: for $\varepsilon=\omega^{-1}$
and $z=\omega^{\omega^\varepsilon}$ the outer exponent $\omega^\varepsilon$
lies in $\NF{\mathfrak m}$, so $z$ is in the core, but $\omega^\varepsilon\notin\R$,
so $z$ is not fixed. Thus a globally non-surjective embedding can act as a
nontrivial automorphism on the part surviving every finite inverse stage.
No limit iterate $j^\lambda$ is defined; a transfinite iteration would need
an additional limit rule, and an intersection of finite images is not such
a rule by itself (compare the base comment after \cref{sse:prop:core}).

\section{Automatic preservation, the Taylor twist, and value actions}\label{sse:at:sec:automatic}

Source 04's Propositions~6.1--6.3 are base results (correspondence table).
Its comment on Proposition~6.2 is kept: it is incorrect to argue that the
rational cut of a real forces its image to equal that real \emph{inside}
$\No$, since that cut also has infinitesimally perturbed realizations; the
Taylor twist $T$ of \eqref{sse:eq:taylorshift} is an explicit witness. The
twist is a formal Taylor construction (no analytic convergence of a possibly
discontinuous derivation of $\R$ is asserted), and it is not exponential, by
\cref{sse:at:thm:exp-displacement}.

\begin{remark}[Value actions of the explicit maps \src{04}]\label[remark]{sse:at:rem:tau}
The value action of \cref{sse:prop:valueaction} is
$\tau_j(a)=\val(j(\omega^{-a}))$. Equivalently, factor a normal form into its
leading monomial, its real leading coefficient, and a factor $1+\varepsilon$;
the last two and their images are valuation units, which gives
$\val(jx)=\tau_j(\val x)$ directly. By \eqref{sse:eq:fieldvalue},
$\tau_{H_g}=g$ for every ordered additive $g$, hence
\begin{equation}\label{sse:at:eq:tauJ}
 \tau_{J_f}=A_f,
\end{equation}
and $\tau_T=\id$ for the Taylor twist. ``Valuation-preserving'' is
ambiguous: preservation of Archimedean comparisons is automatic, whereas
$\val(jx)=\val(x)$ for all $x$ is the much stronger pointwise assertion that
produces exponential rigidity.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Value-group form of the continuity criterion \src{04}; after \lbl{opa:as:thm:topology}]
\label[proposition]{sse:at:thm:continuity}
For a field self-embedding $j$ of $\No$, the image $j(\No)$ is cofinal if and
only if $\tau_j(\No)$ is cofinal in $\No$; so by \cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy}
both are equivalent to continuity of $j$. For an increasing injection $f$,
\[
 J_f\text{ is continuous}\iff f(\No)\text{ is cofinal in }\No .
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The image $\tau_j(\No)$ is an additive subgroup, hence symmetric. If it is
cofinal and $y\ne0$ is given, choose $a$ with $\tau_j(a)<\val(y)$ and put
$x=\omega^{-a}$; then $\val(jx)=\tau_j(a)<\val(y)$, so the positive element
$jx$ exceeds $|y|$. Conversely, if $j(\No)$ is cofinal and $\beta$ is given,
choose $x$ with $jx>\omega^{\beta+1}$; then $\val(jx)\le-(\beta+1)$, and
$\tau_j(-\val x)=-\val(jx)>\beta$. For the double lift, \eqref{sse:at:eq:tauJ}
gives $\tau_{J_f}(\No)=A_f(\No)=\NF{f(\No)}$, which is cofinal exactly when
$f(\No)$ is: if the exponents are bounded above by $b$, every element has
absolute value below $\omega^{b+1}$; if they are cofinal, the monomials are.
\end{proof}
Consequently $J_c$ is strongly summation-preserving but discontinuous,
whereas $J_-$ and $J_{f_{\mathrm{gap}}}$ are proper continuous topological
field embeddings. The bounded image of \cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-hahn} is
discrete as a subspace of $\No$ but has a nondiscrete, densely ordered
\emph{intrinsic} order topology (radii from the subfield only); these are
different topologies. The equivalence of cofinality, coinitiality of
$j(\No_{>0})$ in $\No_{>0}$, continuity at $0$, topological embedding, and
uniform discreteness otherwise, for any ordered field, is source 04's
Theorem~7.1, printed by source 07 (\cref{sse:an:top:criterion}) and for $\No$
by the base.

\begin{remark}[Strong sums are not topological limits \src{04}]\label[remark]{sse:at:rem:strongsum}
The Hahn sum $\sum_{n\ge0}\omega^{-n}$ exists, but its finite partial sums do
not converge to it in the full order topology: every nonzero tail dominates
$\omega^{-\omega}$. Strong summation is a support-and-coefficient operation.
Sequential continuity is an ineffective test on the full class: by
\cref{sse:prop:setdiscrete} every function preserves the (eventually
constant) convergent set-indexed nets, including the discontinuous $J_c$.
The statement is relative to the full class and to set-sized index sets; a
larger-universe net need not be small relative to the original universe.
\end{remark}

\section{Elementary self-embeddings of o-minimal surreal structures (under GBC+ETR)}\label{sse:at:sec:model}

\begin{overview}
\textbf{Hypothesis for this whole section (except the final second route).}
Work in GBC+ETR, with global choice included in GBC, and fix a class
satisfaction relation for the class structure $\Mat$ below. Every lemma,
theorem and remark of this section is proved only under this hypothesis,
which source 04 calls ``a sufficient foundation, not a minimality claim''.
Source 05 has parallel theorems and points here
(\cref{sse:co:thm:bounded-elementary}).
\end{overview}

\subsection{The class-model setting}
Let $\Mat$ be a class structure on $\No$ in a set-sized language, expanding
an ordered field, whose complete first-order theory $\That(\Mat)$ is
o-minimal. The hypotheses include the ordered surreal field and the natural
surreal exponential field
\[
 \Mat_{\exp}=(\No,0,1,+,-,\cdot,<,\exp).
\]
For the latter, the model-of-$\That(\R_{\exp})$ assertion uses van den
Dries--Ehrlich and Wilkie \cite{vdDE,Wilkie}; it is not inferred from the
existence of some unspecified exponential operation. Standard consequences
of o-minimality for ordered-field expansions are used: unary definable sets
are finite unions of points and intervals; endpoints over parameters $A$
belong to $\dclat(A)$; definable Skolem functions exist; definable closure
has the exchange property \cite{pf-Tame}. The proofs spell out how these
set-sized facts are used with the class order, rather than invoking an
undefined compactness theorem for proper-class languages.

\begin{lemma}[Saturation over every set \src{04}; under GBC+ETR with a class satisfaction relation]
\label[lemma]{sse:at:lem:setsaturation}
Every complete one-variable type over a set of parameters in $\Mat$ is
realized in $\Mat$. Consequently every set-sized partial elementary map from a
set model of $\That(\Mat)$ into $\Mat$ extends to an elementary embedding of
that set model.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For a set $A$, the class $\dclat(A)$ is a set: formulas form a set and each
uniquely specified value is obtained by replacement. An algebraic
one-variable type is realized by a point of $\dclat(A)$, since finite
definable sets in a linear order have individually definable elements. A
nonalgebraic type is determined by its strict cut in $\dclat(A)$: every unary
definable set over $A$ has its finitely many endpoints there, so the cut
determines membership in every such set. The two sides are sets and are
strictly separated, so by \eqref{sse:at:eq:eta} a surreal lies strictly
between them, and it realizes the type.

To extend a partial elementary map, well-order the remaining domain of the
set model. At a successor stage transport the complete type of the next
element over the already mapped set and realize it by the preceding
argument. At a limit stage take the union. The domain at each stage is a
set, and the final map is elementary because each formula uses only finitely
many parameters.
\end{proof}

Set-sized elementary submodels containing a prescribed set exist by the usual
Skolem-hull construction (in the stated foundation). In an o-minimal
ordered-field expansion, $\dclat(X)$ of an elementary submodel together with
extra parameters is an elementary submodel, because definable Skolem
functions give the Tarski--Vaught witness property; in particular
$\dclat(X)=X$ when $X\prec\Mat$.

\subsection{Bounded elementary images}
\begin{theorem}[Bounded-image elementary self-embeddings \src{04}; under GBC+ETR with a class satisfaction relation]
\label{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary}
Assume GBC+ETR and a class satisfaction relation for $\Mat$. Let
$K_0\prec\Mat$ be set-sized, and let $b$ be a positive surreal with $k<b$ for
every $k\in K_0$. There is an elementary class self-embedding
$j:\Mat\to\Mat$ fixing $K_0$ pointwise such that
\begin{equation}\label{sse:at:eq:modelbound}
 |j(x)|<b\qquad(x\in\No).
\end{equation}
In particular, under the same hypothesis, the natural surreal exponential
field has bounded-image proper elementary self-embeddings fixing every real,
with any prescribed positive infinite $b$ as a bound on the absolute values
of the image.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Extension step.} Suppose $X\prec\Mat$ is a set-sized elementary
submodel and $\pi:X\to\Mat$ is elementary, with $Y=\pi(X)$ and every element
of $Y$ below $b$. Let $X'\prec\Mat$ be any set-sized elementary submodel
containing $X$ and an additional source element to be processed. Use
ordinary set-sized compactness on the elementary diagram of $X'$ together
with a new constant $z$ and the inequalities $z>x'$ for all $x'\in X'$: every
finite part is realized in $X'$ by a sufficiently large element. Thus there
is a set model $\Nat\succeq X'$ with $z>X'$. Over the elementary submodel $X$,
the element $z$ realizes the unique type at positive infinity; o-minimality
ensures uniqueness, since each unary definable set over $X$ is eventually all
or eventually empty, with finite endpoints in $X$. As $Y\prec\Mat$ and
$\dclat(Y)=Y<b$, the element $b$ has exactly the corresponding type over $Y$.
Therefore $\pi\cup\{z\mapsto b\}$ is a partial elementary map from $\Nat$
into $\Mat$. By \cref{sse:at:lem:setsaturation}, extend it to an elementary
embedding of $\Nat$. Its restriction $\pi'$ to $X'$ extends $\pi$, and
$\pi'(x')<b$ for every $x'\in X'$ because $x'<z$ in $\Nat$.

\emph{Recursion.} Choose a set-like global enumeration of $\No$ by the
ordinals. Start with $X_0=K_0$ and $\pi_0=\id_{K_0}$. At each successor stage
apply the extension step to include the next enumerated source element. At
each set-ordinal limit take the union of the elementary submodels and the
compatible maps; it is again a set-sized elementary submodel and an
elementary map, and its image is still below $b$. The class recursion and
its union are available in the stated foundation. The resulting class map
$j$ is total and elementary with $j(x)<b$ for all $x$; applying this to $-x$
and using $j(-x)=-j(x)$ gives \eqref{sse:at:eq:modelbound}. The image omits
$b$, so it is proper.

For the final assertion take $K_0=\R_{\exp}$, an elementary substructure of
the natural surreal exponential field; every positive infinite $b$ lies above
all of $K_0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[What the theorem does not say \src{04}]\label[remark]{sse:at:rem:notsay}
The constructed embedding is not claimed to preserve strong Hahn sums or the
canonical monomial cross-section. It cannot be ambient-continuous, by
\cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy}. It is not a first-order definable function in
the exponential-field language, by \cref{sse:thm:definable}. Fixing a
set-sized elementary subfield is compatible with boundedness; fixing a
cofinal subclass such as all ordinals is not.
\end{remark}
The protected point $b$ is not part of the source structure being embedded at
that step: it is the image of an auxiliary element $z$ placed above a
\emph{set} model. Trying to place $z$ above the entire proper class $\No$
would be invalid and is unnecessary.

\begin{wnote}
Under GBC+ETR this answers the base question ``Proper exponential copies''
(\cref{sse:sec:agenda}) in its bounded-range clause: bounded ranges occur for
the actual Gonshor exponential. The maps are not strongly additive and not
monomial; the strong version is \cref{sse:at:q:compression} below. The bounded
\emph{field} copies of \cref{sse:thm:boundedcopy,sse:at:thm:bounded-hahn} are
not exponential.
\end{wnote}

\subsection{Proper cofinal elementary images}
\begin{lemma}[Omission-preserving extension \src{04}; under GBC+ETR with a class satisfaction relation]
\label[lemma]{sse:at:lem:omission}
Let $\pi:X\to Y\subseteq\Mat$ be an elementary map between set-sized
elementary submodels, and let $b\notin Y$. For every set-sized elementary
$X'\prec\Mat$ containing $X$, there is an elementary extension
$\pi':X'\to\Mat$ whose image still omits $b$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The type $\tpat(b/Y)$ is nonalgebraic, because $\dclat(Y)=Y$ and finite
definable sets have individually definable elements. Transport it through
$\pi^{-1}$ to a nonalgebraic type $p$ over $X$. The elementary diagram of $X'$
together with $p(z)$ and $z\ne x'$ for every $x'\in X'$ is consistent: a finite
conjunction from $p$ defines an infinite set already in $X$, which cannot be
covered by finitely many forbidden points of $X'$. Compactness produces a set
elementary extension $\Nat\succeq X'$ with $z\notin X'$ realizing $p$. The
map $\pi\cup\{z\mapsto b\}$ is partial elementary; extend it into $\Mat$ by
\cref{sse:at:lem:setsaturation}. Injectivity and $z\notin X'$ ensure that the
restriction to $X'$ does not hit $b$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proper cofinal elementary self-embeddings \src{04}; under GBC+ETR with a class satisfaction relation]
\label{sse:at:thm:cofinal-elementary}
Assume GBC+ETR and a class satisfaction relation for $\Mat$. For every
set-sized $K_0\prec\Mat$ and every $b\in\No\setminus K_0$ there is a cofinal
elementary class self-embedding of $\Mat$ fixing $K_0$ pointwise and
omitting $b$ from its image.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Maintain an elementary map $\pi:X\to Y$ between set-sized elementary
submodels, extending $\id_{K_0}$, with $b\notin Y$. Alternate two kinds of
stages. A \emph{forth stage} includes the next source element by
\cref{sse:at:lem:omission}. A \emph{cofinality stage} makes the image exceed a
prescribed target landmark $\ell$ while preserving the omission: choose
\[
 y>\dclat(Y\cup\{b\})\quad\text{and}\quad y>\ell,
\]
possible because the displayed definable closure is a set, and choose $x>X$
in the source. Both $x$ over $X$ and $y$ over $Y$ realize the unique
positive-infinity type, so $\pi$ extends to a partial elementary map taking
$x$ to $y$, and hence uniquely to the definable hulls
$\dclat(X,x)\to\dclat(Y,y)$. These hulls are set-sized elementary submodels.
The image omits $b$: otherwise $b\in\dclat(Y,y)\setminus\dclat(Y)$, and
exchange would give $y\in\dclat(Y,b)$, contrary to its choice. Process all
source elements and all target landmarks in a set-like ordinal enumeration
and take unions at limits. The union map is total, elementary, cofinal, and
still omits $b$.
\end{proof}
For $\Mat_{\exp}$ (with $K_0=\R_{\exp}$) this yields, under GBC+ETR, proper
cofinal and therefore continuous (\cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy}) exponential
self-embeddings fixing the designated reals. Unlike \cref{sse:thm:explicit},
this construction does not fix every ordinal: its cofinality stages
deliberately move large elements.

\begin{remark}[Homogeneity, and moving a real infinitesimally \src{04}; under GBC+ETR with a class satisfaction relation]
\label[remark]{sse:at:rem:homogeneity}
The same saturation lemma, with alternating forth and back stages, extends
any set-sized partial elementary map of $\Mat$ to a class automorphism; this
is the standard strong-homogeneity principle of the surreal literature, and
its construction again keeps set-sized maps at every ordinal stage. (The
ordered-field version is printed by source 07, \cref{sse:an:fnd:thm:homogeneity},
after \lbl{saut:thm:orderedhomogeneity}.) It shows why exponential
automorphisms need not fix the actual copy of $\R$: in the countable language
of exponential fields, $\dclat_{\Mat_{\exp}}(\varnothing)$ is a countable
subset of $\R$. Choose $r\in\R$ outside it and an infinitesimal
$\varepsilon>0$. Then $r$ and $r+\varepsilon$ have the same cut over that
definable closure, hence the same complete one-variable type, and a class
automorphism of $\Mat_{\exp}$ can send $r$ to $r+\varepsilon$. It moves growth
values somewhere else, as required by \cref{sse:at:thm:exp-displacement}; it
is not an exponential $1$-automorphism.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Internal definability, by a calculus argument}
The base proves \cref{sse:thm:definable} through the fixed field (an infinite
definable subfield contains an interval). Source 04 states the same theorem,
``in any o-minimal expansion of an ordered field, a definable unital field
self-embedding is the identity; parameters are allowed'', and proves it by
o-minimal calculus.
\begin{proof}[Second route to \cref{sse:thm:definable} \src{04}]
Let $j$ be definable and additive. Definable differentiability on cells
provides a point $a$ at which $j$ is differentiable. Additivity gives
\[
 \frac{j(a+h)-j(a)}h=\frac{j(h)}h,
\]
so $j$ is differentiable at $0$ with some derivative $c$, and the same
identity gives derivative $c$ at every point. The definable mean value
theorem, valid over o-minimal ordered fields, implies that $j(x)-cx$ is
constant on every interval; it vanishes at $0$, so $j(x)=cx$ for every $x$.
Finally $j(1)=1$ gives $c=1$.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
Source 04 places this theorem inside its GBC+ETR section, but it concerns one
formula and uses only first-order consequences of o-minimality; the base's
proof shows that no class-theoretic hypothesis is needed. It rules out
internal definability of the proper embeddings above, not their external
definability from normal forms or a class well-order, and it shows that
naming any nonidentity field self-embedding as a new function symbol destroys
o-minimality of the expansion.
\end{wnote}

\section{Exponential growth-scale rigidity for embeddings}\label{sse:at:sec:exp}

Source 04's Lemma~9.1 is base \cref{sse:lem:amplify} (with $k=\id$) together
with \cref{sse:lem:twomap}: if $j,k:F\to F$ are field embeddings, $k(F)$ is
cofinal and $j\ne k$, then $\{j(x)-k(x)\}$ is cofinal and coinitial, by the
twisted Leibniz rule $d(ax)=j(a)d(x)+d(a)k(x)$ for $d=j-k$. Source 04 says
this isolates the part of the repository's growth-scale argument that does
not require surjectivity (EAR \lbl{lem:amplify}, for unital endomorphisms, in
\fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity}).

\paragraph{Setting.} Let $F$ be an ordered field, $E:(F,+)\to(F_{>0},\cdot)$ an
increasing group isomorphism with inverse $\log$, and $w:F^\times\to\Gamma$ a
surjective nontrivial convex valuation, so that $\nu=w\circ E$ is the base's
profile. Suppose an exponential field self-embedding $j$ induces an ordered
additive embedding $\tau_j:\Gamma\to\Gamma$ with $w(j(u))=\tau_j(w(u))$. For
the natural valuation of $\No$ this compatibility is automatic
(\cref{sse:prop:valueaction}).

\begin{theorem}[Exponential embedding displacement \src{04}; abstract form of \cref{sse:thm:expvalue}]
\label{sse:at:thm:exp-displacement}
If $j\ne\id$, then
\begin{equation}\label{sse:at:eq:cofinaltau}
 \{\tau_j(\gamma)-\gamma:\gamma\in\Gamma\}
\end{equation}
is cofinal and coinitial in $\Gamma$. In particular, if $\tau_j=\id$, then
$j=\id$. Surjectivity of $j$ is not required.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For $D(x)=j(x)-x$, exponential compatibility gives
\begin{equation}\label{sse:at:eq:expprofile}
 w(E(D(x)))=w(E(jx))-w(E(x))=w(j(E(x)))-w(E(x))
 =\tau_j(w(E(x)))-w(E(x)).
\end{equation}
By \cref{sse:lem:amplify}, $D(F)$ is cofinal and coinitial in $F$. Since $E$
is increasing and onto, $E(D(F))$ is cofinal and coinitial among positive
elements. Convexity of $w$ means that $0<u<v$ implies $w(u)\ge w(v)$. Given
$\gamma\in\Gamma$, choose a positive element of valuation strictly larger than
$\gamma$ and one of valuation strictly smaller; coinitiality and cofinality
of $E(D(F))$ then give values of $w\circ E\circ D$ on both sides of $\gamma$.
Thus the left side of \eqref{sse:at:eq:expprofile} has cofinal and coinitial
range, contained in \eqref{sse:at:eq:cofinaltau}. If $\tau_j=\id$, that range is
$\{0\}$, which cannot be cofinal in a nontrivial ordered abelian group.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
The base proves the surreal case (\cref{sse:thm:expvalue}, with
$\nu=\val\circ\exp$). The abstract statement is EAR \lbl{thm:cofinal}
(\fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity}), printed there for
exponential automorphisms although its proof uses no surjectivity; and it is
already the statement of the generic Lean theorem
\declat{cofinal_value_displacement} (\fn{Surreal/Algebra/ExponentialProfile.lean}),
which takes a unital endomorphism \code{$\sigma$ : F ->+* F}. Source 04 did not
inspect that file. The instantiation with the actual surreal exponential is
pending there.
\end{wnote}

\begin{corollary}[No nontrivial exponential $1$-self-embeddings \src{04}; $=$ \cref{sse:thm:exprigidity}]
\label[corollary]{sse:at:cor:oneexp}
A self-embedding of the natural surreal exponential field preserving the
leading term of every nonzero element is the identity. More strongly,
preservation of natural valuation values alone forces the identity.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Leading-term preservation implies $\val(jx)=\val(x)$. The value action exists
by \cref{sse:prop:valueaction}, and pointwise preservation means $\tau_j=\id$.
Apply \cref{sse:at:thm:exp-displacement} with $E=\exp$, $w=\val$.
\end{proof}
No strong additivity or real-coefficient fixation is needed. For
automorphisms the implication bears directly on the question about
exponential $1$-automorphisms printed as Question~5.4 in KKS version~3
\cite{KKS}. Source 04 writes that the repository ``already proposes a
negative answer in its growth-scale report'' and that ``the proof here
states and establishes the non-surjective extension, rather than treating
that proposal as a machine-checked black box''.
\begin{wnote}
At source 04's pin the growth-scale report already stated the non-surjective
extension as its \lbl{cor:embedding} (``Self-embedding version''), and its
answer to KKS Question~5.4 as \lbl{thm:no} and \lbl{cor:question54}; the
generic Lean counterparts \declat{eq_id_of_commute} and \declat{eq_id_of_profile}
also take endomorphisms. Source 04 inspected only the opening 235 lines of that
article. \Cref{sse:at:cor:oneexp} is therefore a re-proof, by a different
route (through \cref{sse:at:thm:exp-displacement}), not a new extension.
\end{wnote}
There is no conflict with the bounded exponential embeddings of
\cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary} (under GBC+ETR): their value-group
embeddings are not the identity. A map whose image is small in the ambient
order can have very large displacement from the identity.

\begin{theorem}[Faithfulness on cofinal exponential embeddings \src{04}; abstract form of \cref{sse:thm:expunique}]
\label{sse:at:thm:pair-rigidity}
Let $j,k:F\to F$ be field embeddings commuting with $E$ and inducing the same
action on the nontrivial convex valuation $w$, that is
$w(j(u))=w(k(u))$ for all $u\ne0$. If $k(F)$ is cofinal in $F$, then $j=k$. In
particular, on cofinal exponential self-embeddings of $\No$ the natural
value-group action determines the embedding uniquely.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Set $d=j-k$. Equality of the value actions gives
\[
 w(E(d(x)))=w(j(E(x)))-w(k(E(x)))=0\qquad(x\in F).
\]
Choose $s>1$ with $w(s)<0$. Every positive valuation unit $u$ satisfies
$s^{-1}<u<s$; otherwise convexity would contradict $w(u)=0$. Therefore
$-\log s<d(x)<\log s$ for all $x\in F$, and the two-map form of the
amplification lemma (\cref{sse:lem:twomap}) forces $d=0$.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
This strengthens EAR \lbl{cor:two-profiles} (``Comparing two automorphisms by
profile'') and its Lean counterpart \declat{eq_of_profile_eq}
(\fn{ExponentialProfile.lean}), both of which need one of the two maps to be
surjective (the Lean statement takes \code{$\tau$ : F $\simeq$+* F}), to one map
with merely \emph{cofinal} image. The cofinal strengthening is not
formalized. The base's \cref{sse:thm:expunique} is the surreal case, proved
there through the finite kernel \eqref{sse:eq:expkernel}; source 05's
two-profile theorem and source 07's two-embedding comparison point here.
\end{wnote}
The cofinality hypothesis is used in the twisted Leibniz identity. The proof
does not establish that two arbitrary bounded-image exponential embeddings
with the same value action coincide (\cref{sse:at:q:twobounded}). Comparing a
single self-embedding with the identity has no such problem, because the
identity is cofinal.

\subsection{A criterion for monomial lifts to preserve exponentiation}
Let $I=\NF{\No_{>0}}$ be the additive class of purely infinite surreals,
including $0$. For the Gonshor exponential, $\exp(I)$ is exactly the class of
monomials $\omega^y$. Define the additive order isomorphism $e_*:I\to\No$ by
\begin{equation}\label{sse:at:eq:estar}
 \exp(x)=\omega^{e_*(x)}\qquad(x\in I).
\end{equation}
This packages the usual normal-form exponential formula without confusing
exponentiation with the omega-map \cite{Gonshor}.

\begin{proposition}[Exponential criterion for monomial lifts \src{04}]\label[proposition]{sse:at:prop:expcriterion}
Let $g$ be an ordered additive self-embedding of $\No$. The field lift $H_g$
commutes with the Gonshor exponential if and only if
\begin{equation}\label{sse:at:eq:expcriterion}
 g(e_*(x))=e_*(H_g(x))\qquad(x\in I).
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
$H_g$ maps $I$ into $I$, since $g$ preserves the sign of exponents. On $I$
the two sides of exponential commutation are $H_g(\exp x)=\omega^{g(e_*(x))}$
and $\exp(H_gx)=\omega^{e_*(H_g(x))}$; the omega-map is injective, so this
gives necessity and commutation on $I$. Every surreal splits uniquely as
$x=x_\infty+r+\varepsilon$ with $x_\infty\in I$, $r\in\R$ and $\varepsilon$
infinitesimal. The map $H_g$ preserves this decomposition, fixes $r$, and
preserves the strong power-series sum $\exp(\varepsilon)=\sum_{n\ge0}
\varepsilon^n/n!$. Therefore commutation on $I$ implies commutation on all
three components, and hence on $x$.
\end{proof}
The condition is exact but still global. Without an additional
strong-additivity property of $g$, testing it on a generating list of
monomials does not justify its extension through arbitrary strong sums; this
is one of the principal obstacles in classifying exponential Hahn lifts. For
the double lifts $J_-$ and $J_{f_\beta}$ (that is, $g=A_{f_-}$ or $g=A_{f_\beta}$)
the base shows failure directly (paragraph after \cref{sse:thm:expvalue}).

\section{The omega-map, other languages, omnific integers, and complexification}\label{sse:at:sec:extra}

\begin{proposition}[Omega compatibility \src{04}; $=$ \eqref{sse:eq:omegafunctional} and \cref{sse:thm:omegarigidity}]
\label[proposition]{sse:at:prop:omega}
For a field lift $H_g$ of an ordered additive embedding $g$, commutation with
the omega-map, $H_g(\omega^x)=\omega^{H_g(x)}$ for all $x$, is equivalent to
\begin{equation}\label{sse:at:eq:omegafixed}
 g(x)=H_g(x)\qquad(x\in\No).
\end{equation}
For any field self-embedding $j$, commutation with the omega-map together with
pointwise preservation of the natural valuation forces $j=\id$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
$H_g(\omega^x)=\omega^{g(x)}$, and the omega-map is injective. For the second
assertion apply $\val$ to $j(\omega^x)=\omega^{j(x)}$ to get $-x=-j(x)$.
\end{proof}
The second clause is the automorphism observation of the literature with
surjectivity removed \cite{KKS}. It is tempting to solve
\eqref{sse:at:eq:omegafixed} by claiming that every exponent in a normal form
is simpler than the number. That induction is invalid: generalized epsilon
numbers satisfy $x=\omega^x$, so the exponent can be the number itself;
fixed-point structure and non-well-founded dependency patterns cannot be
discarded. KKS's questions on nontrivial omega-preserving automorphisms and
on extending order automorphisms of generalized epsilon numbers remain the
reference points \cite{KKS}. Source 04 gives the displayed reductions, not a
complete classification of their solutions.

\begin{remark}[Other readings of ``self-embedding'' \src{04}]\label[remark]{sse:at:rem:readings}
Before field structure is required the problem is much looser: the first
lift gives ordered additive embeddings with arbitrary order-relocation data,
and $x\mapsto ax$ ($a>0$) is an ordered additive automorphism, unital only
for $a=1$. An $\R$-linearity requirement should say that it uses the
designated copy of $\R$, not only the residue field. The total exponential
transports additive to multiplicative problems,
$h\mapsto\mathcal M_h=\exp\circ h\circ\log$: an ordered additive embedding $h$
gives an ordered multiplicative embedding of $\No_{>0}$, generally not a
field map. No unital field self-embedding of a real closed field can reverse
its uniquely determined order. Preserving birthdays \emph{without} order
permits arbitrary permutations within birthday levels; preserving the prefix
tree alone permits branch swaps; preserving an external well-order is again
unrelated to $<$ or birthdays. Naming $\R$, the monomials, $\Oz$, birthdays,
simplicity or a coding of sets changes the elementary-embedding question: a
field map fixing $\R$ pointwise is an embedding of the field with constants
for all reals, but not automatically elementary in a language with a
predicate for a larger designated subring. There is no finite list of all
expansions; the useful exhaustive framework is an explicit embedding
specification (carrier, language, size convention, definability convention,
image requirements) with a theorem for each named combination.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Differential self-embeddings}
For the Berarducci--Mantova derivation $\partial$ (constant field $\R$,
$\partial\omega=1$) source 04's Proposition~10.2 is base
\cref{sse:prop:differential}. The translation parameter
$j(\omega)=\omega+c_j$ is studied by source 06
(\cref{sse:sr:exp:translationparameter}). The explicit $J_c$ fixes $\omega$ and
$\R$, but that alone is not a proof of differential compatibility.

\begin{proposition}[Rational exponent dilations are not differential \src{04}]\label[proposition]{sse:at:prop:ratdil}
For a rational $q>0$, $q\ne1$, the exponent dilation $S_q=H_{a\mapsto qa}$ does
not commute with $\partial$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
$S_q(\omega)=\omega^q$. Writing $q=m/n$ and differentiating the algebraic
relation $(\omega^q)^n=\omega^m$ gives $\partial(\omega^q)=q\omega^{q-1}$.
Commutation would give $\partial(S_q\omega)=S_q(\partial\omega)=S_q(1)=1$, but
$q\omega^{q-1}$ is infinite or infinitesimal for $q\ne1$.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
It also follows at once from \cref{sse:prop:differential}, since
$\omega^q-\omega\notin\R$. It is a different property from EAR
\lbl{thm:dilation} (rational dilations do not lift \emph{exponentially}).
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[Known differential extension theorems \src{04}]\label[remark]{sse:at:rem:hfield}
Composition and substitution on transseries subfields are natural candidate
constructions, but their domains must be checked: a theorem about
omega-series or a specified hyperserial subfield does not by itself supply a
total self-map of $\No$ commuting with the chosen derivation, a distinction
already significant in \cite{sr-BM19}. A safe extension theorem states the
source subfield, the admissible substituted argument, preservation of all
relevant sums, and the chain rule. Strong positive global theorems are known
in the ordered valued differential language: Aschenbrenner, van den Dries and
van der Hoeven prove that the natural transseries embedding into the
Berarducci--Mantova field is elementary and establish its universality for
Hardy fields \cite{sr-ADH}; van den Dries, Ehrlich and Mildenberger prove that
isomorphisms between set-sized substructures that are models of the relevant
$H$-field model companion, with constant field $\R$, extend to automorphisms
of the full surreal $H$-field \cite{an-VdDEhrlichH}. These should not be
presented as unsolved. Their language does not name arbitrary strong Hahn
sums, the monomial cross-section, or the Gonshor exponential; simultaneous
preservation of those structures remains a separate condition.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Omnific integers}
Source 04's Proposition~11.1 is base \cref{sse:prop:oz} (same proof), and
applies to every double lift $J_f$. The bounded $J_c$ therefore preserves the
discrete omnific integer-part structure as well as the field while being
proper and discontinuous; there is no analogue of the Archimedean assertion
that fixing integers and reals forces all field points to be fixed.

\begin{remark}[The Taylor twist does not preserve $\Oz$ \src{04}]\label[remark]{sse:at:rem:taylorOz}
For the real $r_0$ with $\Delta(r_0)=1$, $\Delta^2(r_0)=0$,
\[
 T\bigl(r_0\omega^{1/2}\bigr)=r_0\omega^{1/2}+\omega^{-1/2}.
\]
The input is omnific and the output is not. Thus pointwise natural-value
preservation and leading-term preservation (both hold for $T$) do not imply
preservation of the omnific ring; compare the base's $U_\beta$
(\cref{sse:prop:unitlift}). None of these assertions claims elementarity of
the pair $(\No,\Oz)$: preservation and reflection of the predicate, and
commutation with floor, are the precise claims.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Complexification}
\begin{proposition}[Conjugation-compatible extensions \src{04}; after \lbl{opa:as:thm:complexembed}]
\label[proposition]{sse:at:prop:complex}
Let $\No(i)$ carry its standard conjugation.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item A field self-embedding $j$ of $\No$ has exactly the two extensions
$j_\pm(a+ib)=j(a)\pm i\,j(b)$ fixing the real form; both commute with
conjugation.
\item Conversely, every field self-embedding of $\No(i)$ commuting with
conjugation is $j_+$ or $j_-$ for $j$ its restriction to $\No$.
\item $|j_\pm(z)|=j(|z|)$ with $|a+ib|=\sqrt{a^2+b^2}$; if $j$ is exponential,
the logarithmic modulus is preserved: $\log|j_\pm(z)|=j(\log|z|)$ for $z\ne0$.
\end{enumerate}
Naming $i$ selects the positive extension.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
(i) is the base paragraph on $\No(i)$ in \cref{sse:sec:modeltheory}. (ii) The
fixed field of conjugation is $\No$; for $a\in\No$,
$\overline{\sigma(a)}=\sigma(\bar a)=\sigma(a)$, so $\sigma$ maps $\No$ into
itself, and $\sigma(i)$ is one of the two roots $\pm i$ of $X^2+1$. (iii) A
field embedding of $\No$ preserves order and therefore positive square roots,
so $j(\sqrt{a^2+b^2})=\sqrt{j(a)^2+j(b)^2}$; apply $\log$ and exponential
compatibility.
\end{proof}
Without naming conjugation or a real form, arbitrary embeddings of the
algebraically closed field $\No(i)$ need not reduce to embeddings of the
designated real field; this is another language-dependent interpretation.
\begin{wnote}
Clause (ii) is the restriction step of the proof of
\lbl{opa:as:thm:complexembed} (\fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms}),
which classifies the \emph{strong} conjugation-compatible embeddings of
complex Hahn fields; for automorphisms it is the centralizer statement
\lbl{saut:thm:axis}, and for elementary embeddings of birthday cutoffs
\lbl{hset:thm:two-lifts}. Source 04 cites none of these.
\end{wnote}

\section{Cutoffs and universe-polymorphic formalization}\label{sse:at:sec:universes}

For an ordinal $\lambda$, $\No_{<\lambda}$ is a set in ZFC and need not be a
field. Source 04 states: ``The precise classical criterion is that
$\No_{<\lambda}$ is a subfield exactly when $\lambda$ is an epsilon number; in
that case it is also closed under the natural exponential and is an
elementary extension of the real exponential field'' \cite{vdDE}, adding that
the original paper has an erratum to a support-order-type lemma
(Lemma~4.5) whose main field and exponential conclusions remain the relevant
results \cite{at-vdDEerratum}; the paper must not be used without accounting
for the erratum.
\begin{wnote}
Printed as source 04's claim. Source 02 makes the same claim; the ``only if''
direction is proved in the note after \cref{sse:pf:rem:epsilon}. The
collection states the characterization as van den Dries--Ehrlich's (the subsection
\lbl{found:sub:cutoffs} of \fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/foundations}), and
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/birthday-cutoffs-and-hereditary-sets} cites the ``if''
direction. The closure under the exponential is the source's claim and was not checked here.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[Regular cutoffs and model-theoretic constructions \src{04}]\label[remark]{sse:at:rem:cutoff}
For an uncountable regular cardinal $\kappa$, $\No_{<\kappa}$ is a
$\kappa$-bounded Hahn field over its own exponent group (base
\eqref{sse:eq:kappahahn}). It satisfies interpolation for cuts whose two sides
have size less than $\kappa$, and, for a language of size less than $\kappa$,
the cut argument of \cref{sse:at:lem:setsaturation} gives saturation over
parameter sets of size less than $\kappa$. Its cardinality is $2^{<\kappa}$,
not automatically $\kappa$: a back-and-forth of length $\kappa$ covering the
whole carrier needs a size hypothesis such as $\kappa^{<\kappa}=\kappa$, and
without one, saturation over fewer than $\kappa$ parameters cannot simply be
iterated through a larger carrier while keeping all domains of size
$<\kappa$. The explicit Hahn constructions are less sensitive
(\cref{sse:prop:cutofftransfer}): if an increasing $f$ maps $\No_{<\kappa}$ into
itself, both lifts are defined on the cutoff, $c$ and $f_-$ are examples, and
their range and fixed-support formulas keep their form. Birthday rigidity and
the initial-image argument also restrict, since the canonical options of an
element of birthday $<\kappa$ form sets of size $<\kappa$. A construction that
starts by fixing every real uses a base set of size $|\R|$; it is not a
$<\kappa$-parameter construction when $|\R|\ge\kappa$.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{The type-theoretic version.} A universe-polymorphic sign-sequence
type has the schematic form $\No_U:\mathrm{Type}(u+1)$, with lengths drawn
from $u$-small ordinals (base \cref{sse:sec:foundations}). It is a type but not
$u$-small; its function space and powerset live at a larger level, and a proof
claiming otherwise would undo the size barrier behind the Burali--Forti
paradox. Normal forms must carry a proof that their support is $u$-small: a
Hahn series on the large exponent type with an arbitrary large well-ordered
support is not automatically a surreal in $\No_U$. Replacement becomes
preservation of \code{Small.\{u\}} under images and explicit equivalences. An
embedding $\No_U\to\No_V$ obtained by changing universes is not a
self-embedding of one fixed carrier; elementarity, saturation and convergence
claims depend on which parameter sets, supports and nets are small. Universe
lifting is legitimate; collapsing the distinction is not.

\paragraph{Local workspaces.} A reliable proof-engineering pattern keeps every
individual algebraic calculation inside a common small Hahn workspace: collect
the supports of finitely many surreals and of a set-sized summable family,
take the additive subgroup or monoid they generate, record its smallness,
compute there, and transport back through the normal-form equivalence. The
class-theoretic model constructions of \cref{sse:at:sec:model} have a different
local invariant (at each stage the partial map, its elementary domain and
image, and the compactness language are sets; only the final union is a
class) and need a separate formalized satisfaction and recursion layer.

\section{Integration with the ProveIt repository}\label{sse:at:sec:lean}

\subsection{What source 04 inspected}
At the pin \code{a006a77a0a8bacdffe089b70d3b963b17228a089} source 04 inspected
the surreal README, the opening 235 lines of the growth-scale report
\fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity/article.tex} (blob
\code{ffb9a14697e7}), and three files in
\fn{Algebra/SurrealNumbers/Surreal/Foundations/}:
\fn{NormalFormExponentAutomorphisms.lean} (full returned source, blob
\code{a78aedc6ad3f}), \fn{ExponentAutomorphismStrongSums.lean} (through line
120, blob \code{f1a81bf562a1}) and \fn{SignSequenceExpLog.lean} (opening 95
lines, blob \code{07fe74404032}). No Lean build or transitive axiom audit was
run, and no new theorem is labeled machine checked on the strength of a
filename or README claim. Its audit adds that a request for
\fn{Algebra/SurrealNumbers/docs/FORMALIZATION.md} ``returned an empty content
field'', so no claim rests on that ledger.
\begin{wnote}
The empty FORMALIZATION.md was a fetch artefact: the ledger exists in the
repository. The four blobs named are unchanged between source 04's pin and
the write-time repository.
\end{wnote}

\subsection{Existing interfaces}
\fn{NormalFormExponentAutomorphisms.lean} supplies a small-support
exponent-transport construction whose input is an \emph{ordered additive
equivalence} of \code{SignSequence.\{u\}} (Lean \code{$\simeq$+o}); visible
declarations include \declat{mapExponents}, \declat{exponentRingEquiv} and
\declat{exponentAutomorphism}, transported through
\declat{SmallNormalForm.cutEvaluationRingEquiv}, with formulas on real constants
and omega-powers in \declat{exponentAutomorphism_ofReal} and
\declat{exponentAutomorphism_omegaPower}. \fn{ExponentAutomorphismStrongSums.lean}
supplies \declat{StronglySummable.exponentAutomorphism},
\declat{stronglySummable_exponentAutomorphism_iff},
\declat{exponentAutomorphism_strongSum}, \declat{valuation_exponentAutomorphism}
and \declat{exponentOrderAutomorphism}; its strong-sum equality has an explicit
\code{Small.\{u\}} hypothesis on the index type, whereas a summability-transfer
statement can be formulated for a larger index universe -- exactly the
distinction between a locally summable family and a total sum in a fixed
small-support carrier. \fn{SignSequenceExpLog.lean} describes itself as an
\emph{infinitesimal} exponential/logarithm development, defines \declat{infExp}
and \declat{infLog}, and proves the strong power-series identities; it is a
dependency for the infinitesimal part of \cref{sse:at:prop:expcriterion}, but
not by itself for \cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary,sse:at:thm:exp-displacement}
specialized to the Gonshor exponential.
\begin{wnote}
Every declaration named here was verified to exist (write-time check):
\declat{mapExponents} (\fn{NormalFormExponentAutomorphisms.lean}:49),
\declat{exponentRingEquiv} (:85), \declat{exponentAutomorphism} (:109),
\declat{exponentAutomorphism_ofReal} (:120),
\declat{exponentAutomorphism_omegaPower} (:128);
\declat{SmallNormalForm.cutEvaluationRingEquiv} (\fn{SmallNormalFormFieldEquiv.lean}:51);
\declat{StronglySummable.exponentAutomorphism}
(\fn{ExponentAutomorphismStrongSums.lean}:27),
\declat{stronglySummable_exponentAutomorphism_iff} (:37),
\declat{exponentAutomorphism_strongSum} (:46, with \code{[Small.\{u\} $\iota$]}, as
source 04 says), \declat{valuation_exponentAutomorphism} (:79),
\declat{exponentOrderAutomorphism} (:96); \declat{infExp}, \declat{infLog}
(\fn{SignSequenceExpLog.lean}:22, :26). Source 04's statement that the
exponent input is an ordered additive \emph{equivalence} is correct. In the
repository the field lift exists only for equivalences; no proper
$\No\to\No$ self-embedding, no first lift of a non-additive $f$, and no
declaration about maps preserving \code{IsPrefix}/\code{Simpler} is
formalized. The generic displacement lemmas \declat{eq_id_of_commute},
\declat{eq_id_of_profile}, \declat{cofinal_value_displacement}
(\fn{Surreal/Algebra/ExponentialProfile.lean}) take endomorphisms; the cofinal
two-map strengthening \cref{sse:at:thm:pair-rigidity} is not formalized.
\end{wnote}

\subsection{A staged implementation plan \src{04}}
\paragraph{Stage A: order embeddings on supports.} Generalize the
exponent-transport layer from ordered additive equivalences to injective
ordered additive maps. Inverse maps are unnecessary for preservation of
multiplication or strong sums. Replace uses of surjectivity in
coefficient-extensionality arguments with an explicit split: the queried
exponent is in the image, or its coefficient is zero. Prove support image
equality and range membership before packaging a field map.

\paragraph{Stage B: first and double Hahn lifts.} Implement arbitrary
increasing exponent relocation as an additive map, separate from
multiplicative relocation. Establish the composition law
\eqref{sse:eq:addfunctor} and the exact multiplicativity criterion. Then obtain
the double lift by composition of interfaces, not by a second independent
arithmetic proof; the image formula should become a reusable
support-membership equivalence.

\paragraph{Stage C: exact invariants.} Formalize rigidity of order
automorphisms of reverse well-ordered supports; this yields the fixed-support
formulas without transfinite coefficient recursion. Add the finite-iteration
composition law and the support-intersection lemma for
\cref{sse:at:thm:coreformula}. Formalize the concrete $c$ and $f_-$ examples with
their omitted monomials, as regression theorems against accidental
surjectivity assumptions.

\paragraph{Stage D: topology and exponential algebra.} Prove the
continuity--cofinality theorem first for an arbitrary ordered field. Prove the
displacement lemma generically from the twisted Leibniz identity. Then add an
abstract ordered exponential and a convex valuation to prove
\cref{sse:at:thm:exp-displacement,sse:at:thm:pair-rigidity}. Only the final
specialization should depend on the global surreal exponential, keeping the
hard surreal input out of the short algebraic rigidity proof.

\paragraph{Stage E: class and universe model theory.} Formalize the
saturation, compactness and ordinal-recursion proofs of
\cref{sse:at:sec:model} either in an explicit set/class theory or as
universe-bounded theorems with correctly stated cardinal hypotheses. Do not
encode an unrestricted class elementary embedding as an ordinary small Lean
function without explaining the universe shift and the range of allowable
types.

\paragraph{Suggested interface, not claimed executable code.} The following
specification pseudocode avoids pretending that exact Mathlib constructor
names or the required proofs already exist:
\begin{verbatim}
FirstLift(f : order embedding No No) : additive order embedding No No
FieldLift(g : additive order embedding No No) : field embedding No No
DoubleLift(f) := FieldLift(FirstLift(f))

support_FirstLift(f, x) = image(f, support(x))
FirstLift(g) o FirstLift(f) = FirstLift(g o f)
range_DoubleLift(f) = H(H(range(f)))
fixed_DoubleLift(f) = H(H(fixed(f)))
continuous(j) <-> cofinal(range(j))

exp_compatible(j) and pointwise_value_preserving(j) -> j = identity
\end{verbatim}
A checked contribution should include all support-smallness hypotheses,
coefficient-zero-outside-image lemmas, and a dependency report.

\subsection{The finite regression script}
Source 04's \code{finite\_checks.py} is shipped as
\fn{code/04-atlas-finite_checks.py}, with its recorded output
\fn{data/04-atlas-finite_check_results.json}. It uses only the Python
standard library and exact rational arithmetic (seed 20261003): prefix and
doubling maps on all sign words of length at most five (63 words), the
rational maps $c$, $f_-$, $f_{\mathrm{gap}}$ (monotonicity, range, inverse,
exact iterates $c^n$, no fixed points of $f_{\mathrm{gap}}$), first and double
lifts on random finite Hahn polynomials with rational exponents (additivity,
multiplicativity, order, composition, fixed-point criteria, ordinal fixation
by $J_-$), and the formal Taylor twist on coefficient polynomials (inverse and
multiplicativity). It reports 22,169 checks, all passing. It prints its JSON
record to standard output only and writes no file. Its own scope statement:
exact finite rational/sign/Hahn-polynomial regression checks only; not
verified are arbitrary ordinal signs, infinite Hahn summability, class
recursion, elementary embeddings, and Lean compilation.
\begin{wnote}
A rerun on a fresh copy at write time passed with the same 22,169 checks; the
output equals the recorded file except for the Python version string
(3.13.5 recorded) and line endings.
\end{wnote}

\section{Proof-obligation ledger and audit of invalid arguments of source 04}\label{sse:at:sec:ledger}

\toprule
Component (source 04) & Status and dependency (as stated by source 04; locations here) \\
\midrule
\endhead
Normal forms, sign cuts, real closedness & Classical background \cite{Conway,Gonshor}; used with set-sized supports. \\
Double Hahn lift and exact image & Proved (\cref{sse:thm:addlift,sse:thm:doublelift}); no class compactness. \\
Bounded and ordinal-fixing explicit maps & Proved (\cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-hahn,sse:thm:explicit}); no exponential claim. \\
Fixed fields and iterated-image core & Proved (\cref{sse:at:thm:coreformula} and the base fixed-support formulas); finite iterate intersection only. \\
Continuity iff cofinality & Proved for ordered fields (\cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy,sse:at:thm:continuity}). \\
Bounded and proper cofinal elementary maps & Proved (\cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary,sse:at:thm:cofinal-elementary}); GBC+ETR, o-minimality, definable Skolem functions and exchange. \\
Surreal exponential model theory & Established external input \cite{vdDE,Wilkie}; not inferred from local series alone. \\

\subsection{A compact audit of common invalid arguments \src{04}}\label{sse:at:sec:audit}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*)]
\item \emph{``Every field embedding fixes $\R$ because it fixes $\Q$.''} It fixes
real residues, but infinitesimally moved real representatives have the same
rational cuts; the Taylor twist \eqref{sse:eq:taylorshift} is an explicit
counterexample.
\item \emph{``Every order-and-simplicity embedding is the identity.''}
Surjectivity, birthday nonincrease, or ambient canonical-cut preservation is
needed; prefix and block maps are proper counterexamples without them.
\item \emph{``The image is a field, so it cannot be bounded.''} A bounded
nonconvex field copy can omit all sufficiently small ambient positive scales
and is then discrete as an ambient subspace, while remaining intrinsically
densely ordered (\cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-hahn}).
\item \emph{``Strong sums are limits of finite partial sums.''} Not in the full
order topology: set-indexed convergent nets are eventually constant, and
strong sums are a different support operation (\cref{sse:at:rem:strongsum}).
\item \emph{``An order relocation of exponents is multiplicative.''} Only if
the exponent map is additive; the second Hahn lift repairs this without
pretending the first lift was multiplicative.
\item \emph{``A pointwise valuation-preserving field map is rigid.''} It need
not be; the Taylor twist is a nontrivial example. Exponential compatibility
turns the additive error into a detectable change of multiplicative scale.
\item \emph{``Every normal-form exponent has smaller birthday.''} Generalized
epsilon numbers invalidate this; it cannot justify a recursion solving
omega-preservation equations.
\item \emph{``All automorphisms form a class group in GBC.''} Their graphs are
proper classes, not class elements; factorization and composition assertions
are statements about class variables, or move to a specified higher universe.
\item \emph{``Compactness adds a number above all surreals.''} The bounded
elementary construction adds an element above a set model only; at no point
is a proper-class elementary diagram passed to ordinary compactness.
\item \emph{``A file called ExpLog proves the global exponential.''} The
inspected repository file proves only the infinitesimal power-series clauses;
a global specialization needs a separate global construction and its laws.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Research questions of source 04}\label{sse:at:sec:questions}

Source 04 states that its questions ask for additional classification or
compatibility, not that every formulation is known to be globally open. The
base's questions (\cref{sse:sec:agenda}) are unlabelled and are cited by name.

\subsection{Classification and explicit constructions}
\begin{question}[Beyond strong monomial embeddings \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:beyond}
Give useful necessary and sufficient conditions for an arbitrary field
self-embedding to factor into coefficient motion, a monomial transport, and a
residual strongly additive or non-strong component. Which parts of an
automorphism decomposition survive without surjectivity?
\end{question}
The first target is a one-sided factorization theorem with an explicit image
condition; arbitrarily transferring inverses from the automorphism case would
conceal precisely the new obstruction. Compare the base question ``Beyond
monomial images''.

\begin{question}[Strong exponential compression \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:compression}
Does there exist a bounded-image self-embedding of the natural surreal
exponential field that is strongly $\R$-linear? Can it additionally send
monomials to monomials?
\end{question}
The bounded elementary theorem answers the question without the strong
requirements (under GBC+ETR, \cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary}). In the
monomial subclass, \eqref{sse:at:eq:expcriterion} is the exact equation to
solve. A proof of impossibility would be equally informative and would
separate first-order exponential structure from strong summation. Compare the
base question ``Proper exponential copies''.

\begin{question}[Ordinal fixation with exponentiation \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:ordinalexp}
Is there a proper exponential self-embedding fixing every ordinal and every
real? How does the answer change when strong summation or monomial
preservation is imposed?
\end{question}
The field-only version is settled affirmatively by \cref{sse:thm:explicit}; the
exponential version is not supplied by that construction (the base shows
$J_-$ is not exponential, after \cref{sse:thm:expvalue}). Its image would
necessarily be cofinal, so \cref{sse:at:thm:pair-rigidity} applies to any
candidate's value action.

\begin{question}[Realizing value-group embeddings \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:realizing}
Characterize the ordered additive maps $\tau:\No\to\No$ that occur as natural
value-group actions of cofinal exponential self-embeddings.
\end{question}
Uniqueness is proved (\cref{sse:at:thm:pair-rigidity}); existence is the
remaining lifting problem. Cofinal displacement of $\tau-\id$ is necessary for
every nonidentity candidate but should not be mistaken for a sufficient
condition. Compare the base question ``Characterize exponential value
actions''.

\begin{question}[Two bounded embeddings with equal value action \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:twobounded}
Can distinct bounded-image exponential self-embeddings induce the same natural
value-group map? If not, replace the cofinality step in
\cref{sse:at:thm:pair-rigidity} by an argument valid for bounded images.
\end{question}
This identifies the exact limit of the present comparison proof. Compare the
base question ``Remove or justify the cofinality hypothesis''.

\subsection{Omega and differential constraints}
\begin{question}[The omega self-consistency equation \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:omega}
Classify the ordered additive embeddings $g$ satisfying $g=H_g$, first in
restricted normal-form subfields and then on the full class. Determine how
prescribed motion of generalized epsilon numbers constrains an extension.
\end{question}
This is the strong monomial version of the omega-map classification questions
in the literature; a viable proof must handle self-referential exponent
dependencies, not use an invalid birthday induction. Compare the base question
``$\omega$-map compatibility''.

\begin{question}[Differential extension with extra structure \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:differential}
Which extensions supplied by $H$-field homogeneity can simultaneously preserve
strong Hahn sums, the Gonshor exponential, or a specified action on monomials?
Characterize proper cofinal differential self-embeddings extending prescribed
maps of a fixed set-sized differential subfield.
\end{question}
The necessary normalization is \cref{sse:prop:differential}. The known
homogeneous-universal theorem \cite{an-VdDEhrlichH} already supplies a strong
extension mechanism in its own language; the question concerns additional
compatibility and proper-image requirements, not basic differential
homogeneity. Compare the base question ``Differential centralizers''.

\subsection{Images, fixed fields, and dynamics}
\begin{question}[Which fixed fields occur? \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:fixedfields}
Characterize the real closed subclasses that can be fixed fields of surreal
self-embeddings under each of the field, exponential, and strong monomial
specifications.
\end{question}
The formula $\Fix(J_f)=\NF{\NF{\Fix f}}$ supplies a substantial explicit family
(including $\R$ itself, \cref{sse:at:ex:fgap}) and reduces part of the problem to
fixed sets of order embeddings; it does not show that all real closed
subclasses occur. Compare the base question ``Prescribe the fixed field''.

\begin{question}[Automorphic cores and conjugacy \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:cores}
Classify the possible pairs $(K_\infty(j),j\restriction K_\infty(j))$ and use
them as invariants for conjugacy of proper embeddings. When does the fixed
field determine the core, and when do the two differ essentially?
\end{question}
The compression example \eqref{sse:at:eq:corec} proves that fixed fields and
cores can differ; the exact support formulas give a tractable testing class.
Compare the base question ``Prescribe the reversible core''.

\begin{question}[Birthday distortion \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:birthday}
For a nonidentity field self-embedding $j$, quantify the ordinals on which
$\bday(j(x))>\bday(x)$ must occur. Can the distortion be bounded by a uniform
ordinal function for the explicit double lifts?
\end{question}
\Cref{sse:thm:birthday} proves that some increase is unavoidable. Sharper
bounds would connect the Hahn construction with the sign-sequence
implementation and reveal which cutoff restrictions are optimal. Compare the
base question ``Weaker birthday controls''.

\subsection{Foundations and verified implementation}
\begin{question}[Minimal class assumptions \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:minimal}
Reduce the GBC+ETR hypotheses of the two elementary-embedding constructions
(\cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary,sse:at:thm:cofinal-elementary}) for the
specific theories of real closed fields and real exponential fields. Can their
uniform satisfaction relations and set-sized-stage recursions be constructed
in a weaker theory without losing the parameter-fixing clauses?
\end{question}
This is a proof-theoretic optimization problem; the stronger foundation is
sufficient and no necessity result is claimed. Compare the base question
``Minimal foundational strength''.

\begin{question}[Universe coherence \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:universe}
Construct compatible lifts on a tower of surreal universes and determine which
embedding invariants are absolute under enlargement. In particular, separate
preservation of old small sums from preservation of newly available large
sums.
\end{question}
A commuting diagram of universe lifts is a more meaningful target than an
unqualified ``universal surreal type''. Compare the base question ``Universe
and cutoff absoluteness''.

\begin{question}[The pair $(\No,\Oz)$ \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:omnific}
Which field embeddings preserving the omnific predicate are elementary in the
expanded language? Does adding floor to the language produce further rigidity
for bounded-image embeddings?
\end{question}
The explicit examples already preserve floor and the predicate
(\cref{sse:prop:oz}); what remains is preservation of quantified formulas about
the pair. Compare the base question ``Floor-preserving maps outside the
monomial class''.

\begin{question}[A verified embedding library \src{04}]\label[question]{sse:at:q:library}
Implement Stages A--D of \cref{sse:at:sec:lean}, with the bounded and
ordinal-fixing examples as end-to-end tests. Then formalize the class or
cardinal-bounded saturation lemma needed for Stage E.
\end{question}
This separates a concrete extension of already inspected exponent-transport
code from the substantially different model-theoretic formalization task.
Compare the base question ``Near-term formalization''.
\begin{wnote}
For Stage D, the abstract displacement theorem
(\cref{sse:at:thm:exp-displacement}) and the single-map rigidity are already
formalized generically in \fn{Surreal/Algebra/ExponentialProfile.lean}; the
remaining generic item is the cofinal two-map comparison
(\cref{sse:at:thm:pair-rigidity}).
\end{wnote}

\section{Status and non-claims of source 04}\label{sse:at:sec:status}

\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Status} (title page, README): an unrefereed research manuscript
supplying conventional proofs and an implementation roadmap, not a
Lean-certified development; established results, deductions proved there,
repository proposals and research questions are distinguished. No exhaustive
priority (or novelty) claim is made; the literature comparison is selective.
The title page records ``Research article prepared with ChatGPT''.
\item ``Proved here'' identifies where a proof is supplied, not a certified
claim of historical priority. The exponential embedding results develop an
argument already pursued in the repository's unrefereed growth-scale
manuscript (see the correction after \cref{sse:at:cor:oneexp}: the
self-embedding form was already stated there).
\item GBC+ETR is ``a sufficient foundation, not a minimality claim'', used only
for the class-model constructions of \cref{sse:at:sec:model}; explicit support
substitutions have a weaker, direct definable-class reading.
\item The bounded elementary embedding is not claimed to preserve strong Hahn
sums or the canonical monomial cross-section (\cref{sse:at:rem:notsay}).
\item No complete classification of omega-compatible or differential
embeddings is claimed: ``This article gives the displayed reductions, not a
complete classification of their solutions''; necessary conditions only for
the differential case.
\item Differential universality and homogeneity are acknowledged as
established positive results and are not recast as new or unsolved
(\cref{sse:at:rem:hfield}).
\item The article does not claim a complete classification for every possible
expansion of the surreal structure.
\item The repository was inspected selectively, not cloned, audited or
rebuilt; no Lean build, kernel check, transitive axiom audit or independent
verification of the repository's foundation was performed;
README-level assertions are not substitutes for those checks; files seen
only as directory entries support no claim. ``No new Lean certification
claimed.''
\item The pseudocode is ``specification pseudocode'', not claimed executable
code, and does not pretend that Mathlib constructor names or proofs exist.
\item The 22,169 finite checks are regression tests only; they cannot validate
arbitrary ordinals, infinite Hahn supports, class recursion, model-theoretic
arguments, or Lean compilation.
\item The research questions are not an assertion that every formulation is
globally open.
\item The open-question status of KKS Questions~5.4, 5.6, 5.7 (version~3,
consulted 3 October 2026) is distinguished from the repository's later
unrefereed proposals.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Repository-audit claims of source 04, checked at write time.}
(a) The exponent transport takes an ordered additive equivalence: true.
(b) The thirteen named declarations exist, and the strong-sum theorem carries
a \code{Small.\{u\}} index hypothesis: true (\cref{sse:at:sec:lean}).
(c) \fn{SignSequenceExpLog.lean} is infinitesimal only: true.
(d) FORMALIZATION.md ``returned an empty content field'': a fetch artefact;
the ledger exists. (e) The growth-scale report is unrefereed and does not
claim proof-assistant certification of its main rigidity arguments: as stated
by that report. (f) The claim that the self-embedding extension is
established here rather than in the repository is stale
(\cref{sse:at:cor:oneexp} and the following note).
% ================= source 05
\part{Source 05: Order, simplicity, algebra, topology, exponential structure, and foundations}\label{sse:part:co}

\section{Source 05 at a glance}\label{sse:co:sec:glance}

Source 05 is the manuscript \emph{Self-Embeddings of the Surreal Numbers:
Order, simplicity, algebra, topology, exponential structure, and
foundations}, labelled ``Research article / expanded study'', prepared for
Vladimir Reshetnikov and dated 3 October 2026 (39 pages; repository pin
\code{51b0a69d7385a786661e0169dc13ee7ab02b921c}). It describes itself as
incorporating and extending earlier working notes prepared for the same
project. Its setting is G\"odel--Bernays class theory with global choice
(GBC), the sign-sequence model, and set-sized supports only; the
model-theoretic \cref{sse:co:sec:model} additionally assumes uniform
satisfaction and definable Skolem operations for an o-minimal expansion of
the field $\No$, with GBC plus elementary transfinite recursion (ETR) named
as one sufficient framework. Its finite checks are shipped as
\fn{code/05-comprehensive-verification.py} with the recorded run
\fn{data/05-comprehensive-verification.json} and build record
\fn{data/05-comprehensive-BUILD_AUDIT.json}; its source audit is
\fn{05-comprehensive-SOURCE_AND_PROOF_AUDIT.md}.

Most of source 05 proves the common spine of this report in its own words.
This Part prints only what is new relative to the base text and to the
collection: its local padding description of all order-and-simplicity
embeddings (home of that result for the whole report), an exact
differentiability criterion for the strong weighted maps, a sharper bounded
annulus, a uniform form of set discreteness, the cofinal profile theorem in
a form not requiring exponential compatibility, a continuous non-strong
automorphism, the commutant of the diagonal derivations, its versions of the
conditional elementary embeddings, and its questions, formalization
architecture and audit.

\paragraph{Renames.} Source 05's symbols are renamed to the canon of this
report as follows; every other symbol is as in the source.

\begin{longtable}{P{40mm}P{38mm}P{72mm}}
\toprule
Here & Source 05 & Reason \\
\midrule
\endhead
$j$ (an embedding) & $e$ & base letter for embeddings \\
$x\pref y$, $x\spref y$ & $\sqsubseteq$, $\sqsubset$ & base canon for sign prefixes \\
$c(x)=x/(1+|x|)$ & $C(x)$ & base \cref{sse:prop:compression}; $C$ is the core class in \cref{sse:sec:fixed} \\
$\tau$ (additive exponent embedding), $H_\tau$ & $\phi$, $H_\phi$ & base \cref{sse:thm:fieldlift} \\
$J_f=H_{A_f}$ & $E_f$ & base \cref{sse:thm:doublelift}; $E$ is the exponential of \fn{exponential-automorphism-rigidity} and the footprint $E(A)$ here \\
$H_{\tau,\chi}$, character $\chi$ & $H_{\phi,c}$, $c$ & base \cref{sse:thm:weighted}; $c$ is the compression \\
$\NF{C}$ (series supported in a class $C$) & $\Gamma_C$ and $\mathbb H(G)$ & base notation $\NF{\cdot}$ covers both: an additive group for a subclass, a Hahn field for a subgroup \\
$S_q=H_{a\mapsto qa}$ & $T_q$ & base \cref{sse:prop:zeroderivative}; $T$ is the Taylor shift and $T_{>c}$ truncation \\
$\tau_j$ (value action) & $\rho_e$ & base \cref{sse:prop:valueaction} \\
$\shco_\tau(a)=\tau(a)-a$ & $\Delta(a)$ & $\Delta$ is the coefficient derivation of the base Taylor shift \\
$\partial_\lambda$ (diagonal derivation) & $D_\lambda$ & $D$ is the displacement and $D_m$ the sign repetition \\
$\mathcal M_\varphi=\exp\circ\varphi\circ\log$ & $M_\phi$ & report canon; $M$ also names OPA's monomial maps \\
$\mathfrak M$ (o-minimal class model) & $M$ & avoids $\mathcal M_\varphi$; source 04 writes $\mathcal M$ \\
$f_{\mathrm{ann}}$ (annulus exponent map) & $h$ & $h$ is the increment in difference quotients \\
$b$ (omitted point, external bound) & $p$ & $p$ is the prefix in $P_p$; source 04 also writes $b$ \\
$[a]_{-1}$ (coefficient of $\omega^{-1}$ in $a$) & $\lambda(a)$ in its Section 9.5 & base notation \\
ordinal $+$, $\cdot$ (in words) & $+_{\mathrm{ord}}$, $\cdot_{\mathrm{ord}}$ & base convention \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
Two letters are kept and disclosed: $K$ denotes a set-sized elementary
submodel in \cref{sse:co:sec:model} (not the core $K_\infty(j)$ of
\cref{sse:prop:core}), and $\lambda$ denotes an additive map $\No\to\R$ in
\cref{sse:co:sec:differential} (not a birthday stage).
