\section{Scope and foundations in source 05}\label{sse:co:sec:scope}

Source 05 states its inheritance as follows. The elementary normal-form
mechanisms are classical Hahn-field constructions; prefix concatenation and
the distinction between rigid automorphisms and flexible embeddings belong to
the established surreal-substructure theory \cite{BVH}; the fraction-field
identity for omnific integers and exponential valuation rigidity already
occur in the repository \cite{RepoExp,co-RepoOz}, and are proved again so
that the dependence is mathematical rather than bibliographic. It then lists
as ``developments assembled here'' the local padding description, the
two-level lift with exact fixed fields and cofinality detection, the
topological distinction between sets and the full class, the exponent
criterion for differentiability, bounded elementary exponential copies, and
the cofinal two-embedding profile theorem. It adds that some of these may be
familiar to specialists in another formulation, that ``developed here''
describes the contents of the article and is not a priority claim, and that
the classification of arbitrary exponential, omega-field or differential
self-embeddings is not asserted to be complete.

\begin{remark}[What the list means relative to the collection]\label[remark]{sse:co:rem:developed}
\begin{wnote}
Source 05 inspected only the READMEs of \fn{exponential-automorphism-rigidity}
and \fn{omnific-preserving-automorphisms} and none of
\fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms},
\fn{independent-surreal-copies} or
\fn{discrete-initial-subgroups-and-omnific-normalization}. Item by item:
the local padding description (\cref{sse:co:thm:local}) is new to the
collection, and sources 06 and 07 proved it independently. The two-level lift
is OPA's double lift $M_{1,\iota_\mu}$ (\lbl{opa:as:lem:inner},
\lbl{opa:as:thm:classification}), and its cofinality detection is the
content of \lbl{opa:as:thm:four} together with \lbl{opa:as:thm:topology}
of \fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms}; the exact fixed-field
formulas are \lbl{opa:es:thm:fixedsupport} there for automorphisms, and their
embedding form is also in the base
(\eqref{sse:eq:addfixed}, \eqref{sse:eq:weightedfixed}) and in sources 02,
04 and 07. The topological distinction between sets and the full class is
\lbl{saut:prop:discrete} of \fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms}
(stated there for $\No(i)\supseteq\No$), and the continuity--cofinality
dichotomy for field embeddings is \lbl{opa:as:thm:topology}. The ordinary
derivative analysis of exponent dilations, and the dichotomy ``a nonzero
derivative forces the identity'', are \lbl{saut:thm:zeroderiv} and
\lbl{saut:thm:derivative} there (for automorphisms); the exponent criterion
\cref{sse:co:thm:derivative-criterion} is new. The bounded elementary
exponential copies are new to the collection, conditional on the class
framework of \cref{sse:co:sec:model}, and were found independently by
source 04 (\cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary}). The cofinal profile
theorem is new relative to \lbl{cor:two-profiles} of
\fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity} and its Lean form
\code{eq\_of\_profile\_eq}, which need a surjective comparison map; it is
proved independently in the base (\cref{sse:lem:twomap,sse:thm:expunique})
and in sources 02, 04 and 07.
\end{wnote}
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Foundational distinctions specific to source 05]\label[remark]{sse:co:rem:foundations}
\src{05} The base's foundations (\cref{sse:sec:foundations}) are those of
source 05. Source 05 adds the following distinctions.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item \emph{Weak reducts.} With equality alone a self-embedding is any
injective class map. If only the birthday function is named, permutations
within a birthday level preserve that datum and need not preserve order or
simplicity; if only the comparison of birthdays is named, even the action on
levels may move ordinals. Literal preservation of birthday values is a
stronger demand.
\item \emph{Domains.} An endomorphism has domain all of the chosen carrier.
A substitution defined on a transseries subfield, an embedding of one
birthday fragment into a later fragment, and a full self-embedding are
different assertions; an embedding into an elementary extension of a set
model is not a self-embedding of that model.
\item \emph{Inaccessible cutoffs.} For a strongly inaccessible $\kappa$,
$\No_{<\kappa}=\bigcup_{\alpha<\kappa}\{-,+\}^\alpha$ is a set of
cardinality $\kappa$ with the cut-filling property for cuts with fewer than
$\kappa$ options, and relativizing the surreal constructions to $V_\kappa$
gives a set-sized surrogate. Quantifiers, supports and maps must stay
relativized; a map of the surrogate need not extend to the external class
$\No$. Strong inaccessibility is sufficient, not necessary: for uncountable
regular $\kappa$ the bounded normal-form theorem \eqref{sse:eq:kappahahn}
applies \cite[Proposition~4.7]{vdDE}, and ``$<\kappa$'' may not be replaced
by ``arbitrary set'' inside the carrier. An arbitrary birthday cutoff is not
a field: the finite-birthday surreals are the dyadic rationals, not closed
under division by $3$.
\item \emph{Type theory.} Universe-polymorphic ordinals and sign sequences
need no inaccessible cardinal postulated inside the development; the
interpretation of a hierarchy of universes in a chosen set-theoretic model is
a separate metatheoretic question. An implementation must never use
$\mathrm{Type}:\mathrm{Type}$, nor treat a universe-relative ordinal type as
containing every ordinal of its own larger universe.
\item \emph{Recursion.} Recursion through ordinal stages with set-valued
states (set recursion with class parameters) suffices for the sign
constructions and for set-sized back-and-forth arguments; it is not
class-valued elementary transfinite recursion \cite{HamkinsETR}, which source
05 invokes only for the uniform model theory of \cref{sse:co:sec:model}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}

\section{Order and tree self-embeddings}\label{sse:co:sec:order}

Write $s^-=s\concat(-)$ and $s^+=s\concat(+)$ for the immediate sign
successors of a sign sequence $s$. The following theorem is the home of the
local description of all order-and-simplicity embeddings in this report;
sources 06 and 07 point to it.

\begin{theorem}[Local padding description \src{05}]\label{sse:co:thm:local}
A class assignment $s\mapsto t_s$ is an embedding of $(\No,<,\pref)$ into
itself exactly when the following conditions hold:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item $t_0$ is an arbitrary sign sequence;
\item $t_{s^-}$ and $t_{s^+}$ properly extend $t_s$, and their first new
signs, at coordinate $\bday(t_s)$, are respectively $-$ and $+$;
\item if $\bday(s)$ is a nonzero limit ordinal, then $t_s$ extends
\[
 \bigcup_{\beta<\bday(s)}t_{s\restriction\beta}.
\]
\end{enumerate}
Thus at a successor node one may append an arbitrary nonempty block beginning
with the prescribed sign; at a limit node one may append an arbitrary further
block, possibly empty, to the union of the earlier images.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For an embedding, prefix preservation gives the extension statements. Since
$s^-<s<s^+$, the first new signs have the specified orientations. At a limit,
every earlier image is a prefix of $t_s$, so their union is one as well.

Conversely, transfinite induction on length shows that an input prefix has
an output prefix. Consider two inputs. If one extends the other, their order
is decided by the first sign after the shorter one, which has the same
orientation in the images by (ii). If neither is a prefix of the other, let
$u$ be their longest common prefix. Their images extend $t_{u^-}$ and
$t_{u^+}$ in the appropriate order. These outputs first differ at
coordinate $\bday(t_u)$, with opposite signs. Hence the output order is
correct and the outputs are incomparable in simplicity. This proves strict
order, injectivity, and reflection of simplicity.
\end{proof}
The theorem shows again that strict order preservation together with
one-way preservation of the prefix relation implies reflection, as in
\cref{sse:lem:reflection}; no separate reflection hypothesis is needed.
The prefix maps $P_p$ (append $p$ at the root, no padding elsewhere) and the
repetitions $D_m$ (blocks of length $m$ at successors, no padding at limits)
of \cref{sse:prop:prefix,sse:prop:repeat} are the simplest instances.

\begin{corollary}[Meet and rank \src{05}]\label[corollary]{sse:co:cor:rank}
Every order-and-simplicity embedding $j$ preserves the longest common prefix
of two inputs, in the sense that the longest common prefix of $j(x)$ and
$j(y)$ is $j(u)$ when $u$ is that of incomparable $x,y$; and
$\bday(j(s))\ge\bday(s)$ for every $s$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
For incomparable inputs, the proof of \cref{sse:co:thm:local} locates their
first output disagreement exactly after the image of their common prefix.
The comparable case is immediate. Along the chain of prefixes of $s$, the
birthdays of the images are strictly increasing, so their ordinal ranks
cannot be smaller than the corresponding input ranks.
\end{proof}
The rank clause is the first clause of \cref{sse:prop:treebirthday}; the
meet clause is new here.

Call a simplicity embedding \emph{chain-continuous} if it preserves unions of
nonempty set-sized prefix chains. In \cref{sse:co:thm:local} this is exactly
the prohibition of extra padding at nonzero limit nodes: equality in (iii).
This continuity is in the tree sense, not the order topology.

\begin{proposition}[Least fixed points \src{05}]\label[proposition]{sse:co:prop:treefix}
A chain-continuous order-and-simplicity embedding $j$ has a unique simplest
fixed point,
\[
 z=\bigcup_{n<\omega}j^n(0).
\]
More generally, when $x\pref j(x)$, the same construction started at $x$ is
the least fixed extension of $x$ in the prefix order.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The empty sequence is a prefix of $j(0)$; applying prefix preservation
repeatedly gives an increasing chain. Its union is a set sign sequence.
Chain continuity gives $j(z)=\bigcup_nj^{n+1}(0)=z$. Any fixed point extends
all the iterates of zero, and hence extends $z$. The second assertion uses
the identical argument above $x$.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
Chain continuity is equivalent to the image-closure hypothesis of
\cref{sse:thm:closedtree}: if $j$ is chain-continuous and $(j(x_i))$ is a
set-sized prefix chain in the image, reflection makes $(x_i)$ a chain, and
$j(\bigcup x_i)=\bigcup j(x_i)$ lies in the image; the converse is the first
step of the proof of \cref{sse:thm:closedtree}. So
\cref{sse:co:prop:treefix} is the least-fixed-point part of that theorem
($z=c_j(0)$), by the same iteration. It is printed because sources 06 and 07
cite the padding description together with this clause.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[Prefix maps with fixed points \src{05}]\label[remark]{sse:co:rem:prefixfixed}
The map $P_+$ is proper but fixes $\omega$, because ordinal addition gives
$1+\omega=\omega$. More generally, for nonempty $p$ the $\omega$-fold
concatenation $p\concat p\concat\cdots$ is the simplest fixed point of
$P_p$, by \cref{sse:co:prop:treefix}. A claim that every nontrivial prefix
map strictly increases every birthday would be false.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[First movement \src{05}]\label[corollary]{sse:co:cor:firstmove}
For any nonidentity order self-embedding $j$ there is a least birthday
$\alpha$ at which something moves. Every moved input of birthday $\alpha$ is
sent to a proper sign extension of itself, hence to a strictly larger
birthday.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
At the least such $\alpha$, every canonical option of a moved $x$ of birthday
$\alpha$ is fixed, so $j(x)$ lies between $L_x$ and $R_x$ and extends $x$,
as in the proof of \cref{sse:thm:birthday}; it is a proper extension since
$j(x)\ne x$.
\end{proof}
This does not say that every later birthday increases; a field automorphism
may increase some birthdays and decrease others.

\begin{corollary}[Initial images \src{05}]\label[corollary]{sse:co:cor:initial}
An embedding of $(\No,<,\pref)$ with prefix-initial image is the identity.
In particular every automorphism of the doubly ordered structure is the
identity.
\end{corollary}
The base reaches this by \cref{sse:thm:initial} (initiality alone forces
surjectivity) followed by \cref{sse:prop:treebirthday}. Source 05 argues
directly through birthdays.
\begin{proof}[Second route to \cref{sse:thm:initial} with \cref{sse:prop:treebirthday} \src{05}]
For any $x$, every proper prefix of $j(x)$ is in the image, by initiality.
Reflection of simplicity identifies the preimages of those prefixes with
exactly the proper prefixes of $x$. The two predecessor chains have order
types $\bday(j(x))$ and $\bday(x)$, so the birthdays are equal. Apply
\cref{sse:thm:birthday}.
\end{proof}
The automorphism clause is \cite[Proposition~5.1]{KKS}; the argument
isolates why a proper, noninitial image escapes it. Source 05 adds that an
\emph{initial subfield} (a subfield that is prefix-initial in $\No$) is not
thereby the image of a simplicity embedding: an arbitrary field isomorphism
onto it need not preserve simplicity, and the corollary needs both
hypotheses.
\begin{wnote}
The embedding clause is the case $E=\No$ of \lbl{isg:cf:prop:coherence} of
\fn{docs/surreal/discrete-initial-subgroups-and-omnific-normalization}
(uniqueness of the isomorphism of the numerical and prefix orders from $E$
onto an initial subclass); source 05 does not cite it. By
\cref{sse:thm:initial} a self-embedding of the whole of $\No$ with initial
image is onto, so the initial subfields in source 05's last remark are
those that are not images of $\No$, for instance set-sized ones.
\end{wnote}

\section{Hahn lifts, explicit copies, and the two truncations}\label{sse:co:sec:hahn}

Source 05's two lifts and their image, composition and cofinality statements
are those of \cref{sse:thm:addlift,sse:thm:fieldlift,sse:thm:doublelift}.
It adds the following.

\begin{remark}[One-level reindexing \src{05}]\label[remark]{sse:co:rem:oneLevel}
For an increasing $f$, the additive lift $A_f$ preserves multiplication if
and only if $f$ is additive. Necessity follows by applying $A_f$ to
$\omega^a\omega^b=\omega^{a+b}$, which gives $\omega^{f(a+b)}$ on one side and
$\omega^{f(a)+f(b)}$ on the other; sufficiency is the convolution argument of
\cref{sse:thm:fieldlift} (and then $f(0)=0$, so $A_f=H_f$ is unital). This is
why an arbitrary order map cannot be turned into a field map in one step.
Source 05's finite checks record an explicit failure of multiplicativity for
a one-level reindexing (an exponent map with $f(2)=2/3$ but $2f(1)=1$).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Keeping only one group operation \src{05}]\label[remark]{sse:co:rem:multiplicative}
The additive and multiplicative reducts have their own embedding problems.
For $c>0$, $x\mapsto cx$ is an ordered additive automorphism but a unital field
map only for $c=1$. The first lift supplies proper additive embeddings, and an
entire additive copy of $\No$ can lie in the infinitesimals: for the map
$f_{\mathrm{ann}}$ of \cref{sse:co:thm:bounded} below, every nonzero
$A_{f_{\mathrm{ann}}}(a)$ is infinitesimal. Using the Gonshor exponential and
logarithm, an ordered additive embedding $\varphi$ gives an ordered
multiplicative embedding
\[
 \mathcal M_\varphi:\No_{>0}\to\No_{>0},\qquad
 \mathcal M_\varphi(x)=\exp\bigl(\varphi(\log x)\bigr).
\]
Conjugation by $\exp$ and $\log$ is a bijective correspondence between these
two kinds of group embeddings, preserving composition and surjectivity. It
does not preserve field addition: $\varphi(x)=2x$ gives
$\mathcal M_\varphi(x)=x^2$, and $(1+1)^2\ne1^2+1^2$. Extending by
$\mathcal M_\varphi(-x)=-\mathcal M_\varphi(x)$ embeds the group $\No^\times$;
setting $\mathcal M_\varphi(0)=0$ still gives no field embedding. Thus a
multiplicative group embedding, an exponential field embedding and the field
lift $H_\varphi$ are three different constructions even from the same
additive map $\varphi$.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Bounded field self-copy with a uniform ambient gap \src{05}]\label{sse:co:thm:bounded}
Let
\[
 f_{\mathrm{ann}}(a)=-2+\frac12\cdot\frac{a}{1+|a|},
\]
an increasing map with image $(-5/2,-3/2)$, and let
$j=H_{A_{f_{\mathrm{ann}}}}=J_{f_{\mathrm{ann}}}$. Then $j$ is a proper,
strongly $\R$-linear ordered-field self-embedding, and every nonzero image
element satisfies
\[
 \omega^{-1}<|j(x)|<\omega.
\]
In particular the entire image lies in $(-\omega,\omega)$, and no nonzero
image point lies in $[-\omega^{-1},\omega^{-1}]$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
For nonzero $a$, the leading exponent of $A_{f_{\mathrm{ann}}}(a)$ lies in
$(-5/2,-3/2)$, so $A_{f_{\mathrm{ann}}}(a)$ is infinitesimal; also
$A_{f_{\mathrm{ann}}}(0)=0$. If $x\ne0$ has leading exponent $a$, then $j(x)$
has leading exponent $A_{f_{\mathrm{ann}}}(a)$, which lies strictly between
$-1$ and $1$. Comparison of distinct leading exponents gives the displayed
strict inequalities, whatever the nonzero real leading coefficient. The map
is a field embedding by \cref{sse:thm:fieldlift}, and is not surjective
because it misses $\omega$.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
\Cref{sse:thm:boundedcopy} fixes a prescribed set and $\omega$, with the
weaker bound $|j(y)|<\omega^{\omega^{\gamma+1}}$; the present map fixes no
prescribed set (it sends $\omega$ to $\omega^{\omega^{-2}}$) but places the
image in the annulus $\omega^{-1}<|y|<\omega$. A parameter-fixing annulus
$\Omega^{-1}<|y|<\Omega$ with an explicit $\Omega$ is \lbl{opa:as:thm:parameters}
of \fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms}, and the bounded case
of \lbl{opa:as:thm:four} is the same phenomenon; explicit families of
bounded independent copies are \lbl{isc:thm:explicit} of
\fn{docs/surreal/independent-surreal-copies}. Source 05 cites none of these.
\end{wnote}
There is no conflict with arbitrarily large elements \emph{inside} this field
copy: its order is internally unbounded but bounded in the ambient field, and
its positive elements are internally coinitial at zero but have the external
lower bound $\omega^{-1}$. The image is still a proper class. If its birthdays
were bounded by an ordinal, it would lie in a set of sign sequences, and
applying the inverse embedding to that set would make $\No$ a set by class
replacement. Numerical boundedness and ambient discreteness therefore imply
neither set-sizedness nor bounded birthdays.

\begin{example}[Hahn truncation is not simplicity \src{05}]\label[example]{sse:co:ex:truncation}
Every $H_\tau$ commutes with taking an initial segment of a normal form, with
exponent labels reindexed by $\tau$ (\eqref{sse:eq:truncation}); this is
Hahn truncation, not sign-prefix simplicity. The automorphism $S_2$,
$\sum r_a\omega^a\mapsto\sum r_a\omega^{2a}$, does not preserve simplicity.
Every positive infinite surreal has its first $\omega$ signs positive, and
$\sqrt\omega\ne\omega$, so $\omega\spref\sqrt\omega$. But
$S_2(\omega)=\omega^2$ and $S_2(\sqrt\omega)=\omega$, and
$\omega^2\not\pref\omega$. Preservation of one kind of truncation cannot be
substituted for the other.
\end{example}

\section{Topology and ordinary differentiability}\label{sse:co:sec:topology}

Source 05's continuity theorem is \cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy}, with the same
proof; it adds that the proof applies verbatim to an embedding of any ordered
field into another, with cofinality in the target, which is
\cref{sse:an:top:criterion} (home 07).
\begin{wnote}
The dichotomy is \lbl{opa:as:thm:topology} of
\fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms}, stated there through the
value group; source 05 does not cite it.
\end{wnote}

\begin{theorem}[Sets are uniformly discrete \src{05}]\label{sse:co:thm:small-discrete}
Every set $S\subseteq\No$ is uniformly discrete in the full surreal order
topology: there is $\eta>0$ with $|s-t|>\eta$ for all distinct $s,t\in S$.
It is closed in the precise sense that every $x\notin S$ has an open
interval about it disjoint from $S$. Every convergent net indexed by a set
is eventually equal to its limit.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The class
\[
 \{\,|s-t|^{-1}:s,t\in S,\ s\ne t\,\}
\]
is a set. Choose a positive surreal $B$ larger than every member of it, by a
set cut, and put $\eta=1/B$. Empty or singleton sets cause no difficulty:
choose any positive $B$. For closedness apply the same argument to
$S\cup\{x\}$; the interval $(x-\eta,x+\eta)$ misses $S$. If $(x_i)_{i\in I}$
is a set-indexed net converging to $x$, its range together with $x$ is a set
by class replacement; the separation gives a radius $\eta$ whose
neighbourhood of $x$ contains no other value of the net, so $x_i=x$
eventually.
\end{proof}
This strengthens \cref{sse:prop:setdiscrete}, which isolates each point with
its own radius, to one radius for the whole set.
\begin{wnote}
Closedness, discreteness and eventual constancy of set-indexed nets are
\lbl{saut:prop:discrete} of
\fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms}, proved there for
$\No(i)$ and hence for $\No$; the uniform radius is source 05's addition.
\end{wnote}

\begin{corollary}[Set-sized nets detect nothing \src{05}]\label[corollary]{sse:co:cor:nets}
Every map $g:\No\to\No$, continuous or not, preserves all limits of
set-indexed convergent nets. There is no set of positive radii coinitial at
zero, and no set-sized local base at any point.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
An eventually constant net remains eventually constant after applying any
map. If $R$ were a set of coinitial positive radii, $\{d^{-1}:d\in R\}$
would have a surreal upper bound $B$, and $1/B$ would be smaller than every
member of $R$, a contradiction. A set-sized local base would produce such a
set of radii by choosing an interval inside each basic neighbourhood.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Consequences \src{05}]\label[remark]{sse:co:rem:netconsequences}
The usual characterization of continuity by preservation of nets cannot be
transferred while restricting nets to set-sized domains: its proof builds a
net on a neighbourhood system of the relevant size. An $\On$-indexed ``net''
behaves differently: $\omega^{-\alpha}\to0$ as $\alpha$ runs cofinally
through the ordinals, a class-indexed $\varepsilon$--$\delta$ statement, not
a set sequence and not a Hahn sum over all ordinals. The full class is not
discrete (every interval about $x$ of radius $\varepsilon$ contains
$x+\varepsilon/2$); on a set-sized cutoff only the small-family statement is
available, and applying the full-class theorem to the cutoff as its own
ambient field would be an error. Two consequences: a continuous map from a
connected set-sized topological space into the full surreal line is
constant, since its image is a set with discrete subspace topology; in
particular the topology induced on $\R$ by all surreal radii is discrete.
And Hahn summation is not topological convergence: $\sum_{n\ge1}\omega^{-n}$
exists, but its partial sums do not converge to it, since every nonzero tail
exceeds $\omega^{-\omega}$. Likewise a computation along a set sequence of
small increments cannot establish a surreal derivative; the derivative
statements below quantify over all surreal radii and increments.
\end{remark}

For a class function $g$, $g'(x)=L$ means
$\forall\varepsilon>0\ \exists\delta>0\ \forall h\ (0<|h|<\delta\Rightarrow
|(g(x+h)-g(x))/h-L|<\varepsilon)$. A \emph{finite derivative} is a limit
belonging to the field, as opposed to a quotient tending to the external
symbol $+\infty$; no Archimedean bound on the limit is assumed. This is the
order-topological derivative, not the Berarducci--Mantova derivation.

\begin{proposition}[A nonzero derivative forces the identity \src{05}]\label[proposition]{sse:co:prop:derivative-rigid}
If a field self-embedding $j$ of $\No$ has a finite derivative at one point,
it has the same derivative at every point. If that derivative is nonzero,
then $j=\id$ and the derivative is $1$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Additivity makes the difference quotient equal to $j(h)/h$, independently of
the base point. Suppose its limit is $L$. For fixed $a\ne0$, replacing $h$ by
$ah$ and using multiplicativity gives
\[
 \frac{j(ah)}{ah}=\frac{j(a)}a\cdot\frac{j(h)}h.
\]
Multiplication by a fixed nonzero scalar is a homeomorphism near zero, so
taking limits gives $L=(j(a)/a)L$. If $L\ne0$, then $j(a)=a$ for every
$a\ne0$, and $0$ is fixed too.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
This is \lbl{saut:thm:derivative} of
\fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms}, proved there for field
automorphisms of $\No(i)$ by the same scaling identity; source 05's version
is for self-embeddings of $\No$ and does not cite it. The zero-derivative
dilations of source 05's Theorem~8.2 are \cref{sse:prop:zeroderivative}
(\lbl{saut:thm:zeroderiv}).
\end{wnote}

\section{Strong weighted maps: fixed fields and an exact differentiability criterion}\label{sse:co:sec:monomial}

\begin{remark}[Fixed fields and images of the strong lifts \src{05}]\label[remark]{sse:co:rem:fixedfields}
\leavevmode
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item Combining \eqref{sse:eq:addfixed} and \eqref{sse:eq:fieldfixed}, for
every order self-embedding $f$,
\[
 \Fix(J_f)=\NF{\NF{\Fix(f)}}.
\]
In particular a fixed-point-free $f$ gives $\Fix(J_f)=\R$ (source 05's
Corollary~9.3).
\item The fixed-exponent subgroup $\Fix(\tau)\cap\ker\chi$ in
\eqref{sse:eq:weightedfixed} is additive and divisible, by the two
homomorphism laws and uniqueness of roots in $\R_{>0}$.
\item The image $\NF{\tau(\No)}$ of $H_{\tau,\chi}$ does not depend on
$\chi$. So two distinct weighted embeddings can have the same image and the
same value action and different fixed fields: a description of the image is
not a classification of the embedding.
\item If $j$ is an exponential field self-embedding, its fixed field (real
closed by \cref{sse:prop:fixedgeneral}) is closed under $\exp$ and under the
logarithm of positive elements: exponential closure is immediate, and
logarithmic closure follows from uniqueness of logarithms and commutation
with $\exp$ (source 05's Proposition~11.3).
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}

\begin{example}[A bounded copy fixing a familiar Hahn field \src{05}]\label[example]{sse:co:ex:bounded-fixed}
Take the compression $c(a)=a/(1+|a|)$ of \cref{sse:prop:compression}. Its
only fixed point is $0$, so by \cref{sse:co:rem:fixedfields}(a)
\[
 \Fix(J_c)=\NF{\R}=\R((\omega^{\R})).
\]
Moreover $A_c$ fixes real exponents, and $J_c$ fixes $\R$ and $\omega$. Every
nonzero $A_c(a)$ has leading exponent in $(-1,1)$, so $|A_c(a)|<\omega$;
comparing leading exponents at the outer level gives
\[
 \omega^{-\omega}<|J_c(x)|<\omega^{\omega}\qquad(x\ne0).
\]
This copy is bounded, proper and everywhere discontinuous. It complements
the tighter annulus of \cref{sse:co:thm:bounded}, whose map does not fix
$\omega$.
\end{example}

For the strong weighted maps of \cref{sse:thm:weighted} put
\[
 \shco_\tau(a)=\tau(a)-a .
\]
The phrase ``$\shco_\tau(a)\to-\infty$ as $a\to-\infty$'' means the class
statement $\forall d\in\No\ \exists b\in\No\ \forall a<b\ \
\shco_\tau(a)<d$; the $+\infty$ statement reverses the last inequality.

\begin{theorem}[Exponent criterion \src{05}]\label{sse:co:thm:derivative-criterion}
For $j=H_{\tau,\chi}$, the ordinary derivative is zero everywhere if and only
if
\[
 \tau(a)-a\longrightarrow-\infty\qquad(a\longrightarrow-\infty).
\]
Its difference quotient tends to $+\infty$ at every point if and only if the
same exponent difference tends to $+\infty$. The positive real character
$\chi$ has no effect on either criterion. A finite ordinary derivative exists
precisely when the first condition holds or $j=\id$; in the latter case the
derivative is $1$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
If $h\ne0$ has leading exponent $a$, then $j(h)/h$ is positive, with leading
exponent $\shco_\tau(a)$ and leading coefficient $\chi(a)\in\R_{>0}$.

To prove derivative zero, let $\varepsilon>0$ have leading exponent $d$.
Choose $b$ such that $\shco_\tau(a)<d$ whenever $a<b$, and use the increment
bound $|h|<\omega^{b-1}$. The leading exponent of $h$ then satisfies
$a\le b-1<b$, so $j(h)/h<\varepsilon$. No uniform real bound on $\chi(a)$ is
needed: a strict exponent inequality dominates every positive real
coefficient.

Conversely, suppose $j(h)/h\to0$ and fix $d\in\No$. Use the target
$\varepsilon=\omega^{d-1}$. There is $\delta>0$ such that the quotient is
below this bound whenever $0<|h|<\delta$. If $b$ is the leading exponent of
$\delta$ and $a<b$, the monomial $h=\omega^a$ is allowed. Thus
$\chi(a)\omega^{\shco_\tau(a)}<\omega^{d-1}$, which forces
$\shco_\tau(a)\le d-1<d$. This is the stated limit.

For divergence to $+\infty$, use the same argument with targets of leading
exponent $d$ in the sufficient direction and the monomial target
$\omega^{d+1}$ in the necessary direction. A nonzero finite derivative forces
$j=\id$ by \cref{sse:co:prop:derivative-rigid}, completing the
classification.
\end{proof}
For $\tau(a)=qa$ with a surreal $q>1$, $\shco_\tau(a)=(q-1)a\to-\infty$, and
for $0<q<1$ it tends to $+\infty$; this recovers \cref{sse:prop:zeroderivative}.
\begin{wnote}
The criterion is new relative to the collection and to the other sources;
it answers the derivative-classification question of source 05's earlier
edition, which source 05 removes from its agenda. It treats exactly the
strong weighted sector of \cref{sse:thm:weighted}; the unit twists
\cref{sse:prop:unitlift}, the Taylor shift \eqref{sse:eq:taylorshift} and
non-strong maps are outside it (\cref{sse:co:q:derivatives}).
\end{wnote}

\begin{corollary}[Pointwise values and continuity do not give differentiability \src{05}]\label[corollary]{sse:co:cor:valuation-nondifferentiable}
A nonidentity weighted map $H_{\id,\chi}$ has no finite ordinary derivative
anywhere. In particular, fixing every natural-valuation value and being
continuous do not imply ordinary differentiability.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Its exponent difference is identically zero and so does not tend to
$-\infty$; apply \cref{sse:co:thm:derivative-criterion}. It is an
automorphism, hence continuous by \cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Bounded embeddings diverge \src{05}]\label[remark]{sse:co:rem:boundeddiverge}
Every \emph{bounded} field self-embedding $j$, even one not given by a Hahn
formula, has difference quotient tending to $+\infty$. By
\cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy} there is $\eta>0$ with $|j(h)|>\eta$ for all
$h\ne0$; since $j$ preserves sign, $0<|h|<\eta/M$ implies $j(h)/h>M$. This is
compatible with everywhere discontinuity, and is not a finite derivative.
\end{remark}

\begin{example}[Pointwise valuation does not alone force rigidity \src{05}]\label[example]{sse:co:ex:valuationfixing}
Put $\chi(a)=\exp_{\R}([a]_{-1})$, where $[a]_{-1}$ is the coefficient of
$\omega^{-1}$ in $a$. Coefficient extraction is additive, so $\chi$ is a
positive character, and $H_{\id,\chi}$ is a nonidentity field automorphism
fixing every valuation value, every real and every ordinal (ordinal
exponents have no $\omega^{-1}$ term), while
\[
 H_{\id,\chi}\bigl(\omega^{\omega^{-1}}\bigr)=\exp_{\R}(1)\,\omega^{\omega^{-1}}.
\]
It preserves the omnific predicate (\cref{sse:prop:oz}), cannot preserve the
surreal exponential (\cref{sse:thm:exprigidity}), and has no finite ordinary
derivative (\cref{sse:co:cor:valuation-nondifferentiable}). This is the base
example after \cref{sse:prop:valueaction} with the base $2$ replaced by
$\exp_{\R}(1)$; the derivative clause is source 05's addition.
\end{example}

\section{Exponential comparison, the omega-map and omnific integers}\label{sse:co:sec:exponential}

Source 05's bounded-displacement lemma and exponential valuation rigidity
for self-embeddings are \cref{sse:lem:amplify,sse:cor:boundeddisp,sse:thm:exprigidity},
with the same proofs; it credits them to the repository \cite{RepoExp}. Its
comparison lemma is stated for embeddings between two fields.

\begin{lemma}[Cofinal bounded-difference rigidity \src{05}]\label[lemma]{sse:co:lem:two}
Let $\sigma,\tau:F\to K$ be embeddings of ordered fields, with $\tau[F]$
cofinal in $K$. If $|\sigma(x)-\tau(x)|\le B$ for one $B>0$ in $K$ and every
$x\in F$, then $\sigma=\tau$.
\end{lemma}
This extends \cref{sse:lem:twomap} from self-embeddings $F\to F$ to
embeddings $F\to K$; the proof of \cref{sse:lem:twomap}, through the identity
$d(ab)=\sigma(a)d(b)+\tau(b)d(a)$ and a $b$ with
$\tau(b)>B(1+|\sigma(a)|)/|d(a)|$, applies verbatim, since it never compares
values in $F$ with values in $K$.

With the exponential profile $\nu(x)=\val(\exp x)$ of
\cref{sse:sec:exponential}, additive with kernel the finite surreals
$\mathcal O$ \eqref{sse:eq:expkernel}:

\begin{theorem}[Cofinal profile uniqueness \src{05}]\label{sse:co:thm:two-profile}
Let $\sigma,\tau:\No\to\No$ be field self-embeddings with $\tau[\No]$
cofinal. If
\[
 \nu(\sigma(x))=\nu(\tau(x))\qquad(x\in\No),
\]
then $\sigma=\tau$. In particular, on cofinal exponential field
self-embeddings the induced action on the natural value group is faithful:
$\tau_\sigma=\tau_\tau$ implies $\sigma=\tau$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Since $\nu$ is additive, profile equality puts every difference
$\sigma(x)-\tau(x)$ in $\mathcal O$, so all differences are bounded by
$\omega$; apply \cref{sse:co:lem:two}. If both maps commute with $\exp$ and
have the same value action, then
$\nu(\sigma x)=\tau_\sigma(\nu x)=\tau_\tau(\nu x)=\nu(\tau x)$.
\end{proof}
The first clause assumes no exponential compatibility of either map, only
equality of profiles; the second is \cref{sse:thm:expunique}, and for an
abstract ordered exponential field $(F,E,w)$ it is
\cref{sse:at:thm:pair-rigidity} (home 04). Source 05 notes that the
inspected Lean file's two-profile comparison assumes surjectivity of the
second map, that \cref{sse:co:lem:two} isolates the weaker condition actually
needed for this bounded-difference proof, cofinality, and that it supplies no
new Lean proof of that strengthening. Removing cofinality by the same
argument is invalid: a bounded image cannot provide an arbitrarily large
$\tau(b)$ (compare the base question ``Remove or justify the cofinality
hypothesis'' in \cref{sse:sec:agenda}, and \cref{sse:co:q:noncofinal}).
\begin{wnote}
The surjective case is \lbl{cor:two-profiles} of
\fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity} and its Lean form
\code{eq\_of\_profile\_eq} in
\fn{Surreal/Algebra/ExponentialProfile.lean}, whose first map is a ring
homomorphism and whose second is a ring equivalence (\code{RingEquiv}); the automorphism case of the
faithfulness clause is \lbl{saut:thm:expfaithful} of
\fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms}. The cofinal version
strengthens both; the Lean generalization (replace the \code{RingEquiv} by a
ring homomorphism with cofinal range) is source 05's
\cref{sse:co:q:profile}.
\end{wnote}

\begin{proposition}[Rational dilations do not lift, for embeddings \src{05}]\label[proposition]{sse:co:prop:rationalnonlift}
Let $j$ be an exponential field self-embedding of $\No$ whose value action is
$\tau_j=q\,\id$ for some $q\in\Q_{>0}$. Then $q=1$ and $j=\id$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For every $x$, exponential compatibility and $\Q$-linearity of the additive
map $\nu$ give $\nu(j(x))=\tau_j(\nu(x))=q\nu(x)=\nu(qx)$, so
$\nu(j(x)-qx)=0$ and $j(x)-qx$ is finite. At $x=\omega$ and $x=\omega^2$ write
the finite errors as $\epsilon_1,\epsilon_2$. Multiplicativity
$j(\omega)^2=j(\omega^2)$ gives
\[
 (q^2-q)\omega^2+2q\omega\epsilon_1+\epsilon_1^2-\epsilon_2=0.
\]
After division by $\omega^2$ every term except $q^2-q$ is infinitesimal, so
the rational $q^2-q$ vanishes and $q=1$. Then $\tau_j=\id$, and
\cref{sse:thm:exprigidity} gives $j=\id$.
\end{proof}
Yet every positive rational dilation lifts to the plain-field automorphism
$S_q$; exponential compatibility is a real additional restriction. The value
displacement theorem \cref{sse:thm:expvalue} does not exclude $\tau_j=q\,\id$
by itself, since $(q-1)\id$ has cofinal and coinitial range for $q\ne1$.
\begin{wnote}
For automorphisms this is \lbl{thm:dilation} of
\fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity} and
\lbl{saut:prop:rationalnonlift} of
\fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms}, by the same $X$, $X^2$
computation; the Lean form is \code{surreal\_not\_lift\_dilation} in
\fn{Surreal/HahnSeries/ValueGroupLifts.lean}, with an
\code{OrderedExp} hypothesis. Source 05's version needs no surjectivity.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[Omega-map and omnific integers in source 05]\label[remark]{sse:co:rem:omegaoz}
\src{05} Source 05's omega-map proposition and the self-consistency equation
are \cref{sse:thm:omegarigidity} and \eqref{sse:eq:omegafunctional}; in this
report every normal-form $\omega^x$ is the Conway map, which is not
$\exp(x\log\omega)$ for arbitrary $x$. Its omnific theorem is
\cref{sse:prop:oz} (predicate preserved and reflected by every
$H_{\tau,\chi}$) together with $\operatorname{Frac}(\Oz)=\No$ and pointwise
$\Oz$-rigidity, printed as \cref{sse:pf:thm:oz} (home 02; same proof, after
\lbl{odg:thm:fractions} of \fn{docs/surreal/omnific-diophantine-geometry},
which source 05 credits). Source 05 adds two cautions: the fraction-field
identity uses the full field's ability to bound every \emph{set} of
exponents and cannot be transferred without qualification to small Hahn
fields; and an automorphism of the pair $(\No,\Oz)$ preserves the predicate
rather than fixing it pointwise, with no unrestricted automatic-strongness
theorem for pair embeddings assumed. Source 05 also notes that the
general omega-field question \cite[Question~5.6]{KKS} is not solved.
\end{remark}

\section{Elementary embeddings and internal definability}\label{sse:co:sec:model}

By quantifier elimination every field self-embedding of $\No$ is elementary
in the ordered-field language, formula by formula (\cref{sse:prop:elementary});
so the bounded, cofinal and ordinal-fixing copies are already elementary
there. For the ordered exponential field the background is that $\No$ is an
elementary extension of the real exponential field \cite{vdDE}, recorded as
\cite[Proposition~1.5]{EhrlichKaplan}. Source 05 uses the standard o-minimal
consequences: one-variable types over a definably closed model are determined
by cuts, definable closure has exchange, and definable Skolem hulls are
elementary. These are explicit model-theoretic inputs, not consequences of
order saturation alone.

\paragraph{Standing hypothesis of this section \src{05}.} $\mathfrak M$ is an
o-minimal expansion of the ordered field $\No$ in a set-sized first-order
language, and the ambient class theory provides global choice, a uniform
satisfaction relation for $\mathfrak M$, and definable Skolem operations.
Then the definable closure $\dclco(A)$ of a set $A$ is a set; it is an
elementary submodel, because definable Skolem functions supply witnesses to
existential formulas; and over a definably closed set a nonalgebraic
one-variable type is determined by its cut, since a definable subset of the
line is a finite union of intervals and points with endpoints in the
definable closure. For the pure field language, quantifier elimination
supplies the uniform description directly, and everything below can be read
in GBC without a general truth predicate. For the exponential language
source 05 uses GBC plus elementary transfinite recursion, or explicitly
supplied satisfaction and definable Skolem classes: a sufficient framework,
not a minimality claim. Every statement of this section except
\cref{sse:co:prop:nonstrong,sse:co:thm:definable} is conditional on this
hypothesis.
\begin{wnote}
Source 04 proves the cofinal and bounded elementary embeddings in its own
framework (\cref{sse:at:thm:cofinal-elementary,sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary},
home 04, with \cref{sse:at:lem:setsaturation}); the versions below are
source 05's independent proofs. Source 06, in its exponential section
(\cref{sse:sr:rem:epsstages}, Part V), proposes handling the satisfaction
and definable closures of the exponential structure through set-sized
epsilon birthday stages: these are models of the theory of the real
exponential field, model completeness makes their inclusions elementary, a
set of parameters lies in one stage, and its definable closure can be
computed there. If that route is carried out, it would interpret the class
statements of \cref{sse:co:cor:exp-proper,sse:co:cor:exp-bounded} without a
general satisfaction class and might weaken the GBC+ETR hypothesis. This is
unchecked here.
\end{wnote}

\begin{lemma}[Set types and elementary extensions \src{05}; under the standing hypothesis]\label[lemma]{sse:co:lem:small-types}
Every consistent nonalgebraic one-variable type over a set-sized elementary
submodel of $\mathfrak M$ is realized by a proper class of elements of
$\mathfrak M$. Any elementary isomorphism between set-sized elementary
submodels extends across one new domain element. By alternately extending
domain and range, it extends to an automorphism of $\mathfrak M$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write a nonalgebraic type as its set cut $L<R$. Its class $C$ of separators
is proper: if $C$ were a set, the cut $L\cup C<R$ would have a separator,
which would belong to $C$ while exceeding every member of $C$. All
separators realize the type, by the interval description of definable sets.
Hence a realization can be chosen outside any prescribed set.

Transfer the type of a new domain element through the elementary
isomorphism, choose a realization in the target, and extend to the definable
closures. Equality of complete types makes this extension well defined and
elementary, and both closures are sets. For an automorphism, fix a set-like
class enumeration of $\mathfrak M$ and alternately add the next missing
domain element and the next missing range element, using the same
construction for the inverse map at a back step. At a set limit take the
union. Each stage is a set map, and the final class union has full domain
and range.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
For the pure ordered field, the automorphism clause is
\lbl{saut:thm:orderedhomogeneity} of
\fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms} (ordered-field
isomorphisms between set-sized subfields extend to automorphisms of $\No$,
under global choice); in this report ordered-field homogeneity is
\cref{sse:an:fnd:thm:homogeneity} (home 07). The realization clause is
\cref{sse:at:lem:setsaturation} (home 04); ``by a proper class'' is source
05's addition.
\end{wnote}

\begin{theorem}[Omitting a point \src{05}; uniform satisfaction, definable Skolem operations, global choice]\label{sse:co:thm:omit}
Under the standing hypothesis, let $K\preccurlyeq\mathfrak M$ be a set-sized
elementary submodel and $b\in\mathfrak M\setminus K$. There is a cofinal
proper elementary self-embedding $j:\mathfrak M\to\mathfrak M$ fixing $K$
pointwise and omitting $b$ from its image.
\end{theorem}
This is \cref{sse:at:thm:cofinal-elementary} (home 04).
\begin{proof}[Second route to \cref{sse:at:thm:cofinal-elementary} \src{05}]
Fix a class enumeration $(a_\alpha)_{\alpha\in\On}$ of $\mathfrak M$. Construct
increasing elementary isomorphisms $j_\alpha:K_\alpha\to K'_\alpha$ between
set-sized definably closed elementary submodels, with $b\notin K'_\alpha$,
starting from the identity on $K$.

At a forth step take the next enumerated $a\notin K_\alpha$, transfer its
cut to $K'_\alpha$, and choose a realization
$y\notin\dclco(K'_\alpha\cup\{b\})$, possible by \cref{sse:co:lem:small-types}
since the excluded hull is a set. Extend the partial elementary map to the
definable hulls of $K_\alpha\cup\{a\}$ and $K'_\alpha\cup\{y\}$. Exchange
keeps $b$ omitted: if $b\in\dclco(K'_\alpha,y)$, then, as
$b\notin\dclco(K'_\alpha)$, exchange would put $y$ in $\dclco(K'_\alpha,b)$.

At a cofinality step choose a fresh source element above all of the current
source model; its type is the cut above that model. Choose the target
realization above all of the current target model and above $a_\alpha$,
again outside the definable hull of that target model together with $b$. The
same extension and exchange argument applies.

At a set limit stage take unions; elementary-chain unions and finite
character of definable closure preserve the induction hypotheses, and each
stage remains set-sized. The final union has domain $\mathfrak M$, is
elementary, fixes $K$, omits $b$, and has cofinal image because of the
cofinality steps.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[A cofinal proper exponential copy \src{05}; under GBC+ETR or the standing hypothesis]\label[corollary]{sse:co:cor:exp-proper}
The ordered exponential field of surreals has a cofinal proper elementary
self-embedding fixing $\R$ pointwise and omitting $\omega$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Take $K=\R$ with its exponential structure and $b=\omega$ in
\cref{sse:co:thm:omit}; $\R_{\exp}\preccurlyeq(\No,\exp)$ is the standard
surreal exponential theorem. GBC plus elementary transfinite recursion is one
sufficient class framework for the uniform satisfaction bookkeeping.
\end{proof}
This is an existence construction. It supplies no strong Hahn formula, and no
compatibility with simplicity, the omega-map, the omnific predicate or a
derivation. Since the image is cofinal, the embedding is continuous by
\cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy}; since it is not the identity, its value action
is not the identity, by \cref{sse:thm:exprigidity}.

\begin{lemma}[Bounded extension \src{05}; under the standing hypothesis]\label[lemma]{sse:co:lem:bounded-extension}
Let $f:A\to B$ be an elementary isomorphism between set-sized elementary
submodels of $\mathfrak M$, and let $b$ be greater than every element of $B$.
For every $a\in\mathfrak M$ there is an elementary extension
$f':\dclco(A,a)\to B'$ whose image $B'$ is a set-sized elementary submodel
containing $B$, all of whose elements are still less than $b$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The case $a\in A$ is immediate. Otherwise first ignore the bound and extend
$f$ across $a$, by \cref{sse:co:lem:small-types}, to an isomorphism onto
$C=\dclco(B,y_0)$. Choose $q$ greater than every element of the set $C$. Both
$q$ and $b$ realize the same type over $B$, namely the cut above all of $B$;
this uniqueness of the type uses o-minimality, not merely an order-preserving
map. Hence there is an elementary isomorphism
\[
 \theta:\dclco(B,q)\longrightarrow\dclco(B,b)
\]
fixing $B$ and taking $q$ to $b$. Extend $\theta$ across $y_0$ by
\cref{sse:co:lem:small-types}; its extended domain contains $C$. For every
$c\in C$ the inequality $c<q$ is preserved, so $\theta(c)<b$. Thus
$B'=\theta[C]$ is the required bounded elementary submodel; compose the first
extension of $f$ with this restriction of $\theta$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[A bounded elementary copy below any external bound \src{05}; under the standing hypothesis]\label{sse:co:thm:bounded-elementary}
Let $K\preccurlyeq\mathfrak M$ be a set-sized elementary submodel and let
$b$ be greater than every element of $K$. There is an elementary
self-embedding $j:\mathfrak M\to\mathfrak M$ fixing $K$ pointwise with
\[
 |j(x)|<b\qquad(x\in\mathfrak M).
\]
It is proper and everywhere discontinuous in the ambient order topology.
\end{theorem}
This is \cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary} (home 04). Source 04 moves the
bound by realizing the type at $+\infty$ in a set model obtained by
compactness; source 05 transports an auxiliary upper bound $q$ to $b$ by the
small isomorphism $\theta$ of \cref{sse:co:lem:bounded-extension}.
\begin{proof}[Second route to \cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary} \src{05}]
Start with the identity on $K$. Enumerate the source class and at each stage
apply \cref{sse:co:lem:bounded-extension} to add the next source element while
keeping the target bound $b$. Take unions at set limit stages. Stage domains
and ranges remain sets, elementary and definably closed. Every element of the
final range belongs to some stage and so is below $b$; since the range is a
field, negatives are also below $b$, giving the absolute bound. The final map
has full domain, is elementary and fixes $K$; it omits $b$ and is noncofinal,
so \cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy} gives everywhere discontinuity.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Bounded exponential copy \src{05}; under GBC+ETR or the standing hypothesis]\label[corollary]{sse:co:cor:exp-bounded}
The ordered exponential field of surreals has a proper elementary
self-embedding $j$ fixing $\R$ pointwise with
\[
 \omega^{-1}<|j(x)|<\omega\qquad(x\ne0).
\]
It commutes with the total surreal exponential and logarithm, despite being
everywhere discontinuous, and its difference quotient tends to $+\infty$ at
every point. Moreover its range is bounded above by every positive infinite
$\log^{\circ n}\omega$, $n\in\N$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Take $K=\R$ and $b=\omega$ in \cref{sse:co:thm:bounded-elementary}; the
exponential elementary-extension theorem gives $K\preccurlyeq\mathfrak M$.
Elementarity preserves $\exp$; logarithmic compatibility follows from
uniqueness and surjectivity of exponentiation. The lower bound follows by
applying the upper bound to $j(x)^{-1}=j(x^{-1})$, and the quotient assertion
is \cref{sse:co:rem:boundeddiverge}. For $u$ in the range, $\exp^{\circ n}(u)$
is in the range and so is less than $\omega$; applying $n$ logarithms gives
$u<\log^{\circ n}\omega$.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
This (with \cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary}) answers, only under the
stated class framework, the base question ``Proper exponential copies'' of
\cref{sse:sec:agenda} as to whether a bounded range can occur, and the
corresponding questions of sources 02 and 07. Source 05 records that it
settles a question of its own earlier edition. The map is not explicit: no
strong, monomial or omnific compatibility is claimed.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[What the elementary copies separate \src{05}]\label[remark]{sse:co:rem:elementaryseparation}
The range in \cref{sse:co:cor:exp-bounded} is a proper class lying below
every $\log^{\circ n}\omega$; this is possible because the range is a proper
class, not because a set escaped a cardinality restriction, and its birthdays
remain unbounded. Together, \cref{sse:co:thm:omit,sse:co:thm:bounded-elementary}
separate exponential elementarity from both surjectivity and cofinality.
They make no claim of strong summability, monomial control or simplicity
preservation, and do not contradict exponential valuation rigidity: their
induced value-group embeddings are not the identity. For plain real closed
fields the same back-and-forth explains why a real need not be fixed: a
transcendental real and an infinitesimal perturbation of it have the same cut
over the real algebraic numbers, and a small ordered-field isomorphism
between their real closures extends to an automorphism of $\No$ that does not
preserve the named real subfield.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Lacunary-sum witness \src{05}; GBC]\label[proposition]{sse:co:prop:nonstrong}
There is a field automorphism $\sigma$ of $\No$ fixing $\R$ and $\omega$
pointwise but not preserving a specified set-indexed Hahn sum. In particular
continuity does not imply strong additivity.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Put $t=\omega^{-1}$ and
\[
 z=\sum_{n\ge1}t^{n!},\qquad z_N=\sum_{1\le n\le N}t^{n!}.
\]
This is a valid Hahn sum: its $t$-exponents strictly increase, that is, its
Conway exponents strictly decrease. First, $z$ is transcendental over
$\R(t)$. Suppose $P(t,z)=0$ for a nonzero $P(T,Y)\in\R[T,Y]$ with
$\deg_TP=m$ and $\deg_YP=d$. For all large $N$, $P(t,z_N)\ne0$, since
$P(t,Y)$ is a nonzero polynomial in $Y$ ($t$ being transcendental over $\R$)
with at most $d$ roots, and the $z_N$ are distinct. As a polynomial in $t$ of
degree at most $m+dN!$, the nonzero element $P(t,z_N)$ has $t$-adic order at
most $m+dN!$. But
\[
 P(t,z)-P(t,z_N)=(z-z_N)\,Q(t,z,z_N)
\]
for a real polynomial $Q$; every monomial of $Q(t,z,z_N)$ has nonnegative
$t$-adic order, and $z-z_N$ has order $(N+1)!$. Hence $P(t,z_N)=-(z-z_N)Q$
has order at least $(N+1)!$. Choosing $N$ with $(N+1)!>m+dN!$ is a
contradiction.

Let $F$ be the real closure of $\R(t)$ in $\No$, a set-sized real closed
subfield; $z$ is transcendental over $F$ as well. The positive distances
$|z-a|$, $a\in F$, form a set, so choose $\varepsilon>0$ below all of them.
Then $z$ and $z+\varepsilon$ have the same cut over $F$, hence the same
nonalgebraic one-variable type in the real closed field language. The
resulting elementary isomorphism between their definable closures extends to
an automorphism $\sigma$ of $\No$ by the pure-field case of
\cref{sse:co:lem:small-types}. It fixes $F\ni t,\omega$ and sends $z$ to
$z+\varepsilon\ne z$. All summands $t^{n!}$ are fixed, so
\[
 \sigma\Bigl(\sum_{n\ge1}t^{n!}\Bigr)\ne\sum_{n\ge1}\sigma(t^{n!}).
\]
Every field automorphism is continuous by \cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy}.
\end{proof}
The separation does not treat formal summation as a topological limit. It
complements \cref{sse:cor:strongstabilizer}, which shows that strong Hahn
summability does not give continuity.
\begin{wnote}
The proposition uses only the pure ordered-field case of
\cref{sse:co:lem:small-types}, which by quantifier elimination needs only GBC
with global choice; the extension step is \lbl{saut:thm:orderedhomogeneity}
(\cref{sse:an:fnd:thm:homogeneity}). The witness is new to the collection.
\end{wnote}

\begin{theorem}[Internally definable field maps \src{05}]\label{sse:co:thm:definable}
Let $M$ be an o-minimal expansion of a real closed field. Every unital field
self-embedding whose graph is definable in $M$, with parameters, is the
identity. This applies to the pure surreal field and to its standard
o-minimal exponential structure, with the class interpretation fixed above.
\end{theorem}
This is \cref{sse:thm:definable}, which the base proves by an interval in the
definable fixed field. Source 05 argues through calculus.
\begin{proof}[Second route to \cref{sse:thm:definable} \src{05}]
The differentiability theorem for definable functions gives a nonempty
interval on which the embedding is continuously differentiable. Choose $u<v$
there; strict increase gives $j(v)-j(u)>0$, and the definable mean value
theorem gives a point where the derivative is nonzero.
\Cref{sse:co:prop:derivative-rigid}, whose proof works over any ordered
field with these limits, forces $j=\id$.
\end{proof}
Only ordinary o-minimal calculus is used \cite{co-Coste}. The theorem shows
why the zero-derivative automorphisms $S_q$ cannot have internally definable
graphs, and warns against conflating ``explicit'' (as $S_q$ and $J_f$ are, in
set theory via normal forms) with ``internally definable''.

\begin{remark}[Birthday languages and set-theoretic embeddings \src{05}]\label[remark]{sse:co:rem:birthdaylanguage}
Adding a birthday function, sign-prefix predicates or coding relations can
destroy the elementarity of maps elementary in the pure field. The literal
external equality $\bday(j(x))=\bday(x)$ is already rigid by
\cref{sse:thm:birthday}; but preserving a birthday function as a symbol of an
expanded structure can instead mean $j(\bday(x))=\bday(j(x))$, where ordinal
values may move, and the latter cannot be replaced by the former without
proof. An elementary $j:V\to M$ from large-cardinal theory acts on a sign
sequence as the set $j(s)$, with an external target, an altered ordinal
domain, and possible extra coordinates in $j(\alpha)\setminus j[\alpha]$; a
surreal field map requires absoluteness of the chosen arithmetic between the
universes, and is not the coordinatewise prescription on image coordinates.
Conversely none of the explicit surreal field embeddings determines an
elementary map of $(V,\in)$.
\end{remark}
\begin{wnote}
The large-cardinal map is carried out in
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/large-cardinal-embeddings-and-normal-forms}:
its \lbl{lce:prop:J} gives, from an elementary class embedding $j:V\to M$
into a transitive $M$ with a critical point (for instance a normal ultrapower
by a measurable cardinal), an ordered-field embedding $\mathcal J_j$ of $\No$ fixing
$\R$, preserving the omega-map and prefix simplicity, with
$\bday(\mathcal J_j(x))=j(\bday(x))$, that is, the second reading above.
Source 05 does not cite it; source 02 treats it in Part II.
\end{wnote}

\section{Differential, hyperserial and higher-dimensional variants}\label{sse:co:sec:differential}

A differential self-embedding satisfies $j(\partial x)=\partial(j(x))$ for a
named derivation $\partial$. This is unrelated to whether the \emph{function}
$j$ has an ordinary derivative: the zero-derivative phenomenon of
\cref{sse:prop:zeroderivative} does not say that $S_q$ commutes with any
derivation. For a derivation with constant field exactly $\R$, as in the
Berarducci--Mantova setting \cite{BM,sr-ADH}, every differential
self-embedding fixes $\R$ pointwise (\cref{sse:prop:differential}); this
fails for a derivation with a larger constant field.

\begin{criterion}[A monomial test \src{05}]\label[criterion]{sse:co:crit:deriv}
Suppose $\partial$ is strongly $\R$-linear and annihilates $\R$, and $j$ is
a strongly $\R$-linear field embedding, where strongness includes
preservation of each summable family occurring in the compositions below.
Then $j\partial=\partial j$ if and only if $j(\partial\omega^a)=\partial(j(\omega^a))$
for every Conway monomial $\omega^a$.
\end{criterion}
\begin{proof}
For a normal form $x=\sum r_a\omega^a$, strongness gives
$j(\partial x)=\sum r_aj(\partial\omega^a)$ and
$\partial(j(x))=\sum r_a\partial(j(\omega^a))$, and the monomial equalities
identify the sums. Necessity is immediate.
\end{proof}
This reduces a verification problem; it constructs nothing, and it shows why
an unjustified interchange of a class sum and an operator would be fatal.

\begin{definition}[Diagonal derivations \src{05}]\label[definition]{sse:co:def:diagonal}
For an additive class map $\lambda:(\No,+)\to(\R,+)$, for example extraction
of a fixed normal-form coefficient, put
\[
 \partial_\lambda\Bigl(\sum r_a\omega^a\Bigr)=\sum r_a\lambda(a)\omega^a.
\]
\end{definition}
The output support is contained in the input support. Coefficientwise
addition and $\lambda(a+b)=\lambda(a)+\lambda(b)$ give the Leibniz rule
through finite coefficient convolutions; $\partial_\lambda$ is strongly
$\R$-linear and annihilates $\R$.

\begin{proposition}[Commutant of a diagonal derivation \src{05}]\label[proposition]{sse:co:prop:diagonal-deriv}
For a weighted embedding $j=H_{\tau,\chi}$,
\[
 \partial_\lambda j=j\partial_\lambda
 \iff
 \lambda(\tau(a))=\lambda(a)\quad\text{for all }a\in\No .
\]
The constant field of $\partial_\lambda$ is exactly $\NF{\ker\lambda}$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
On $\omega^a$ the two compositions have the coefficients
$\chi(a)\lambda(\tau(a))$ and $\chi(a)\lambda(a)$ at the exponent $\tau(a)$.
Since $\chi(a)\ne0$, equality is the displayed condition, and
\cref{sse:co:crit:deriv} extends it to every normal form. A series is killed
by $\partial_\lambda$ exactly when all its nonzero terms have exponents in
$\ker\lambda$.
\end{proof}
These are genuine differential self-embeddings for a named derivation, but
not for the Berarducci--Mantova derivation, and their constant fields can be
much larger than $\R$. Specifying the derivation is as important as
specifying the exponential. For example, for $\lambda(a)=[a]_{-1}$ every
ordinal-fixing $J_{f_\beta}$ of \cref{sse:thm:stabilizer} with $\beta<-1$
commutes with $\partial_\lambda$ exactly when $A_{f_\beta}$ preserves the
coefficient of $\omega^{-1}$, which it does, since $f_\beta$ fixes every
exponent $\ge\beta$. \wtag

\begin{remark}[Composition and hyperserial substitutions \src{05}]\label[remark]{sse:co:rem:hyperserial}
Substitution by another positive infinite surreal is a natural candidate
source of embeddings compatible with exponential or hyperexponential
operations. The hyperseries literature gives precise embedding results on
specified hyperseries subfields \cite{sr-Bag24}; extension from a specified
subfield to all of $\No$, with the desired compatibility and summability, must
be verified separately, and a notation $x\mapsto x\circ g$ is not by itself a
total self-embedding theorem (compare the substitution paragraph of
\cref{sse:sec:differential}). The omega-map, the Gonshor exponential,
hyperexponentials and a chosen derivation should each be an explicit symbol
of the language; preserving one does not automatically preserve the others,
and joint compatibility is a promising source of rigidity questions.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Quaternionic extensions \src{05}]\label[remark]{sse:co:rem:quaternion}
Besides the two extensions of a field embedding to $\No(i)$ (``Algebraic
variants'' in \cref{sse:sec:modeltheory}), coordinatewise application of a
field embedding $j$ embeds the quaternion algebra over $\No$ when the three
standard units are fixed, since their multiplication table has integer
coefficients. Allowing the units themselves to move adds an independent
algebraic symmetry problem. Neither extension supplies a contour theory or
an analytic embedding merely from its field properties.
\end{remark}

\section{Formalization architecture and finite checks of source 05}\label{sse:co:sec:formal}

\paragraph{Separate interfaces.} Source 05 proposes separate layers for sign
sequences, the field, normal forms, valuation, exponentiation and
summability, noting that the repository's generic displacement machinery and
sign-sequence work do not mean that all bridges between these layers have
been verified. The following are \emph{interface descriptions}, not
submitted or tested Lean code:
\begin{verbatim}
SignSequence.{u}      -- ordinal-length signs in a larger universe
birthday : SignSequence -> Ordinal.{u}
IsPrefix : SignSequence -> SignSequence -> Prop
OrderEmbedding       -- order preservation and reflection
TreeOrderEmbedding   -- OrderEmbedding + prefix compatibility
SmallCutData         -- explicitly small left and right option types
HahnSummable         -- set support + local finite occurrence
StrongMap            -- preserves HahnSummable and its sums
FieldEmbedding       -- unital ring homomorphism between fields
ValueAction          -- v (e x) = rho (v x), x != 0
ExpCompatibility     -- e (exp x) = exp (e x)
CofinalRange         -- every target has a larger image element
\end{verbatim}
The inspected repository declares \code{SignSequence : Type (u + 1)} with
birthday in \code{Ordinal.\{u\}}; its cut constructor accepts
\code{SmallCutData.\{u, u + 1\}} and combines the simplest-separator theorem
with \code{small\_cut\_fillers}, so existence is supplied explicitly rather
than hidden in a uniqueness statement \cite{RepoSigns,co-RepoCut}. A
universe-polymorphic surreal type is small in one universe only by being
large relative to another and does not contain every ordinal of its own
larger universe; cut filling must carry a smallness parameter, otherwise a
type-level Burali--Forti error is hidden in the interface. In particular
\cref{sse:co:thm:small-discrete} becomes a theorem about appropriately small
families.

\paragraph{A short route to the new proof obligations.}
\begin{enumerate}
\item Reuse the repository's canonical-cut and prefix lemmas to formalize
birthday-nonincreasing rigidity (sign model and order only).
\item Implement successor and limit padding as a recursion and prove
\cref{sse:co:thm:local}; derive rank noncontraction and the initial-image
theorem.
\item Prove the bounded-difference identity for two ring homomorphisms and
\cref{sse:co:lem:two} over arbitrary ordered fields, with cofinality as the
exact hypothesis replacing surjectivity.
\item Formalize the topology dichotomy for ordinary ordered fields before
instantiating it on a surreal type.
\item Formalize $A_f$ and $H_\tau$ first on ordinary set-indexed Hahn groups
and fields; a second module handles the universe-relative normal-form bridge.
\end{enumerate}
The proof of the dilation derivatives should then be split into an algebraic
leading-exponent lemma and an $\varepsilon$--$\delta$ theorem; a proof for
rational exponents only, or monomial inputs only, is not the full assertion.

\paragraph{What source 05 inspected.} At its pinned commit, through the
GitHub connector: \fn{Surreal/Algebra/ExponentialProfile.lean} (opening
declarations and initial ordered-field proofs; the file marks the actual
surreal exponential/valuation instantiations as pending);
\fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity/README.md} (opening 190
lines); \fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms/README.md} (opening
100 lines) and its \fn{13-omnific-isomorphisms-SOURCE_AUDIT.md} (opening 110
lines); \fn{Surreal/Foundations/SignSequence.lean} (opening 115 lines); and
\fn{Surreal/Foundations/SignSequenceCutOperation.lean} (opening 130 lines).
This was not a complete repository audit or a local rebuild, and an
implementation comment was not treated as a substitute for checking
assumptions; in particular the existence premise of a simplest-separator
theorem must not disappear when quoted.
\begin{wnote}
The claims checked against the repository at its HEAD: the
\code{SignSequence} carrier and its universe levels, the cut constructor on
\code{SmallCutData.\{u, u + 1\}}, the pending surreal instantiation of the
profile lemmas (recorded in the project's \fn{FORMALIZATION.md}), the
surjectivity hypothesis of \code{eq\_of\_profile\_eq}, the repository's
answer to \cite[Question~5.4]{KKS} (\fn{exponential-automorphism-rigidity}'s
\lbl{cor:question54}), and the fraction-field identity
(\lbl{odg:thm:fractions}) are all accurate. The files source 05 read are
byte-identical at the placement commit.
\end{wnote}

\paragraph{Reproducible finite checks.} Source 05's script, shipped as
\fn{code/05-comprehensive-verification.py} (standard library only, exact
fractions and sparse symbolic polynomials, deterministic seed $20261003$,
\code{--seed} and \code{--output} options), checks first-difference
comparisons and prefix/meet compatibility of finite sign words up to length
$6$, prefix embeddings, rational compression and the rational ordinal fixer,
finite additive reindexing, nested Hahn convolution under the two-level
lift, weighted characters, composition and omnific predicates in the finite
model, explicit monomial witnesses, the lacunary degree gap, and the twisted
displacement and two-map product identities; it records an explicit
counterexample to multiplicativity of unrestricted one-level reindexing
(\cref{sse:co:rem:oneLevel}). The recorded run,
\fn{data/05-comprehensive-verification.json}, passes $146{,}914$ assertions
in 28 groups. Finite calculations cannot certify set-sized summability,
arbitrary ordinal padding, existence of class maps, the
$\varepsilon$--$\delta$ derivative on all surreals, or the model-theoretic
constructions; those rest on the written proofs and their stated inputs. Its
\code{build.sh}, shipped as \fn{code/05-comprehensive-build.sh}, runs the
checks under their delivery names and rebuilds the delivered PDF.
\begin{wnote}
Rerun on a copy at placement: PASS, $146{,}914$ assertions, output equal to
the delivered record up to line endings. The earlier edition's suite (not
shipped) had further groups (canonical cone, sign repetition, dilation
derivative signs, two-probe amplification) that this rewrite does not keep
under those names.
\end{wnote}
