\section{Research questions of source 05}\label{sse:co:sec:questions}

Source 05 presents these as scoped research targets, not claims that their
precise formulations have never appeared. It records that two tempting
questions are already settled in it, and removed from its agenda: the strong
weighted differentiability question, by \cref{sse:co:thm:derivative-criterion},
and the existence of noncofinal elementary exponential copies, by
\cref{sse:co:cor:exp-bounded} in its class framework (both were questions of
its earlier edition). The base questions cited below are those of
\cref{sse:sec:agenda}, named by title.

\subsection{Near-term formalization projects}

\begin{question}[Cofinal profile formalization \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:profile}
Replace the surjective comparison-map hypothesis in the repository's
two-profile theorem by cofinality, using \cref{sse:co:lem:two}. Bundle the
resulting faithful value-group action for cofinal exponential embeddings in a
universe-relative setting.
\end{question}
This is a small generic ordered-field project; it should precede a surreal
instantiation, so that missing exponential or valuation bridges cannot be
mistaken for a proof of the concrete application.
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Near-term formalization''. The target theorem is
\fn{exponential-automorphism-rigidity}'s \lbl{cor:two-profiles}, Lean
\code{eq\_of\_profile\_eq}; open.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[A programmable tree interface \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:tree}
Formalize the local padding description with explicit successor blocks and
limit padding. Which restrictions on those data preserve a given birthday
cutoff, are chain-continuous, or admit effective descriptions on ordinal
notations?
\end{question}
The implementation should derive simplicity reflection and meet preservation
rather than include them as redundant axioms, and should distinguish tree
continuity from order-topological continuity.

\begin{question}[Reusable Hahn-lift and fixed-field libraries \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:hahnlib}
Formalize $A_f$, $H_{\tau,\chi}$, their composition law, support rigidity and
the exact fixed-field formulas. Can the two-level functor be implemented
without identifying the ambient surreal field with a same-universe Hahn
series type?
\end{question}
The first target is an ordinary set-sized Hahn field; universe lifting and the
surreal normal-form bridge should be separate theorems.
\begin{wnote}
Partial existing Lean material: \code{workspaceEmbedding}
(\fn{Surreal/HahnSeries/WorkspaceEmbedding.lean}, Hahn series along an
injective additive exponent map), \code{hahnLift}
(\fn{Surreal/HahnSeries/ValueGroupLifts.lean}, for order automorphisms of the
exponent group only) and \fn{Surreal/HahnSeries/CharacterTwist.lean}. Not
checked here whether they meet the question's specification.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Formal topology at the correct size \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:topology}
Formalize the cofinality--continuity dichotomy and the collapse of small
convergent nets. Construct a framework for class-indexed neighbourhood nets,
or formulate continuity entirely by neighbourhood predicates, without
positing a set of all open subclasses.
\end{question}
A useful test is to represent both a nowhere-continuous bounded embedding and
its preservation of every small net limit, so that their coexistence is
explicit rather than an apparent inconsistency in the library.

\subsection{Structural classification projects}

\begin{question}[Simultaneous field and simplicity embeddings \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:fieldsimplicity}
Give explicit, assumption-audited constructions or obstructions for proper
self-embeddings preserving both field operations and sign-prefix simplicity.
Does adding fixation of $\R$, chain continuity, or preservation of selected
simplest cuts force rigidity?
\end{question}
The padding theorem alone supplies no field laws, and the Hahn lift alone no
simplicity laws (\cref{sse:co:ex:truncation}); a result must bridge the two
structures, not exchange their notions of truncation.
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Field and simplicity together''. A conditional
construction exists in the collection: \lbl{lce:prop:J} of
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/large-cardinal-embeddings-and-normal-forms}
gives, from an elementary $j:V\to M$ with a critical point, a nonidentity
ordered-field embedding fixing $\R$ and preserving and reflecting prefix
simplicity, and it is proper (\lbl{lce:cs:prop:proper}). In ZFC or GBC
alone the question remains open.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Two noncofinal exponential embeddings \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:noncofinal}
If two exponential self-embeddings have the same induced natural value-group
embedding but both have bounded images, must they agree? Which additional
hypotheses recover the cofinal profile uniqueness theorem?
\end{question}
\Cref{sse:co:thm:bounded-elementary} shows that noncofinal exponential maps
are a genuine case. The bounded-difference proof fails at a precisely
identified step: the comparison map cannot provide arbitrarily large target
values. For plain fields, two bounded images can have uniformly bounded
difference without the maps being equal.
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Remove or justify the cofinality hypothesis'';
source 04 asks the same. Open.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Strong exponential proper copies \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:strongexp}
Can the bounded or cofinal elementary exponential embeddings constructed here
be chosen strongly $\R$-linear, monomial-preserving, or with a prescribed
class of supports? Which ordered-group embeddings actually occur as $\tau_j$?
\end{question}
The rational-dilation obstruction (\cref{sse:co:prop:rationalnonlift})
already excludes many valid ordered-group embeddings; an extension theorem
must address all exponential equations and all relevant set-indexed
summability conditions.
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base questions ``Characterize exponential value actions'' and
``Proper exponential copies'', and
\fn{exponential-automorphism-rigidity}'s \lbl{q:image} (the second sentence
restates it for embeddings). Source 04 gives a criterion for normalized Hahn
lifts to preserve exponentiation (\cref{sse:at:prop:expcriterion}, Part III),
which bears on the first sentence; whether it yields a strong proper
exponential copy is not checked here.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Omega-field self-consistency \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:omegafield}
Analyze proper solutions of $j=H_j$ and the role of choices at omega fixed
points. Can one describe an invariant that decides when a partial monomial
assignment extends to a total omega-compatible field embedding?
\end{question}
Recursion on normal-form exponents is not automatically well founded: an
epsilon-type number can be its own leading exponent. This is a concrete
obstruction to a superficial uniqueness proof.
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``$\omega$-map compatibility'' and
\lbl{opa:as:q:exp} of \fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms}.
Source 05 does not mention that \lbl{lce:prop:J} of
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/large-cardinal-embeddings-and-normal-forms}
already supplies, conditionally on an elementary $j:V\to M$ with a critical
point, proper (\lbl{lce:cs:prop:proper}) field embeddings $\mathcal J_j$
commuting with the omega-map
($\mathcal J_j(\omega^a)=\omega^{\mathcal J_j(a)}$); that report shows they
are not strongly additive, so they do not solve
$j=H_j$ in the strong monomial sector, which stays open.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Ordinals and omnific integers \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:ordinalsoz}
Classify embeddings of the pair $(\No,\Oz)$ under cofinality, and determine
how pointwise fixation of all ordinals interacts with exponential, omega-map
or differential compatibility. Which hypotheses force an abstract pair
embedding to be strongly additive?
\end{question}
The proper ordinal-fixing plain-field copy is explicit (\cref{sse:thm:explicit});
pointwise fixation of every omnific integer is rigid by
$\operatorname{Frac}(\Oz)=\No$. Results about surjective pair automorphisms
must not be transferred silently to proper embeddings.
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Floor-preserving maps outside the monomial
class''. Proper strong $\Oz$-preserving embeddings with each combination of
cofinality and coinitiality of the value group are
\lbl{opa:as:thm:four}, and parameter-fixed ones \lbl{opa:as:thm:parameters};
automatic strongness for the pair is the subject of
\fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms} Part III. The
classification asked here is open.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Fixed-field realization \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:fixedfield}
Which real closed subclasses arise as fixed fields of arbitrary surreal
self-embeddings? Which arise in the strong monomial sector, the exponential
sector, or the differential sector?
\end{question}
The strong weighted answer reduces to $\NF{\Fix\tau\cap\ker\chi}$ and the
double lift gives $\NF{\NF{\Fix f}}$; determine their limitations and the
closure constraints imposed by named functions.
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Prescribe the fixed field''. The common fixed
field of all automorphisms is $\Q^{\mathrm{rc}}$ (\lbl{saut:prop:fixedfields}
of \fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms}); open otherwise.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Beyond monomial-to-term maps \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:unitfactors}
Classify strong embeddings whose monomial images have nontrivial unit factors
rather than a single term. Which summability and separation conditions on the
unit factors suffice for multiplicativity and injectivity?
\end{question}
The character $\chi(a)$ is real-valued; replacing it by a general positive
unit introduces support unions and possible infinite coefficient
interactions, and the finite convolution proof is no longer enough.
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Beyond monomial images''. The unit twists
$U_\beta$ (\cref{sse:prop:unitlift}) and the Taylor shift
\eqref{sse:eq:taylorshift} are examples with controlled unit factors; the
coefficient flow \lbl{saut:thm:coeffflow} and OPA's Taylor maps
$\mathcal T_{d,\delta}$ are further ones. The classification is open.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Ordinary derivatives beyond the strong sector \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:derivatives}
For embeddings not known to preserve Hahn sums, what structural conditions
force derivative zero, a finite derivative, or divergence? Can a non-strong
field automorphism have ordinary derivative zero everywhere?
\end{question}
\Cref{sse:co:thm:derivative-criterion} settles the strong monomial-to-term
sector only. A finite nonzero derivative is already rigid; the variable cases
are zero derivative and failure of a finite limit.
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``A scale-sensitive calculus''. Open.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Distinguished differential compatibility \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:differential}
Find usable monomial assignments satisfying \cref{sse:co:crit:deriv} for the
Berarducci--Mantova derivation. Study the common commutant of that
derivation, the exponential and specified hyperserial operations among
proper self-embeddings.
\end{question}
The diagonal derivations $\partial_\lambda$ are a controlled test case but
must not be confused with the distinguished derivation; constant fields and
the domain of any composition must be part of every statement.
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Differential centralizers''. A necessary
condition is \cref{sse:prop:differential}, sharpened by the translation
parameter $j(\omega)=\omega+c_j$ of \cref{sse:sr:exp:translationparameter}
(home 06). Open.
\end{wnote}

\subsection{Foundational and computational projects}

\begin{question}[Minimal class-theoretic assumptions \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:classtheory}
Determine the weakest class theory supporting the uniform bounded-copy and
omission constructions for the surreal exponential field. Can a fixed
syntactic presentation remove the need for the full satisfaction apparatus
used here?
\end{question}
GBC plus elementary transfinite recursion is a sufficient setting, not a
lower bound; the generic algebraic and sign-sequence arguments use much less.
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Minimal foundational strength''. Source 06's
epsilon-stage interpretation (\cref{sse:sr:rem:epsstages}) is a candidate
answer to the second sentence; unchecked (see the note at the start of
\cref{sse:co:sec:model}).
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Coherent universe-relative maps \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:coherent}
Given embeddings on a cofinal system of birthday cutoffs, when can they be
chosen compatibly and assembled into a full class embedding? Conversely,
which full class embeddings restrict to self-maps of a prescribed unbounded
family of cutoffs?
\end{question}
A separately existing embedding at each cutoff is not a coherent family; the
target birthday bound may depend on both cutoff and map, and neither global
choice nor a cardinality count supplies compatibility.
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Universe and cutoff absoluteness''. For the
converse, every class map restricts to self-maps of the stages
$\No_{<\lambda}$ for a closed unbounded class of $\lambda$ (``Coherent
restrictions'' in \cref{sse:sec:formal}; \cref{sse:sr:found:prop:localization},
home 06); a \emph{prescribed} family is not covered. Open.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Effective descriptions and minimal rigid languages \src{05}]\label[question]{sse:co:q:effective}
For computable notations for restricted surreal expressions, which of the
explicit lifts admit effective evaluation and equality testing? Separately,
what small expansions of the field language force every internally definable
or every structure-preserving embedding to be the identity?
\end{question}
These are different questions: a finite expression procedure does not
compute arbitrary ordinal-length signs, and set-theoretic definability is not
definability inside an o-minimal field.

\paragraph{Suggested sequence of work \src{05}.} Begin with the generic
cofinal two-map lemma, small-cut birthday rigidity and the topology
dichotomy; next implement the two Hahn lifts and their support and
fixed-field statements; only then combine the normal-form, valuation and
exponential interfaces. The last stage should address either a strong
exponential construction or rigidity under simultaneous field and simplicity
preservation, with a separate class-theoretic dependency audit.

\section{Status and non-claims of source 05}\label{sse:co:sec:status}

\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Status.} Source 05 is an unrefereed research article with
written proofs and explicitly scoped background results. It is not
independently refereed, includes no new Lean certificate, and no
repository-wide Lean build was run. No claim of historical priority is made;
``developed here'' describes its contents (\cref{sse:co:rem:developed}).
\item \emph{Credited repository results.} Exponential valuation rigidity and
the omnific fraction-field identity are credited to the repository and
proved again; the generic Lean source was inspected separately from the
pending concrete surreal exponential/valuation instantiations. The repository
answer to \cite[Question~5.4]{KKS} is supported mathematically by the proof,
but source 05 claims no independent publication, priority or community
verification of it.
\item \emph{Scope of classifications.} No complete classification of
arbitrary exponential, omega-field or differential self-embeddings is
claimed. The weighted theorem classifies a specified sector only; the
two-level lift is a representation, not a classification of all field
embeddings. \cite[Question~5.6]{KKS} is not solved.
\item \emph{Conditional results.} The elementary-copy constructions
(\cref{sse:co:sec:model}) rest on uniform satisfaction, definable Skolem
operations, o-minimality and global choice; GBC+ETR is a sufficient
framework, not a minimal one. These copies carry no strong-sum, monomial,
simplicity, omega-map, omnific or derivation compatibility.
\item \emph{Lean.} The cofinal strengthening of the two-profile comparison
has no new Lean proof; the interface list of \cref{sse:co:sec:formal} is
design, not code.
\item \emph{Finite checks.} They test implementations and algebraic
identities, not transfinite recursion, arbitrary Hahn summability, full-class
$\varepsilon$--$\delta$ limits, proper-class existence, class truth, or the
elementary-copy constructions.
\item \emph{Sources and audit.} Repository reads were targeted, through the
GitHub connector at a pinned commit; no clone, rebuild or full proof build was
completed, which limits any statement about the repository's overall
coverage. The arXiv metadata of \cite{KKS} was checked on 3 October 2026 and
pages 4 and 10 of its v3 PDF were inspected for the valuation and
leading-term conventions and Questions~5.4 and~5.6; the experimental HTML,
with a different internal date, was not used as a separate version. The
primary PDF of \cite{vdDE} was consulted, including Proposition~4.7 on
printed page 183. Conway, Gonshor and Coste were not audited cover to cover;
Coste's archive access returned a bot challenge, so its o-minimal calculus
inputs are stated rather than freshly audited. Exact-phrase literature
searches are not evidence that a result has no antecedent.
\item \emph{Earlier drafts.} Earlier project drafts were read and used as
working material, not as peer-reviewed authority.
\item \emph{Build record.} The delivered PDF had 39 pages, 16 research
questions, no LaTeX errors, no undefined references, no overfull boxes and
no replacement glyphs (\fn{data/05-comprehensive-BUILD_AUDIT.json}); the
repository was not modified.
\end{itemize}
\begin{wnote}
Source 05 is the expanded edition of an earlier manuscript of the same batch,
which is not a separate source of this report (see the provenance note of
the report). Its repository claims were checked at placement and none is
false; its omission of \fn{surcomplex-field-automorphisms},
\fn{independent-surreal-copies} and
\fn{discrete-initial-subgroups-and-omnific-normalization} is corrected label by
label in this Part.
\end{wnote}

% sub-prefix sse:sr: ; drafted for batch 84 (w84)
% ======================================================================
\part{Source 06: Structure, Rigidity, and Foundations}\label{sse:part:sr}

\section{Source 06 at a glance}\label{sse:sr:sec:glance}

Source 06 is \emph{Self-Embeddings of the Surreal Numbers: Structure,
Rigidity, and Foundations. A systematic study with explicit constructions,
proofs, and a research agenda}, subtitled ``A study of the ProveIt
development'' and dated 3 October 2026 (47 pages; 10 sections, 22
bibliography entries, 11 numbered research questions plus unnumbered target
lists; no code). Its repository reading is pinned to ProveIt commit
\code{79049d58cc66341825ac5b1009ad7fd3b7bebf31}. Its foundational setting is
G\"odel--Bernays class theory with Global Choice (GBC), with the Replacement
and comprehension instances for the class maps involved stated explicitly;
surreals are set-length sign sequences and Conway normal forms have set
supports. Uniform truth predicates and class-valued recursions (ETR,
Kelley--Morse) are named whenever used, and large-cardinal constructions are
kept conditional.

About half of source 06 is a \emph{credited synthesis} of collection
material: Part \lbl{opa:part:as} of \fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms},
\fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity} (EAR),
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/large-cardinal-embeddings-and-normal-forms}
(LCE) and \fn{docs/surreal/omnific-diophantine-geometry} (ODG). Those statements
are reprinted once below under their collection labels, as reprints and never
as new results. Its genuinely new material is the exponential defect cocycle
(\cref{sse:sr:exp:defect}), the translation parameter
(\cref{sse:sr:exp:translationparameter}), the club localization
(\cref{sse:sr:found:prop:localization}), the transcendence corollary
\cref{sse:sr:logic:transcendental}, the exponential automorphisms moving
$\omega$ (\cref{sse:sr:exp:moveomega}), the omega-series restriction
(\cref{sse:sr:prop:omegarestriction}) and a set of literature consequences
in \cref{sse:sr:sec:exp}; the Bagayoko--van der Hoeven fixed-point theorem is
printed here as the home of that overlap (\cref{sse:sr:ord:fixedintersection}).

\paragraph{Renames.} Source 06's symbols are renamed to the report's
canonical notation as follows.

\midrule
\endhead

Two letters keep their source meaning and are disclosed: $c_j\in\R$ is the
\emph{translation parameter} of a differential embedding (not the compression
$c$ and not a real character), and $\alpha_j$ is the \emph{exponential
defect}. $K_\Gamma=\R((t^\Gamma))$ is a Hahn field with subscript, not the
iterated-image core $K_\infty$. Section 5 of source 06 uses the small-monomial
convention $t^\gamma=\omega^{-\gamma}$; it is announced where used.

\section{Order, simplicity, and birthdays (source 06, Section 2)}\label{sse:sr:sec:order}

Source 06 calls an \emph{ordered-simplicity embedding} a class map preserving
and reflecting both $<$ and $\pref$; its image is a surreal substructure in
the sense of \cite{BVH}. Injectivity together with preservation of the two
relations already gives reflection (\cref{sse:lem:reflection}). Its prefix
maps, birthday lemma, birthday rigidity, automorphism rigidity and
initial-image theorem are the base's results, with the same proofs
(see the table). Source 06 adds two comments: for numerical order alone the
inequality $\bday(x)\le\bday(fx)$ fails (translation by $1$ sends $-1$ to $0$);
and, by the proof of \cref{sse:thm:birthday}, at the first birthday where an
arbitrary order embedding moves a number it moves it to a strictly later
birthday (later birthdays may still decrease; compare source 05's first-move
corollary). The following material is additional.

\begin{remark}[Prefix and block examples \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:prefixblocks}
The image $P_p[\No]=\{y:p\pref y\}$ is convex and has no endpoints, so $P_p$
is an order homeomorphism onto an open convex class. Concatenation is not
field translation: for the one-sign word $+$, $P_+(0)=1$ and
$P_+(-1)=1/2$, whereas $-1+1=0$.

More generally, choose nonempty words $a_-,a_+$ whose first signs are $-$
and $+$ respectively, and let $D_{a_-,a_+}(x)$ replace every sign $\sigma$
of $x$ by $a_\sigma$, concatenated over the ordinal length of $x$. This is an
ordered-simplicity embedding (prefixes concatenate compatibly, and the first
new block begins with the sign deciding each comparison); it fixes $0$ and
preserves unions of nonempty set-sized prefix chains. These are established
examples of surreal substructures \cite[Example~4.2]{BVH}. Repeating each sign
a nonzero ordinal $\kappa$ times gives $D_\kappa$ with
$\bday(D_\kappa x)=\kappa\cdot\bday(x)$ (ordinal product); for integers this
is \cref{sse:prop:repeat}, and the ordinal sign blocks are printed in
\cref{sse:pf:prop:block} (home 02). Even $D_2$ is not additive: it sends $1$
to $2$ and $1/2$ (signs $+-$) to $++--=5/4$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Chain continuity in the local description \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:chaincont}
Source 06's Proposition~2.2 is the local padding description
\cref{sse:co:thm:local} (home 05): an ordered-simplicity embedding $F$ is
determined by a root word $r=F(\varnothing)$, nonempty successor words
$w_{s,\sigma}$ beginning with $\sigma$ (so $F(s\concat\sigma)=F(s)\concat
w_{s,\sigma}$), and possibly empty limit tails $v_s$ (so
$F(s)=(\bigcup_{\alpha<\lambda}F(s\restriction\alpha))\concat v_s$ when
$\bday(s)=\lambda$ is a nonzero limit); conversely any such data define one.
Its proof is the same. Source 06 adds a proof of the clause that $F$ preserves
unions of nonempty set-sized prefix chains \emph{exactly when every limit
tail $v_s$ is empty}: the clause holds for the chain of all proper prefixes
of a word of limit length by definition of $v_s$, and every nonempty prefix
chain without a maximum is cofinal among the proper prefixes of its union,
so this special case gives the general one.
\end{remark}

\begin{example}[A fixed-point-free substructure \src{06}; {\cite[Example~4.14]{BVH}}]\label[example]{sse:sr:ex:inc}
Let $\Incsr$ have root $+$, successor words $\sigma\concat+$ and limit tail $+$
at every nonzero limit. Ordinal induction gives
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:ord:incbirthday}
 \bday(\Incsr(x))=2\bday(x)+1>\bday(x),
\end{equation}
so $\Incsr$ is an ordered-simplicity embedding with no fixed point. The
nonempty limit tails are exactly why the iteration-and-union construction of
a fixed point (\cref{sse:thm:closedtree}) fails for it.
\end{example}

\begin{remark}[Relative brackets \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:relbracket}
Base \cref{sse:prop:cutrigid} shows that preserving \emph{ambient} canonical
brackets forces the identity. A proper surreal substructure $S=f[\No]$
instead satisfies the \emph{image-relative} bracket identity
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:ord:relativebracket}
 f(\{L\mid R\}_{\No})=\{f[L]\mid f[R]\}_{S}.
\end{equation}
Indeed, if $x=\{L\mid R\}$ and $f(y)$ lies between $f[L]$ and $f[R]$, then
$L<y<R$, so $x\pref y$ and $f(x)\pref f(y)$; hence $f(x)$ is the simplest
point of $S$ in the image cut. This proves the remark following
\cref{sse:prop:cutrigid}.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[All prefix-tree automorphisms \src{06}]\label[proposition]{sse:sr:prop:treeauts}
For a class choice of signs $\varepsilon_s\in\{-1,1\}$, one for every sign
sequence $s$, the map
\[
 \Sigma_\varepsilon(x)(\alpha)=\varepsilon_{x\restriction\alpha}\,x(\alpha)
 \qquad(\alpha<\bday(x))
\]
is an automorphism of the prefix tree $(\No,\pref)$, and every
automorphism of $(\No,\pref)$ has this form. $\Sigma_\varepsilon$ preserves
numerical order exactly when all $\varepsilon_s=1$ (then it is the identity),
and reverses it exactly when all $\varepsilon_s=-1$ (then it is $x\mapsto-x$).
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The map independently keeps or interchanges the two children at each node;
prefixes transform compatibly, and the inverse is recovered by recursion
along the length of a target sequence. Conversely, a prefix-tree automorphism
preserves ordinal height and permutes the two immediate children of each
node, which defines $\varepsilon$. If it preserves order, then
$s\concat-<s\concat+$ forces $\varepsilon_s=1$ at every node; if it reverses
order, $\varepsilon_s=-1$ at every node. Class-indexed choices are class
parameters.
\end{proof}
Thus ``preserves the surreal tree'' needs a statement about branch
orientation; the base's paragraph ``Simplicity without order'' gives the
existence half.

\begin{example}[Order automorphisms fixing every real and every ordinal \src{06}]\label[example]{sse:sr:ex:orderfix}
Let $\mathfrak m$ be the convex class of infinitesimals and put
$\dblsr_{\mathfrak m}(x)=2x$ for $x\in\mathfrak m$, $\dblsr_{\mathfrak m}(x)=x$
otherwise. Multiplication by $2$ is an increasing bijection of $\mathfrak m$,
and convexity makes $\dblsr_{\mathfrak m}$ a global order automorphism. It
fixes every real and every ordinal ($0$ is the only real infinitesimal and
nonzero ordinals are not infinitesimal) but moves every nonzero
infinitesimal; it is not additive, since
$\dblsr_{\mathfrak m}(1+\epsilon)=1+\epsilon\ne1+2\epsilon$. So the order
reduct is far from rigid even over $\R\cup\On$; this does not conflict with
the algebraic rigidity theorems. Likewise the compression
$c$ of \cref{sse:prop:compression} is a homeomorphism onto the convex class
$(-1,1)$ fixing $0$ and sending $1$ to $1/2$: properness, bounded image,
continuity and fixed familiar points are separate restrictions.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[Fixed points of a prefix map \src{06}]\label[example]{sse:sr:ex:prefixfixed}
For nonempty $p$, let $p^{\frown\omega}$ be countably many concatenated copies
of $p$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:ord:prefixfixed}
 \Fix(P_p)=P_{p^{\frown\omega}}[\No]=\bigcap_{n<\omega}P_p^n[\No].
\end{equation}
A fixed point begins with every finite repetition of $p$, hence with their
union; conversely $p\concat p^{\frown\omega}=p^{\frown\omega}$ (ordinal
absorption $\bday(p)+\bday(p)\cdot\omega=\bday(p)\cdot\omega$), which fixes
every extension of it. For instance $P_+$ fixes exactly the surreals whose
signs begin with $\omega$ consecutive pluses. This is ordinal absorption in
the sign string, not cancellation in the field.
\end{example}

The intersection formula \eqref{sse:sr:ord:prefixfixed} is a case of the
following established theorem. This report prints it here as the home of
the overlap with source 07 (\cref{sse:an:ord:fixed-core} points here).

\begin{theorem}[Fixed points are the iterated-image core \src{06}; {\cite[Proposition~5.2]{BVH}}]\label{sse:sr:ord:fixedintersection}
For every ordered-simplicity embedding $f:\No\to\No$,
\[
 \Fix(f)=\bigcap_{n<\omega}f^n[\No].
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof \src{06}, using ambient common prefixes]
The inclusion $\subseteq$ is immediate. Write $A$ for the intersection and
suppose $A\setminus\Fix(f)\ne\varnothing$. Choose $x_0$ in it of least
birthday and let $x_n$ be its unique $n$th inverse image. By injectivity every
$x_n$ lies in $A$, and none is fixed (else $x_0=f^n(x_n)=x_n$ would be).
By \cref{sse:prop:treebirthday}, $\bday(x_n)\le\bday(fx_n)=\bday(x_{n-1})$,
and minimality gives equality, so all $x_n$ have the same birthday. They are
distinct and strictly monotone numerically (order reflection propagates
$x_0<x_1$ to $x_n<x_{n+1}$); assume $x_0<x_1<\cdots$, the other case being
symmetric.

Let $u_n$ be the longest common prefix of $x_n,x_{n+1}$. Equal birthdays and
distinctness give $x_n<u_n<x_{n+1}$ and $\bday(u_n)<\bday(x_0)$. Moreover
$f(u_{n+1})\pref u_n$, being a common prefix of $f(x_{n+1})=x_n$ and
$f(x_{n+2})=x_{n+1}$. Hence
\[
 \bday(u_{n+1})\le\bday(f(u_{n+1}))\le\bday(u_n).
\]
This nonincreasing sequence of ordinals stabilizes, after which
$f(u_{n+1})=u_n$. Each sufficiently late $u_n$ therefore lies in every
$f^k[\No]$, hence in $A$. But $u_n<u_{n+1}$ and $f(u_{n+1})=u_n$ prevent $u_n$
from being fixed, contradicting the minimal birthday of $x_0$.
\end{proof}
The intersection may be empty, as for $\Incsr$ in \eqref{sse:sr:ord:incbirthday}.

\begin{wnote}
Compare the field category: for a field self-embedding the iterated-image
core $K_\infty(j)$ can strictly contain $\Fix(j)$ (\cref{sse:prop:core} and
\eqref{sse:eq:Jcore}). Simplicity preservation is what collapses the core to
the fixed class here.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[Nonempty fixed classes \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:fixednonempty}
If $f$ also preserves unions of nonempty set-sized prefix chains, the iterates
of $0$ form a prefix chain and $x_\omega=\bigcup_nf^n(0)$ is the simplest fixed
point; this is the case $u=0$ of $c_j(u)$ in \cref{sse:thm:closedtree}, and
\cref{sse:co:prop:treefix}. The whole fixed class is then a surreal
substructure closed under those unions \cite[Corollary~5.14]{BVH}; this is
\cref{sse:thm:closedtree}.
\end{remark}

\section{Field embeddings and normal forms (source 06, Section 3)}\label{sse:sr:sec:field}

Source 06 notes that a field self-embedding of $\No$ fixes $\Q$ and every real
algebraic number (an algebraic number is pinned down by its polynomial and an
isolating rational interval), and preserves order. Its lifting theorems are
the base's \cref{sse:thm:addlift,sse:thm:fieldlift,sse:thm:weighted}; source 06
states that they organize standard Hahn-field arguments with the exponent and
character constructions of \cite{sr-RepoOPA,KKS}, ``not a claim that the
lifting method is new''.

\begin{remark}[A formula for the value action \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:valueformula}
The value action of \cref{sse:prop:valueaction} is computed by
$\tau_f(a)=\val(f(\omega^{-a}))$, and for an automorphism $\tau_f$ is an
automorphism. Preserving comparisons of values, or \eqref{sse:eq:valueaction},
is automatic, while $\val(fx)=\val(x)$ is a substantial further requirement
\cite{RepoExp,KKS}. This is \lbl{opa:as:lem:natural}.
\end{remark}

\subsection*{Two explicit proper copies}
Let $P_\pm$ be the one-sign prefix maps, order isomorphisms onto $\No_{>0}$ and
$\No_{<0}$ (\cref{sse:prop:prefix}). Their additive lifts have images
\[
 A_{P_+}[\No]=\mathbb J,\qquad A_{P_-}[\No]=\mathfrak m,
\]
the additive groups of purely infinite surreals (with $0$) and of
infinitesimals, by \eqref{sse:eq:addimage}. Hence the double lifts
$J_{P_\pm}=H_{A_{P_\pm}}$ are proper strongly $\R$-linear field embeddings with
images $\NF{\mathbb J}$ and $\NF{\mathfrak m}$; both images are real closed,
being isomorphic to $\No$. Since $P_+(0)=1$ and $P_-(0)=-1$,
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:field:Fomega}
 J_{P_+}(\omega)=\omega^{\omega},\qquad J_{P_-}(\omega)=\omega^{1/\omega}.
\end{equation}

\begin{proposition}[Cofinal and bounded copies \src{06}]\label[proposition]{sse:sr:field:propercopies}
The image of $J_{P_+}$ is cofinal in $\No$ but not dense:
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:field:notdense}
 J_{P_+}[\No]\cap(\omega,2\omega)=\varnothing.
\end{equation}
Every element of the image of $J_{P_-}$ is Archimedean-smaller than
$\omega^{1/n}$ for every positive integer $n$; in particular its absolute value
is below $\omega$, and no positive image element is as small as $\omega^{-1}$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Above any exponent there is a larger purely infinite ordinal, and a monomial
with that exponent dominates any prescribed normal form; this gives
cofinality. An element of $(\omega,2\omega)$ has leading exponent $1\notin\mathbb
J$, proving \eqref{sse:sr:field:notdense}. For $J_{P_-}$ the leading exponent
$a$ of a nonzero image element is infinitesimal, so $-1/n<a<1/n$ and the
leading monomial is Archimedean-smaller than $\omega^{1/n}$ and larger than
$\omega^{-1/n}$; a nonzero real leading coefficient does not change these
comparisons.
\end{proof}
The bound sharpens, for this unparametrized copy, the bounds of
\cref{sse:thm:boundedcopy} and \cref{sse:co:thm:bounded}. The bounded image is
still a proper class, and boundedness is measured by the external element
$\omega$, which the image omits. Cofinality without density separates
algebraic elementarity, cofinality, density and continuity (the image is
closed and nowhere dense by \cref{sse:thm:closedimage}).

\begin{example}[A character moving a leading coefficient \src{06}]\label[example]{sse:sr:ex:character}
With $\chi(a)=\exp_\R(\ctsr(a))$, where $\ctsr$ is the normal-form constant
term (additive), the weighted embedding $H_{\id,\chi}$ of \cref{sse:thm:weighted}
fixes every real and every valuation value but sends $\omega$ to $e\omega$.
(The base's example uses $\chi(a)=2^{[a]_{-1}}$, which fixes $\omega$.)
Every $H_{\tau,\chi}$ preserves the constant coefficient and preserves and
reflects $\Oz$ (\cref{sse:prop:oz}). Preserving and reflecting the
canonical-monomial predicate means $\chi=1$; it does not mean fixing
monomials individually. Composition acts on characters through the exponent
map, $(\tau_1,\chi_1)(\tau_2,\chi_2)=(\tau_1\tau_2,\chi_2\cdot(\chi_1\circ\tau_2))$,
so the two kinds of coordinates form a semidirect, not a direct, product.
\end{example}

\begin{remark}[The omega-map equation is not a birthday induction \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:omegacaution}
$H_{\tau,\chi}(\omega^a)=\omega^{H_{\tau,\chi}(a)}$ for all $a$ forces $\chi=1$
and then $\tau=H_\tau$, which is \eqref{sse:eq:omegafunctional}. It is unsafe
to conclude $\tau=\id$ from this by induction on birthdays: a generalized
epsilon number $a=\omega^a$ has a self-dependent normal form. The
omega-structure questions do not reduce to the unconstrained lifting theorem.
\end{remark}

\begin{example}[A contracting flow moving $\omega$ to $\omega+1$ \src{06}]\label[example]{sse:sr:ex:contracting}
Let
$\partial_{\ctsr}\bigl(\sum_ar_a\omega^a\bigr)=\sum_ar_a\ctsr(a)\omega^{a-1}$.
Additivity of $\ctsr$ gives the Leibniz rule on monomials, so
$\partial_{\ctsr}$ is a strongly $\R$-linear derivation which strictly
lowers exponents. Its exponential is
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:field:Us}
 \mathcal U_s\Bigl(\sum_ar_a\omega^a\Bigr)
 =\sum_{a,\,n\ge0}r_a\binom{\ctsr(a)}{n}s^n\omega^{a-n}
 \qquad(s\in\R),
\end{equation}
that is, $\mathcal U_s(\omega^a)=\omega^a(1+s\omega^{-1})^{\ctsr(a)}$. The
support lies in $\supp(x)-\N$, so the Hahn support lemma gives summability;
Leibniz's formula gives multiplicativity, and the exponential identity,
justified coefficientwise, gives $\mathcal U_s\mathcal U_{s'}=\mathcal U_{s+s'}$.
So $\mathcal U_s$ is a strongly $\R$-linear automorphism with inverse
$\mathcal U_{-s}$, fixing every real and every leading term, yet
\[
 \mathcal U_1(\omega)=\omega+1,\qquad \mathcal U_1(\omega^\omega)=\omega^\omega .
\]
It does not preserve the canonical-monomial predicate. This illustrates the
contracting-derivation mechanism of \cite{KKS}; in the convention
$\val(t^a)=a$ a contracting shift is $a\mapsto a+1$.
\end{example}

\begin{wnote}
$\mathcal U_s$ differs from the base's unit twist $U_\beta$ of
\eqref{sse:eq:unitlift}: $U_\beta$ uses the coefficient $[a]_\beta$ with
$\beta<0$ and fixes $t=\omega^{-1}$, whereas $\mathcal U_s$ uses $[a]_0$ and
moves $t$ (to $t(1+st)^{-1}$); its inverse comes from the flow property, not
from fixing the binomial units. Neither preserves $\Oz$ in general:
$\mathcal U_1(\omega)=\omega+1\in\Oz$, but
$\mathcal U_1(\omega^{1/2})=\omega^{1/2}+\tfrac12\omega^{-1/2}+\cdots\notin\Oz$
although $\omega^{1/2}\in\Oz$.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[The Taylor automorphism \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:taylor}
Source 06's Theorem~3.8 is the base's Taylor automorphism $T$ of
\eqref{sse:eq:taylorshift}, written $\mathcal T_{d,1}$ here: for a derivation
$d:\R\to\R$ with $d(\theta)=1$ at a transcendental $\theta$,
$\mathcal T_{d,1}(\sum r_a\omega^a)=\sum_{a,n}\frac{d^n(r_a)}{n!}\omega^{a-n}$
is a strongly additive field automorphism fixing every canonical monomial,
every real algebraic number and every leading term, with
$\mathcal T_{d,1}(\theta)=\theta+\omega^{-1}$; it is not $\R$-linear. Its proof
is the base's (the composite with $\mathcal T_{-d,1}$ has the coefficient
$\sum_{n=0}^k(-1)^n/(n!(k-n)!)=[k=0]$ at total iterate $k$).
It separates four requirements: fixing the residue field does not fix the real
representatives; strong additivity does not imply strong $\R$-linearity;
fixing all monomials does not fix all normal forms; and preserving each
leading term does not force triviality. It is not asserted to preserve $\Oz$:
for an infinitesimal exponent $a>0$, the omnific integer $\theta\omega^a$
acquires the term $\omega^{a-1}$ with negative exponent.
\end{remark}

\begin{wnote}
This is \lbl{saut:thm:coeffflow} of \fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms}
(there $\Phi_d$, over $\mathbb C$ and with a general shift $\delta$) and the Taylor
flow of Part \lbl{opa:part:as} of \fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms};
source 06 does not cite the surcomplex report. The general maps
$\mathcal T_{d,\delta}$ with a gap are in \cref{sse:sr:prop:taylorexplicit}.
\end{wnote}

\paragraph{Ordinary field automorphisms need not respect Hahn sums.} Source
06 proves the following separation, which is \cref{sse:co:prop:nonstrong}
(home 05: an automorphism fixing $\R$ and $\omega$ but not a specified Hahn
sum) by a different witness, the pseudolimit argument behind
\cite[Example~4.4]{KKS}. Its automorphism also fixes every $\omega^{1/n}$.

\begin{proof}[Second route to \cref{sse:co:prop:nonstrong} \src{06}]
Consider the valid decreasing support
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:field:nonstrongseries}
 a=\sum_{n\ge1}\omega^{1/n},\qquad \varepsilon=\omega^{-\omega}.
\end{equation}
Let $K_{\mathrm P}$ be the real Puiseux-series field in $\omega^{-1}$, a
set-sized real closed subfield. Since the support of $a$ has unbounded
denominators, $a\notin K_{\mathrm P}$; for every $b\in K_{\mathrm P}$ the
nonzero difference $a-b$ has a rational leading exponent, hence
$|a-b|>\varepsilon$. So $a$ and $a+\varepsilon$ have the same cut over
$K_{\mathrm P}$, and real closedness of $K_{\mathrm P}$ gives an ordered
$K_{\mathrm P}$-isomorphism between the real closures of $K_{\mathrm P}(a)$ and
$K_{\mathrm P}(a+\varepsilon)$ taking $a$ to $a+\varepsilon$. Set-sized
homogeneity of the ordered field $\No$ (\cite[Remark~2.10]{KKS};
\cref{sse:an:fnd:thm:homogeneity}, \lbl{saut:thm:orderedhomogeneity}) extends it
to a field automorphism $\sigma$; only a set-sized partial map is extended.
Then $\sigma$ fixes $\R$, $\omega$ and every $\omega^{1/n}$, but
$\sigma(\sum_n\omega^{1/n})=a+\varepsilon\ne a=\sum_n\sigma(\omega^{1/n})$.
\end{proof}
Specifying the images of the monomials in one expansion does not determine
the image of its infinite sum under an arbitrary field automorphism. The
same example separates strong automorphisms from all automorphisms in the
finite pointwise-convergence topology \emph{on maps}: the neighbourhood
$\{\tau:\tau(\omega)=\omega,\ \tau(a)=a+\varepsilon\}$ contains no strongly
additive automorphism. (That topology on maps is not the order topology on
values.) Replacing $1/n$ by $-1/n$ in \eqref{sse:sr:field:nonstrongseries}
would give an increasing support with no greatest element, for which the
Hahn sum is undefined.

\begin{remark}[Closed discrete images, and four test maps \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:closeddiscrete}
Source 06's Theorem~3.9 is \cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy} with the extra
equivalent condition that $f[\No_{>0}]$ is coinitial in $\No_{>0}$, and the
extra conclusion that a noncofinal image is \emph{closed} and uniformly
discrete (as in \lbl{opa:as:thm:topology}, which source 06 credits). Proof of
closedness: if $\varepsilon>0$ lies below every positive image element,
distinct image points are at distance $\ge\varepsilon$; an interval of radius
$\varepsilon/3$ about a point outside the image contains at most one image
point, and shrinking the radius avoids it. The test maps show why the field
hypothesis matters:
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{llll}
\toprule
Map & Structure preserved & Cofinal image & Continuous\\
\midrule
$A_{P_+}$ & ordered additive group & yes & no\\
$A_{P_-}$ & ordered additive group & no & yes\\
$J_{P_+}$ & ordered field & yes & yes\\
$J_{P_-}$ & ordered field & no & no\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
For additive maps the relevant condition is positive coinitiality and
inversion is unavailable: nonzero purely infinite elements cannot approach
$0$, while the infinitesimals are coinitial among positive surreals. Passing
to the field lifts exchanges which feature of the additive image controls
small positive values. By \eqref{sse:sr:field:notdense} a continuous cofinal
field self-embedding need not have a dense image. The dichotomy for
embeddings between arbitrary ordered fields is \cref{sse:an:top:criterion}
(home 07).
\end{remark}

\section{Fixed parameters, ordinals, and logic (source 06, Section 4)}\label{sse:sr:sec:logic}

Fixing $\R$, fixing $\omega$, fixing every ordinal, and being definable in a
specified language are different restrictions; source 06 separates them by
explicit tests.

\begin{example}[An ordinal-fixing automorphism moving $\omega^{\omega^{1/2}}$ \src{06}]\label[example]{sse:sr:logic:ordinals}
Let $h(x)=2x/(1+x)$ for $0<x<1$ and $h(x)=x$ otherwise. It is an increasing
bijection of $\No$ (its inverse on $(0,1)$ is $y\mapsto y/(2-y)$) fixing every
ordinal, and it is not additive. Its double lift $J_h=H_{A_h}$ is a strongly
$\R$-linear field \emph{automorphism} (\cref{sse:thm:doublelift}). It fixes
$\R$ and every ordinal pointwise (every ordinal has a Cantor normal form with
ordinal exponents and positive integer coefficients, and $h$ fixes those
exponents at both levels), and it preserves and reflects $\Oz$
(\cref{sse:prop:oz}). It is not the identity:
\[
 J_h\bigl(\omega^{\omega^{1/2}}\bigr)=\omega^{\omega^{2/3}},\qquad
 J_h(\omega^{1/2})=\omega^{1/2},
\]
since $h(1/2)=2/3$ gives $A_h(\omega^{1/2})=\omega^{2/3}$, while $A_h(1/2)=1/2$.
The two equalities are incompatible with $J_h(\omega^x)=\omega^{J_h(x)}$ at
$x=\omega^{1/2}$, so $J_h$ does not commute with the omega-map; and it is not
simplicity-preserving, since an order-and-simplicity automorphism is the
identity (\cref{sse:prop:treebirthday}).
\end{example}

\begin{wnote}
The qualitative content of source 06's Proposition~4.1 (a nonidentity strong
automorphism fixing $\R\cup\On$ and preserving $\Oz$) is the automorphism
clause of \cref{sse:thm:stabilizer} with $A=\varnothing$, together with
\cref{sse:prop:oz}, since $J_{g_\beta}=H_{A_{g_\beta},1}$. What the base does
not give is this witness: $J_{g_\beta}$ only moves exponents below $\beta<0$
and fixes every $x$ with $E(\{x\})\subseteq[\beta,\infty)$, in particular
$\omega^{\omega^{1/2}}$ (whose footprint is $\{1/2\}$). The witness is what
\cref{sse:sr:logic:transcendental} needs. Source 06's comparison sentence (its
Proposition~4.1 ``handles one particular proper class of parameters by a
separate explicit construction'') should be read beside
\cref{sse:thm:stabilizer}, which fixes $A\cup\R\cup\On$ for every set $A$.
\end{wnote}

\begin{corollary}[Ordinals and reals do not generate \src{06}]\label[corollary]{sse:sr:logic:transcendental}
Let $K$ be the subfield of $\No$ generated by $\R\cup\On$. Then
$\omega^{\omega^{1/2}}$ is transcendental over $K$. In particular, adjoining all
ordinals to the reals and taking relative real closure does not produce
$\No$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
$J_h$ fixes $K$. An increasing field automorphism fixing $K$ fixes every
element algebraic over $K$: it permutes the finite ordered list of roots in
$\No$ of a polynomial over $K$, and an increasing permutation of a finite
linear order is the identity (as in \cref{sse:prop:fixedgeneral}). Since
$J_h$ moves $\omega^{\omega^{1/2}}$, that element is not algebraic over $K$.
\end{proof}
The field generated by a class is defined by finite expressions, so no set of
all generators is postulated. Source 06 adds that this is a direct application
of the lifting method, ``not a claim that the method itself is new''.

\begin{theorem}[Parameter-fixed copies \src{06}; reprint of \lbl{opa:as:thm:parameters}]\label{sse:sr:logic:parameters}
For every set $A\subseteq\No$ there are proper strongly $\R$-linear
exact-monomial field self-embeddings fixing $A\cup\R$ pointwise and preserving
and reflecting $\Oz$. They can be chosen with a discrete bounded image, or with
a continuous nondiscrete image, in the full ambient order topology.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Mechanism \src{06}]
Collect the normal-form exponents of all members of $A$, and then the
exponents of those exponents; this two-level footprint is a set
(\eqref{sse:eq:footprint}). Choose an order embedding of the exponent order
fixing that set and compressing, or inserting a gap, beyond it. The first lift
fixes all exponents occurring in $A$ and the second lift fixes $A$. A bounded
compression gives the discrete example, a cofinal gap insertion the continuous
one; the cited report proves the order constructions and the closure of the
images.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
In this report the same conclusions are \cref{sse:thm:stabilizer},
\cref{sse:thm:boundedcopy} and \cref{sse:cor:strongstabilizer} (which also fix
$\On$ in the cofinal case), with $\Oz$ from \cref{sse:prop:oz}; the collection
statement \lbl{opa:as:thm:parameters} adds that no field automorphism of $\No$
carries one image onto the other and an explicit $\Omega>1$ with
$\Omega^{-1}<|y|<\Omega$ on the nonzero discrete image. The theorem is about a
\emph{set} of parameters; it does not permit replacing $A$ by $\No$.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[Elementary is not definable \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:definability}
Source 06's Proposition~4.4 and Theorem~4.5 are \cref{sse:prop:elementary} and
\cref{sse:thm:definable}. In particular $J_{P_-}[\No]$ and $J_{P_+}[\No]$ are
elementary subfields in $L_{\mathrm{or}}=\{0,1,+,-,\cdot,<\}$; this asserts
nothing about the ambient topology, the named real section, birthdays,
set-indexed sums, or the expanded language with $\Oz$
(\cref{sse:sr:thm:ozelementary}). The exponent-doubling automorphism $S_2$ is
explicit in set theory, yet no formula of $L_{\mathrm{or}}$ with finitely many
parameters defines it; likewise the nontrivial exponential automorphisms
obtained from homogeneity have no parameter-definable graph in the exponential
field. O-minimality must not be transferred to enrichments: simplicity, an
ordinal predicate, $\Oz$, a normal-form operation or $\BMsr$ change the
definable sets substantially. A class-sized back-and-forth for set parameter
fields is needed for the abundance of automorphisms (\cref{sse:sr:rem:backforth}).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Computability and changes of presentation \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:computability}
Computable self-embeddings require a specified presentation. Finite sign
strings admit effective prefix and block substitution, and finitely supported
normal forms admit exact symbolic reindexing when the exponent map is
computable in the chosen notation; neither gives an algorithm whose finite
inputs range over all ordinals or all surreals. A computable map on a notation
system must respect the identification of codes for the same surreal and be
injective after the quotient; on canonical sign sequences no such ambiguity
arises, but the proper-class size remains. Computability questions are best
posed on a selected set of notations, a set-sized subfield, or a provably
closed symbolic fragment. Maps of raw game forms, of their value classes and
of sign sequences are different objects until a compatibility theorem
connects them, and changing the set universe adds new signs and supports
(compare the base's last paragraph of \cref{sse:sec:modeltheory}).
\end{remark}

\section{The omnific predicate (source 06, Section 5)}\label{sse:sr:sec:oz}

This section of source 06 is a credited synthesis of Part \lbl{opa:part:as} of
\fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms} \cite{sr-RepoOPA} and of
\fn{docs/surreal/omnific-diophantine-geometry}; every statement below is a
reprint under its collection label, with source 06's proof or proof sketch,
and none is new. Adding $\Oz$ reconstructs the coefficient field and forces
automorphisms to respect every Hahn sum, while proper embeddings can preserve
and reflect $\Oz$ and still move an ordinary real.

Throughout this section use the small-monomial convention
$t^\gamma=\omega^{-\gamma}$, $x=\sum_{\gamma\in S}r_\gamma t^\gamma$ with $S$ a
well-ordered set, $\val(x)=\min S$, and
\[
 \Pi=\{x:\supp(x)\subseteq\No^{<0}\},\qquad \Oz=\Z+\Pi,
\]
so $x\in\Oz$ iff its support is nonpositive and $\ctsr(x)=[t^0]x\in\Z$
(this is \eqref{sse:eq:oz}). $\Pi$ is the purely infinite ideal of $\Oz$; its
elements need not be positive.

\begin{remark}[Fractions \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:fractions}
$\operatorname{Frac}(\Oz)=\No$ is \lbl{odg:thm:fractions}, printed in this report
as \cref{sse:pf:thm:oz} (home 02). Source 06's proof: for
$x=\sum_{\gamma\in S}r_\gamma t^\gamma$ choose $\eta>0$ above the set $S$; then
$b=t^{-\eta}$ and $a=xt^{-\eta}$ lie in $\Pi$ and $x=a/b$. Consequently every
automorphism $\sigma$ of $\Oz$ extends uniquely to a field automorphism of
$\No$, by $\widetilde\sigma(a/b)=\sigma(a)/\sigma(b)$ (cross multiplication
preserves equality of fractions, and the extension of $\sigma^{-1}$ is the
inverse). The support bound matters: it does not apply to a Hahn field on a
fixed set-sized value group, where a set support can be cofinal.
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[Reconstruction \src{06}; after \lbl{odg:def:thm:ideal}, \lbl{odg:def:thm:multiplier}]\label[lemma]{sse:sr:lem:reconstruction}
For $z\in\Oz$,
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:oz:pi-test}
 z\in\Pi\iff\exists y\in\Oz\ \ z^2=2y^2 .
\end{equation}
The multiplier ring $\mathcal A=\{a\in\No:a\Pi\subseteq\Pi\}$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:oz:multiplier}
 \mathcal A=\R+\Pi,\qquad \mathcal A^\times=\R^\times .
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Forward: $y=z/\sqrt2\in\Pi$. Reverse: with integer constant coefficients $m,n$
of $z,y$, constant extraction is multiplicative on series with nonpositive
support, so $m^2=2n^2$ and $m=n=0$. For the multiplier ring, nonpositive
support suffices; conversely a nonzero coefficient of $a$ at $\delta>0$
produces, after multiplication by $t^{-\delta}\in\Pi$, a nonzero constant term.
A nonconstant element of $\mathcal A$ has negative valuation, so its inverse
has positive valuation and is not in $\mathcal A$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Omnific automorphisms fix the real coefficients \src{06}; reprint of \lbl{opa:cor:allautos}]\label[proposition]{sse:sr:oz:automorphisms-real}
Every field automorphism $F$ of $\No$ with $F(\Oz)=\Oz$ fixes $\R$ pointwise and
satisfies $\ctsr(Fx)=\ctsr(x)$ for all $x\in\No$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
By \eqref{sse:sr:oz:pi-test}--\eqref{sse:sr:oz:multiplier}, $F$ preserves
$\Pi$, $\mathcal A$ and $\R$ setwise; its restriction to $\R$ is an ordered
field automorphism, hence the identity. Order is preserved (positives are
nonzero squares), so integer inequalities show that $F$ preserves the
infinitesimals $\mathfrak m$. In $\No=\Pi\oplus\R\oplus\mathfrak m$, $F$
preserves each summand and fixes the middle one, which is the $\ctsr$
coordinate.
\end{proof}
The multiplier condition quantifies over \emph{all} purely infinite elements;
an embedding into a proper subfield need not transport it to all target
witnesses, which is the obstruction to applying this proof to proper maps.

\begin{lemma}[Scalar detection of summability \src{06}; reprint of \lbl{opa:as:thm:detect}]\label[lemma]{sse:sr:oz:detector}
Put $\langle x,y\rangle_0=\ctsr(xy)$ (nondegenerate: multiplying by
$t^{-\gamma}$ tests the coefficient at $\gamma$). A set-indexed family $(x_i)$ is
Hahn summable iff for every $y\in\No$ only finitely many
$\langle x_i,y\rangle_0$ are nonzero; it suffices to test $y$ with countable
support and coefficients in $\{0,1\}$. For a summable family,
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:oz:pair-sum}
 \Bigl\langle\sum_ix_i,y\Bigr\rangle_0=\sum_i\langle x_i,y\rangle_0
\end{equation}
with a finite sum on the right.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For well-ordered $S,T$, $S\cap(-T)$ is finite (an infinite subset would give
an increasing sequence in $S$ whose negatives decrease in $T$). Applied to
$S=\bigcup_i\supp x_i$, $T=\supp y$ this gives the forward implication and
\eqref{sse:sr:oz:pair-sum}. Conversely, monomial tests $y=t^{-\gamma}$ force
pointwise finiteness. If the union of supports were not well ordered, pass to
a decreasing sequence $\gamma_0>\gamma_1>\cdots$ in it and distinct indices
$i_n$ with $[t^{\gamma_n}]x_{i_n}\ne0$ and $[t^{\gamma_m}]x_{i_n}=0$ for
$m>n$. Choose $c_n\in\{0,1\}$ successively with
$\sum_{m\le n}c_m[t^{\gamma_m}]x_{i_n}\ne0$ (at most one choice is forbidden,
and later choices do not alter this row). Then $y=\sum_nc_nt^{-\gamma_n}$ is a
countably supported surreal pairing nontrivially with every $x_{i_n}$, a
contradiction.
\end{proof}
The lemma detects the sum as well as its existence, by nondegeneracy; it does
not claim first-order definability of indexed summation.

\begin{theorem}[Automatic strongness of the omnific stabilizer \src{06}; reprint of \lbl{opa:as:thm:main}]\label{sse:sr:oz:automatic-strong}
Every automorphism of $\Oz$ extends uniquely to an automorphism of $\No$ which
is strongly $\R$-linear with strongly $\R$-linear inverse.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Let $F$ be the extension (\cref{sse:sr:rem:fractions}) and $(x_i)$ summable.
Every target test is $y=F(z)$ by surjectivity, and
\cref{sse:sr:oz:automorphisms-real} gives
$\langle F(x_i),F(z)\rangle_0=\langle x_i,z\rangle_0$; so $(F(x_i))$ is
summable by \cref{sse:sr:oz:detector}. With $s=\sum_ix_i$ and $w=\sum_iF(x_i)$,
$\langle F(s),F(z)\rangle_0=\langle s,z\rangle_0=\sum_i\langle x_i,z\rangle_0
=\langle w,F(z)\rangle_0$ for all $z$, so $F(s)=w$. Apply the same to $F^{-1}$;
fixation of $\R$ gives linearity.
\end{proof}
The collection obtains it from the constant-term-covariant isomorphism theorem
\lbl{opa:as:thm:ctiso}. Surjectivity supplies every target detector before an
image sum is invoked. The theorem concerns the omnific stabilizer, not every
field automorphism of $\No$.

\begin{lemma}[The bottom gap \src{06}; reprint of \lbl{opa:as:lem:gap}]\label[lemma]{sse:sr:lem:gap}
Let $\Gamma,\Delta$ be nonzero divisible ordered abelian groups (sets, or the
surreal additive class with set supports), $K_\Gamma=\R((t^\Gamma))$,
$\Pi_\Gamma=\{x:\supp x\subseteq\Gamma^{<0}\}$ and
$\mathcal I_\Gamma=\Z+\Pi_\Gamma$. For an ordered additive embedding
$\tau:\Gamma\hookrightarrow\Delta$ put
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:oz:gap-definition}
 \gapsr_\tau=\{c\in\Delta:n|c|<h\text{ for every }n\ge1\text{ and }h\in\tau(\Gamma)^{>0}\}.
\end{equation}
Then $\gapsr_\tau$ is the largest convex subgroup of $\Delta$ meeting
$\tau(\Gamma)$ only in $0$; it is divisible; $\gapsr_\tau=0$ iff
$\tau(\Gamma)^{>0}$ is coinitial in $\Delta^{>0}$; and the sum
$\tau(\Gamma)+\gapsr_\tau$ is direct, with $\tau(\Gamma)$ the outer
lexicographic coordinate.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}[Proof \src{06}]
Downward closure in absolute value gives convexity; for $c,d$ in the gap,
comparing each with $h/(2n)$ gives $n|c+d|<h$, and division by positive
integers preserves the condition. A nonzero image element is not smaller in
absolute value than itself, so the gap meets the image only in $0$. If a
convex subgroup $D$ disjoint from the image away from $0$ contained $c$ with
$n|c|\ge h>0$ for an image element $h$, convexity would put $h/n\in D$; this
proves maximality and the block ordering (a difference of distinct outer
coordinates dominates every gap correction). If positive coinitiality fails,
a positive $c$ below every positive image element lies in the gap by
divisibility. No completeness of either group is used.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Bottom-gap classification \src{06}; reprint of \lbl{opa:as:thm:classification}]\label{sse:sr:oz:classification}
Strongly additive field embeddings $\phi:K_\Gamma\hookrightarrow K_\Delta$ with
$\phi(t^\gamma)=t^{\tau(\gamma)}$ and $\phi(\mathcal I_\Gamma)\subseteq\mathcal I_\Delta$
correspond exactly to ordered field embeddings
$\rho:\R\hookrightarrow\R((t^{\gapsr_\tau}))$, via
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:oz:block-map}
 H^{\rho}_{\tau}\Bigl(\sum_\gamma r_\gamma t^\gamma\Bigr)=\sum_\gamma\rho(r_\gamma)t^{\tau(\gamma)},
 \qquad \rho=\phi|_\R .
\end{equation}
Every such embedding satisfies $\val(\phi x)=\tau(\val x)$,
$\lcsr(\phi x)=\lcsr(x)$ for $x\ne0$, and $\ctsr(\phi x)=\ctsr(x)$, and preserves and
reflects both $\mathcal I$ and $\Pi$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof \src{06}]
An ordered embedding of $\R$ into a real Hahn field has $\phi(r)=r+{}$an
infinitesimal. For $\gamma>0$, $rt^{-\gamma}\in\Pi_\Gamma$, and the existential
test \eqref{sse:sr:oz:pi-test} (valid in these integer parts) puts
$\phi(r)t^{-\tau(\gamma)}$ in $\Pi_\Delta$; so every exponent $c$ of $\phi(r)$ is
nonnegative and below every positive element of $\tau(\Gamma)$, that is,
$c\in\gapsr_\tau$. No strongness is used for this; strongness then forces
\eqref{sse:sr:oz:block-map}. Conversely, for an ordered $\rho$ and a well-ordered
source support $S$, the blocks $\tau(\gamma)+\supp\rho(r_\gamma)$ lie in distinct
cosets of $\gapsr_\tau$ ordered as $S$, so their union is well ordered; for a
summable family only finitely many members contribute at each outer exponent.
This proves admissibility and strong additivity; multiplicativity follows from
Hahn convolution, $\rho(rs)=\rho(r)\rho(s)$ and additivity of $\tau$. The first
block starts at $\tau(\val x)$ with the original leading coefficient; negative
and positive outer blocks keep their sign; the zero block has constant
coefficient $r_0$ and equals $r_0$ when $r_0\in\Z$; distinct blocks cannot
cancel. This gives the remaining assertions.
\end{proof}
It is a classification within the strong exact-monomial category; character
factors, unit corrections and nonstrong embeddings are outside it, and $\ctsr$
is not multiplicative on the whole Hahn field.

\begin{corollary}[Surjectivity and coefficient rigidity \src{06}; reprint of \lbl{opa:as:cor:onto}, \lbl{opa:as:cor:rigidity}]\label[corollary]{sse:sr:oz:gap-rigidity}
$H^{\rho}_{\tau}$ is onto exactly when $\tau$ is onto, and then
$\gapsr_\tau=0$ and $\rho=\id_\R$. For fixed $\tau$, every embedding of
\cref{sse:sr:oz:classification} fixes $\R$ pointwise exactly when
$\tau(\Gamma)^{>0}$ is coinitial in $\Delta^{>0}$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Surjectivity forces every target value into $\tau(\Gamma)$; an onto $\tau$ has
zero gap, an ordered embedding $\R\to\R$ is the identity, and reindexing by
$\tau^{-1}$ gives the inverse. Zero gap forces coefficient rigidity without
surjectivity; for a nonzero gap the Taylor sections of
\cref{sse:sr:prop:taylorexplicit} are nontrivial.
\end{proof}
Positive coinitiality is the condition here; cofinality of the value image,
used in the continuity criterion, is a different requirement.

\begin{proposition}[Taylor sections and a proper embedding moving a real \src{06}; reprint of \lbl{opa:as:lem:taylorsection}, \lbl{opa:as:thm:taylorcriterion}, \lbl{opa:as:thm:explicit}, \lbl{opa:as:prop:overlap}, \lbl{opa:as:cor:image}]\label[proposition]{sse:sr:prop:taylorexplicit}
Let $b\in\R$ be transcendental and $d:\R\to\R$ a $\Q$-derivation with
$d(b)=1$ (choice on the set $\R$ only).
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item For $\delta>0$,
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:oz:taylor-section}
 \rho_{d,\delta}(r)=\sum_{n\ge0}\frac{d^n(r)}{n!}t^{n\delta}
\end{equation}
is an ordered field embedding with constant term $r$ and
$\rho_{d,\delta}(b)=b+t^\delta$; for $\delta\in\gapsr_\tau$ it is an
admissible coefficient section in \cref{sse:sr:oz:classification}.
\item Let $\mu(a)=a+\sqrt{a^2+1}$, an order isomorphism $\No\to\No^{>0}$ with
$\mu^{-1}(y)=(y-y^{-1})/2$, and let $\tau=A_\mu$, i.e.
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:oz:inner-lift}
 \tau\Bigl(\sum_as_a\omega^a\Bigr)=\sum_as_a\omega^{\mu(a)} .
\end{equation}
Then $\tau$ is an ordered additive embedding with image $\mathbb J$, and
$\gapsr_\tau$ is exactly the class of finite surreals; in particular
$1\in\gapsr_\tau$.
\item The map $\Theta_d=H^{\rho_{d,1}}_{\tau}$,
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:oz:explicit-map}
 \Theta_d\Bigl(\sum_\gamma r_\gamma t^\gamma\Bigr)=\sum_\gamma\sum_{n\ge0}\frac{d^n(r_\gamma)}{n!}t^{\tau(\gamma)+n},
\end{equation}
is a proper strong ordered-field self-embedding with $\Theta_d^{-1}(\Oz)=\Oz$,
$\ctsr\circ\Theta_d=\ctsr$, $\Theta_d(b)=b+\omega^{-1}$, $\Theta_d(\omega)=\omega^\omega$,
fixing every real algebraic number.
\item The ordinary reals in the image are
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:oz:real-intersection}
 \Theta_d[\No]\cap\R=\ker d,
\end{equation}
a proper real closed subfield of $\R$; the image still contains the copy
$\rho_{d,1}(\R)$ of $\R$, different from the embedded $\R$.
\item $\Theta_d(\omega^1)=\omega^\omega$ while $\omega^{\Theta_d(1)}=\omega$: carrying
monomials to monomials is not commuting with $\Omega$.
\item $\ctsr(t\,\Theta_d(x))=0$ for every $x\in\No$, although $t\ne0$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof \src{06}]
(a) The iterated Leibniz rule gives multiplicativity coefficientwise; the
constant term $r$ gives injectivity and order. (b) $\mu$ is onto $\No^{>0}$,
so $\tau$ has image the purely infinite group and $\tau(1)=\omega$ ($\tau$ is
not a field map). Every positive purely infinite element dominates every finite
one; an infinite $c$ with leading exponent $a>0$ dominates the positive image
element $\omega^{a/2}$, so it is not in the gap. (c) is
\cref{sse:sr:oz:classification}; properness holds since $1\notin\tau[\No]$, so
$t\notin\Theta_d[\No]$; $\Theta_d(\omega)=t^{\tau(-1)}=\omega^{\omega}$.
(d) If $\Theta_d(x)$ is real, every nonzero outer block vanishes, so $x=r$ is
real, and $\rho_{d,1}(r)$ is constant iff $d(r)=0$. $\ker d$ is real closed:
an element of $\R$ algebraic over it is killed by $d$ (differentiate its
separable minimal polynomial). (e) is immediate. (f) Every support exponent of
$\Theta_d(x)$ is $\tau(\gamma)+n$ with $n\ge0$, never $-1$, since a nonzero purely
infinite value is not an integer.
\end{proof}
This is the collection's construction \lbl{opa:as:thm:explicit} with its
choice $\mu_{13}$; (d) is \lbl{opa:as:prop:overlap} and (d), (f) are
\lbl{opa:as:cor:image}; the boundary (e) is recorded after
\lbl{opa:as:cor:homogeneity}; the exact admissibility criterion for Taylor
sections is \lbl{opa:as:thm:taylorcriterion}. Item (f) shows that tests from
the image alone cannot reproduce the nondegeneracy argument used for
automorphisms; and $\ctsr\circ\Theta_d=\ctsr$ does not imply
$\Theta_d\circ\ctsr=\ctsr$ (at $b$ it would assert $b+t=b$).

\begin{theorem}[Predicate preservation is not elementarity \src{06}; reprint of \lbl{opa:as:thm:elementary}]\label{sse:sr:thm:ozelementary}
The embedding $\Theta_d$ preserves and reflects $\Oz$ but is not elementary as an
embedding of $(\No,\Oz)$, and its restriction to $\Oz$ is not an elementary
ring embedding.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof \src{06}]
$\R$ is first-order definable in $(\No,\Oz)$: \eqref{sse:sr:oz:pi-test} defines
$\Pi$, the multiplier condition defines $\mathcal A$, and $\{0\}$ with the units
of $\mathcal A$ is $\R$. An elementary embedding of the pair preserves this
definition, but $\Theta_d(b)=b+t\notin\R$; the new target witness is
$t^{-1}\in\Pi$ with $(b+t)t^{-1}=bt^{-1}+1\notin\Pi$. For the ring, the fraction
field is uniformly interpretable by pairs $(a,b)$, $b\ne0$, modulo $ad=bc$, with
$\Oz$ as the fractions $(z,1)$; an elementary ring embedding would induce an
elementary embedding of the interpreted pair. The real $b$ is represented
internally by $(b\omega)/\omega$.
\end{proof}
These distinctions explain why rigidity of omnific automorphisms coexists with
abundant proper self-embeddings.

\section{Exponential, differential, and hyperserial constraints (source 06, Section 6)}\label{sse:sr:sec:exp}

An exponential-field embedding satisfies $j(\exp x)=\exp(jx)$, an omega-field
embedding $j(\omega^x)=\omega^{jx}$. They differ already at $x=1$
($\exp 1=e\in\R$, $\omega^1=\omega$), so an omega-field embedding fixes $\omega$
while an exponential one need not. Preserving the real powers
$x^r=\exp(r\log x)$ ($x>0$, $r\in\R$) is a third requirement; it follows from
preserving $\exp$ and fixing $\R$, but does not recover $\exp$.

\begin{remark}[Exponential homogeneity and its interpretation \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:epsstages}
$(\No,\exp)$ is an elementary extension of $\R_{\exp}$ \cite{vdDE}. In the
class framework of Kaplan, Krapp and Serra, every elementary embedding of a
set-sized elementary exponential subfield into $\No$ extends to an
exponential automorphism of $\No$ \cite[Remark~2.10 and Section~5]{KKS};
the hypothesis is elementarity in the exponential language, which an
arbitrary ordered-field embedding of such a subfield need not satisfy. Source
06 proposes a coherent interpretation of the class statements without a truth
predicate for arbitrary class structures: the set-sized birthday stages
$\No_{<\lambda}$ at epsilon numbers $\lambda$ are models of the real exponential
theory, model completeness makes their inclusions elementary, a set of
parameters lies in one stage and its definable closure can be computed there
\cite{vdDE,sr-ADH}.
\end{remark}

\begin{wnote}
This route bears on the conditional hypotheses (GBC+ETR or a class
satisfaction relation) of the bounded and cofinal elementary exponential
copies of Part III and Part IV (\cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary},
\cref{sse:at:thm:cofinal-elementary}): it is a proposal recorded by source 06,
not a proof that those hypotheses can be dropped. Unchecked here.
\end{wnote}

\begin{proposition}[Moving the distinguished infinite argument \src{06}; imported literature, in the class framework of \cite{KKS}]\label[proposition]{sse:sr:exp:moveomega}
For every $a>\R$ there is an exponential-field automorphism $j$ of $\No$ fixing
$\R$ pointwise with $j(\omega)=a$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
By o-minimality, every subset of the line definable over the elementary
submodel $\R$ is a finite union of points and intervals with endpoints in
$\R\cup\{\pm\infty\}$, so any two elements above every real have the same
complete type over $\R$. Hence the map fixing $\R$ and sending $\omega$ to $a$
is partial elementary. Definable Skolem functions of an o-minimal expansion of
an ordered field extend it to an elementary isomorphism between the definable
closures of $\R\cup\{\omega\}$ and $\R\cup\{a\}$, which are sets. Apply the
homogeneity theorem of \cref{sse:sr:rem:epsstages}.
\end{proof}
Taking $a=\omega^2$ gives an exponential automorphism not preserving the
omega-map. The construction provides no preservation of Conway summation or
simplicity; those are additional requirements, not consequences of being
elementary in a finitary language. This is a consequence of
\cite[Remark~2.10]{KKS}, not stated as such in the collection.

\begin{remark}[Exact values detect exponential maps \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:exprigid}
In contrast, the action on exact values detects every exponential
self-embedding: source 06's Lemma~6.2, Lemma~6.3 and Theorem~6.4 are
\cref{sse:lem:amplify}, \eqref{sse:eq:expkernel} and \cref{sse:thm:exprigidity}
(EAR \lbl{lem:amplify}, \lbl{cor:embedding}), without surjectivity, a prior
fixing of $\R$, or strong additivity. Its proof of the converse half of
\eqref{sse:eq:expkernel} is: $\val(\exp x)=0$ makes $\exp x$ a positive unit, so
$n^{-1}<\exp x<n$ for an integer $n>1$, and the increasing logarithm gives
$-\log n<x<\log n$. Source 06 records, correctly, that Kaplan--Krapp--Serra's
April 2026 version proves rigidity for strongly $\R$-linear exponential
1-automorphisms and asks whether strong linearity can be removed
\cite[Proposition~5.2 and Question~5.4]{KKS}; a 1-automorphism fixes every
exact value, so the repository argument (EAR \lbl{cor:question54}) gives the
negative answer and a stronger self-embedding statement, and that question
should not be listed as open relative to ProveIt. Its Corollary~6.5
(faithfulness of the value action for exponential \emph{automorphisms}) is
weaker than \cref{sse:thm:expunique}; it is \lbl{saut:thm:expfaithful} of
\fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms} and EAR \lbl{cor:faithful},
neither cited by source 06. Uniqueness of lifts does not give existence;
existence is examined in \cite{RepoExp}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Omega-map rigidity \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:omegarigid}
Source 06's Proposition~6.6 is \cref{sse:thm:omegarigidity}; it extends the
automorphism statement \cite[Proposition~5.5]{KKS} without further argument.
Omega-compatibility forces $\tau_j(x)=j(x)$, and for a strong Hahn lift $H_\tau$
it is the self-consistency equation $\tau=H_\tau$
(\eqref{sse:eq:omegafunctional}). Any omega-field embedding preserves the
equation $x=\omega^x$, hence maps generalized epsilon numbers to generalized
epsilon numbers; this is a necessary condition, not an extension theorem.
Neither this calculation nor ordered-field homogeneity answers
\cite[Questions~5.6--5.7]{KKS}.
\end{remark}

Fix the Berarducci--Mantova derivation $\BMsr$, with $\ker\BMsr=\R$,
$\BMsr\omega=1$, $\BMsr\exp x=(\exp x)\BMsr x$; it is strongly additive and
makes $\No$ a Liouville closed $H$-field \cite{BM}. A differential-field
embedding satisfies $\BMsr j=j\BMsr$.

\begin{proposition}[The translation parameter \src{06}]\label[proposition]{sse:sr:exp:translationparameter}
Every differential-field self-embedding $j$ of $(\No,\BMsr)$ fixes $\R$
pointwise and satisfies
\[
 j(\omega)=\omega+c_j\qquad\text{for a unique }c_j\in\R .
\]
For compositions, $c_{j\circ k}=c_j+c_k$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The kernel equation sends each real into $\R$; the restriction to $\R$ is an
ordered field embedding $\R\to\R$, hence the identity by rational cuts. Next
$\BMsr(j\omega-\omega)=j(1)-1=0$, so the difference is real. Finally apply $j$ to
$k\omega=\omega+c_k$ and use $j(c_k)=c_k$.
\end{proof}
This strengthens \cref{sse:prop:differential} (which has the first two
assertions for any derivation with constant field $\R$ and $\partial\omega=1$)
by the additivity of $c$; it is the home of the overlap with sources 02, 04, 05
and 07, and was not found as a statement in the collection.

\begin{proposition}[The exponential defect of a differential embedding \src{06}]\label[proposition]{sse:sr:exp:defect}
For a differential-field self-embedding $j$ of $(\No,\BMsr)$ put
\[
 \alpha_j(x)=\log\bigl(j(\exp x)\bigr)-j(x).
\]
Then $\alpha_j:\No\to\R$ is additive and vanishes on every finite surreal, and
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:exp:defectidentity}
 j(\exp x)=\exp\bigl(jx+\alpha_j(x)\bigr).
\end{equation}
$j$ preserves $\exp$ iff $\alpha_j=0$; $j$ preserves every real power of every
positive surreal iff $\alpha_j$ is $\R$-linear. For two differential
self-embeddings,
\[
 \alpha_{j\circ k}=\alpha_j\circ k+\alpha_k .
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
$j(\exp x)>0$ since $j$ is increasing, so $\alpha_j$ is defined. Using the
logarithmic derivative and $\BMsr j=j\BMsr$,
\[
 \BMsr\log\bigl(j(\exp x)\bigr)=\frac{j(\BMsr\exp x)}{j(\exp x)}
 =j(\BMsr x)=\BMsr j(x),
\]
so $\alpha_j(x)\in\ker\BMsr=\R$. Additivity follows from $\exp(x+y)=\exp x\exp y$
and the logarithm of a product. For finite $x$ with standard part $r$, both
$j(x)$ and $\log(j(\exp x))$ have standard part $r$, because $j$ fixes $\R$
(\cref{sse:sr:exp:translationparameter}) and preserves infinitesimals; their
difference is real and infinitesimal, hence $0$.
Identity \eqref{sse:sr:exp:defectidentity} is the definition and characterizes
exponential preservation. For $r\in\R$,
\[
 j\bigl((\exp x)^r\bigr)=\exp\bigl(rj(x)+\alpha_j(rx)\bigr),\qquad
 j(\exp x)^r=\exp\bigl(rj(x)+r\alpha_j(x)\bigr),
\]
and every positive surreal is an exponential, so preservation of all real
powers is $\R$-linearity of $\alpha_j$. Applying
\eqref{sse:sr:exp:defectidentity} twice, with $j$ fixing reals and $\alpha_j$
vanishing on them, gives the composition law.
\end{proof}
The proposition constrains embeddings that exist. It does not assert that
every additive functional vanishing on finite elements is realized by a
self-embedding of $\No$ (\cref{sse:sr:q:defects}); \cref{sse:sr:prop:kernelnonrigid}
realizes such data on a particular subfield.

The next two statements are consequences of imported literature, recorded by
source 06 and not proved in this report beyond the cited theorems. Let
$\mathbb T$ be the field of logarithmic-exponential transseries, identified
with its canonical image in $\No$ with variable $\omega$; it is an elementary
differential subfield of $(\No,\BMsr)$ \cite{sr-ADH}. The homogeneity theorem of
\cite{an-VdDEhrlichH} extends an isomorphism between set-sized submodels of the
complete theory of $\omega$-free, newtonian, Liouville closed $H$-fields with
small derivation and constant field $\R$ to an automorphism of the surreal
differential field (it refers to models of that theory, not to all
differential subfields).

\begin{proposition}[Every translation parameter occurs \src{06}; imported literature: \cite{sr-ADH,an-VdDEhrlichH}, in the class framework of \cite{an-VdDEhrlichH}]\label[proposition]{sse:sr:prop:translationrealized}
For every $c\in\R$ there is a differential-field automorphism $j$ of
$(\No,\BMsr)$ with $c_j=c$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Transseries substitution $f\mapsto f\circ(\omega+c)$ is an automorphism of
$\mathbb T$ (inverse: substitution by $\omega-c$) commuting with
differentiation by the chain rule. The homogeneity theorem extends it to a
differential automorphism of $\No$.
\end{proof}
Choosing an extension for each $c$ does not by itself give a coherent action
of $(\R,+)$ on $\No$; that would require an additional construction.

\begin{proposition}[A zero translation parameter does not force rigidity \src{06}; imported literature: {\cite[Fact~5.7]{sr-KaplanPow}} with \cite{an-VdDEhrlichH}]\label[proposition]{sse:sr:prop:kernelnonrigid}
There is a differential-field automorphism $j$ of $(\No,\BMsr)$ with
$j(\omega)=\omega$ (so $c_j=0$) and $j(\exp\omega)=e\exp\omega$; in particular
$j$ does not preserve $\exp$ and $\alpha_j(\omega)=1$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
By \cite[Fact~5.7]{sr-KaplanPow}, for every additive $\alpha:\mathbb T\to\R$
vanishing on finite transseries there is a strongly $\R$-linear differential
automorphism $s_\alpha$ of $\mathbb T$ with $s_\alpha(\omega)=\omega$ and
$s_\alpha(\exp f)=\exp(s_\alpha(f)+\alpha(f))$. Choose an $\R$-linear such
$\alpha$ with $\alpha(\omega)=1$ (extend a vector-space basis; $\omega$ is not
finite). Then $s_\alpha(\exp\omega)=e\exp\omega$. Differential homogeneity
extends $s_\alpha$ to $\No$; the failure of exponential compatibility is
witnessed inside $\mathbb T$.
\end{proof}
The extension theorem does not promise global preservation of strong sums or
real powers.

\begin{remark}[Exponential and differential constraints are distinct \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:expdiff}
The exponential automorphism of \cref{sse:sr:exp:moveomega} with $j\omega=\omega^2$
cannot be differential, since $\BMsr(\omega^2)=2\omega\ne1$. If both structures
are imposed, \cref{sse:sr:exp:translationparameter} still applies, as a
necessary condition. A field automorphism $j$ transports $\partial$ to
$j\partial j^{-1}$ and is then an isomorphism between two differential
structures on one field, which is weaker than being an automorphism of one
specified differential structure; the uniqueness-up-to-isomorphism theorem of
\cite{an-VdDEhrlichH} must be read with this distinction.
\end{remark}

Let $\OmSeriessr=\R\langle\!\langle\omega\rangle\!\rangle$ be the field of
omega-series: the smallest subfield of $\No$ containing $\R$ and $\omega$ and
closed under $\exp$, $\log$ and all existing set-indexed Conway sums; it is a
proper class and a proper subfield of $\No$. Berarducci and Mantova construct a
composition $\OmSeriessr\times\No^{>\R}\to\No$, $(f,g)\mapsto f\circ g$, whose
right substitutions are strongly $\R$-linear exponential-field embeddings
\cite[Theorem~6.3]{sr-BM19}; it restricts to omega-series and, on $\mathbb T$, to
transseries composition.

\begin{proposition}[Restriction to omega-series \src{06}; uses {\cite[Theorem~6.3]{sr-BM19}}]\label[proposition]{sse:sr:prop:omegarestriction}
If $j:\No\to\No$ fixes $\R$, preserves $\exp$, and commutes with all summable
families, then
\begin{equation}\label{sse:sr:exp:omegarestriction}
 j(f)=f\circ j(\omega)\qquad(f\in\OmSeriessr).
\end{equation}
If $j$ also commutes with $\BMsr$, its restriction to $\OmSeriessr$ is
substitution by $\omega+c_j$; in particular, under these combined hypotheses,
fixing $\omega$ forces pointwise fixation of $\OmSeriessr$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
$j(\omega)>\R$ because $j$ fixes $\R$ and is increasing. The maps $j$ and
$f\mapsto f\circ j(\omega)$ agree on $\R$ and on $\omega$, and both preserve the
field operations, $\exp$, $\log$ (inverse of $\exp$ on positives) and
summation. The class where they agree is therefore a subfield containing
$\R\cup\{\omega\}$ and closed under these operations, so it contains
$\OmSeriessr$; equivalently, use uniqueness of the omega-series substitution.
The differential case is \cref{sse:sr:exp:translationparameter}.
\end{proof}
It does not give fixation of $\No$, because $\OmSeriessr\ne\No$.
\begin{wnote}
Source 07's \cref{sse:an:ana:omega-series-classification} classifies strong
exponential self-embeddings \emph{of} $\OmSeriessr$ (as $C_g$, all
