automorphisms; the differential ones are $C_{\omega+c}$). The proposition here
concerns restrictions of global self-embeddings of $\No$; the two proofs use
the same generation argument.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[Substitution and the global composition obstruction \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:composition}
For $g\in\OmSeriessr^{>\R}$ the chain rule is
$\BMsr(f\circ g)=((\BMsr f)\circ g)\,\BMsr g$, so substitution commutes with
$\BMsr$ when $\BMsr g=1$; the translates $\omega+c$ are the immediate examples
(compare $C_g$ in \cref{sse:sec:differential}). On all of $\No$,
\cite[Theorem~8.4]{sr-BM19} excludes a global composition compatible with
$\BMsr$ in the following sense: derivative-zero elements give constant
functions, positive derivatives give increasing functions, and the chain rule
holds. The reason is a proper class of log-atomic solutions of a growth
differential equation, which such a composition would convert injectively into
elements of derivative $1$, whereas these all lie in the set $\omega+\R$. The
theorem must not be expanded into a prohibition of every binary operation
called composition, or of every individual embedding; its force lies in the
simultaneous functional requirements.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Hyperserial fields and remaining targets \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:hyperserial}
Write $\No^{\mathrm{hyp}}_\lambda$ for Bagayoko's field of hyperserial strength
bounded by an infinite additively indecomposable ordinal $\lambda$ (a
proper-class restriction on hyperserial representations, not a birthday stage).
Its embeddings preserve real coefficients, sums, products, monomials and the
hyperlogarithms and hyperexponentials of that strength; if $a$ is
$\lambda$-atomic (each $L_\gamma(a)$, $\gamma<\lambda$, a single monomial) there is a
unique such embedding $\No^{\mathrm{hyp}}_\lambda\to\No$ sending $\omega$ to $a$
and preserving the indices distinguishing nested expansions, and right
substitutions by $\log\omega$ and $\exp\omega$ are inverse automorphisms of
$\No^{\mathrm{hyp}}_\omega$ \cite[Theorem~2, Section~7.5, Corollary~3]{sr-Bag24}.
These are constructive results on an intermediate proper-class field, not a
global right substitution on $\No$. Ehrlich--Kaplan show that the canonical
transseries image, the exponential-logarithmic series field and the
omega-series field are initial exponential subfields \cite{EhrlichKaplan};
initiality of an image subtree must not be strengthened to simplicity
preservation by every embedding with that image. Source 06's list of targets:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
\item characterize the value-group actions admitting global exponential lifts
(lifts are unique by \cref{sse:thm:expunique} and source 06's Corollary~6.5;
existence is the substantial part);
\item classify omega-field self-embeddings, distinguishing automorphisms,
strong Hahn lifts, and maps preserving generalized epsilon parameters;
\item determine which translations of omega-series extend to embeddings of
$\No$ preserving both $\exp$ and $\BMsr$, with which further summability;
differential homogeneity alone does not answer this;
\item describe the kernel $c_j=0$ for differential automorphisms and its
intersections with exponential, monomial and strong-summation constraints
(\cref{sse:sr:prop:kernelnonrigid} shows the unrestricted kernel is
nontrivial);
\item extend the constructive hyperserial embedding theorems to more general
arguments, treating nested indices and compatibility between strength bounds.
\end{enumerate}
The canonical embedding $\mathbb T\to\No$ is elementary separately as an
ordered exponential field and as an ordered differential field \cite{sr-ADH},
and Kaplan's September 2026 version establishes elementarity after adjoining
the uniform real-power map and $e$ \cite[Section~6.2]{sr-KaplanPow}. These do not
give elementarity in the full differential-exponential language; that question
and extension theorems for the combined structure are separate from the
homogeneity theorems for the reducts.
\end{remark}

\section{Foundations, localization, and large cardinals (source 06, Section 7)}\label{sse:sr:sec:found}

\begin{remark}[Frameworks and recursions \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:frameworks}
Each surreal is a set and each stage $\No_{<\alpha}$ is a set; $\No$ is a proper
class (Replacement on birthdays). An injective class map cannot have a
set-sized image, so no self-embedding lands in one $\No_{<\alpha}$, while a
numerically bounded interval can contain a proper class. Source 06 lists four
precise formulations, complementing the three registers of
\cref{sse:sec:foundations}: (1) ZFC with definable-class notation, general
assertions being metatheoretic schemes; (2) GBC, quantifying over individual
class maps, with Global Choice supplying enumerations (some references call
it NBG; the choice convention is stated explicitly); (3) a set-stage or
universe-relative formulation, where the surrounding universe remains part of
the meaning of ``all subsets'' and ``all summable families''; (4) an added
recursion principle such as ETR, or Kelley--Morse, when a construction needs
a uniform truth predicate or a genuine class-valued recursion. In none of these
are proper classes members of classes, so ``the class of all class
self-embeddings'' is not an object; a family is coded by one relation
$F(i,x,y)$. Class quantifiers are model-relative: in a two-sorted model
$(M,\mathcal X)$ they range over $\mathcal X$, which need not contain every
externally available subclass \cite{sr-RepoUniv}. An $\On$-long recursion that
produces a set from a set-sized history is ordinary transfinite recursion, not
an application of ETR (canonical sign prefixes, the dependency closure of one
arithmetic computation, and reindexing a set support are of this form); a
uniform truth recursion for an arbitrary class structure consults a proper
class of earlier instances, and GBC does not in general prove that such
recursions have solutions, even of height $\omega$ \cite{at-ClassForcing,HamkinsETR}.
\end{remark}

The base's \cref{sse:sec:formal} gives the following argument as prose;
source 06 states and proves it, and this report prints it here as the home
of the overlap (source 07's \cref{sse:an:form:club} points here).

\begin{proposition}[Localization to birthday stages \src{06}; in GB with the Replacement and comprehension instances for $j$]\label[proposition]{sse:sr:found:prop:localization}
Let $j:\No\to\No$ be a class function. There is a closed unbounded class
$\mathcal C$ of nonzero limit ordinals such that
\[
 j[\No_{<\lambda}]\subseteq\No_{<\lambda}\qquad(\lambda\in\mathcal C),
\]
and $\mathcal C$ can be chosen to consist of epsilon numbers. Restrictions
inherit injectivity and all preserved finitary operations and relations. A
fixed witness to nonidentity or to global nonsurjectivity persists at every
sufficiently large selected stage.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Class Replacement makes
$F(\alpha)=\sup\{\bday(j(x))+1:x\in\No_{<\alpha}\}$ an ordinal for every
$\alpha$; $F$ is nondecreasing and $x\in\No_{<\alpha}$ implies
$\bday(j(x))<F(\alpha)$. Let
\[
 \mathcal C_F=\{\lambda:\lambda\text{ a nonzero limit ordinal and }F(\alpha)<\lambda\text{ for all }\alpha<\lambda\}.
\]
Unbounded: above $\gamma$ put $\delta_0=\gamma+1$,
$\delta_{n+1}=\max\{\delta_n,F(\delta_n)\}+1$, $\lambda=\sup_n\delta_n$ (a
set-length recursion); for $\alpha<\lambda$ choose $\alpha<\delta_n$, so
$F(\alpha)\le F(\delta_n)<\delta_{n+1}<\lambda$. Closed: if $\lambda$ is a
nonzero limit of members of $\mathcal C_F$ and $\alpha<\lambda$, choose
$\eta\in\mathcal C_F$ with $\alpha<\eta<\lambda$; then $F(\alpha)<\eta<\lambda$.
For $x\in\No_{<\lambda}$ choose $\bday(x)<\alpha<\lambda$; then
$\bday(j(x))<F(\alpha)<\lambda$, and Replacement gives the set graph of the
restriction.

The epsilon numbers ($\omega^\lambda=\lambda$, ordinal exponentiation) form a
closed unbounded class; for a direct proof of simultaneous unboundedness take
$\delta_0$ an epsilon number above $\gamma$ and $\delta_{n+1}$ the least epsilon
number above $\delta_n$ and $F(\delta_n)$; continuity of ordinal
exponentiation makes the supremum an epsilon number, and the same estimate puts
it in $\mathcal C_F$. Closedness follows from the two closedness assertions.
Injectivity and finitary identities restrict immediately. If $j(a)\ne a$,
every selected $\lambda>\bday(a)$ contains a moved point; if $b\notin j[\No]$,
every selected $\lambda>\bday(b)$ contains a point omitted by the restriction.
\end{proof}
It localizes a map that already exists; it does not construct a coherent global
map from unrelated local witnesses.

\begin{corollary}[Automorphisms and exact local ranges \src{06}]\label[corollary]{sse:sr:found:cor:ranges}
If $j$ is bijective, there is a closed unbounded class of epsilon stages on
which $j$ restricts to a bijection. If $j$ is merely injective, one can instead
arrange $j[\No_{<\lambda}]=j[\No]\cap\No_{<\lambda}$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Simultaneous closure under finitely many maps follows by taking the maximum of
their bounds $F$. In the bijective case use $j$ and $j^{-1}$. In the injective
case let $R(y)$ be the unique preimage of $y$ if $y\in j[\No]$ and $0$
otherwise; closure under $j$ and $R$ gives the equality.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Families and cardinal stages \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:cardinalstages}
A set-indexed family coded by one class relation is handled by taking the
supremum over the product of its index set and $\No_{<\alpha}$; no class of maps
is formed. There are also arbitrarily large closed cardinal stages of any
prescribed infinite regular cofinality $\mu$: build an increasing continuous
$\mu$-sequence of infinite cardinals, each next one above the current cardinal
and its $F$-value, and take the supremum $\kappa$; regularity of $\mu$ gives
$\operatorname{cf}(\kappa)=\mu$. The resulting cardinal need not be regular or
inaccessible; localization neither assumes nor produces inaccessibles.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[What a bounded stage preserves \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:stages}
By the classical stage theorem, $\No_{<\lambda}$ is a subfield precisely when
$\lambda$ is an epsilon number, and then it is real closed and closed under
$\exp$ and $\log$ \cite{vdDE}. So a class field embedding restricts, on the
epsilon stages of \cref{sse:sr:found:prop:localization}, to set-sized field
self-embeddings, elementary in the ordered-field language by quantifier
elimination. Closure under $j$ alone does not make a stage elementary in an
expanded language, and $\forall\lambda\,\exists j_\lambda$ does not give one
global map (the stage maps may disagree on overlaps). For an uncountable
cardinal $\kappa$, the order of $\No_{<\kappa}$ fills cuts whose two sides have
total size less than $\operatorname{cf}(\kappa)$ (the birthdays are bounded below
$\kappa$, so a simplest separator stays inside), while a cofinal set of
ordinals below $\kappa$ gives an omitted end cut \cite{an-VdDEhrlichH}: a
bounded-stage argument must keep its cardinal bound.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Global extension needs an extension proof \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:backforth}
For the ordered-field reduct, source 06 gives the back-and-forth that extends
an ordered isomorphism between set-sized real closed subfields of $\No$ to an
automorphism, using Global Choice and set-sized stages (a transcendental $a$ is
sent to a simplest separator $b$ of the transferred cut; polynomial signs over
a real closed field are determined by root positions; unions of chains of real
closed fields are real closed). This is the ordered-field homogeneity theorem
printed as \cref{sse:an:fnd:thm:homogeneity} (home 07), which is
\lbl{saut:thm:orderedhomogeneity} of
\fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms}; source 06 does not cite the
latter. For richer structures a separate extension theorem is required: the
homogeneous universal $H$-field theorem of \cite{an-VdDEhrlichH} applies to its
specified theory and common constant field $\R$, and the saturation of the
underlying order cannot alone give exponential or differential homogeneity.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Elementarity is not set-theoretic elementarity \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:kunen}
For a fixed formula $\phi$, preservation of $\phi^{\No}(\bar a)\leftrightarrow
\phi^{\No}(j\bar a)$ is meaningful formula by formula; a uniform truth relation
is additional information, and ``one $j$ satisfies every instance'' must not be
replaced by ``for every instance some $j$ exists''. As in
\cref{sse:sec:foundations}, an elementary embedding of the ordered field $\No$
is not an elementary embedding of $(V,\in)$, and a least moved birthday has no
automatic large-cardinal meaning. Transporting $\in$ from $V$ to $\No$ along a
class bijection gives a coding-dependent structure, for which elementarity is
membership-elementarity under conjugation and is subject to Kunen's
inconsistency under the usual choice assumptions \cite{pf-HKP}; literal
membership between sign-code sets is representation-dependent and is not that
transported copy.
\end{remark}

Ordinary self-embeddings need no large cardinals, but some infinitary
preservation profiles have exactly that strength \cite{sr-RepoLCE}.

\begin{theorem}[Measurable first failure \src{06}; reprint of \lbl{lce:mf:thm:spectrum}]\label{sse:sr:found:prop:firstfailure}
Let $\Psi:\No\to\No$ be an $\R$-linear class map preserving every countable
Hahn-summable family and its sum. If $\Psi$ is not strong for all set-indexed
Hahn sums, the least cardinality of a family on which sum preservation fails is
an uncountable measurable cardinal.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Mechanism \src{06}]
Nonsummability has a countable witness (infinitely many terms at one exponent,
or an increasing sequence of exponents), so countable strength implies that
every summable family has a summable image. Choose a first failing cardinal
$\kappa$, a witnessing family and a target coefficient with nonzero sum defect;
the combined input support is a reverse well-ordered set $X$, and restriction
plus coefficient extraction give a functional $\psi:\R^X\to\R$ preserving
point-finite sums of fewer than $\kappa$ vectors, each point-finite family
having only finitely many nonzero scalar images. Let $\mathcal I_\psi$ consist of
the subsets of $X$ on which every supported vector is annihilated by $\psi$;
it is closed under unions of fewer than $\kappa$ sets, and
$\mathcal P(X)/\mathcal I_\psi$ is finite (countably many disjoint nonzero
elements would give a point-finite family with infinitely many nonzero scalar
images). Its atoms give $\kappa$-complete ultrafilters
$\mathcal U_1,\dots,\mathcal U_m$; a real function is constant on a set in any
countably complete ultrafilter (successive bisection), so
$\psi=\sum_{r=1}^mc_r\operatorname{ev}_{\mathcal U_r}$. If all $\mathcal U_r$ were
$\kappa^+$-complete, $\psi$ would preserve the witnessing $\kappa$-indexed sum;
so one is not, its least partition into small pieces has size $\kappa$, and
pushing it forward gives a nonprincipal $\kappa$-complete ultrafilter on
$\kappa$. Every restriction and partition is a set. This is the
complete-ultrafilter factorization of \cite{sr-RepoLCE}; compare \cite{sr-Bergman}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[The measurable construction: exponential and omnific clauses \src{06}; reprint of \lbl{lce:prop:J}, \lbl{lce:lem:exptransfer}, \lbl{lce:thm:Oz}; under a measurable cardinal]\label[proposition]{sse:sr:prop:measurable}
Let $j:V\to M\subseteq V$ be a normal ultrapower by a measure on a measurable
cardinal $\kappa$, and let $\mathcal J_j:\No\to\No$ apply $j$ to sign codes and
include $\No^M$ into $\No^V$. Besides the properties of \cref{sse:pf:thm:large}
(ordered field, $\R$ fixed, prefix simplicity preserved and reflected,
$\bday(\mathcal J_jx)=j(\bday x)$, $\mathcal J_j(\Omega(x))=\Omega(\mathcal J_jx)$),
it satisfies
\[
 \mathcal J_j(\exp x)=\exp(\mathcal J_jx),\quad
 \mathcal J_j(\log y)=\log(\mathcal J_jy)\ (y>0),\quad
 \ctsr(\mathcal J_jx)=\ctsr(x),
\]
and preserves and reflects $\Oz$. It fixes every surreal of birthday below
$\kappa$, moves $\kappa$ to $j(\kappa)$ and omits $\kappa$ from its image; it
preserves every Hahn sum with fewer than $\kappa$ terms but not the sum of
$(\omega^{-\alpha})_{\alpha<\kappa}$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Absoluteness argument \src{06}]
All prefixes of an old sign sequence are old; the externally simplest separator
of an old set cut is a prefix of its internally simplest separator, so lies in
$M$, and absolute sign inequalities identify the two. Set-like recursion on
canonical options then identifies the field operations and the omega-map in
$M$ and $V$. The same applies to Gonshor's exponential, whose recursive cuts
use simpler option exponentials, finite Taylor polynomials, field operations
and inequalities with the same natural indices and real constants in both
universes; elementary transfer followed by this identification gives the
exponential identity, and uniqueness of the inverse gives $\log$. The
canonical-normal-form absoluteness lemma gives $\Oz$ and constant-coefficient
preservation. Since $\kappa$ is a prefix of $\mathcal J_j(\kappa)$ but is not in
the image, the image is not initial despite prefix preservation and
reflection. For the family $(\omega^{-\alpha})_{\alpha<\kappa}$ the image of the
sum has terms at all indices below $j(\kappa)$, while the sum of the images has
indices below $\kappa$ only; the coefficient at exponent $-\kappa$ detects the
discrepancy.
\end{proof}
This is a joint preservation example with a precisely located first failure,
not an automorphism or a strong embedding; it is a class map defined from a set
measure, not a membership-elementary $V\to V$, and $M\subseteq V$ is not assumed
elementary. Compatibility with $\BMsr$ is unproved; the collection asks for the
required naturality or absoluteness theorem in \lbl{lce:cs:q:derivations}.
Together with \cref{sse:sr:found:prop:firstfailure} it gives the collection's
exact equivalence \lbl{lce:mf:thm:equivalence}: there is no measurable cardinal
iff every countably strong coefficient-fixing, $\Oz$-preserving field
self-embedding is strong. The countable hypothesis is essential: an embedding
failing a countable Hahn sum lies outside the theorem, and whether such a
countable failure can coexist with coefficient fixing and omnific preservation
is a separate question (\cref{sse:sr:agenda:countable}). Neither order continuity
nor finite algebraic elementarity may be substituted for countable Hahn
strength.

\begin{remark}[A formalization boundary \src{06}]\label[remark]{sse:sr:rem:formalboundary}
A natural implementation separates set-small sign and normal-form
constructions; bounded-stage embeddings with explicit coherence; and
class-theoretic metatheorems with declared choice, recursion or large-cardinal
hypotheses. Smallness belongs in the statement of every option and summation
construction, and the collection's foundational interfaces are the place to
record those hypotheses \cite{sr-RepoFound}. A universe-polymorphic theorem is a
family of level-specific statements, not a single carrier containing all
universes.
\end{remark}

\section{Formalization status recorded by source 06 (its Section 8)}\label{sse:sr:sec:lean}

Source 06 compares manuscript scopes with the Lean development theorem by
theorem, using inspected definitions and proof bodies together with the
mapping ledger \cite{sr-RepoCode,sr-RepoLedger}. ``Actual'' means the repository's
sign-sequence surreal carrier at a fixed universe. The table reports
source-level scope, not a fresh compilation, kernel recheck, or transitive
axiom audit.

\begingroup\small
\begin{longtable}{P{34mm}P{114mm}}
\toprule
Topic & Construction and precise boundary (source 06) \\
\midrule
\endhead
\bottomrule
\endfoot
Actual normal forms &
\code{SmallNormalFormFieldEquiv.lean} defines \code{cutEvaluationOrderRingIso},
an ordered ring equivalence between lower-universe-small formal normal forms
and the actual \code{SignSequence} field, built from separately supplied
addition, multiplication, extraction and comparison theorems. It is therefore
incorrect to describe every subsequent exponent construction as merely a
surrogate Hahn-field calculation. \\[3pt]
Actual exponent automorphisms &
\code{NormalFormExponentAutomorphisms.lean} and
\code{OmnificExponentAutomorphisms.lean} construct actual field and omnific-ring
automorphisms from an ordered additive \emph{automorphism} of the exponent
carrier, proving real-coefficient fixation, exact monomial and support
transport, inverse laws and positive exponent dilations. Their input is
bijective; they do not formalize the proper-embedding bottom-gap
classification. \\[3pt]
Strong sums and valuation &
\code{ExponentAutomorphismStrongSums.lean} proves preservation and reflection
of strong summability for arbitrary index universes; the actual strong-sum
identity additionally assumes the index type small in the birthday universe,
exactly the domain of the existing sum. Leading exponents, the additive
valuation with infinity, and the order are transported. \\[3pt]
Omnific reconstruction &
\code{OmnificAutomorphisms.lean} constructs the unique fraction-field extension
of an actual omnific-ring automorphism and proves order preservation, fixation
of embedded reals, finiteness and infinitesimality equivalences and
standard-part preservation; these are automorphism results, consistent with the
coefficient-moving proper embeddings of \cref{sse:sr:prop:taylorexplicit}. \\[3pt]
Character twists &
\code{HahnSeries/CharacterTwist.lean} constructs a Hahn-field automorphism from
a multiplicative character, preserving support and fixing constants;
\code{Surcomplex/CharacterAutomorphisms.lean} transports it to the actual
surcomplex field and Gaussian omnific ring. A general complex character need not
preserve the real axis. \\[3pt]
Exponential rigidity &
\code{Algebra/ExponentialProfile.lean} contains generic displacement,
visible-scale, self-embedding, value-action and coarsening rigidity proofs. In
\code{HahnSeries/ValueGroupLifts.lean} the actual-surreal rational-dilation
nonlifting theorem accepts \code{E : OrderedExp SignSequence} as a hypothesis;
constructing the canonical global surreal exponential in that interface is a
separate obligation. \\
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

\begin{wnote}
Every claim of this table, and of the two paragraphs below, was checked true at
the dossier commit and re-checked at the placement commit \code{e1395c66c}:
the eight modules exist under \fn{Algebra/SurrealNumbers/Surreal/};
\code{cutEvaluationOrderRingIso} is an order-ring equivalence
\code{SmallNormalForm.\{u\}} to \code{SignSequence.\{u\}};
\code{mapExponents} takes an ordered additive \emph{equivalence}
\code{SignSequence.\{u\}} to itself;
\code{exponentAutomorphism\_strongSum} carries a \code{Small.\{u\}} hypothesis on
the index type while the summability transport does not;
\code{omnificAutomorphismExtension} takes an omnific-ring equivalence;
\code{eq\_id\_of\_commute} and \code{eq\_id\_of\_visible\_scale} take a ring
endomorphism of \code{F} with \code{E} only a monotone homomorphism; and
\code{surreal\_not\_lift\_dilation} takes \code{E : OrderedExp SignSequence.\{u\}}
as a hypothesis. The automatic-strongness theorem and the proper-embedding
classification of Part \lbl{opa:part:as} appear in
\fn{docs/FORMALIZATION.md} only as inventory rows.
\end{wnote}

The generic \code{eq\_id\_of\_commute} concerns unital endomorphisms and
\code{eq\_id\_of\_visible\_scale} does not need surjectivity of the exponential,
so no bijectivity or strong-summation hypothesis should be silently added to the
mathematical self-embedding rigidity statement. The canonical-surreal
conclusions, including the answer to KKS Question~5.4, still need the canonical
exponential for an unconditional Lean specialization
\cite{RepoExp,sr-RepoCode,sr-RepoLedger}; the existing finite and infinitesimal
exponentials do not supply that global structure. The omnific automatic
strongness theorem and proper-embedding classification were not located as
mapped Lean proofs; appearance in the ledger's manuscript inventory is not
implementation, and source-level inspection does not certify every imported
dependency.

Source 06's suggested order of continuation:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
\item generalize exponent transport from ordered additive equivalences to
ordered additive embeddings, proving support transport and preservation of
lower-universe-small support before bundling the ring embedding and its
valuation formula; surjectivity should appear only in the inverse theorem;
\item formalize $\gapsr_\tau$ and its separated cosets, giving the
admissibility proof for coefficient sections before any iterated sum, then
integer-part preservation and reflection from block signs;
\item implement the scalar detector (\cref{sse:sr:oz:detector}) with its
countable triangular witness; constant-term covariance plus surjectivity then
gives automatic strongness by finite scalar equations, while the
proper-embedding version must keep a separate target-test hypothesis;
\item build the canonical global exponential, related exactly to the existing
finite and infinitesimal operations, before specializing the generic rigidity
API (the omega-map is not a substitute), with universe-lift compatibility and
small-support statements at each step.
\end{enumerate}
A compatible family of typed constructions is a foundation for class-level
claims, not permission to form a set containing all surreal self-maps.

\section{Research questions of source 06}\label{sse:sr:sec:questions}

Source 06 notes that several broadly phrased questions are already answered in
the collection or the literature: proper field self-embeddings exist,
ordered-field elementarity is automatic, omnific automorphisms are strongly
$\R$-linear, and exact-value exponential self-embeddings are rigid. Its
statuses describe the sources it examined, not a worldwide priority or
exhaustive-literature claim; each target has a first step.

\begin{question}[Elementarity of coefficient-fixing pair embeddings \src{06}]\label[question]{sse:sr:agenda:elementarity}
Which strong exact-monomial, $\R$-fixing self-embeddings are elementary in
$(\No,+,\cdot,<,\Oz)$?
\end{question}
This is the collection's \lbl{opa:as:q:elementary}. Moving a real coefficient
is an obstruction (\cref{sse:sr:thm:ozelementary}), but fixing $\R$ is not known
to suffice; $J_{P_\pm}$ are test cases. First step: a formula with a finite
parameter tuple separating source and image, or a relative quantifier
elimination or extension theorem; real-closed-field quantifier elimination
does not decide the expanded language.

\begin{question}[Countable strongness for proper pair embeddings \src{06}]\label[question]{sse:sr:agenda:countable}
Can a proper field self-embedding fixing $\R$ and preserving and reflecting
$\Oz$ fail to preserve a countable Hahn sum?
\end{question}
Automorphisms are covered by \cref{sse:sr:oz:automatic-strong}; proper
embeddings may lack target detectors, and the measurable first-failure theorem
assumes countable strength, so it does not settle this, with or without
measurable cardinals. First step: characterize when the image supplies enough
constant-term pairings to detect a countable nonsummability witness.

\begin{question}[Removing the exact-monomial assumption \src{06}]\label[question]{sse:sr:q:monomial}
Classify strongly additive omnific-preserving proper embeddings whose images
of monomials may have lower terms.
\end{question}
\Cref{sse:sr:oz:classification} answers this in its exact-monomial category; the
contracting-derivation examples (\cref{sse:sr:ex:contracting}) show that the
category misses automorphisms. Intermediate problem: impose a uniform triangular
form on monomial images and prove a summability criterion, checking both
well-ordered support and finite coefficient incidence. Compare the base
question ``Beyond monomial images'' in \cref{sse:sec:agenda}.

\begin{question}[Omega-field automorphisms and strong proper maps \src{06}]\label[question]{sse:sr:q:omegafield}
Do nonidentity automorphisms of the field $\No$ commute with the omega-map?
Which proper such embeddings can be constructed without large-cardinal
assumptions, and which can be strong?
\end{question}
The automorphism question is \cite[Questions~5.6--5.7]{KKS}. Exact-value
preservation forces identity (\cref{sse:thm:omegarigidity}) but does not classify
the value actions. The measurable construction (\cref{sse:sr:prop:measurable},
\cref{sse:pf:thm:large}) already gives a \emph{proper, nonstrong} omega-field
embedding under its assumption, even preserving $\exp$ and simplicity; it gives
no automorphism or strong example. Within strong monomial lifts the equation
$\tau=H_\tau$ is a starting point, but generalized epsilon numbers prevent a
naive birthday induction. Compare the base question ``$\omega$-map
compatibility''.

\begin{question}[Existence of exponential lifts \src{06}]\label[question]{sse:sr:q:explifts}
Which ordered additive automorphisms of the natural value group arise from
exponential-field automorphisms of $\No$?
\end{question}
Uniqueness follows from exact-value rigidity; existence is the classification
problem. Source 06 records that the repository rules out positive rational
dilations other than the identity and changes confined to bounded value layers
\cite{RepoExp}. First step: a criterion via the logarithmic structure of
monomials, then a test of whether compatible data on elementary birthday stages
lift coherently; for proper embeddings, images and uniqueness separately.
Compare the base question ``Characterize exponential value actions'' and EAR
\lbl{q:image}.

\begin{question}[Realization of differential exponential defects \src{06}]\label[question]{sse:sr:q:defects}
Which real-valued additive maps vanishing on $\mathcal O$ occur as
$\alpha_j(x)=\log(j(\exp x))-j(x)$ for a global differential self-embedding?
\end{question}
\Cref{sse:sr:exp:defect} gives necessary identities and a composition law and
characterizes zero defect and real-power compatibility; Kaplan's transseries
constructions realize substantial local families \cite{sr-KaplanPow}, but their
extension by differential homogeneity need not preserve global strongness or
powers. First step: an extension theorem for a specified defect on a set-sized
differential submodel, then a coherence analysis.

\begin{question}[Joint exponential and differential embeddings \src{06}]\label[question]{sse:sr:q:joint}
Classify maps preserving both $\exp$ and the fixed $\BMsr$, with optional strong
summation and omnific preservation.
\end{question}
$j(\omega)=\omega+c$ is necessary for differential embeddings and the defect must
vanish. Transseries translations are compatible with both structures on their
domain; extending them globally with both properties is stronger than the
separate homogeneity theorems. Focused target: a coherent real-parameter family
of global extensions; the obstruction to global composition does not by itself
exclude such a restricted family. A related collection question,
\lbl{lce:cs:q:derivations}, asks whether $\BMsr$ is absolute under the
inner-model comparison of the measurable construction; that naturality needs an
analysis of its defining recursion and log-atomic data and does not follow from
field or exponential elementarity.

\begin{question}[When does a field lift preserve simplicity? \src{06}]\label[question]{sse:sr:q:simplicity}
Give necessary and sufficient conditions on an ordered additive embedding $\tau$
for $H_\tau$ to preserve prefix simplicity.
\end{question}
The lifting theorem alone does not answer this; simplicity preservation by the
inner parametrization $A_{P_+}$ must not be passed to $H_{A_{P_+}}$ without
proof. Approach: translate the sign-expansion comparison theorem for normal forms
into local conditions on $\tau$, first on two-term forms. Source 06 calls this a
targeted program, not an assertion that no special cases are known. Compare the
base question ``Field and simplicity together'' and source 05's
``Simultaneous field and simplicity embeddings''.

\begin{question}[Stabilizers of large parameter classes \src{06}]\label[question]{sse:sr:q:stabilizers}
Compute the common fixed field of the automorphisms fixing every real and every
ordinal, with or without omnific or strong-sum constraints.
\end{question}
\Cref{sse:sr:logic:ordinals} shows the stabilizer is nontrivial and
\cref{sse:sr:logic:transcendental} gives a moved transcendental element. Source 06
records that the collection computes several fixed fields for specified shear
subgroups \cite{sr-RepoOPA} (unchecked here); those differ from the fixed field of
the whole stabilizer. First target: order reindexings supported in one interval
between ordinal parameters. Compare the base question ``The stabilizer
structure''.
\begin{wnote}
Partial information in this report: by \cref{sse:thm:stabilizer} the common fixed
field of the strong automorphisms fixing $\R\cup\On$ contains no element moved
by some $J_{g_\beta}$, and by \cref{sse:sr:logic:transcendental} it omits
$\omega^{\omega^{1/2}}$; it contains the relative real closure of the field
generated by $\R\cup\On$ (\cref{sse:prop:fixedgeneral}). Not answered.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Images beyond the full normal-form categories \src{06}]\label[question]{sse:sr:q:images}
Which cofinal field self-embedding images are closed in the ambient order
topology? Which proper images can be dense?
\end{question}
Noncofinal images are uniformly discrete and closed
(\cref{sse:sr:rem:closeddiscrete}); full normal-form subfields and strong
exact-monomial images are closed (\lbl{opa:as:thm:closed},
\cref{sse:thm:closedimage}). These do not classify cofinal, possibly nonstrong
images. Ordinary sequences cannot detect the distinction in $\No$
(\cref{sse:prop:setdiscrete}); neighbourhood criteria or justified class-indexed
approximations are required. Compare the base question ``Closed images beyond
strong monomial maps''.

\begin{question}[Compatible local data \src{06}]\label[question]{sse:sr:q:localdata}
Characterize the set-stage embedding systems whose union is an available global
class embedding preserving the chosen operations.
\end{question}
\Cref{sse:sr:found:prop:localization} supplies compatible restrictions of a given
map, not a selection of compatible maps from unrelated existence claims. A useful
formulation fixes a class-coded cofinal chain of stages, specifies restriction
maps, and proves the extension property at successors and the small-support
property at limits. Compare the base question ``Universe and cutoff
absoluteness''.

\paragraph{Formalization projects (source 06).} The most direct are the general
proper normal-form lift, the prefix-derived $J_{P_\pm}$ with their topology
criterion, the finite algebraic proof of definability rigidity, and the explicit
ordinal-fixing automorphism $J_h$. The defect identities of
\cref{sse:sr:exp:defect} can be proved abstractly for an ordered
differential-exponential field with real constant field before any surreal
instantiation. The canonical exponential interface, automatic omnific
strongness, and the measurable first-failure theorem need distinct further work
and should not be bundled into one completion claim.

\section{Status and non-claims of source 06}\label{sse:sr:sec:status}

Source 06's own audit, README and the batch dossier record the following; their
substance is kept here.
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Repository pin and access.} Repository material was pinned to
\code{79049d58c} (full identifier in \cref{sse:sr:sec:glance}) and external
sources checked on
3 October 2026. All collection labels it cites exist (verified for this report
at \code{e1395c66c}, by which every report and Lean file it read is unchanged).
\item \emph{Synthesis, not discovery.} Results already in the repository are
attributed as such; exact-value exponential rigidity and the proper omnific
embeddings are existing repository results, treated as a synthesis rather than
a second claim of discovery. A consequence proved in source 06 is not thereby
claimed new in the literature. The normal-form lifting method is not claimed new.
\item \emph{Lean.} No new Lean theorem is claimed. The implementation assessment
is a source audit (a proof term distinguished from an inventory entry, a generic
result from a canonical-surreal instantiation, an automorphism API from a
proper-embedding theorem); no fresh repository build, kernel recheck or
transitive axiom audit was run.
\item \emph{What was reviewed.} The proofs of the two-level lifts, the
ordinal-fixing automorphism, the topology criterion, scalar detection, the
defect identities and birthday localization were independently reviewed during
preparation; the final source compiled with pdfLaTeX with no unresolved
references, warnings, or overfull/underfull boxes, and the 47-page PDF was
rendered and visually reviewed; the archive was checked against its SHA-256
sums. (Not reproduced for this report; no PDF of source 06 is staged.)
\item \emph{Conventions.} In the decreasing $\omega$-exponent convention
$\sum_{n\ge1}\omega^{1/n}$ is an admissible normal form and
$\sum_{n\ge1}\omega^{-1/n}$ is not; in the increasing $t$-exponent convention
$\gamma\mapsto\gamma+1$ is the contracting valuation shift; coefficient families
are reindexed under composition, so their composition with exponent permutations
is generally semidirect, not direct. Source 06 says its explicit formulas avoid
the opposite signs, and an unqualified direct-product reading, ``in the
corresponding examples and factorization display'' of \cite{KKS}; these are
observations about concrete formulas, not a claim that the structural program of
\cite{KKS} fails. (Not checked for this report; compare the sign remarks of
source 07.)
\item \emph{Errata.} The 2001 van den Dries--Ehrlich paper has an erratum
correcting a quantitative support bound, leaving the closure results used here
unchanged \cite{vdDE}; the erratum to the 2021 Ehrlich--Kaplan paper corrects
reference numbering \cite{EhrlichKaplan}.
\item \emph{Open, not resolved.} The unrestricted omega-field automorphism problem,
the expanded omnific elementarity question, countable strongness for proper pair
embeddings, and the realization of global differential defects.
\item \emph{Conditionality.} Existing large-cardinal constructions are stated with
their assumptions (\cref{sse:sr:prop:measurable}); the exponential and
differential homogeneity consequences (\cref{sse:sr:exp:moveomega},
\cref{sse:sr:prop:translationrealized}, \cref{sse:sr:prop:kernelnonrigid}) rest on
the cited class frameworks and theorems; local or intermediate-field composition
results are not promoted to a global composition law on $\No$.
\item \emph{Limited assertions.} The Taylor automorphism is ``not asserted to
preserve $\Oz$'' (it does not, \cref{sse:sr:rem:taylor}); a research question is a
proposed direction, not a claim that it is open in the whole literature; no
repository file was modified by source 06.
\end{itemize}

\begin{wnote}
Corrections and qualifications made in this Part: credits added for re-proofs
of \lbl{saut:prop:discrete}, \lbl{saut:thm:monomial}, \lbl{saut:thm:coeffflow},
\lbl{saut:thm:expfaithful} and \lbl{saut:thm:orderedhomogeneity}, which source 06
does not cite; its Proposition~4.1 recorded as covered by \cref{sse:thm:stabilizer}
and \cref{sse:prop:oz}; its Corollary~6.5 recorded as weaker than
\cref{sse:thm:expunique}; the comparison sentence after its Theorem~4.3 read
beside \cref{sse:thm:stabilizer}. No mathematical error was found in source 06.
\end{wnote}

\part{Source 07: Order, simplicity, normal forms, analytic structure, and foundations without paradoxes}\label{sse:part:an}

\section{Source 07 at a glance}\label{sse:an:sec:glance}

Source 07 is the manuscript \emph{Self-Embeddings of the Surreal Numbers:
Order, simplicity, normal forms, analytic structure, and foundations without
paradoxes}, a ``research article prepared for Vladimir Reshetnikov'' dated
3 October 2026 (42 pages; one standalone 2{,}944-line LaTeX source with 22
bibliography entries and 18 research questions; no code, no data, no
checksum file). It inspected the repository at the same commit as the base,
\code{b0d4af4f30b24529fec877e2ad4a5952888146e9}. Its foundational setting is
G\"odel--Bernays class theory with Global Choice (GBC) for the
back-and-forth and homogeneity arguments, ZFC formula schemes for the
explicit maps, set-length sign sequences, and set-supported normal forms
written both with large monomials $\omega^a$ and with small monomials
$t^g=\omega^{-g}$. Its main contributions beyond the base are an exact
criterion for convex images of order-and-simplicity embeddings
(\cref{sse:an:ord:convex}), lifts of arbitrary increasing ordinal maps
(\cref{sse:an:ord:ordinal-lift}), the continuity criterion for an
embedding between arbitrary ordered fields (\cref{sse:an:top:criterion}),
a truncation characterization of strong exact-monomial maps
(\cref{sse:an:hahn:truncation}), a conditional classification of the strong
exponential self-embeddings of the omega-series field
(\cref{sse:an:ana:omega-series-classification}), and, in the pure field
language under GBC, a homogeneity theorem with an omitting-types refinement
that determines exactly which elements are unavoidable
(\cref{sse:an:fnd:thm:homogeneity,sse:an:fnd:prop:omit,sse:an:fnd:cor:unavoidable}).
It also found the false sentence in
\fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms} that was corrected
before this report was placed (\cref{sse:an:sec:audit}).

\paragraph{Renames.} Source 07's symbols are renamed to the report's
notation as follows.

\begingroup\small
\begin{longtable}{P{38mm}P{36mm}P{76mm}}
\toprule
Here & Source 07 & Reason \\
\midrule
\endhead
$\bday(x)$ & $\ell(x)$ & Report notation for the birthday (length). \\
$x\pref y$, $x\spref y$ & $x\preceq_s y$, $x\prec_s y$ & Report macros; same relation. \\
$P_p[\No]$ & $C_p$ (prefix cone) & Report writes the image of $P_p$; $C$ is the base's class $C=\bigcap_n(-1/n,\infty)$ and 07's $C_g$ is substitution. \\
$j$ (a double-order embedding) & $F$ & $F$ is an ordered field in \cref{sse:an:top:criterion}; 07's $F_\tau$ is renamed below. \\
$D_{w}$ (block map with words $w=(w_-,w_+)$) & $B$ & $B=\NF{\No_{\ge0}}$ in the base (\eqref{sse:eq:B}); $D_m$, $D_\delta$ are the repetition special cases. \\
$\sigma_\eta$ (tree automorphism) & $T_\eta$ & $T$ is the base's Taylor automorphism. \\
$A_f$, $f$ & $A_h$, $h$ & Report notation for the additive lift. \\
$H_\tau$ & $F_\tau$ ($=A_\tau$) & Report notation for the field lift; \emph{$A_f$ is not a field embedding unless $f$ is additive}. \\
$H_{\tau,\chi}$ & $F_{\tau,\chi}$ & Report notation (base \cref{sse:thm:weighted}). \\
$S_c$ & $D_c$ & Exponent dilation; $D_m$ is sign repetition and $D(a)$ displacement. \\
$\mathcal T_{d,\delta}$ & $T_{d,\delta}$ & As in \fn{omnific-preserving-automorphisms}; the base's $T$ is the case $\delta=1$. \\
$[a]_0$ & $\operatorname{ct}(a)$ & Constant coefficient, as in base \eqref{sse:eq:oz}. \\
$\operatorname{gap}_\tau$ & $G_\tau$ & $G$ is the base's group $\NF{C}$; the collection writes $\operatorname{gap}_\tau$. \\
$H_A$ (field lift of an additive $A$) & $J_A$ & $J$ is reserved for double lifts $J_f=H_{A_f}$. \\
$J_{P_{(-)}}=H_{A_{P_{(-)}}}$ & $J$, $A$, $h$ of 07 Thm.~5.2 & 07's $h(s)=(-)\concat s$ is the prefix map $P_{(-)}$; its double lift is a $J_f$. \\
$\Omega(x)=\omega^x$ & $W(x)$ & Report notation for the omega-map. \\
$\mathcal A=\R\langle\!\langle\omega\rangle\!\rangle$ & $\mathcal A$ & Kept; \emph{not} the additive lift $A_f$ (Part~V uses its own macro for it). \\
$\partial_{\mathcal A}$, $\partial_{\mathcal A,g}$ & $D$, $D_g$ & $D$ is the displacement in the base. \\
$j_c$ (differential automorphism) & $J_c$ & $J$ is reserved for double lifts. \\
$\Q(A)^{\mathrm{rc}}$ & $K_A$ & $K_\infty$ is the iterated-image core. \\
$x|_U$ & $x|U$ & Truncation to a final segment $U$; base $T_{>c}x=x|_{(c,\infty)}$. \\
$\mathcal J_j$ & $J(x)=j(x)$ & Large-cardinal map from $j:V\to M$. \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

\begin{remark}[Conventions of source 07]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:conventions}
An \emph{automorphism} is a bijective self-embedding whose inverse preserves
the same structure, and for relational structures an embedding also
reflects the named relations; one-way preservation is always stated
separately. Preserving a subclass $A$ may mean $j(A)\subseteq A$,
$j^{-1}(A)=A$, $j(A)=A$, or pointwise fixation; these differ for a proper
embedding. For $A=\R$ and a field embedding, setwise preservation
$j(\R)\subseteq\R$ already gives pointwise fixation, by rational cuts
inside $\R$; merely inducing the identity on the residue field gives
neither. Likewise ``preserving canonical monomials'' ($j(\omega^a)$ is a
monomial) is weaker than $j(\omega^a)=\omega^{j(a)}$, which links two levels
of a normal form \src{07}.
\end{remark}

\section{Order, simplicity and the sign tree \src{07}}\label{sse:an:ord:section}

Source 07 calls an embedding of $(\No,<,\pref)$ a \emph{double-order
embedding}; its images are the surreal substructures of
Bagayoko--van der Hoeven \cite{BVH}. Its prefix maps, repetition maps,
birthday rigidity and automorphism rigidity coincide with the base results
of \cref{sse:sec:tree}. This section prints what it adds. Throughout, $j$
denotes a double-order self-embedding of $\No$.

\subsection{Convex images}
Every set of surreals has strict upper and lower bounds, given by Conway
cuts; in particular $\No$ has no set-sized cofinal or coinitial subset.
The following characterization is attributed by source 07 to
\cite[Proposition~4.18]{BVH}; source 07 includes the order-theoretic
argument to make the size qualification explicit.

\begin{proposition}[Convex images of double-order embeddings \src{07}; after {\cite[Prop.~4.18]{BVH}}]\label[proposition]{sse:an:ord:convex}
A convex subclass $C\subseteq\No$ is the image of a double-order embedding
$\No\to\No$ if and only if $C$ has no set-sized cofinal subset and no
set-sized coinitial subset.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Necessity: transport a set cofinal or coinitial in $C$ back to $\No$, where
every set has strict upper and lower bounds.

Sufficiency. First, every nonempty convex class has a unique simplest
member. Choose $c\in C$ of minimal birthday. If $c$ were not a prefix of
some $d\in C$, their longest common prefix would either equal $d$ or lie
strictly between $c$ and $d$; in both cases it is a member of $C$ (by
convexity in the second case) with smaller birthday than $c$, a
contradiction. Thus $c$ is a prefix of every member, which also gives
uniqueness.

Next, for sets $L<R$ contained in $C$ there is a member of $C$ strictly
between them. If both are nonempty, their ambient Conway bracket lies in
$C$ by convexity. If only $R$ is nonempty, the absence of a set-sized
coinitial subset supplies $c\in C$ below $R$, and $\{c\mid R\}$ is a witness
in $C$. The case of nonempty $L$ alone is symmetric, and the empty cut has
a witness because $C\ne\varnothing$. The witnesses form a nonempty convex
class and hence have a simplest member.

Recursively send each $x$ to the simplest element of $C$ between the
already constructed images of $L_x$ and $R_x$. The prefix and fork
comparisons prove that this is a double-order embedding. For
surjectivity, suppose $y\in C$ has minimal birthday among omitted elements.
Its proper prefixes in $C$ are all in the image. Take the Conway cut of
their preimages, separated according to their position below or above $y$.
Each canonical option of this input is bounded on the appropriate side by
a defining option, since otherwise it would be a simpler witness for that
input cut. Hence $y$ satisfies the inequalities that define the image of
the input, and that image is a prefix of $y$. If it were a proper prefix,
it would itself be one of the boundary elements of the defining cut, which
is impossible. So the image is $y$, a contradiction.
\end{proof}

In particular every set-defined open cut $\{y:L<y<R\}$ with $L<R$ sets is
such an image: adjoining internal points to the appropriate boundary gives
strict internal bounds. An open-looking description with class-sized
boundaries does not supply this. For example, the class of finite surreals
has $\N$ as a cofinal subset and so is not the image of any order
self-embedding of all of $\No$ \src{07}. The prefix cones $P_p[\No]$ of
\cref{sse:prop:prefix} are the simplest examples.

\begin{wnote}
The proposition is new to the collection (no report or other source
manuscript states it). The attribution to \cite[Prop.~4.18]{BVH} is source
07's and was not checked against that paper.
\end{wnote}

\subsection{Local coding data and ordinal lifts}
Source 07's Theorem~3.4 describes every double-order embedding by a root
word $p=j(0)$, nonempty successor words $w_{s,\varepsilon}$ beginning with
$\varepsilon$, and possibly empty limit words $v_s$; this is
\cref{sse:co:thm:local}, printed in Part~IV. Its Proposition~3.2 (constant
words $w_-$, $w_+$ of a common length $\mu$, with
$\bday(D_w s)=\mu\cdot\bday(s)$) is the constant-word case, and includes the
concatenation products of \cite[Section~3]{BVH}.

\begin{remark}[Two continuities \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:chaincont}
In the coding of \cref{sse:co:thm:local}, $j$ preserves the longest common
prefix $s\wedge t$: incomparable inputs have images diverging immediately
after $j(s\wedge t)$. Moreover $j$ preserves ambient unions of nonempty
set-sized prefix chains exactly when every limit word $v_s$ is empty;
preserving the union of the empty chain additionally requires $p=0$. This
prefix-tree continuity is unrelated to continuity in the numerical order
topology: the sign doubling $D_2$ preserves chain unions, but every positive
input maps above $1$ and every negative input below $-1$, so the inverse
image of $(-1,1)$ is the nonopen singleton $\{0\}$ (compare
\cref{sse:prop:repeat}). Prefix insertion $P_p$, whose image is convex, is an
order-topological embedding.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[Ordinal lifts \src{07}]\label[corollary]{sse:an:ord:ordinal-lift}
Every strictly increasing class function $h:\On\to\On$ admits a
double-order self-embedding $j$ of $\No$ with
\[
 \bday(j(s))=h(\bday(s))\quad(s\in\No),\qquad j(\alpha)=h(\alpha)\quad(\alpha\in\On).
\]
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Use the coding of \cref{sse:co:thm:local}. Take the root word $+^{h(0)}$.
At a node of length $\alpha$, let $\mu_\alpha>0$ be the unique ordinal with
$h(\alpha)+\mu_\alpha=h(\alpha+1)$ and replace the input sign $\varepsilon$
by the word $\varepsilon^{\mu_\alpha}$ of $\mu_\alpha$ copies of
$\varepsilon$; it is nonempty because $h$ is strictly increasing. At a
node of nonzero limit length $\lambda$, append the all-plus word of length
$\nu_\lambda$, where $\sup_{\beta<\lambda}h(\beta)+\nu_\lambda=h(\lambda)$;
this tail exists, possibly empty, because $\sup_{\beta<\lambda}h(\beta)\le
h(\lambda)$. Induction on $\bday(s)$ gives the birthday formula. All-plus
inputs have all-plus outputs, which gives $j(\alpha)=h(\alpha)$.
\end{proof}
Thus arbitrary strictly increasing ordinal behavior occurs under the two
order constraints alone. No preservation of membership and no
set-theoretic elementarity follows from such a lift \src{07}.

\begin{remark}[Invisible modifications \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:localized}
Source 07's Theorem~3.6 is \cref{sse:pf:prop:localized} applied to the set
$A\cup\R$, proved by the same method: a modification confined to one prefix
cone. Source 07 takes an infinite limit ordinal $\gamma$ above every
birthday in $A$, puts $q=+^\gamma\concat(-)$, and lets $j$ insert one plus
sign after $q$ inside $P_q[\No]$ and be the identity outside; $q$ is
omitted, the cone misses $A$, every real, and every ordinal (its members
have a minus sign at position $\gamma$). For fixing just $\R$ and $\On$ one
can take $q=+^\omega\concat(-)$, the sign sequence of $\omega-1$. Hence no
set of test values, even a whole birthday stage, certifies that a class
double-order embedding is the identity. The statement asserts no
compatibility with field operations.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Rigidity: initial images and prefix growth}
The base proves that an order embedding with simplicity-initial image is
onto (\cref{sse:thm:initial}), and that a double-order automorphism is the
identity (\cref{sse:prop:treebirthday}). Source 07's Corollary~3.9 reaches
the identity directly, without passing through surjectivity.

\begin{proof}[Second route to the identity conclusion after \cref{sse:thm:initial} \src{07}]
Let $j$ be a double-order embedding with simplicity-initial image. Every
proper prefix of $j(x)$ belongs to the image, and by reflection its preimage
is a proper prefix of $x$. Thus $j$ maps the chain of proper prefixes of $x$
isomorphically onto that of $j(x)$, so $\bday(j(x))=\bday(x)$ for every $x$.
Apply \cref{sse:thm:birthday}.
\end{proof}
An initial \emph{image} alone does not make an order embedding rigid:
$x\mapsto x+1$ is onto $\No$. Likewise ``initial'' in the literature on
ordered-field embeddings must not be read as saying that the embedding
preserves simplicity \src{07}.

\begin{remark}[Prefix growth forces identity \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:prefixgrowth}
An increasing map $j:\No\to\No$ with $x\pref j(x)$ for every $x$ is the
identity. If $j(x)>x$, put $y=j(x)\concat(-)$. Then $y$ extends $x$ by the
same first new sign as $j(x)$, so $x<y$ and hence $j(x)<j(y)$; but
$y\pref j(y)$ forces $j(y)<j(x)$. If $j(x)<x$, use $y=j(x)\concat(+)$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Tree automorphisms \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:treeaut}
For any class assignment $\eta$ of a sign $\eta(s)\in\{-1,1\}$ to every node
$s$, identifying signs with $\pm1$,
\[
 \sigma_\eta(x)(\xi)=\eta(x\restriction\xi)\,x(\xi)\qquad(\xi<\bday(x))
\]
is a birthday-preserving automorphism of $(\No,\pref)$; transfinite
decoding recovers the input signs. Conversely every automorphism of
$(\No,\pref)$ has this form: it fixes the root, preserves or swaps the two
children at each node, and preserves unions of prefix chains. It preserves
numerical order exactly when every $\eta(s)=+1$, and reverses it exactly
when every $\eta(s)=-1$, giving $x\mapsto-x$. This makes precise the base's
branch-swap discussion in \cref{sse:sec:tree}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Relative brackets \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:relbracket}
For a double-order embedding with image $I$ the correct naturality law is
$j(\{L\mid R\})=\{j[L]\mid j[R]\}_I$, the simplest witness \emph{inside} $I$:
a simplest witness in $\No$ is a prefix of every other witness, and the two
relations transport this. The relative bracket can differ from the ambient
one: sign doubling sends $1=\{0\mid\varnothing\}$ to $2$, while the ambient
$\{0\mid\varnothing\}$ is still $1$. Ambient bracket preservation forces the
identity (\cref{sse:prop:cutrigid}).
\end{remark}

\subsection{Fixed points}\label{sse:an:ord:fixed-core}
The identity $\Fix(j)=\bigcap_{n<\omega}j^n[\No]$ for every double-order
self-embedding is \cite[Proposition~5.2]{BVH}, printed with a proof as
\cref{sse:sr:ord:fixedintersection} in Part~V. Source 07's proof is shorter:
it uses two predecessors instead of an infinite backward orbit.

\begin{proof}[Second route to \cref{sse:sr:ord:fixedintersection} \src{07}]
Write $K$ for the intersection. Fixed points lie in $K$, and injectivity
gives $j[K]=K$. Suppose $x\in K$ is a nonfixed element of minimal birthday,
and let $y=j^{-1}(x)$ and $z=j^{-1}(y)$, both in $K$. Simplicity embeddings
do not decrease birthdays (\cref{sse:prop:treebirthday}), so
$\bday(z)\le\bday(y)\le\bday(x)$; a smaller birthday would make $y$ or $z$
fixed by minimality, and then $x$ would be fixed. So the three birthdays are
equal, the elements are distinct, and since $j$ is increasing they form a
monotone chain, say $z<y<x$ (the other case is symmetric). The common
prefixes $m=x\wedge y$ and $n=y\wedge z$ have smaller birthday, lie in $K$
(every $j^k[\No]$ is closed under common prefixes), and are therefore fixed.
Preservation of common prefixes gives $j(n)=j(y)\wedge j(z)=m$, so $n=m$.
But $n$ lies strictly between $z$ and $y$, and $m$ strictly between $y$ and
$x$, which is impossible.
\end{proof}
The intersection is defined by quantification over the set $\omega$, so no
collection of proper classes is formed; it may be empty \src{07}.

\begin{example}[Prefix maps and a fixed-point-free embedding \src{07}]\label[example]{sse:an:ex:fixfree}
For $p\ne0$ let $p^\omega$ be the concatenation of $\omega$ copies of $p$.
Then
\[
 \Fix(P_p)=P_{p^\omega}[\No].
\]
If $P_p(x)=x$, induction gives $p^n\pref x$ for every $n$, hence $p^\omega\pref x$;
conversely $p\concat p^\omega=p^\omega$, so every extension of $p^\omega$ is
fixed. This refines the base's least fixed extension $c_j$ of
\cref{sse:thm:closedtree} for $j=P_p$.

On the other hand a double-order embedding can have no fixed point at all.
In the coding of \cref{sse:co:thm:local} take root word $+$, every successor
word equal to the single input sign, and every limit word $+$. Birthday
induction gives $\bday(j(x))=\bday(x)+1$ (at a limit the lengths before the
extra sign have supremum $\bday(x)$), so $j$ has no fixed points; in fact
$j(x)>x$ for every $x$, an ordinal $\alpha$ going to $\alpha+1$. The
obstruction is the extra sign at limit branches: internal suprema exist in
the image but are not the ambient unions. This separates double-order
embedding, prefix-chain continuity and existence of fixed points.
\end{example}

\section{Normal-form lifts \src{07}}\label{sse:an:hahn:sec}

Source 07's two Hahn lifts, weighted lifts, fixed-field formula and
omnific compatibility coincide with base
\cref{sse:thm:addlift,sse:thm:fieldlift,sse:thm:weighted,sse:prop:oz}. It
organizes, and cites, the embedding results of
\fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms}. This section prints its
additional statements and examples.

\begin{remark}[When the additive lift is a field map \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:addconverse}
For an increasing $f:\No\to\No$, the additive lift $A_f$ is a unital field
embedding \emph{exactly} when $f$ is additive. Sufficiency is
\cref{sse:thm:fieldlift}. Conversely, a unital multiplicative $A_f$ applied
to $\omega^a\omega^b=\omega^{a+b}$ gives
$\omega^{f(a)+f(b)}=\omega^{f(a+b)}$, so $f(a+b)=f(a)+f(b)$ by uniqueness of
normal forms. Thus an arbitrary order embedding lifts once to an additive
embedding and \emph{twice} to a field embedding.
\end{remark}

\begin{example}[A constant-coefficient character \src{07}]\label[example]{sse:an:ex:ctchar}
The map $\chi(a)=2^{[a]_0}$, with $[a]_0$ the constant coefficient of $a$,
is a positive real character because coefficient extraction is additive.
With $\tau=\id$, $H_{\id,\chi}$ is a strongly $\R$-linear automorphism fixing
every real and every natural value, but $H_{\id,\chi}(\omega)=2\omega$. By
\eqref{sse:eq:weightedfixed} its fixed field consists exactly of the normal
forms whose exponents have constant coefficient $0$. Fixing the value map
and the coefficient field therefore still permits multiplicative changes of
the chosen representatives of Archimedean classes. (The base's example
after \cref{sse:prop:valueaction} uses $[a]_{-1}$ instead and fixes $\omega$.)
\end{example}

\begin{remark}[Ordinal-fixing automorphisms fixing a set \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:stabilizer}
Source 07's Theorem~4.7 states: \emph{for every set $B\subseteq\No$ there is
a nonidentity strongly $\R$-linear field automorphism of $\No$ fixing $B$ and
every ordinal pointwise and preserving $\Oz$}. Its construction modifies the
exponent order only in a bounded window: with $c<0$ below the two-level
footprint $E(B)$ of \eqref{sse:eq:footprint}, let $\mu_c$ be the identity
outside $(c-1,c)$ and $\mu_c(c-1+u)=c-1+2u/(1+u)$ for $0<u<1$ (inverse
$w\mapsto w/(2-w)$ on $(0,1)$), and take $J_{\mu_c}$. It moves
$\omega^{\omega^{c-1/2}}$ to $\omega^{\omega^{c-1/3}}$. A parameter-free
instance uses $\mu(x)=x/(x+2)$ on $(-1,0)$ and the identity elsewhere: its
double lift fixes every real and every ordinal and sends
$\omega^{\omega^{-1/2}}$ to $\omega^{\omega^{-1/3}}$. The argument for
ordinals does not need exponents smaller than the ordinal, so it covers
epsilon numbers.
\end{remark}
\begin{wnote}
Verified: this is covered by the base. The automorphism clause of
\cref{sse:thm:stabilizer} gives, for every set $A$, a nonidentity strongly
$\R$-linear automorphism $J_{g_\beta}$ fixing $A\cup\R\cup\On$; since
$J_{g_\beta}=H_{A_{g_\beta}}$ is a weighted monomial embedding with $\chi=1$,
\cref{sse:prop:oz} gives preservation and reflection of $\Oz$ and of the
omnific floor. Source 07's window map $\mu_c$ differs from $g_\beta$ only in
modifying a bounded interval instead of a lower half-line. Part~V's
\cref{sse:sr:logic:ordinals} is the case $B=\varnothing$.
\end{wnote}

\subsection{Truncation compatibility}
For a final segment $U$ of the exponent order write
$x|_U=\sum_{a\in\supp(x)\cap U}r_a\omega^a$; thus the base's $T_{>c}x$ is
$x|_{(c,\infty)}$.

\begin{proposition}[Strongness is truncation compatibility \src{07}]\label[proposition]{sse:an:hahn:truncation}
Let $f:\No\to\No$ be an $\R$-linear field embedding with
$f(\omega^a)=\omega^{\tau(a)}$ for every $a$. The following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $f$ is strongly additive;
\item $f=H_\tau$;
\item $f\bigl(x|_{\tau^{-1}(U)}\bigr)=f(x)|_U$ for every $x$ and every final
segment $U$.
\end{enumerate}
In (iii) it suffices to take $U=(b,\infty)$ and $U=[b,\infty)$ for all
surreal $b$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Here $\tau$ is an ordered additive embedding, by the converse part of
\cref{sse:thm:weighted}. Strong additivity applied to the family of
normal-form terms of $x$ gives (ii), and (ii) gives (iii) termwise.
Conversely, subtract the identities (iii) for $U=[b,\infty)$ and
$U=(b,\infty)$. The left side is $0$ if $b\notin\tau(\supp x)$, and is
$r_a\omega^{\tau(a)}$ if $b=\tau(a)$ with $a\in\supp x$; the right side is
exactly the term of $f(x)$ at exponent $b$. This determines every
coefficient of $f(x)$ and proves $f=H_\tau$, which is strongly additive by
\cref{sse:thm:fieldlift}.
\end{proof}
The condition concerns commutation with truncations at \emph{every} cut,
including cuts in the gaps of $\tau(\No)$; at $b=\tau(c)$ it is the base's
covariance \eqref{sse:eq:truncation} with $\chi=1$. Merely asking for a
truncation-closed \emph{image} is much weaker: the image of every
automorphism is $\No$ \src{07}.
\begin{wnote}
New to the collection. Compare \lbl{opa:as:cor:monodet} of
\fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms}: an $\Oz$-preserving field
automorphism fixing every monomial is the identity, because such
automorphisms are automatically strong (\lbl{opa:as:thm:main}). The
proposition replaces strongness, for embeddings sending monomials to
monomials, by an equivalent truncation condition.
\end{wnote}

\subsection{The Taylor flow and omnific integers}
Source 07's Proposition~4.9 is the coefficient Taylor flow
\[
 \mathcal T_{d,\delta}\Bigl(\sum_{g\in S}r_gt^g\Bigr)
 =\sum_{g\in S}\sum_{n\ge0}\frac{d^n(r_g)}{n!}t^{g+n\delta}\qquad(\delta>0),
\]
for a derivation $d:\R\to\R$ with $d(b)=1$ at a chosen transcendental $b$:
a strongly additive field automorphism with inverse $\mathcal T_{-d,\delta}$
that fixes every monomial, every natural value and every leading
coefficient, and sends $b$ to $b+t^\delta$. This is \lbl{saut:thm:coeffflow}
and display \lbl{opa:as:eq:taylor}; the base's $T$ of \eqref{sse:eq:taylorshift}
is the case $\delta=1$, and Part~V treats it in
\cref{sse:sr:rem:taylor}. Source 07's proof is the same
support argument ($S+\N\delta$ is well ordered with finite fibers), with the
binomial cancellation $\sum_{i=0}^k(-1)^i/(i!(k-i)!)=0$ for $k>0$ for the
inverse.

\begin{remark}[The flow is defined everywhere but does not preserve $\Oz$ \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:taylorOz}
The construction works on arbitrary normal forms: overlap between shifted
supports causes finite coefficient addition, not failure of definition.
What fails is omnific preservation. For the chosen $b$,
\[
 \mathcal T_{d,\delta}\Bigl(\frac b2\,\omega^\delta\Bigr)
 =\frac b2\,\omega^\delta+\frac12\notin\Oz,
 \qquad \frac b2\,\omega^\delta\in\Oz .
\]
Source 07 records that this corrects a sentence of
\fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms}; see
\cref{sse:an:sub:taylorcorrection}.
\end{remark}

Source 07's Theorem~4.10 (the coefficient-gap classification of strongly
additive field embeddings $J$ with $J(t^g)=t^{\tau(g)}$ and
$J(\Oz)\subseteq\Oz$, as the maps
$J_{\tau,\rho}(\sum r_gt^g)=\sum\rho(r_g)t^{\tau(g)}$ with
$\rho:\R\to\R((t^{\operatorname{gap}_\tau}))$) is
\lbl{opa:as:thm:classification} of
\fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms}, including its clause
$J(\R)\subseteq\R((t^{\operatorname{gap}_\tau}))$ without strongness, with
the same proof (\lbl{opa:as:def:gap}, \lbl{opa:as:lem:gap}); Part~V prints
it as \cref{sse:sr:oz:classification}. Source 07 states it for
$\No\to\No$ and credits the repository.

\begin{remark}[Sharpness of the gap criterion \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:gapsharp}
If $\operatorname{gap}_\tau\ne0$, choose $0<\delta\in\operatorname{gap}_\tau$
and $\rho(r)=\sum_{n\ge0}d^n(r)t^{n\delta}/n!$; then $J_{\tau,\rho}$ moves $b$
to $b+t^\delta$ while preserving and reflecting $\Oz$. For
$\mu(a)=a+\sqrt{a^2+1}$, an order isomorphism $\No\to\No_{>0}$ with inverse
$y\mapsto(y-y^{-1})/2$, the additive embedding $\tau=A_\mu$ has the purely
infinite normal forms as image, so its fixed points are $0$ only and
$\Fix(H_{A_\mu})=\R$; every positive element of $\tau(\No)$ is positive
infinite, so $1\in\operatorname{gap}_\tau$. Hence
$\mathcal T_{d,1}\circ H_{A_\mu}=J_{A_\mu,\rho}$ is an explicit proper
$\Oz$-preserving field embedding with $b\mapsto b+\omega^{-1}$. If instead
$\tau(\No)^{>0}$ is coinitial, then $\operatorname{gap}_\tau=0$ and every
embedding in the classification fixes the canonical reals. The purely
infinite group is a subgroup of the exponent group, not a convex subgroup
of the field: it contains $\omega$ and $0$ but not $1$.
\end{remark}
\begin{wnote}
$\mu=\mu_{13}$ of display \lbl{opa:as:eq:mus} and its inner lift
\lbl{opa:as:lem:inner}; the Taylor section and its admissibility criterion
are \lbl{opa:as:lem:taylorsection} and \lbl{opa:as:thm:taylorcriterion}.
Part~V prints the same explicit map as \eqref{sse:sr:oz:explicit-map}.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[Strongness, continuity and Hahn sums \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:nonstrong}
Source 07's Section~4.8 uses the class homogeneity of the surreal ordered
field to build a field automorphism fixing $\omega$ but moving
$a=\sum_{n\ge1}\omega^{1/n}$ to $a+\omega^{-\omega}$; this is the example
\eqref{sse:sr:field:nonstrongseries} of Part~V (pure field language; compare
\cref{sse:an:fnd:thm:homogeneity}). Source 07 adds that there is no conflict
with order continuity of an automorphism: the finite partial sums of $a$ do
not converge to $a$ in the order or valuation topology, because the first
omitted monomial $\omega^{1/(m+1)}$ exceeds $1$. A Hahn sum is specified by
support and coefficients; its existence asserts no topological convergence
of partial sums. Field operations, ambient order continuity and commutation
with all Hahn sums are three separate requirements; a field automorphism has
the first two and can fail the third. Normal-form prescriptions do not
determine an arbitrary field embedding from its values on selected
generators or from its value action.
\end{remark}

\section{Continuity and set nets \src{07}}\label{sse:an:top:section}

Continuity of a class map $f$ at $a$ is the ordinary formula
$\forall\varepsilon>0\,\exists\delta>0\,\forall x\,
(|x-a|<\delta\Rightarrow|f(x)-f(a)|<\varepsilon)$ with class parameter $f$;
no object of all open subclasses is needed, and the same language applies to
ordered fields of set or class size.

\begin{theorem}[Continuity and cofinal image \src{07}; after \lbl{opa:as:thm:topology}]\label{sse:an:top:criterion}
Let $F$ and $K$ be ordered fields and $j:F\to K$ an order-preserving field
embedding. The following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $j$ is continuous at $0$ for the order topologies of $F$ and $K$;
\item $j$ is continuous everywhere;
\item $j(F_{>0})$ is coinitial in $K_{>0}$;
\item $j(F)$ is cofinal in $K$.
\end{enumerate}
If these conditions fail, $j(F)$ is a discrete subspace of $K$, although its
intrinsic ordered-field topology is not discrete.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Suppose (i), and let $0<\varepsilon\in K$. Choose $\delta>0$ in $F$ for this
neighborhood; then $0<j(\delta/2)<\varepsilon$, proving (iii). Conversely,
given (iii) and $\varepsilon$, choose $d>0$ in $F$ with $j(d)<\varepsilon$;
for $|x|<d$, order preservation gives $|j(x)|=j(|x|)<j(d)<\varepsilon$, which
is (i). Additivity transports continuity at $0$ to every point, and
continuity at any point back to $0$, so (i)$\iff$(ii). Conditions (iii) and
(iv) are equivalent by reciprocation, $j(1/x)=1/j(x)$.

If the image is not cofinal, choose $B>0$ in $K$ above every element of
$j(F)$. By symmetry $B$ bounds all absolute values in the image, and applying
this to $j(1/x)$ gives $|j(x)|>B^{-1}$ for $x\ne0$. So an ambient interval of
radius $B^{-1}$ about an image point contains no other image point. By
contrast every intrinsic interval of radius $d>0$ about $0$ contains $d/2$.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
This is the home statement of the report for continuity versus cofinality.
The case $F=K=\No$ is base \cref{sse:thm:topdichotomy} and
\lbl{opa:as:thm:topology} of \fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms},
where it is phrased through the value-group image $\tau_f(\No)$; source 07
does not cite that theorem. The same statement is reproved in Part~II
(source 02's Theorem~9.1), Part~III (\cref{sse:at:thm:continuity}) and
Part~IV (source 05's Theorem~7.1). When the conditions fail, $j$ is
nowhere continuous, since continuity at one point gives (i).
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[Subspace versus intrinsic topology \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:topremarks}
An ordered-field isomorphism onto its image is always a homeomorphism for
the image's intrinsic order topology; the theorem concerns the subspace
topology from $K$. An automorphism of $\No$ is onto and so continuous;
continuity does not distinguish the identity among automorphisms. Even the
inclusion $\R\hookrightarrow\No$ is discontinuous: its image is bounded by
$\omega$, and the interval of radius $1/\omega$ about a real contains no
other real. For a Hahn lift $H_A$ of an ordered additive embedding $A$,
\begin{equation}\label{sse:an:top:liftcriterion}
 H_A\text{ is order-continuous}\iff A(\No)\text{ is cofinal in }\No ,
\end{equation}
because cofinal $A(\No)$ makes the monomials of the image dominate every
surreal, while $A(s)<b$ for all $s$ gives $|H_A(x)|<\omega^b$. Only leading
exponents are used, not an exchange of limits with infinite sums (compare
\cref{sse:thm:fieldlift,sse:cor:strongstabilizer}).
\end{remark}

Source 07's Theorem~5.2 (a strongly $\R$-linear proper field self-embedding
fixing $\R$ with $\omega^{-1}<|J(x)|<\omega$ for $x\ne0$, hence discrete image
and nowhere continuous) is \cref{sse:co:thm:bounded} of Part~IV. Its map is
different: the double lift of the one-sign prefix map $P_{(-)}$.

\begin{proof}[Second route to \cref{sse:co:thm:bounded} \src{07}]
The prefix map $P_{(-)}(s)=(-)\concat s$ is an order isomorphism of $\No$
onto $\No_{<0}$: every negative surreal begins with a minus sign, and deleting
it (reindexing the remaining final segment by its order type) gives the
unique preimage. By \cref{sse:thm:addlift}, $A=A_{P_{(-)}}$ is an ordered,
strongly $\R$-linear additive embedding whose image is the class of normal
forms with only negative exponents, that is, the infinitesimals
$\mathfrak m$; so $A$ is an ordered additive isomorphism $\No\cong\mathfrak m$
and not a field map ($A(1)=\omega^{-1}$). By \cref{sse:thm:fieldlift} the
double lift $J=J_{P_{(-)}}=H_A$ is a strongly $\R$-linear field embedding
fixing $\R$, with image the normal forms whose exponents lie in
$\mathfrak m$. For $x\ne0$ the leading exponent of $J(x)$ is infinitesimal,
so $|J(x)|<\omega$, and applying this to $J(1/x)=1/J(x)$ gives
$|J(x)|>\omega^{-1}$. The surreal $\omega$ is not in the image. The
discreteness and discontinuity follow from \cref{sse:an:top:criterion}.
\end{proof}
Here the image is a bounded real-closed subfield of proper-class size:
boundedness in the surreal order does not imply set size. Preservation of
all Conway-summable families does not imply order continuity \src{07}.

Source 07's Proposition~5.3 is \cref{sse:prop:setdiscrete} (via the cut
$\{0\mid D\}$ below the set $D$ of nonzero deviations; collection
\lbl{saut:prop:discrete}).

\begin{remark}[A class-indexed witness to discontinuity \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:classnet}
By \cref{sse:prop:setdiscrete}, $1/n$ does not converge to $0$ (the
neighborhood of radius $1/\omega$ excludes every term), and every map
$\No\to\No$ preserves the limits of all convergent set-indexed nets, since
eventual constancy is preserved; such nets give no continuity test. The
full class has no set-sized local base at $0$. Class-indexed nets do give
tests without forming a collection of classes: $u_\alpha=\omega^{-\alpha}$
($\alpha\in\On$) converges to $0$ in the sense that for every
$\varepsilon>0$ there is $\alpha_0$ with $0<u_\alpha<\varepsilon$ for all
$\alpha\ge\alpha_0$ (take $\alpha_0>-b$ for $b$ the leading exponent of
$\varepsilon$; ordinals are cofinal). For the map $J=J_{P_{(-)}}$ above,
$J(u_\alpha)=\omega^{-A(\alpha)}>\omega^{-1}$ because $A(\alpha)$ is a
positive infinitesimal, so the image net never enters the
$\omega^{-1}$-neighborhood of $0$. All of this uses only the class
parameters $J$ and $u$, set quantifiers and ordinal tail conditions.
\end{remark}

\section{Exponentiation, the omega-map and the derivation \src{07}}\label{sse:an:ana:section}

Source 07's displacement lemma, exponential valuation rigidity,
value-displacement proposition and two-embedding uniqueness coincide with
base \cref{sse:lem:amplify,sse:thm:exprigidity,sse:thm:expvalue,sse:thm:expunique}
and with Part~III's \cref{sse:at:thm:pair-rigidity} (source 07's
Proposition~6.5 is stated, as there, for an abstract ordered field $F$ with a
total increasing exponential $E$ and a nontrivial convex valuation $w$). Its
Proposition~6.1 is \cref{sse:thm:omegarigidity} with the remark after it.
Source 07 presents the rigidity theorem as a repository theorem
(\fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity}, \lbl{cor:embedding}),
``not a new result claimed by the present article''. Throughout, strong
additivity includes summability of the image family, and a strongly
$\R$-linear map fixes the distinguished coefficient field pointwise.

\begin{remark}[Two exponential operations \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:twoexp}
The omega-map $\Omega(x)=\omega^x$ parametrizes canonical monomials; Gonshor's
$\exp$ is an increasing isomorphism $(\No,+)\to(\No_{>0},\cdot)$. Write
$a^{[x]}=\exp(x\log a)$ for the exponential-field power of a positive base.
Then $\omega^{[x]}$ and $\Omega(x)$ are different functions: from
$\exp(\omega)=\Omega(\omega)$ and $\log\omega>1$,
$\omega^{[\omega]}=\exp(\omega\log\omega)>\exp(\omega)=\Omega(\omega)$
\cite{vdDE,sr-ADH}. An exponential-field embedding preserves the powers,
$j(a^{[x]})=j(a)^{[j(x)]}$, since commuting with $\exp$ gives commuting with
$\log$ on positive elements; this does not give $j(\Omega(x))=\Omega(j(x))$.
Conversely, the omega-map law forces $j(\omega)=\omega$ (as $\Omega(1)=\omega$
and $j(1)=1$) but says nothing by itself about $\exp$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[A tempting induction fails \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:epsinduction}
If $j$ is strongly $\R$-linear and commutes with $\Omega$, then
$j(\sum_ir_i\Omega(a_i))=\sum_ir_i\Omega(j(a_i))$. This is a constraint, not
a proof that $j=\id$: the exponents $a_i$ need not have smaller birthday than
the original surreal, and at a generalized epsilon number $e=\Omega(e)$ the
identity refers back to $e$ itself. An induction needs a genuinely
well-founded rank or a separate treatment of such fixed points. The existence
and description of nontrivial omega-field automorphisms, and the extension
of order automorphisms of the generalized epsilon numbers, are
\cite[Questions~5.6--5.7]{KKS}; the displayed identity resolves neither.
Source 07 notes that the pointwise-valuation clause is the self-embedding
version of \cite[Proposition~5.5]{KKS}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[An exponential automorphism moving values \src{07}; conditional]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:expwitness}
Source 07, like Part~V's \cref{sse:sr:exp:moveomega}, obtains a nonidentity
exponential-field automorphism $J$ of $\No$ fixing $\R$ with
$J(\omega)=\omega+1$ (indeed $J(\omega)=g$ for any prescribed $g>\R$): by
o-minimality, $\omega$ and $\omega+1$ have the same type over $\R$;
definable Skolem functions give an elementary isomorphism between the
set-sized definable closures of $\R\cup\{\omega\}$ and $\R\cup\{\omega+1\}$;
and the strong homogeneity of the surreal exponential field extends it
\cite{vdDE,KKS}. This rests on that imported homogeneity theorem in its class
framework and asserts no preservation of infinite sums. Source 07 adds an
explicit witness that its value action is nontrivial: for
$y=\exp(\exp\omega)$,
\[
 \frac{J(y)}{y}=\exp\bigl(\exp(\omega+1)-\exp\omega\bigr)
 =\exp\bigl((e-1)\exp\omega\bigr),\qquad \val(J(y))-\val(y)<0,
\]
because the exponent is positive infinite. So an automorphism can fix the
value of $\omega$ while changing the value of a larger exponential
expression, as \cref{sse:thm:expvalue} requires. The mechanism is not
specifically surreal: for $(F,E,w)$, the class $\{x:w(E(x))=0\}$ is a proper
convex additive subgroup, bounded in $F$, and an exponential self-embedding
fixing every $w$-value has its displacement in it (base \cref{sse:thm:profile});
the bound must live in the same field as the endomorphism.
\end{remark}

Source 07's Proposition~6.6 states, for a differential field self-embedding
$j$ of $(\No,\partial_{\mathrm{BM}})$ (using only
$\ker\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}=\R$ and $\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}\omega=1$
\cite{BM}), that $j$ fixes $\R$ pointwise and $j(\omega)=\omega+c$ for a
unique $c\in\R$: this is \cref{sse:prop:differential} and Part~V's
\cref{sse:sr:exp:translationparameter} (which adds $c_{j\circ k}=c_j+c_k$).
Its additional clause is the following.

\begin{proposition}[The omega-map removes the translation \src{07}]\label[proposition]{sse:an:ana:differential-constraint}
Let $j$ be a differential field self-embedding of $(\No,\partial_{\mathrm{BM}})$,
so that $j(\omega)=\omega+c$ with $c\in\R$. If $j$ commutes with the
omega-map, or merely sends every canonical monomial to a canonical monomial,
then $c=0$, that is, $j(\omega)=\omega$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
With the omega-map, $j(\omega)=j(\Omega(1))=\Omega(j(1))=\Omega(1)=\omega$.
Under the weaker hypothesis, $j(\omega)=\omega+c$ must be a monomial; for
$c\ne0$ its normal form $\omega+c\,\omega^0$ has two terms.
\end{proof}
This is a necessary constraint, not global rigidity. Every real
translation of $\omega$ occurs in a global differential automorphism $j_c$:
substitution of $\omega+c$ is an exponential and differential automorphism
of the field $\mathbb T$ of logarithmic-exponential transseries embedded in
$\No$ with variable $\omega$ (inverse: substitution of $\omega-c$; chain
rule since $(\omega+c)'=1$; \cite{sr-BM19}), $\mathbb T$ is an elementary
differential subfield \cite[Theorem~1]{sr-ADH}, and the homogeneity theorem
