for Liouville-closed, $\omega$-free, newtonian $H$-fields with small
derivation and constants $\R$ extends it \cite{an-VdDEhrlichH}. Part~V
prints this argument after \cref{sse:sr:exp:translationparameter}. The
extension is obtained in the differential language only: it need not
preserve $\exp$, Conway sums or the omega-map, and $c\ne0$ already excludes
the last. This is why differential automorphisms must be distinguished from
the intersection of the differential and exponential categories \src{07}.

\subsection{The omega-series field}
Let $\mathcal A=\R\langle\!\langle\omega\rangle\!\rangle$ be the field of
omega-series: the smallest subfield of $\No$ containing $\R$ and $\omega$ and
closed under $\exp$, $\log$ and all defined set-indexed sums. It is a proper
class and a proper subfield of $\No$. Source 07 uses three imported results,
which it distinguishes:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item \cite[Theorem~6.3]{sr-BM19}: for $g>\R$ there is a unique substitution
$C_g:\mathcal A\to\No$, $C_g(f)=f\circ g$, strongly $\R$-linear, preserving
$\exp$, with $C_g(\omega)=g$; composition is associative where defined.
\item \cite[Corollary~3.20]{an-Bag25}: $(\mathcal A^{>\R},\circ,\omega)$ is a
group, so compositional inverses exist inside $\mathcal A$; this is stronger
than the existence of substitutions alone.
\item \cite[Theorem~A]{an-Mantova26}: an account that uses this inversion
result and gives monotonicity in the substituted argument.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{theorem}[Strong exponential self-embeddings of omega-series \src{07}; conditional on (a)--(c)]\label{sse:an:ana:omega-series-classification}
Assume the imported results (a)--(c). Every strongly $\R$-linear
exponential-field self-embedding $j:\mathcal A\to\mathcal A$ is $C_g$ for the
unique $g=j(\omega)\in\mathcal A^{>\R}$, and every such self-embedding is an
automorphism of $\mathcal A$. Those commuting with $\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}$ are
exactly the translations $C_{\omega+c}$, $c\in\R$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Order preservation and fixation of the reals give $g>\R$. Both $j$ and $C_g$
fix the real coefficients, send $\omega$ to $g$, preserve the field
operations and $\exp$, hence $\log$, and commute with all the specified
sums. The elements on which they agree form a subfield containing the
generators and closed under these operations; minimality of $\mathcal A$
gives $j=C_g$. Let $g^{\mathrm{inv}}\in\mathcal A^{>\R}$ be the inverse from
(b). Associativity and the identity substitution give
$C_{g^{\mathrm{inv}}}\circ C_g=\id=C_g\circ C_{g^{\mathrm{inv}}}$, so $C_g$ is
onto, and evaluation at $\omega$ gives uniqueness of $g$. If $C_g$ commutes
with $\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}$, evaluation at $\omega$ gives
$\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}g=1$, so $g-\omega\in\ker\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}=\R$.
Conversely the chain rule on $\mathcal A$ gives
$\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}(f\circ(\omega+c))=(\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}f)\circ(\omega+c)$.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
New to the collection as a statement. It is conditional on the three cited
results, which were not re-proved or checked here. Part~V proves the
restriction identity $j(f)=f\circ j(\omega)$ on $\mathcal A$ for global
strong exponential maps of $\No$ (\cref{sse:sr:prop:omegarestriction}); source 07
adds surjectivity from Bagayoko's inverses and the differential
classification.
\end{wnote}

\begin{remark}[Composition, covariance and the ambient domain \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:covariance}
Parameters compose in reverse order, $C_g\circ C_h=C_{h\circ g}$, so the strong
exponential automorphisms of $\mathcal A$ form the opposite of the group
$\mathcal A^{>\R}$ under composition, and the differential ones form the
additive group of real translations. For example $C_{2\omega}$ is a strong
exponential automorphism with inverse $C_{\omega/2}$, but it is not
differential, since $\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}(2\omega)=2$. Requiring
$j(\omega)=\omega$ makes every embedding in the theorem the identity. With
$\partial_{\mathcal A}=\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}|_{\mathcal A}$ the chain rule reads
$\partial_{\mathcal A}C_g=(\partial_{\mathcal A}g)\,C_g\,\partial_{\mathcal A}$;
as $\partial_{\mathcal A}g>0$, the operator
$\partial_{\mathcal A,g}=(\partial_{\mathcal A}g)^{-1}\partial_{\mathcal A}$ is a
derivation and $C_g$ is a differential isomorphism
$(\mathcal A,\partial_{\mathcal A})\to(\mathcal A,\partial_{\mathcal A,g})$.
For real $\lambda>0$, $\partial_{\mathcal A}C_g=\lambda C_g\partial_{\mathcal A}$
holds exactly when $g=\lambda\omega+c$ with $c\in\R$ (evaluate at $\omega$,
then use the constant field; the chain rule gives the converse). This changes
the differential structure; it adds no automorphisms of the original
differential field. The theorem classifies embeddings of $\mathcal A$, not of
$\No$: a global strong exponential map fixing $\R$ and $\omega$ fixes
$\mathcal A$ by the generation argument but leaves the rest of $\No$
untouched by the proof, and $C_g:\mathcal A\to\No$ for general $g>\R$ need
not be an endomorphism of $\mathcal A$, much less of $\No$.
\end{remark}

Source 07's Proposition~6.8 (an exponential derivation $D$ of $\No$ with a
uniform bound $\val(D(y))\ge\val(y)+\gamma$ is zero) is the case of
\lbl{thm:derivation-valued} of
\fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity}, which needs neither the
order nor convexity, proved by the same amplification; in particular
$\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}$ is not globally contracting (\lbl{cor:no-contraction}
there), while its smallness on the valuation ring is a local condition.

\begin{remark}[Operator exponentials and the remaining intersections \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:intersections}
An operator exponential $\sum_nD^n(x)/n!$ of a derivation is not a general
definition: summability and multiplicativity need hypotheses, and the
previous paragraph excludes a derivation that respects $\exp$ and contracts
every value uniformly. A valid flow must specify its domain and prove
summability there (compare the controlled shift in \eqref{sse:eq:taylorshift}).
The established intersections are: value fixation with exponential
compatibility forces the identity (\cref{sse:thm:exprigidity}); value fixation
with the omega-map forces the identity (\cref{sse:thm:omegarigidity});
derivation preservation forces real fixation and $j(\omega)\in\omega+\R$;
adding the omega-map fixes $\omega$; adding $\exp$ and strong additivity fixes
all of $\mathcal A$. None of the last deductions, without value fixation, is a
classification on all of $\No$. The global composition programme also has a
precise boundary: a well-behaved composition $\No\times\No^{>\R}\to\No$ is
conjectured in \cite[Remark~4.1]{KKS}, while \cite[Definition~8.1 and
Theorem~8.4]{sr-BM19} show that $\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}$ cannot support a global
composition with their compatibility package (chain rule, monotonicity from
the derivative sign); this excludes that package, not every composition and
not individual embeddings. Source 07 lists as unresolved directions: the strong
exponential self-embeddings of all of $\No$ and their action beyond
$\mathcal A$; which differential automorphisms preserve $\exp$ globally;
whether the omega-map, $\exp$, $\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}$ and summation jointly
force the identity without a value hypothesis; and cofinal versus
noncofinal exponential embeddings with prescribed value action. The
homogeneity, substitution and valuation theorems settle, respectively, an
extension problem in one language, a generated-subfield problem and an
identity-kernel problem; none substitutes for the others.
\end{remark}

\section{Foundations: objects and quantifiers \src{07}}\label{sse:an:fnd:sec:foundations}

Source 07 works with $\No=\bigcup_{\alpha\in\On}\{-,+\}^\alpha$, a proper
class of set-length sign sequences with set-supported normal forms; there
is no sign sequence of length $\On$, and $\{L\mid R\}$ requires sets
(take $L=\No$, $R=\varnothing$). Every set of surreals is bounded above and
below by an ordinal exceeding the lengths of its members, which is why
``saturated over every set of parameters'' does not mean saturated over
every subclass \cite{vdDE}. This agrees with base \cref{sse:sec:foundations};
source 07 adds the following points.

\paragraph{Individual maps, coded families and collections.} In GBC a map
$J:\No\to\No$ is a class of ordered pairs; by class Replacement its
restriction to a set has a set graph, which permits $J[S]$, transported cuts
and transported supports, and compositions, ranges and inverses of
bijections are again classes. There is no class of \emph{all} class maps;
notation such as $\operatorname{Aut}(\No)$ is a predicate on class variables
with its composition laws, the convention also used in
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/surreal-fields-across-universes}. A
\emph{specified} family is different: a class $F\subseteq I\times\No\times\No$,
with $I$ a set or a class of sets, codes maps $F_i$ by its slices, so a set
group acting on $\No$ or an ordinal-indexed family of embeddings needs no
collection of classes. Quantifying over every class map uses second-order
quantifiers; treating the whole collection as an object needs a further type
level or a theory of hyperclasses. The same discipline applies to topology:
a subclass $U$ is open when each point lies in an interval inside $U$, and
continuity is the local neighborhood condition.

\paragraph{ZFC, GBC and stronger recursion.} Explicit formulas for embeddings
can be handled in ZFC: for a fixed formula $\Phi(x,y,p)$ defining a unique
output, preservation of an operation is a first-order assertion, and
statements about all definable maps are schemes. Coding formulas by natural
numbers does not remove this: a uniform evaluator for arbitrary
set-theoretic formulas would be a forbidden definable truth predicate for
the universe. One may instead use a fixed definable family, bounded formula
complexity, or a stronger theory with the required satisfaction relation; the
explicit constructions need none of these. For noncanonical global extension
arguments source 07 uses GBC including Global Choice (the common convention
for NBG in the surreal literature), which supplies an $\On$-indexed
enumeration of $\No$ and proves first-order \emph{set-valued} transfinite
recursion with class parameters: the history below any ordinal is a set,
local recursions agree, and their graphs form a class by comprehension. This
is not the principle $\mathsf{ETR}$ for class-valued stages, whose additional
strength is analyzed by Gitman--Hamkins \cite{at-GitmanHamkins} (compare
\cite{HamkinsETR}); truth predicates for arbitrary class structures are a
familiar source of that requirement. Kelley--Morse theory gives more than is
needed, and a principle choosing a class witness for every set parameter is a
further issue not assumed merely by naming KM \cite[Section~2]{an-AntosGitman}.
The extension proofs below choose individual sets and keep set-sized partial
maps at every stage.

\section{Elementarity and homogeneity in the field language \src{07}}\label{sse:an:fnd:sec:elementary}

\begin{remark}[The language determines elementarity \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:language}
For $L_{\mathrm{of}}=\{0,1,+,-,\cdot,<\}$, quantifier elimination for real
closed fields makes every unital field embedding $J:\No\to\No$ elementary
(base \cref{sse:prop:elementary}); evaluating the quantifier-free equivalent
gives both a formula-by-formula reading and a uniform satisfaction definition
for this particular structure, without a truth predicate for $(V,\in)$. It
says nothing about birthdays, simplicity, the canonical reals, strong sums,
the omega-map, $\exp$ or a derivation. For the exponential language,
$(\No,\exp)$ is a model of the theory of the real exponential field
\cite{vdDE} and Wilkie's model completeness \cite{Wilkie} makes
exponential-field embeddings elementary. For the class carrier source 07
offers, besides the scheme reading, a set-model route: the set-sized
exponential fields $\No_{<\varepsilon}$, $\varepsilon$ an epsilon number, are
elementary and form a cofinal exhaustion, so a tuple and the image of any
set stage lie in a later stage and ordinary set-model elementarity gives the
class statement, with no unrestricted class satisfaction theorem. (Part~V
makes the same observation for exponential homogeneity.)
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Ordered-field homogeneity \src{07}; under GBC; after \lbl{saut:thm:orderedhomogeneity}]\label{sse:an:fnd:thm:homogeneity}
Assume GBC with Global Choice. Every ordered-field isomorphism $f:A\to B$
between set-sized subfields of $\No$ extends to a class ordered-field
automorphism of $\No$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
First extend $f$ to the relative real closures of $A$ and $B$ in $\No$.
These are sets (set-many polynomials, each with finitely many roots), and the
order-preserving isomorphism of real closures extending $f$ is unique. So
assume $A$, $B$ real closed.

\emph{Extension step.} Let $p:D\to E$ be an isomorphism of set-sized real
closed subfields and $x\in\No\setminus D$. Then $x$ is transcendental over
$D$, since a real closed field has no proper ordered algebraic extension.
Put $L=\{p(d):d\in D,\ d<x\}$ and $R=\{p(d):d\in D,\ x<d\}$; these are sets,
$L<R$, and $L\cup R=E$. The simplest separator $y=\{L\mid R\}$ lies outside
$E$, hence is transcendental over $E$, and $x\mapsto y$ extends $p$ to an
ordered isomorphism $D(x)\to E(y)$: factor a polynomial over the real closed
field $D$ into linear factors and quadratic factors of constant sign; the
signs of the linear factors are determined by the cut of $x$, which was
transported exactly. Extend again to relative real closures.

\emph{Back-and-forth.} Fix an $\On$-indexed enumeration $(a_\alpha)$ of $\No$
(Global Choice). Starting from $f$, at stage $\alpha$ apply the extension
step to put $a_\alpha$ into the domain if necessary, then apply it to the
inverse partial isomorphism to put $a_\alpha$ into the range; take unions at
limits. A union of an increasing chain of real closed fields is real closed,
since each polynomial occurs at an earlier stage. For each $\alpha$ the
partial fields, their isomorphism and the history below $\alpha$ are sets,
so this is the set-valued recursion available in GBC. The class union is
total, onto by the back steps, and preserves the ordered-field operations,
because each finite computation occurs at one stage.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
This is \lbl{saut:thm:orderedhomogeneity} of
\fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms}, proved there by the same
back-and-forth; source 07 does not cite it (it compares Ehrlich's absolutely
saturated models \cite{an-EhrlichAbsolute} and the universes report). It is
printed here as the report's home statement because source 07 makes the
size bookkeeping and the separator choice explicit.
\end{wnote}

For example, the identity on $\R$ extends to an ordered isomorphism between
the relative real closures of $\R(\omega)$ and $\R(\omega+1)$ sending $\omega$
to $\omega+1$, since both realize the same cut over $\R$; the theorem gives a
nonidentity automorphism of $\No$ fixing every real and imposing none of the
other structures studied in this report \src{07}.

\begin{proposition}[Omitting a prescribed transcendental element \src{07}; under GBC]\label[proposition]{sse:an:fnd:prop:omit}
Assume GBC with Global Choice. Let $A\subseteq\No$ be a set-sized real
closed subfield and $b\in\No\setminus A$. There is a field self-embedding
of $\No$ fixing $A$ pointwise whose image omits $b$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Run only the forth steps of the preceding proof, starting from $\id_A$, and
maintain that $b$ is transcendental over the current range $E$. In an
extension step at $x$, let $S$ be the relative real closure of $E(b)$ in
$\No$, a set; with $L,R$ as before put $C=\{s\in S:L<s<R\}$ and
$y=\{L\cup C\mid R\}$, which exists because $L\cup C<R$. Then $y$ realizes the
cut $(L,R)$, and $y\notin S$, since $y\in S$ would give $y\in C$ and hence
$y<y$. So $y$ is transcendental over $E(b)$, and therefore $b$ remains
transcendental over the relative real closure of $E(y)$. At limit stages any
algebraic relation would already occur earlier. The final domain is all of
$\No$, and $b$ is transcendental over, hence absent from, the image.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
This is the pure-field, GBC-only analogue of the omitting theorems for
o-minimal class models in Part~III (\cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary} and
its omission lemma) and Part~IV (\cref{sse:co:thm:omit}), which for the
exponential language need GBC+ETR with a class satisfaction relation.
\end{wnote}

\begin{corollary}[Exactly which elements are unavoidable \src{07}; under GBC]\label[corollary]{sse:an:fnd:cor:unavoidable}
Assume GBC with Global Choice. Let $A$ be a set of surreals and
$\Q(A)^{\mathrm{rc}}$ the relative real closure of $\Q(A)$ in $\No$. For a
surreal $b$ the following are equivalent:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $b\in\Q(A)^{\mathrm{rc}}$;
\item every field self-embedding of $\No$ fixing $A$ fixes $b$;
\item every field automorphism of $\No$ fixing $A$ fixes $b$;
\item $b$ lies in the image of every field self-embedding of $\No$ fixing $A$.
\end{enumerate}
The quantification over maps is class-theoretic quantification, not an
intersection over a set of all maps.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
An embedding fixing $A$ fixes $\Q(A)$. The finitely many roots in $\No$ of a
polynomial over $\Q(A)$ form a finite ordered set mapped injectively and
increasingly into itself, so each root is fixed; hence (i) implies (ii), (iii)
and (iv). If $b\notin\Q(A)^{\mathrm{rc}}$, \cref{sse:an:fnd:prop:omit} applied to
the set-sized real closed field $\Q(A)^{\mathrm{rc}}$ gives an embedding fixing
it whose image omits $b$, refuting (iv) and (ii). For (iii), let $(L,R)$ be the
cut of $b$ over $\Q(A)^{\mathrm{rc}}$ and $c=\{L\cup\{b\}\mid R\}$. Then $c>b$
and $c$ realizes the same cut, so the ordered isomorphism over
$\Q(A)^{\mathrm{rc}}$ sending $b$ to $c$ extends through real closures and then,
by \cref{sse:an:fnd:thm:homogeneity}, to an automorphism of $\No$ moving $b$.
\end{proof}
\begin{wnote}
New to the collection. The case $A=\varnothing$ of (i)$\iff$(iii) is
$\Fix(\operatorname{Aut}(\No))=\Q^{\mathrm{rc}}$ in
\lbl{saut:prop:fixedfields} of \fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms};
the corollary extends it to an arbitrary set of parameters and to
self-embeddings and their images. Compare base \cref{sse:prop:fixedgeneral}.
\end{wnote}

This answers a parameter-constrained version of the self-embedding problem in
the pure field language. Naming simplicity, $\Oz$, $\exp$ or a derivation can
change the equivalences, because the extension step no longer applies in the
expanded language \src{07}.

\begin{remark}[Limits of the homogeneity method \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:homlimits}
\Cref{sse:an:fnd:thm:homogeneity} does not start from a proper-class partial
map: its extension step realizes a \emph{set} cut, and fixing a proper
subclass or meeting proper-class many constraints needs another argument.
Nor does real closedness alone give the theorem for arbitrary proper-class
target fields; the separator property is essential. The proof cannot simply
be repeated after adding $\exp$, a derivation or simplicity: polynomial
factorization is what reduces the ordered-field extension step to a cut.
Model completeness gives elementarity of maps that already preserve the
structure; it neither constructs them nor realizes their expanded-language
types from order saturation alone.
\end{remark}

\section{Set-sized fragments and universes \src{07}}\label{sse:an:fnd:sec:universes}

For a set ordinal $\lambda$, $\No_{<\lambda}=\{x:\bday(x)<\lambda\}$ is a set, so
its self-maps, automorphism group and order topology are ordinary set
objects. It is a subfield of $\No$ exactly when $\omega^\lambda=\lambda$
(ordinal exponentiation), and for these epsilon cutoffs it is real closed and
elementary in the surreal exponential field \cite{vdDE}. A regular
uncountable $\kappa$ is an epsilon number, and $\No_{<\kappa}$ realizes every
cut with fewer than $\kappa$ parameters (regularity bounds their lengths, and
the simplest separator has length below $\kappa$); hence it is
$\kappa$-saturated as a real closed field, since a nonalgebraic one-variable
type over a real closed subfield of size below $\kappa$ is determined by its
cut. Its cardinality is $2^{<\kappa}$. If $2^{<\kappa}=\kappa$, ordinary
back-and-forth of length $\kappa$ extends every partial elementary map with
domain of size below $\kappa$ to an automorphism; for $\kappa=\omega_1$ the
continuum hypothesis suffices, and an inaccessible $\kappa$ is another
sufficient choice. These are hypotheses for this saturation-to-homogeneity
argument only, not necessary conditions for explicit self-embeddings; when
$|\No_{<\kappa}|>\kappa$, $\kappa$-saturation alone does not justify a
back-and-forth through the whole carrier \src{07}. Compare base
\eqref{sse:eq:kappahahn} and \cref{sse:prop:cutofftransfer}.

A global map need not preserve a preselected cutoff; Replacement only puts
the image of a set stage inside a later stage. The club of invariant stages
(\cref{sse:an:rem:club}) supplies cofinally many invariant cutoffs for
specified maps and finitary operations; these need not be regular cardinals,
preserving a carrier does not preserve its exponential, and closure under
finitary operations does not give closure under unrestricted set-indexed
Hahn sums.

For a transitive set universe $U$, $\No^U$ consists of the sign sequences and
lengths in $U$: a proper class from inside $U$, a set from outside. For
$U=V_\kappa$ with $\kappa$ inaccessible it is $\No_{<\kappa}$; a general inner
model may lack sign sequences of the outer universe, so its ordinal height
does not determine its surreal carrier. Saturation does not transfer across
universes: if a sign sequence $s$ is new in an outer universe but all its
proper prefixes are old (take a new one of least length), its left and right
proper prefixes form an outer-set cut of old surreals that no old surreal
separates, since every separator has $s$ as a prefix and restriction would
recover $s$ inside. The old field may therefore cease to be saturated over the
outer universe's parameter sets; the universes report
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/surreal-fields-across-universes}
investigates this. In type theory, a universe-polymorphic carrier lives one
level up from its small option families; a theorem that every small cut has a
separator must keep its smallness hypothesis and cannot be reapplied to the
whole carrier. Source 07 recommends two formalization routes, explicit set
cutoffs with closure and transport hypotheses, or an encoded background model
of set or class theory, and warns that no large-cardinal hypothesis should be
inferred from a universe annotation without stated semantics.

\section{Set-theoretic elementary embeddings \src{07}}\label{sse:an:fnd:sec:large}

Under explicitly stated large-cardinal and amenability hypotheses, suppose
$j:(V,\in)\to(M,\in)$ is elementary with $M\subseteq V$ a transitive inner
class containing all ordinals. Applying $j$ to sign-sequence codes gives
$\mathcal J_j(x)=j(x)$, an ordered-field embedding of $\No$ by absoluteness of
the set-based surreal arithmetic; here elementarity refers to the membership
language, much stronger data than ordered-field elementarity. If
$x:\alpha\to\{-,+\}$ then $j(x)$ has domain $j(\alpha)$ and
$j(x)(j(\beta))=x(\beta)$ for $\beta<\alpha$, which says nothing about the
coordinates in $j(\alpha)\setminus j[\alpha]$; likewise for an infinite
support $S$, $j(S)$ can strictly contain $j[S]$. So the map obtained by
applying $j$ to a whole normal-form code must be distinguished from a Hahn lift
transporting each original term over the original index set. Conversely, an
elementary self-embedding of the ordered field $\No$ yields no elementary
embedding of $(V,\in)$; Kunen-type inconsistency theorems concern the latter
and do not prohibit the former \cite{pf-HKP}. Relating the two would need an
explicit interpretation of sets and membership in an expanded surreal
structure and a proof that the map preserves it \src{07}.
\begin{wnote}
This is the subject of
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/large-cardinal-embeddings-and-normal-forms}:
\lbl{lce:prop:J} proves that $\mathcal J_j$ is an ordered-field embedding fixing
$\R$, commuting with the omega-map, preserving and reflecting prefix
simplicity and elementary in the field language, and \lbl{lce:thm:defect}
computes the exact defect between ambient and supportwise transport (the
``critical-point defect analysis'' that source 07 cites). All of it is
conditional on the elementary embedding, not an existence theorem for large
cardinals. Base \cref{sse:sec:foundations} makes the same separation.
\end{wnote}

\section{A staged formalization programme \src{07}}\label{sse:an:sec:formalization}

Source 07 proposes proving local algebra first for arbitrary fields and
ordered groups, and letting the surreal representation enter only where it
supplies a cut, a normal form or an analytic operation, so that no global
class claim is hidden in a type signature. Its Theorem~11.1 (a closed
unbounded class of limit $\kappa$ with $\No_{<\kappa}$ closed under finitely
many named class functions and total finitary operations; for a field
self-embedding, adding $0$, $1$, $+$, $-$, $\cdot$ and the inverse totalized by
$0^{-1}=0$ makes the stage a subfield invariant under the embedding; for an
automorphism, adding its inverse makes the restrictions onto) is
\cref{sse:sr:found:prop:localization} with \cref{sse:sr:found:cor:ranges}
of Part~V, by the same rank-closure argument; the base gives the argument as
prose in \cref{sse:sec:formal}.

\begin{remark}[What the club theorem does not say \src{07}]\label[remark]{sse:an:rem:club}\label{sse:an:form:club}
It is a set-valued rank-closure argument. It does not assert that one cutoff
is invariant under every class map, and it does not assume that all class
maps form a class. A set-indexed family is accommodated only when a single
class evaluation relation for the family is supplied; a family of arbitrary
class functions cannot be inserted as a set parameter by notation. Closure
under finitary operations does not imply closure under externally set-indexed
Hahn sums, since a support can be cofinal in the cutoff; summation needs its
own size and support hypotheses. This is where a proof-assistant interface
should separate an algebraic field embedding from an embedding of a specified
summability structure.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Existing repository interfaces.} Source 07 read the following
declarations at the pinned commit, without a fresh Lean build. All of them
exist at the current repository head, in
\fn{Surreal/HahnSeries/WorkspaceEmbedding.lean} and
\fn{Surreal/Foundations/SignSequenceHahnEmbedding.lean} (paths relative to
\fn{Algebra/SurrealNumbers/}).

\begingroup\small
\begin{longtable}{P{52mm}P{98mm}}
\toprule
Declaration & Role in the programme (source 07) \\
\midrule
\endhead
\code{workspaceEmbedding} & Transports a Hahn workspace along a strictly increasing additive exponent map; the starting point for the second normal-form lift. \\
\code{support\_workspaceEmbedding}, \code{workspaceEmbedding\_injective} & Identify the transported support and prove injectivity, needed for image descriptions and properness witnesses. \\
\code{mapExponentsFamily}, \code{hsum\_mapExponentsFamily} & Preserve the support and finite-fiber conditions of summable families and prove commutation with Hahn sums. \\
\code{addVal\_workspaceEmbedding} & Expresses the induced valuation map; to be kept separate from any theorem that this map is the identity. \\
\code{hahnEmbedding}, \code{hahnOrderRingHom} & Connect a small real Hahn workspace with the sign-sequence surreal field; a bridge from abstract series arguments to the repository's carrier. \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

\paragraph{Six targets.} Source 07's sequence of new formalization targets:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the generic displacement identity and bounded-displacement rigidity for
an ordered field endomorphism (finite algebra and inequalities only);
\item the exponential detector with an explicit convex valuation and one
positive exponential scale outside its valuation ring, after which the
surreal case needs only the interface for the canonical exponential and
valuation;
\item birthday-nonincrease rigidity on ordinal-length sign sequences,
separately from arithmetic, with the key lemma identifying the canonical
prefix cut with a cone of extensions;
\item reuse of the Hahn workspace maps for double lifts, support images,
composition, properness witnesses and the set-parameter stabilizer (the
first lift needs a strictly increasing exponent map, the second also
additivity);
\item continuity versus cofinality for arbitrary ordered-field embeddings
(\cref{sse:an:top:criterion}), then its instance for double lifts, with
continuity encoded locally or on a set carrier and no type of all open
subclasses;
\item the cutoff closure theorem in a universe-relative development, recording
exactly which global statements are represented by coherent families of
smaller theorems.
\end{enumerate}
Each target has a finite specification; none needs a datatype of all
externally existing surreals or of all class functions. Conversely, checking
a bounded model should not be described as verifying an unrestricted
proper-class statement without a separate transfer argument \src{07}.
Compare the base's layers in \cref{sse:sec:formal}.

\section{Source audit and corrections of source 07}\label{sse:an:sec:audit}

Source 07's repository inspection was targeted, at the pinned commit. It read,
under \fn{Algebra/SurrealNumbers/}: \fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms}
(normal-form lifts, proper embeddings, coefficient drift; the portions used
were checked and the main examples reproved);
\fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity} (the bounded-displacement
argument and its exponential application, independently checked, and noted to
include self-embeddings already, as \lbl{cor:embedding} there does);
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/surreal-fields-across-universes} (as
background, not as a certificate for every global assertion);
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/large-cardinal-embeddings-and-normal-forms}
(ambient versus supportwise transport; large-cardinal hypotheses never
inferred from field embeddings); and the two Lean files of
\cref{sse:an:sec:formalization} (signatures and proof bodies; no fresh full
build). It did not read the whole repository, every preserved manuscript or
every imported proof dependency.

\subsection{A correction concerning Taylor supports \src{07}}\label{sse:an:sub:taylorcorrection}
The omnific report contains an accurate theorem that the coefficient Taylor map
is a strong automorphism. A later sentence, after its explicit
proper-embedding examples, said that the ordinary Taylor section cannot be
applied to arbitrary normal forms because its integer shifts can meet. Taken
as a claim about the termwise Taylor extension, this is incorrect. If $S$ is a
well-ordered small-monomial support and $\delta>0$, then $S+\N\delta$ is well
ordered and each target exponent has only finitely many decompositions
$s+n\delta$; intersections are allowed, and finite multiplicity is the
requirement. In the large-monomial convention the same statement concerns
$S-\N\delta$. The proper exponent embedding in the coefficient-gap
construction is needed for preservation of the omnific integers and for the
block geometry, not for the existence of the Taylor automorphism
(\cref{sse:an:rem:taylorOz,sse:an:rem:gapsharp}).
\begin{wnote}
Confirmed and already corrected in the collection. The sentence was in
Section~27.2 of \fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms}, after its
Theorem~27.3: ``The ordinary Taylor section alone is not a map on arbitrary
normal forms, since supports could meet its integer shifts''. It contradicted
the report's own display \lbl{opa:as:eq:taylor}, the flow $\mathcal T_{d,\delta}$
that \lbl{saut:thm:coeffflow} proves to be a strong automorphism defined on
every normal form. Commit \code{f06e67d10} (3 October 2026), made before this
report was placed, replaced it by: the ordinary Taylor flow alone does not give
those maps, since $\mathcal T_{d,\delta}$ is a strong automorphism but never
preserves $\Oz$, and the proper exponent embedding separates the scales;
followed by a dated correction note quoting the old wording and crediting this
manuscript. No statement, label or numbering of that report changed, and no
result was retracted. Source 07's own wording (``no repository file was
modified as part of preparing the article'') refers to its preparation and
remains true.
\end{wnote}

\subsection{Two sign conventions in the KKS preprint \src{07}}\label{sse:an:sub:kks}
Source 07 reports that in the author PDF of \cite{KKS} it inspected, the
series displayed in Example~4.4 has exponents $-1/n$, an increasing sequence
which is not summable under the stated Conway normal-form convention, and
that $+1/n$ gives the decreasing-exponent series of the intended Puiseux
argument (used in \cref{sse:an:rem:nonstrong}). It also reports that a
displayed derivation in Example~4.3 shifts a small-monomial exponent by $-1$
while describing contraction for the valuation $\val(t^g)=g$, whereas a $+1$
shift has the asserted direction. Source 07 calls these local formula issues
and uses neither displayed expression.
\begin{wnote}
These are source 07's claims about an external preprint. They were
\emph{not} checked against \cite{KKS} for this report. Part~V independently
uses the decreasing support $\omega^{1/n}$ in its version of the
Example~4.4 argument.
\end{wnote}
Source 07 treats \cite[Question~5.4]{KKS} (nontrivial exponential
$1$-automorphisms) differently from a notation issue: the repository gives a
direct proof excluding them under the stated meaning of fixing leading terms
(\lbl{cor:question54} of \fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity};
base \cref{sse:thm:exprigidity}), source 07 checked that argument, and it
therefore does not list that question as open. This is a mathematical check
of the argument, not a claim of external peer review of the repository
manuscript or that no other author has proved the same statement.

\section{Research questions of source 07}\label{sse:an:sec:questions}

Source 07 separates questions left open in the literature it checked from
refinements suggested by its constructions; a proposed question is not a
claim that its answer is unknown throughout the literature, and the first
step is always to fix a language, a support convention and a foundational
setting. Its immediate priorities are the generic rigidity formalization, the
explicit double-lift interfaces, and a precise exponential lifting criterion.
Base questions are those of \cref{sse:sec:agenda}, cited by title.

\subsection{The omega-map and analytic intersections}

\begin{question}[Omega-compatible field self-embeddings \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:omega}
Classify the unital field self-embeddings with $j(\omega^x)=\omega^{j(x)}$.
Which are proper, and which are strongly additive? The automorphism problem
is \cite[Questions~5.6--5.7]{KKS}. A tractable first target is an extension
criterion for an order map on generalized epsilon numbers, with all the
compatibility conditions imposed by the remaining normal forms.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``$\omega$-map compatibility'' and
\lbl{opa:as:q:exp}. Conditionally on a nontrivial elementary
$j:V\to M$ as in
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/large-cardinal-embeddings-and-normal-forms},
\lbl{lce:prop:J} supplies field self-embeddings commuting with the omega-map
(\cref{sse:an:fnd:sec:large}); this gives examples, not a classification.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[The image of the exponential value action \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:valueaction}
Which ordered additive self-embeddings $\tau$ of the value group lift to an
exponential field self-embedding? Rigidity controls the identity fiber and
cofinality controls uniqueness between two lifts, but neither gives existence.
Find necessary and sufficient conditions, first for a precisely specified
family of $\tau$.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Characterize exponential value actions'' and
\lbl{q:image} of \fn{docs/surreal/exponential-automorphism-rigidity} (stated
there for automorphisms).
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Removing the cofinality assumption \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:cofinality}
If two exponential self-embeddings induce the same value map, must they
coincide without a cofinal-image hypothesis? The missing step is that a value
in one image must amplify a nonzero difference beyond a fixed ambient bound.
Replace that step or construct an explicit pair for which it fails.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
Same question as the base's ``Remove or justify the cofinality hypothesis''
and Part~IV's question on two noncofinal exponential embeddings.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Cofinality of exponential images \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:boundedexp}
Which order-cofinality types occur for the image of an exponential
self-embedding of all of $\No$? In particular, does a bounded-image example
exist? The bounded strong field embedding of
\cref{sse:an:top:section} does not commute with $\exp$ and does not answer this.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
Answered \emph{conditionally} in this report: under GBC together with
$\mathsf{ETR}$ and a class satisfaction relation for the exponential
language, Part~III's \cref{sse:at:thm:bounded-elementary} and Part~IV's
\cref{sse:co:cor:exp-bounded} give proper elementary exponential
self-embeddings with bounded image (in Part~IV, $\omega^{-1}<|e(x)|<\omega$
for $x\ne0$). Without those hypotheses (in ZFC, or in plain GBC) this report
gives no answer. Compare the
base question ``Proper exponential copies''.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Simultaneous exponential and differential structure \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:expdiff}
Classify the self-embeddings preserving both $\exp$ and
$\partial_{\mathrm{BM}}$, optionally fixing $\omega$. An extension theorem in
either language alone does not give homogeneity in the combined language. A
first target is extending a prescribed omega-series substitution to the whole
combined structure.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Differential centralizers''. On $\mathcal A$
the strong ones are classified, conditionally, by
\cref{sse:an:ana:omega-series-classification}.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Differentiation together with the omega-map \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:diffomega}
A differential embedding sends $\omega$ to $\omega+c$ with $c\in\R$, and the
omega-map removes the translation (\cref{sse:an:ana:differential-constraint}).
Does fixing both operations force all higher scales, or can a nontrivial map
remain? The equation $j(\omega)=\omega$ is only a starting constraint.
\end{question}

\subsection{Series structure and integer parts}

\begin{question}[Beyond exact-monomial lifts \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:beyondmono}
Develop a classification allowing $j(t^g)=c_gt^{\tau(g)}(1+u_g)$ and a deformed
coefficient section. State separately the algebraic cocycle conditions and the
support conditions keeping every required family summable; the exact-monomial
case should be a proved special case.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Beyond monomial images''.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[When is strong additivity automatic? \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:autostrong}
Find structural hypotheses on a proper self-embedding preserving and
reflecting $\Oz$ that force strong additivity. An automorphism argument
using all multiplier tests need not work in a proper image, because the
missing test elements may carry exactly the lost information. Seek a sharp
condition on the supply of multipliers in the image.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
For automorphisms this is \lbl{opa:as:thm:main} (automatic strongness of
omnific automorphisms); the question concerns proper embeddings. For
exact-monomial maps, \cref{sse:an:hahn:truncation} reduces strongness to
truncation compatibility.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Initiality of algebraic images \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:initial}
Which additive or field self-embeddings have an image that is initial in the
sign tree? Distinguish this from requiring the map to preserve simplicity: an
order-and-simplicity embedding with initial image is rigid, while a field
isomorphism onto an initial subfield is a different problem. Find effective
normal-form criteria in a restricted family.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
For \emph{order-preserving} self-maps of all of $\No$, which includes every
field self-embedding and every ordered additive one, base
\cref{sse:thm:initial} already answers this: the image is simplicity-initial
exactly when the map is onto. The question keeps content for maps between
cutoffs, for embeddings of set-sized fields into $\No$ (initial embeddings
in the sense of \cite{EhrlichKaplan}), and for additive maps that are not
order preserving.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Conjugacy of proper copies \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:conjugacy}
Classify the images of double lifts up to ambient field automorphism, and
then up to automorphisms preserving $\Oz$, $\exp$ or the derivation.
Value-image geometry and the coefficient section are candidate invariants. Do
not transfer a classification in one language to a richer one without proof.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Fixed points, intersections and iteration \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:fixed}
For an explicit proper embedding $j$, determine $\Fix(j)$,
$\bigcap_{n<\omega}j^n(\No)$ and the corresponding value subgroups. For
double lifts, begin with the iterated images of the underlying order map.
Describe when these intersections keep real closedness, strong summation, or
the structure of a surreal substructure.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
Partly answered in the base: \cref{sse:prop:core} (the core $K_\infty(j)$ is
real closed and the hierarchy stabilizes at the countable intersection),
\eqref{sse:eq:hcore} for monomial lifts, and the computation \eqref{sse:eq:Jcore}
for $J_-$; compare the base question ``Prescribe the reversible core''. For
double-order embeddings, \cref{sse:sr:ord:fixedintersection} identifies the
intersection with $\Fix(j)$.
\end{wnote}

\subsection{Simplicity and controlled deformation}

\begin{question}[Sharp birthday-growth bounds \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:growth}
For natural embedding families, determine the least useful ordinal function
$B$ with $\bday(jx)\le B(\bday(x))$. Equality with the identity bound is
rigid, so the first nontrivial bounds should say exactly where a sign-tree or
normal-form construction increases complexity.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Weaker birthday controls''. For double-order
embeddings, \cref{sse:an:ord:ordinal-lift} shows that every strictly
increasing $h:\On\to\On$ occurs as an exact birthday function.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Rigidity from selected cut operations \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:cuts}
The full canonical-cut operation rigidifies the structure
(\cref{sse:prop:cutrigid}). Which restricted collection of cut constructors
suffices? Separate finite cuts, bounded-birthday cuts, sign-chain limits and
cuts subject to cardinality bounds. A good answer includes a rigidity theorem
and a proper embedding showing that a nearby weaker condition fails.
\end{question}

\begin{question}[Order-and-simplicity versus arithmetic \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:treearith}
Characterize the order-and-simplicity self-embeddings that also preserve
addition, multiplication or a prescribed set of arithmetic operations. The
prefix and localized-cone examples generally fail arithmetic and the
normal-form lifts generally fail simplicity, so the intersection is a
substantive classification problem.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
Near-duplicate of the base question ``Field and simplicity together''.
Conditionally on a nontrivial elementary $j:V\to M$, \lbl{lce:prop:J} gives
nonidentity field self-embeddings preserving and reflecting prefix
simplicity (\cref{sse:an:fnd:sec:large}).
\end{wnote}

\subsection{Foundations and proof-assistant targets}

\begin{question}[Coherent cutoff embeddings \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:coherent}
Give necessary and sufficient coherence conditions on a family
$j_\kappa:\No_{<\kappa}\to\No_{<\kappa}$ that produce a global class
self-embedding. The club theorem gives restrictions of an existing map; it
does not glue arbitrary local choices. A first target is a construction over
a specified club sequence of invariant stages.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Universe and cutoff absoluteness'' and Part~V's
\cref{sse:sr:found:cor:ranges}.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Axiom strength of homogeneity statements \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:axioms}
Locate the exact principles needed for each global extension theorem: set
recursion with class parameters, Global Choice, or a stronger class recursion
principle. Distinguish homogeneity of a fixed class structure from an
assertion needing a truth predicate for the whole universe.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Minimal foundational strength''.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[A reusable formal embedding library \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:library}
Implement interfaces for ordered maps, sign-tree maps, strong Hahn maps,
valued-field maps and exponential or differential maps, with explicit
forgetful constructions. Make failed implications into formal
counterexamples where a suitable set-sized model is available. The generic
amplification theorem and the continuity criterion are small, high-value
first milestones.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
Compare the base question ``Near-term formalization''.
\end{wnote}

\begin{question}[Ambient transport and supportwise transport \src{07}]\label[question]{sse:an:q:transport}
For elementary maps between ambient set-theoretic models, compare the map on a
surreal code with the external map on its original normal-form terms.
Determine exact support thresholds for equality and for landing inside the
target model, keeping all hypotheses on models, class definability and
support size.
\end{question}
\begin{wnote}
Partly addressed, conditionally, by \lbl{lce:thm:defect} of
\fn{docs/foundations-and-computation/large-cardinal-embeddings-and-normal-forms},
which computes the exact defect $\mathcal J_j(x)$ minus the supportwise
transport.
\end{wnote}

\section{Status and non-claims of source 07}\label{sse:an:sec:status}

The following limitations of source 07 (article and \code{README.txt}) are
kept as stated.
\begin{itemize}
\item It is a mathematical research exposition with complete proofs of its
principal constructions and deductions. Repository claims were checked
selectively; no new Lean certification, exhaustive classification or claim of
literature priority is asserted. The set-parameter stabilizer and the
cofinal uniqueness statement are presented as deductions, not priority claims.
\item No fresh Lean build was run; the Lean interfaces were read at the pinned
commit. No repository file was modified.
\item The repository inspection was targeted, not a reading of the whole
repository, every preserved manuscript or every imported dependency.
\item Deep background theorems (differential homogeneity, composition,
substitution and inversion of omega-series, exponential homogeneity) are
cited with their roles and not reproved; results resting on them are
conditional on them.
\item A research question is a proposed direction, not a claim that its status
has been exhaustively settled across the literature.
\item Compilation and visual inspection certify presentation, not theorems; the
mathematical support is the proofs and the cited results. No finite numerical
experiment is offered as evidence for a class-sized assertion.
\item The KKS remarks are local formula issues, not a claim that the
programme of \cite{KKS} fails; the exponential-rigidity check is not a claim of
external review or priority.
\item The club theorem gives no cutoff invariant under every class map, forms
no class of all class maps, and closure under finitary operations does not
give closure under set-indexed sums.
\item Checking a bounded model is not verification of a proper-class
statement without a transfer argument.
\item Homogeneity in each separate language does not give homogeneity in the
combined exponential and differential language.
\item The verification checked the constructions at their logical bottlenecks:
set size of supports, reverse well-ordering, finite coefficient multiplicity,
convolution, positivity, properness, and forward preservation versus
reflection; the rigidity arguments use explicit finite identities.
\end{itemize}
\begin{wnote}
Collection status: source 07 cites \fn{omnific-preserving-automorphisms} and
\fn{exponential-automorphism-rigidity} but not
\fn{docs/surcomplex/surcomplex-field-automorphisms}, whose
\lbl{saut:prop:discrete}, \lbl{saut:thm:coeffflow},
\lbl{saut:thm:orderedhomogeneity} and \lbl{saut:prop:fixedfields} it reproves
or extends; those credits are given above. Its one finding about the
collection (\cref{sse:an:sub:taylorcorrection}) was confirmed and corrected.
\end{wnote}
\appendix

Result family & Mathematical basis & Verification status in this delivery \\

\endhead

The accompanying \code{verify.py} uses exact rational arithmetic. It checks
finite sign-order and prefix identities, the rational branch formulas and
iterates, finite Hahn relabeling identities, and binomial coefficients.
These tests are designed to catch transcription mistakes. They do not
prove statements about arbitrary ordinal-length signs, proper classes,
real coefficients, or all well-ordered supports. The mathematical proofs
are the argument for those generalizations.

\begin{wnote}In the collection this program is
\fn{code/03-hahn-lifts-verify.py}, byte-identical to the delivered
\fn{verify.py}; it writes \fn{verification.json} beside itself, and its
delivered record is \fn{data/03-hahn-lifts-verification.json}. Run on a fresh
copy at placement it passed 195,579 checks and reproduced the record up to
line endings. The delivered \fn{verification.txt}, a byte copy of the record,
is not shipped. The finite checks of sources 02, 04 and 05 are described in
their Parts; sources 06 and 07 shipped none.\end{wnote}

\section{Source audit and reproducibility}\label[appendix]{sse:app:sources}

The repository inspection was pinned to commit
\code{b0d4af4f30b24529fec877e2ad4a5952888146e9}. The principal recent paper was
read in arXiv version \code{2509.22374v3}, whose submission history dates
that version to 23 April 2026. Version pinning is intentional: question
numbers and foundational conventions should not be silently transferred
between versions. Rendered HTML dates need not be publication or
submission dates. The relevant PDF page containing Questions~5.4 and~5.6
was also visually inspected during research.

The report uses no bundled external font files and no external figures.
The source is a single \code{article.tex}, with its bibliography included.
A standard TeX Live installation with \code{newtx}, \code{amsmath},
\code{tcolorbox}, \code{hyperref}, and \code{cleveref} can build it with
\code{latexmk -pdf article.tex}. The included build script and README
record the same procedure. This delivery contains no uncompiled Lean
file represented as a verified result; the API sketch in
\cref{sse:sec:formal} is explicitly a design sketch.

\begin{wnote}This paragraph describes source 03's delivery. The present
\fn{article.tex} is the merged report; it builds with \code{latexmk -pdf}
under pdf\LaTeX{} (MiKTeX was used), and its bibliography is the union of
the six sources'. The delivered README is replaced by the report README; the
delivered build script is \fn{code/03-hahn-lifts-build.sh} and still names
the delivered file names. The pins of the other sources are in
\cref{sse:app:provenance}.\end{wnote}

\subsection{The superseded edition 01}
Manuscript 01 (\emph{Self-Embeddings of the Surreal Numbers: Order,
simplicity, field structure, exponential rigidity, and foundations}) is an
earlier edition of 05: 89\% of its distinct eight-word sequences occur in
05, all 52 of its labels reappear there unchanged, the package layout,
preamble and title coincide, 01's pin and PDF precede 05's, and 05 says that
it ``incorporates and extends earlier working notes prepared for the same
project''. 05 also settles two of 01's research questions (an exact
criterion for the vanishing derivative, \cref{sse:part:co}, and the
existence of noncofinal elementary exponential copies, under the class
framework of \cref{sse:part:at}). Nothing of 01's mathematics is missing from
05. Nothing of 01 is shipped; it survives in its archive. Three things of
01 do not reappear in 05:
\begin{itemize}
\item one audit bullet, saying that the repository's simplest-separator
theorem has an explicit existence premise and that filling a cut is a
separate statement (true: \fn{existsUnique_simplest_separator} in
\fn{Surreal/Foundations/SignSequenceSimplicity.lean} and
\fn{exists_separator_of_small_sets} in
\fn{SignSequenceCutOperation.lean});
\item the first paragraph of its proof of the omission theorem, which 05
moved into a lemma on small types;
\item its finite-check program, a different program from 05's
(61,042 against 146,914 assertions). Its groups for prefix cones
(\code{canonical\_cone}, \code{sign\_prefix\_*}), repetition by two and three
(\code{sign\_repeat\_2\_*}, \code{sign\_repeat\_3\_*}), explicit dilation
difference quotients (\code{derivative\_q\_gt\_1}, \code{derivative\_q\_lt\_1})
and numerical two-probe displacement (\code{two\_map\_displacement},
\code{two\_probe\_amplification}) have no counterpart group in 05's program,
which tests a random padding map and symbolic product identities instead.
01's program passes on a fresh copy and reproduces its record up to line
endings.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{What each source contributed}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{03 (base).} The text of Part~I and Appendices A and B, with
every label prefixed \lbl{sse:} and every reference updated; its section
$n$ is Section $n+1$ here, and its numbered statements keep their positions
within their sections. Its finite checks are shipped.
\item \textbf{02.} The local tree embeddings fixing a set and all ordinals,
blocks of ordinal length, the bounded map $J_c$ fixing $\omega$, elementarity
in the order and group languages, cutoff cardinalities, the conditional
large-cardinal section, the lexicographic word action and iterates, and its
questions and plan (\cref{sse:part:pf}). Its finite checks are shipped.
\item \textbf{04.} The sharpest bound for the bounded double lift, an
embedding with fixed field exactly $\R$, the core formula, the conditional
bounded and cofinal elementary embeddings, abstract exponential value
displacement and cofinal pair rigidity, the exponent-lift criterion for
commuting with $\exp$, a Lean integration plan and an audit of invalid
arguments (\cref{sse:part:at}). Its finite checks are shipped.
\item \textbf{05.} The local-padding description of all order-and-simplicity
embeddings, the derivative criterion, a continuous non-strong automorphism,
diagonal derivations, its own route to the elementary embeddings with a
logarithmic range bound, and its questions (\cref{sse:part:co}). Its finite
checks and build record are shipped.
\item \textbf{06.} A credited synthesis of the omnific-pair results of the
collection, the exponential defect cocycle, the translation parameter, club
localization, the fixed-point theorem of Bagayoko--van der Hoeven, a Lean
status table and its agenda (\cref{sse:part:sr}). It shipped no code.
\item \textbf{07.} Convex tree images, the omega-series classification
(conditional on imported literature), unavoidable points as a relative real
closure, homogeneity, the correction of a sentence of
\fn{omnific-preserving-automorphisms}, two alleged sign slips in KKS, a staged
formalization program and its questions (\cref{sse:part:an}). It shipped no
code.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Where the merge had to choose}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Base 03 rather than 05} (\cref{sse:sec:sources}): 03 has the
weaker hypotheses on the shared theorems and no ETR, satisfaction class or
large cardinal; 05's additions enter as \cref{sse:part:co}.
\item \textbf{One home per shared result.} A result proved by several
members but not by the base is printed by one home source and pointed to by
the others: local padding (05), local tree embeddings and ordinal blocks
(02), elementary embeddings and abstract exponential rigidity (04),
continuity for embeddings between arbitrary ordered fields and homogeneity
(07), club localization, the Bagayoko--van der Hoeven fixed points and the
translation parameter (06), $\operatorname{Frac}(\Oz)=\No$ (02). Different proofs are kept
as second routes; each Part's correspondence table accounts for every
numbered item of its source.
\item \textbf{Notation.} The base's symbols are used throughout; the renames
are listed in \cref{sse:sec:conventions} and in each Part.
\item \textbf{Collection results.} Every re-proof of a result already in
the collection is printed once with the existing label (\cref{sse:sec:status}
and the Parts), never as new; sentences of the sources claiming such
results as developments of their own are corrected in \wtag{} notes.
\item \textbf{A correction elsewhere.} Source 07 (\cref{sse:part:an}) showed
that a sentence of \fn{docs/surreal/omnific-preserving-automorphisms}
(Section~27.2) about the Taylor section was false as stated; it was
corrected in commit \code{f06e67d10}, before the placement.
\item \textbf{Checked claims.} The repository claims of the six sources
(declaration names, file contents, labels of other reports) were checked at
the placement commit; none was found false, and the Lean status statements
are repeated as checked. The claim of sources 02 and 04 that the birthday
cutoff $\No_{<\lambda}$ is a subfield exactly when $\lambda$ is an epsilon
number was checked, and its converse is proved in a \wtag{} note
(\cref{sse:pf:rem:epsilon}). Source 07's two alleged sign slips in KKS were not
checked.
\end{itemize}
